Two nullable `Booking` columns (`startDisplayTimezone`/`endDisplayTimezone`, mirroring the place columns' authority rule) + `bookingEndZones` as the one "what zone is each end in, as far as we know" answer, read by both `tripZoneCrossings` and `eventDisplayZones` — so a pinned pair partitions the itinerary like real airports do. A chip per leg (`WhenField` span `zones`), **editable only when no place answers**; placed endpoints stay read-only statements.
  - **Slice 4d — the shelf's schedule sheet types in the day's zone — done (2026-07-26, session 128):** slices 4a-4c wired the two forms and stopped; the Trip-mode `ScheduleSheet` (`DayView`, ADR-0116 §5) also has a `WhenField` and also built instants — in `trip.timezone` — so an idea slotted at 19:00 on a day outside the primary zone reappeared shifted, with no chip to say so. `authoringZone(base, {date, time}, evidence)` (`lib/places.ts`) generalizes `EventForm`'s local two-pass `derivedZone` (rule 8) and is now the one answer for **any** surface turning a wall-clock into an instant; it reads the single `ZoneEvidence` (so `EventForm` stopped hand-assembling a context, session-102 rule) and stands in the **day's noon** when no time is typed yet, so a fresh draft starts in its day's zone rather than the primary. The sheet gained the chip (editable only when no place answers, writing the same `displayTimezone` override), its `nextSlot` prefill now reads the clock it will be typed on, and the schedule toast confirms the time in the event's own zone. ADR-0107 session-128 amendment.
- **Plan mode's day machinery is single-zone** (open, from session 128) — `PlanDay` computes gaps, gap labels, the gap-fill create and the drag-to-another-day round-trip in one base zone (`trip.timezone`). It is internally consistent, so session 128 deliberately left it whole rather than fixing the authoring half; moving it to per-day zones is one change covering all four, and it must settle whether a cross-day drag keeps the wall-clock or the instant. Amend ADR-0107 when built.
- **Flight/zone-crossing legibility in the view** — **done (2026-07-24, session 93):** both day timelines (`EventCard` + Plan `BuilderRow`) now show the **elapsed duration** on transport rows (always) and any zone-shifted row, sitting on a meta line with the shift pill under the time so it doesn't crowd the row — a zone shift no longer misreads the span (`07:15→11:00 −3` reads as its true 6h45). `eventDurationLabel` (`lib/places.ts`, instant-based, per-category unit via `eventDurationUnit`, ADR-0084). Long titles **clamp to two lines** (`.wp-event-title-txt`, `-webkit-line-clamp`) so a two-airport route name can't blow up the card; the hard/soft tag stays a sibling and never clips.
- **Long-route layout in the day timelines** — **done (2026-07-24, sessions 94–95):** a route too long for one line (two full official place names) wrapped into a block in Plan mode and truncated away the **destination** in Trip mode, because both surfaces rendered the stored title string. Session 94 rendered the route as separate values and added a stacked SVG rail — which still truncated at ~150px and looked like debris. **Session 95 replaced it:** place names are **shortened for display** (`lib/place-label.ts` — strips generic category phrasing like `נמל התעופה`/`Airport`, so nine patterns cover every airport/station without a per-place dictionary; display-only, never destructive, fails to "no change"), which puts the route back on one inline line; if even that overflows the row goes **destination-primary** (`מ־origin` meta) — never clamped. One **deterministic** decision drives title+meta (`ui/route-display.tsx` — a pure function of the shortened names via `ROUTE_INLINE_MAX_CHARS`); an earlier measured version made Plan mode fall back where Trip mode didn't (the builder row has less title width), so a shared threshold replaced it — same input on both surfaces, no divergence, and no ResizeObserver. Shortening lives in the two shared title components (`EventTitle` for events, `BookingTitle` for bookings) so Trip/Plan/hero/Index all agree; the detail + form keep full names. Rail, its icons, and the session-93 title clamp deleted. ADR-0059 §3 session-95 amendment. **Related, since built:** a user-set place nickname (below) is the durable way to get `נתב״ג`, and an airport now derives the city it serves above the stripping (ADR-0166 §18).
- **A stored route title reads as a route everywhere** — **done (2026-07-24, session 101):** session 95 shortened inside the two title **components**, which left every surface that receives a **title string** printing the stored `origin ← destination` raw — full official names plus the low-sitting text arrow. Reported from a real trip: a soft event's overlap flag read `חופף ל-נמל התעופה בן גוריון ← נמל התעופה הבינלאומי קפלאוויק` beside a flight card reading `בן גוריון ← קפלאוויק`. A stored title now has a display form: `lib/route-title.ts` owns both directions (`routeTitle` moved out of `booking-edit`, `parseRouteTitle`, one `ROUTE_TITLE_ARROW`; the space-padded separator is what makes parsing safe, and an unmatched title comes back untouched — "no change", never a wrong name), and `ui/TitleLabel` renders any title route-aware, so the conflict flag (`EventCard`/`Board`/`EventForm`), the hard-edit/delete gate, both ⋯ menu headers, transition rows and the day-row fallbacks agree with the rest of the app. Copy that wraps a title is **split around the node** (`conflictWarn.before/after`, the `confirm.*Body` strings) rather than interpolating it; `shortRoute` replaces four copies of "shorten both endpoints"; `shortTitleText` covers the string-only spots (change-feed narration, SR labels). Arrow sweep: `arrows.route` gone from copy and the lint guard extended to `i18n/**` literals + JSX template literals, the TimePicker's CSS `content: '↩'` → `<Icon name="undo">`, unused `ICONS.fxUp`/`fxDown` deleted, `Modal`/`Sheet`/`RowManageSheet` titles widened to `ReactNode`. ADR-0059 §3 session-101 amendment.
- **Place nickname / short label** — **done (2026-08-08, as Workstream E's manual override; [ADR-0166 §18](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md)).** `Place.nickname`, set on the rename form, outranking the derived city label and the name-stripping below it. Deferred since session 91 on "build when the long-name clutter warrants it" — what warranted it was not clutter but **ambiguity**: `P931` cannot say whether Ben Gurion serves Tel Aviv or Jerusalem, and a person can.
- **Trip destination + primary timezone + shared `ZonePicker`** (ADR-0113 — **building in slices; the foundational slice of the multi-zone workstream above**):
  - **Slice 1 — backend + data model — done (2026-07-24, session 82):** `Trip` structured destination fields (`destinationGooglePlaceId`/`Lat`/`Lng`/`CountryCode`, nullable) + migration, mirrored in `@waypoint/shared` (`tripSchema`/`createTripSchema`), persisted in `createTrip`. The **trip-agnostic `/destinations/search` + `/destinations/resolve`** endpoints (new `DestinationsController`/`Service` in the places module): geo-type-restricted autocomplete (`DESTINATION_PRIMARY_TYPES`) + a `geocode` on `google-places.client.ts` (reads ISO `countryCode` off `addressComponents`, stays Pro-tier) → `geo-tz` derived `timezone` + `candidateZones` for known multi-zone countries (`resolveDestinationSchema`/`destinationResultSchema`). Authed by the global guard, **per-user** throttled (the `PlacesThrottlerGuard` tracker now keys per-user when there's no `tripId`), no persistence. `api-contract.md` updated.
  - **Slice 2 — shared `ZonePicker` — done (2026-07-24, session 83):** net-new `ui/primitives/ZonePicker` (via `Modal`/`useOverlay`) — a searchable sheet over `Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')` (the full IANA set, no shipped dataset), rows reading `City · GMT±N`, suggested candidates first (device zone + the trip's place zones + current value), search by city/zone/offset. Exported `zoneLabel`/`zoneCity`/`zoneOffset` helpers. **Replaced trip settings' hardcoded 5-item `TZ_OPTIONS` `<select>`** with a select-shaped trigger opening it. The per-event zone chip (ADR-0110 §3) reuses the same primitive when that lands — one primitive, (now two of) three call sites.
  - **Slice 3 — creation-flow destination picker — done (2026-07-24, session 84):** the creation form's free-text `destination` is now a `ui/DestinationPicker` (a trigger + search sheet reusing PlacePicker's `.pp-*` styling) over a lighter creation hook `lib/useDestinationSearch.ts` (session token + pause-gated debounce, no trip / no persist / no outbox) calling `searchDestinations`/`resolveDestination` on `lib/api.ts`. A pick sets the display name + structured fields + the **derived-default primary timezone** shown inline as a tappable chip; a **multi-zone country** shows the soft "spans several zones" note and opens the shared `ZonePicker` (suggested = device + `candidateZones`). A "use as typed" fallback keeps creation unblocked (name only, zone = device default). `createTrip` sends the structured fields + chosen timezone. **Origin stays derived** from the outbound flight (ADR-0107 §3/§5, not stored). Currency-from-country still deferred. **ADR-0113 fully implemented.**
  - **Follow-up — settings destination picker — done (2026-07-24, session 87):** trip settings' free-text `destination` input becomes the same `ui/DestinationPicker` (`DetailsEditor` in `TripSettings`), so an edited destination sets the structured fields + (on a resolved pick) the derived primary timezone, exactly like creation. `updateTripSchema` gains the four destination structured fields — **nullable** here (unlike `createTripSchema`) so a "use as typed" edit clears the now-stale coordinates rather than leaving the old place's point behind; `TripsService.updateTrip` persists them. The wire's `null`-to-clear is coerced back to the local `undefined` shape at both merge points via a shared `coerceTripPatch` (`lib/cache.ts`, used by the cache mirror + the in-memory `applyControlChangeToTrip`). Unlike creation, a "use as typed" pick keeps the trip's existing zone (an established trip already has a meaningful one). Backend + cache tests.

### Map panel — second pass (post-Phase-6 field report, triaged in `planning/2026-07-26-session-135-map-panel-second-pass-triage-and-phasing.md`)

Fifteen items reported from **using** the finished tab. Four are defects, three are documented decisions the owner is reversing, two are new surfaces with money attached, the rest are ergonomics on a shell proportioned against a mockup. The session note carries the per-item trace (root cause + file + line) and the per-phase reading list; the phases below are one branch / one PR each, ordered cheap-and-decided first.

**A third pass added eight more (#16–#23) and five phases (7–11), triaged in `planning/2026-07-28-session-145-eight-more-from-the-field-triaged.md`.** They share this heading, this numbering and this ordering rule: they are reports against the same shipped tab, so a second `#1` or a second `Phase 1` in the same epic would be unreadable. Two of the eight only **answer an open question** on a phase already listed here (#19 settles session 135's #5 question; #20 joins Phase 3's zoom ladder), and one — #18 — is work that was **wrongly parked behind Phase 6a's cost gate** and is in fact free. See Phases 7–11 below.

**Camera fix (2026-07-27, session 139)** (`planning/2026-07-27-session-139-refit-when-dwarfed.md`): the camera never zoomed back **in**. ADR-0121 §7's re-fit guard was a bare bounds-containment check, so once the view was wide — a day whose places are hours apart, or a bad first fit — every later, tighter set was contained by it and nothing was ever owed: a category chip, `אולי`, `מה נשאר`, changing the day, even narrowing to a single pin, all left the frame out. The guard is now contained **and** filling at least `MAP_REFIT_FILL_SHARE` (0.4) of the view on at least one axis. ADR-0121 §7 amended; it also removes at its root the second hazard session 134 worked around. **The share is a legibility number and wants Phase 3's device pass** — same tuning cluster as `MAP_ZOOM`, and note it makes #15's too-close `SINGLE_PIN` = 15 more visible, since filtering to one pin now actually zooms there.

**Third-pass reports (2026-07-27, session 138) — both fixed in one branch** (`planning/2026-07-27-session-138-now-cue-and-distance-sort.md`): the tab now says what is **happening now** (it had no "now" at all — `placeBlock` was a two-state ahead/behind partition, so a 13:00-14:00 lunch read as `מה שלפנינו` at 13:54 while the board said `עכשיו`; it now reads `deriveNow` via `currentDestination`, and the cue is an amber `עכשיו` tag plus a **pulsing** pin ring — the canvas's second amber cue, amending ADR-0121 §6); and **locating you no longer sorts you** (the session-134 on-open offer landed on a flag that also drove the list order, so the day silently left schedule order when coordinates arrived — split into `located`, a fact, and `sortByDistance`, an intent only the chip states). Also answered: the day connector is Plan-mode-only **on purpose** (ADR-0121 §10 — with the order on the pins, the line's remaining job is revealing the day's shape, a planning question); reopening that is a §10 amendment, not a bug, and belongs with Phase 3's canvas work if it is wanted.

**Phase 1 shipped in session 136** (`planning/2026-07-27-session-136-map-phase-1-scope-and-references.md`): the day strip's tap now clears `כל הימים` as an intent, a linked booking's way-in emits both entries, a pin tap raises the sheet and centres its row, the pin's number renders on the row, and both modes open day-scoped (ADR-0109 amended). It also answered Phase 4's triage — see that line.

**Phase 2 shipped in session 141** (`planning/2026-07-27-session-141-the-split-earns-its-screen-built.md`, designed in session 140): [ADR-0122](decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md) is built — the two fixed rows are **one decluttered row floating over the canvas** (the split is the whole body, the pane runs under the chips, and the fit's top inset is derived from the same constant that writes `--map-controls-h`), three controls at rest with the facets opening in place and the categories as glyph + count, `קרוב עכשיו` in the sheet's own top row, the list-sliver `peek` retired for a **`map`** stop that is the sheet's top row and nothing of the list, `full` a third `SnapStop` variant that stops below the row, the drag a region with a slop threshold / window listeners / capture at drag start / a velocity term, the handle a real ARIA splitter, the pre-prompt canvas furniture, and a pin tap that moves nothing because the tapped place surfaces as a **card on the canvas**. **Two things it hands on, both to Phase 3 (see that line):** the location permission has no way in at the map extreme until `recentre` is revised, and §7's card-driven **bottom** camera inset was deliberately not built — carrying it means a `MapPane` prop that flips on a tap, which ADR-0122 §9 forbids, so the camera's own phase owns it. **The numbers are still unspent:** `half`'s fraction, the flick threshold, and whether the row and the card read correctly over real tiles all need the device pass, and none of them was seen in the build session.

**Pin sizing (2026-07-27, session 142)** (`planning/2026-07-27-session-142-pin-size-is-a-share-of-the-canvas.md`): the pins were tuned for a canvas half the size of the one they ship on. `.map-pin` was a fixed 28×34px from `mockups/map-embedded-v1.html`, and ADR-0122 then made the stops move the pane by more than 2× — 545px at `map` against 263px at `half` — so 34px was 6.2% of one canvas and 12.9% of the other. [ADR-0123](decisions/0123-map-pin-size-is-a-share-of-the-canvas.md): **a pin is a share of the canvas it sits on**, `clamp(34px, 8% of the pane's height, 46px)` resolved in CSS against `container-type: size` on `.map-pane` — so the floor keeps `half` byte-for-byte unchanged, the map extreme gains 28%, and tablet/desktop are answered by the same rule with no breakpoint. Every dimension is a fraction of one `--pin-u` (so the ghost tier's "smaller" and the me-dot become **ratios**), one registered `@property` length animates the whole pin over the sheet's own curve, and the camera's fit clearance is **derived** from the size the pin will actually be rather than a flat 64px. Zoom and density were rejected as parameters (both resize pins under a moving finger). **The device pass happened the same day (session 143, `planning/2026-07-27-session-143-pin-size-recalibrated-on-a-phone.md`)** and the shape held while both numbers moved: the 0.08 share and 46px cap were set against ADR-0122's 390×844 budget (a 545px canvas), but the owner's phone has a shorter viewport — **~501px**, derived from the reported screenshots, where the pin badge went 67 → 79 device px — so the pins grew 18% where the arithmetic promised 28% and never reached the cap. Now **0.11 and 56px** (~55px on that device, 1.6× the original); floor unchanged, `half` untouched with real margin (its 243px canvas against a floor that holds under 309px, so even 0.14 leaves it alone). Consequence recorded in `constants.ts` and the ADR: the growth band is 309→509px, so a phone at the map extreme is **at the cap** and **`MAX_H` is the knob** for any further re-tune, not the share. A 1:1 preview of every rung (live stop toggle, plus share/cap dials on the measured geometry) was built and shared for the pass. It is deliberately **not** in `mockups/`: it hand-ports the pin CSS, where the catalog's standard is the shipped sheets inlined by `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` (ADR-0097). **Small follow-up if the device pass wants it in-repo:** an `APP-CSS:` manifest naming `styles/tokens.css` + `ui/domain/map-pane.css`, run the inliner, `.prettierignore`, catalog entry — which also upgrades it from a copy to the branch's actual CSS at 1:1. ADR-0121 §6/§7 amended; ADR-0123 amended by the recalibration. **Still open, and it is a case rather than a number:** past ~56px a teardrop's tip gets vaguer about which building it marks and coincident pins overlap sooner, so look at a **dense all-days day** before `MAX_H` moves again.

- **Phase 3 — the camera answers the tap** — **done (2026-07-28, sessions 151 + 152), then CORRECTED off a real map (session 153, [ADR-0129](decisions/0129-map-camera-moves-like-a-camera.md)):** the owner used the shipped ladder on a rendered canvas and three things were wrong. **Selection pans and never zooms** (being moved for tapping a pin you can already see is unwanted — ADR-0121 §7's rule restored for the two cases it was right about, and Google's own POI behaviour), so **zooming is now an intent** with two callers: an arrival, and the place card's **badge** (session 148's affordance reused, not a new control). **How far to zoom is derived from the distance to nearby pins**, not from a constant, fed through the existing fit; `MAP_ZOOM.PLACE` survives as the no-neighbours fallback. **And nothing portals:** the API cannot be asked for a smooth transition (`fitBounds` animates "depending on an internal heuristic", `panTo` only within one viewport, `moveCamera` never), so the ease is ours — `moveCamera` once per frame, one duration, collapsing to a single step under reduced motion, with the FIRST framing landing rather than easing out of a placeholder camera. **Read ADR-0129 before touching camera motion again: it is the one piece of this epic that a real device has actually corrected.** Originally: **done (2026-07-28, sessions 151 + 152):** [ADR-0127](decisions/0127-map-camera-answers-the-tap.md) (the zoom ladder + the arrival defect) and [ADR-0128](decisions/0128-map-dot-tier-and-the-cards-camera-reserve.md) (the `dot` tier + the card's camera reserve), amending ADR-0121 §7 and §6 and ADR-0122 §7. **The zoom ladder names one constant** — `MAP_ZOOM.PLACE`, read by the lone-pin centre, the selection and locate alike — with focus zooming **in only** (never out, which is the half of §7 that was protecting anything), `MAX_FIT` kept separate and one step tighter, and #20's repeat tap stepping in **statelessly** from the map's own zoom. **The arrival defect is fixed by ownership, not a fourth guard:** an arrival focus IS the frame, and it wins from either side of the race, so the ordering is irrelevant rather than out-timed. **The `dot` tier** degrades a pin below `DOT_BELOW` — hue kept, glyph/number/tip dropped — keyed on **zoom** (**amended session 154:** the tier applies to _precision_, not to _priority_, so `עכשיו` and `התחנה הבאה` are not degraded at all — which also turns the degradation into a promotion by contrast; today's other pins do demote, since exempting them would make the tier a no-op in day scope) (not the canvas, which is ADR-0123's distinction) and done entirely in CSS off a pane data attribute, so no marker re-renders and no prop is added; it answers §6's clustering trigger inside the pin grammar. **The card's camera reserve** is read through a **ref** so a pin tap still moves nothing, and is **clamped to what the axis affords after the top inset** — measured, the card's full band fits on no phone at any stop, and an unclamped reserve would have cost the top inset instead, which is worse and silent.

  **`MAP_PIN`'s dials are NOT open** — session 143 calibrated them against a real phone via a measured screenshot ratio, and `constants.ts` records that `MAX_H` is the knob. What is left there is a **case to look at, not a number to pick**: a dense day in all-days scope, which is also the case the `dot` tier now changes.

  **The device pass owns 12 open questions, and they sort into three kinds — which is half of what [ADR-0146](decisions/0146-the-device-pass-gets-an-instrument.md) settled.** They were all called "numbers" for eight sessions and some of them never were:
  - **Seven preferences, which now have a live control.** `MAP_ZOOM.PLACE` / `MAX_FIT` / `STEP_IN_MAX` / `DOT_BELOW`, `MAP_REFIT_FILL_SHARE` (0.4), `MAP_DRAG_ZOOM.PX_PER_LEVEL` (120) and `TAP_GAP_MS` (500). Session 195 built the **dev-only tuning panel** that makes these answerable in one sitting instead of eighteen rebuild-and-look cycles — steppers over real tiles, an invariant check, and a copy-pasteable block of what was chosen. The panel is the instrument; **the sitting is still owed**, and it needs a real browser key on a real phone.
  - **Five look questions, which need no control at all** — just the real app on a phone. ADR-0126's three (do the crosshair and the frame glyph read as distinct over real tiles; is the 44px furniture band heavy at `half` on a 360×640 phone; does the `באזור` pill read as tappable) and ADR-0130's two (does the maybe's hatch read as **texture** over real tiles at the 34px floor rather than as noise; does the 0.72 aside ratio separate a handful of today's maybes from tens of general ones with all of them actually on screen — ADR-0130 §4 checked both in a headless renderer, neither on a phone). The panel carries them as a checklist so the sitting records its own answers.
  - **Plus ADR-0123's standing case**, which is a case to view rather than a number to pick: a dense all-days day, which the `dot` tier now changes. `MAP_PIN`'s dials stay closed (session 143 calibrated them; `MAX_H` is the knob).

  **Three things left the cluster in session 195 without being tuned, and the count was wrong in both directions.** `half`'s fraction and `MAP_CARD_BODY_H` are **readings, not preferences** — the sheet is already draggable to any height, so the fraction is read off the gesture the app has (a slider there is also the one override that fights ADR-0122 §9), and 130 is a constant only because ADR-0128 §2 forbids a layout read on this screen, so the panel measures the rendered card instead. And ADR-0145's **centre-anchoring** question was already spent by §3's amendment the day it was raised — both anchors were felt on a phone and the split by gesture is the answer. The line also still named `MAP_DRAG_ZOOM.SPAN_SHARE`, which session 194 deleted as a concept.

  **Do the remaining 12 in one sitting on a phone with a browser key.** Tuning them separately is how they drifted apart before, and every one of them is currently a reasoned default rather than a seen one.

- **Phase 4 — a continuing stay is on every day it covers** — **done (2026-07-27, session 137).** Triaged branch (b) in session 136 and **confirmed against a real two-night stay**: the authoring path is correct end to end (span-type booking → `buildSpanSeed` sets `endDate` → `lodging.ambientWhenMultiDay` → `spanDays` emits the middle-day `DayUsage`), and once the hotel had a place it appeared on every day it covers, ambient nights included. The report was purely prominence, so the fix is the un-fade: the middle-night pin and row now render at full strength, and what marks a night as ambient is that it has **no number and no clock**, not that it is greyed. The invariant held and stays test-guarded — an ambient day is still unnumbered, so no real stop is renumbered. ADR-0109 §5 amended; ADR-0054 untouched (its §4 scopes "ambient" to the day timeline/glance, and the Day view's teal band was never faded).
- **Phase 5 — every place-bearing surface reaches the map** — **done (2026-07-28, session 148; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s 2026-07-28 amendment + `mockups/map-reach-v1.html`).** Both halves shipped: `מפה` on every event and booking in both modes, and a placeless booking that says so. The audit's first answer — that a row tapping through to `BookingDetail` needs no second way in — was **overruled by the owner**, and it had missed why: `EventCard`'s labelled `מפה` sits inside `.wp-event-actions`, which is `max-height: 0` until the card expands, so an unexpanded day event had no way to the map at all and the settle variant had none in any state. The placement is the row's **category badge** (teal ring + corner pin), which is a measured decision — a trailing control took `Ichiran Ramen` to two lines at 390px and a long Hebrew builder title to five at 360px. Also settles §8's open question: `מפה` and `ניווט` are **not** an atomic pair (directions are a live on-the-ground verb; orientation is mode-neutral). Not on Home's board, not on the shelf — see the amendment for both reasons.
- **Hero 2.0 — the board opens instead of growing controls** — **DESIGNED (2026-08-03, session 210; [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md), session note [`planning/2026-08-03-session-210-the-hero-lift-design-session.md`](planning/2026-08-03-session-210-the-hero-lift-design-session.md), mockups [`hero-lift-v1`](../mockups/hero-lift-v1.html) + [`hero-horizon-v1`](../mockups/hero-horizon-v1.html)); **PLANNED**: [`planning/2026-08-03-session-210-the-hero-lift-build-plan.md`](planning/2026-08-03-session-210-the-hero-lift-build-plan.md) — five phases, one PR each, with the three gaps the ADRs do not answer.** The brief's "expansion or overlay?" was a false binary and the answer is a third word: a **promotion**. The board LIFTS — one object gaining elevation, on `.wp-dragghost`'s existing measured-box mechanism — and it is **still a back layer** (ADR-0103/0090 admit no exemption; what is rejected is the sheet's grammar). What it lifts is a **horizon** (`עכשיו → ועוד עכשיו → הבא בתור → אחר כך`), and only **איפה** / **פתק** / **הסדרה** earn the interaction; everything else was a reflow of what the collapsed board already carries. Reads, hand-offs and settles, **no time edits**, so ADR-0011 is never reached. Ships: the lifted hero as a `Modal` with a FLIP off a **measured** box (never a constant — three prior bugs), the swing character at `--t-base` in / `--t-quick` out with a **landing beat**, `ועוד N עכשיו` **retired to a readout** (forced: a tappable board is a `<button>` and Chrome tears the board apart at a nested one — a probe reports 1 of 4 children left), a **content-sized bounded** hero with one scroller (ADR-0148 §1), a **derived** rebuff with no colour and no text when there is nothing to open, a **`board` density on `SettleControl`** (only the ground changes; `.wp-settle-btn` is paper, which is ADR-0158 §15 on a new surface), and the per-variant rules (`in-transit` drops the settle verbs and the day rail; `group-split` cannot collapse an equal; `free` does not lift). **Deliberately unbuilt, with reasons recorded:** a note on the **next** event (it is what turns the typical case into the heavy one), any authoring in the hero, and a third day slot under `אחר כך` (that request is the Day tab). **PHASE 1 BUILT (2026-08-03):** the collapsed board is a tap target and has lost its only interactive child. `Board` gains `onLift?` and renders a `<button class="wp-board is-tappable">` when it is passed (one class carrying the element reset plus `--press-scale-lg`, the line ADR-0140 §2 owes every large surface) and the `<div>` it always was when it is not — so the CALLER decides which variants are liftable and the board stays presentational. `.wp-board-also-toggle` is gone, replaced by `.wp-board-also-read`: same dot, same `t.board.alsoNow(n)` words, one ink rung quieter (`--on-dark-dim`, because a statement of fact is not an invitation to press), no chevron and no open state, so `useState` leaves the component entirely. The rows it revealed do not render collapsed any more and have no home until phase 3, which is a **deliberate one-PR feature loss**. `Home` does **not** pass `onLift` yet — shipping a pressable board with nothing to open would be the dead tap ADR-0160 §9 exists to prevent, so the capability lands before the wiring. The guard for §4 is a test proving Chrome's eviction cannot come back: a liftable board must have **no** `button`/`a`/`input` descendant AND must still own its divider, next row and rail as children — verified to fail (by reintroducing a nested button) before being kept, because a snapshot cannot see this and neither can a test that only checks the pieces exist. The also-row shift assertion moved from behind the retired toggle onto `group-split`, the same row's other host, where it renders unconditionally. **PHASE 2 BUILT (2026-08-03):** the horizon's data, with nothing rendering it. `lib/hero-horizon.ts` — `heroHorizon()` assembles `{ now[], next, then }` with per-point depth (place + `placeId` + `bookingId` for the hand-offs, notes, settle outcome) and `canLift()` answers §9's question. Both pure and **clock-free by construction**: `nowAll`/`nextAll` come in from `deriveNow` rather than being re-derived, so the board and the hero cannot disagree about what is happening, and the tests need no `setSimulatedNow` because nothing reads a clock. **Two things worth knowing before phase 3.** (a) `§9` and `§10` look like they conflict and do not: `canLift` returns false when `now` is empty, which IS the whole of `free`'s case, so nothing anywhere tests a `variant`. And the **settle verbs deliberately do not count** toward it — every event is settleable (ADR-0139 §2), so counting them would make the predicate true for every board with a now event and the rebuff would only ever fire on `free`; a lift revealing two buttons and no new information is the animation-for-its-own-sake the brief's sub-question 1 forbids. (b) **A booked event's notes are read from the BOOKING as well as the event**, because on the booked path a note lands on the booking (ADR-0152 phase 5b: the event is server-materialized from a seed and has no client id), so asking only for `eventId` finds nothing on exactly the events most likely to carry one. **This diverges from `EventCard`'s mark**, which counts `eventId` alone — a day row can show no mark while the lifted hero shows the note. The hero's side is right (§3 promises "the note about this stop"), but changing the mark changes every day row, so it is flagged rather than silently fixed on both. **Owner call needed before phase 3 renders it.** `then` is typed with a title and an instant and **no id**, so §12's one-line condition is encoded rather than remembered: attaching a place, note or hand-off to `אחר כך` means editing the interface, which is the moment to reread §12. **Deviation from the plan, deliberate:** phase 2 ships the lib and its tests only, not the `Home.tsx` wiring the plan put here — an assembled horizon nothing reads is dead code, so the wiring lands in phase 3 where it is consumed. **PHASE 3 BUILT (2026-08-03):** the lifted hero RENDERS, statically. `ui/domain/HeroLift.tsx` + `hero-lift.css` — head pinned, ONE scroller, foot pinned (ADR-0148 §1's bounded card, second consumer), the horizon's parts (`איפה` with the map way-in and `ניווט`, `פתק` with a count of what it is not showing, the booking reach, `הסדרה`, `הבא בתור`, `אחר כך`), and `Home` wires `onLift` gated on `canLift`. Presentational like `Board` beside it: every time arrives pre-formatted in its own zone and every verb arrives as a callback. **Two primitives gained a variant rather than a sibling.** `Modal` gains a fourth, `lift` — top-anchored and content-sized, which is what makes it none of `sheet`/`dialog`/`full` — so the overlay registration, the focus contract and the exit are all the shipped ones and ADR-0103/0090 need no exemption; **both** `modal-lift-in` and `modal-lift-out` exist, never `reverse`, which `styles/exit-animations.contract.test.ts` fails the build over. And `SettleControl` gains its `board` density: ADR-0139's rule holds exactly (a density, not a vocabulary — the words, marks and hues are untouched) and the only change is the GROUND, because `.wp-settle-btn` is `var(--card)`/`var(--ink)` paper and this host is inverted in both themes (ADR-0158 §15). Verified against the app's own CSS through the mockup's `APP-CSS` block: 18% `--ok` fill, 42% border, `#7fd6a6` ink, 38px. **Two things the build found.** (a) `Home`'s horizon must take **`shownNext`**, not `deriveNow`'s `nextAll` — a hotel CHECK-OUT is an END transition `deriveNow` cannot surface, so the board sometimes shows a next that is not `deriveNow`'s, and a horizon built off `nextAll` would name a different "next" than the board it grew out of. (b) The booking reach was wired on the **`next` slot only**, so a NOW event with a booking had no way through while `canLift` counted it — extracted to a `Reach` part that belongs to any point. Caught by the Home-level wiring test, which is the seam neither half's unit tests can see. **Deviations from the plan, both deliberate:** the `lift` variant's entrance is a **placeholder fade** (phase 4 replaces it with the FLIP off the measured box plus the swing — a phase whose subject is shape should not ship a motion character), and `Home` gained a test file it never had (`Home.lift.test.tsx`) because the wiring is exactly what the two unit suites cannot observe. **Follow-up, deliberately not in that PR:** the two hero mockups still declare the horizon's part CSS in their own proposal blocks now that it has shipped in `hero-lift.css`. Adding that file to their `APP-CSS` manifests and deleting the duplicates would keep §4's "the mockup reads the app" check alive for the parts as well as for §4's two rules — worth doing before phase 4 edits either file. **AMENDED FROM A DEVICE (2026-08-03, three owner reports against the built phases 1-3; [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md)'s amendment §A/§B/§C).** (a) **The rebuff is withdrawn and the lift got wider.** _"It does lift but only when there's an event happening"_ — `canLift` required a non-empty `now`, so the board was un-pressable through every **gap**, which is most of a real day. §9 and §10 both rested on a conflation the ADR made and did not notice: _nothing is happening now_ is not _nothing to show_. In a gap the horizon still holds `הבא בתור` with its place, note and booking reach, plus `אחר כך` — the moment the lift is worth the most. §10's argument was about the **shelf** (`GlanceCard`), a different question. An empty tap is now the rare end-of-day case and stays **silent**: no nudge, owner's call, so phase 5 is retired before it was built and `BEAT.REBUFF` came back out of `lib/one-shot.ts` rather than being left as a name nobody claims. (b) **The landing is CENTRED, not top-anchored.** §5 argued for the top from the mockup's measurement; on a phone a top-anchored card reads as a panel that arrived from somewhere, which is the sheet grammar §2 rejects. (c) **A class collision laid the horizon out in a ROW.** `className="hero-point lead"` — and `.lead` is already a GLOBAL class in `screens.css` (the Glance card's row: `display: flex; align-items: baseline; justify-content: space-between`), so the title, the note and the settle strip rendered side by side in one band. Now `data-lead`, which cannot collide with a class. **The rule this app already had and this file broke: every class it adds is prefixed** (`wp-`, `hero-`, `map-`, `prep-`) — an unprefixed modifier is a global, and `lead`/`big`/`row`/`main` are all taken. Invisible to every test, because each part rendered correctly and only their arrangement was wrong. **Two things the build must re-do rather than trust:** every pixel number is webfont-dependent and was measured on a fallback font, and the landing box needs an e2e assertion against the settled element (jsdom reports every rect as zero, so the unit suite cannot see this class of bug). **PHASE 4 BUILT (2026-08-03) — the motion, and the reported defect it actually fixes** ([ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md)'s second amendment §D-§G). The report was _"now it became a simple overlay rendering the hero twice instead of lifting up"_, with a screenshot of the collapsed board sitting behind the lifted card — so the first fix is not motion at all: **the collapsed board now hides while lifted** (`visibility`, never `display`, because the descent measures that box and Home's layout must not collapse), and the placeholder fade is **deleted** rather than kept, since an object that travels _and_ fades is still two boards cross-dissolving. `lib/useLiftFlight.ts` is the FLIP: `width`/`height` as real layout so text is crisp at both ends (§5's promise — a transform FLIP would scale the text, and the board is already near-full-width so there is no scale to spend), `transform` left free for the 9°/46px swing, and `position: fixed` borrowed only for the flight's duration so **CSS stays the single owner of where the hero settles** and JS only reads it. Two-pass measured because **`height` does not interpolate to `auto`** and the hero is content-sized; WAAPI rather than a transition, following `useFlipRows`, so there is no inline style left for React to diff and no "release the height" timer to get wrong. `lib/motion.ts` gains `measuredBox` — the zero-rect guard generalized out of `beginTripHandoff` rather than written a second time. **Four things only a real browser could say.** (a) The lifted hero **is** a `.wp-board`, so it matched `.app[data-mode='trip'] .wp-board` and replayed the **Plan→Trip going-live climax** on every open — 600ms of `--t-cinematic` from `brightness(0.55)`, which is why the card read flat and dim, and an ADR-0140 budget violation besides. (b) That same selector out-specifies `.wp-board.is-landing`, so **§7's landing beat never played**; excluding `.is-landing` was measured and is worse (dropping out of the rule and back in RESTARTS the power-on, flashing the board dim after every close), so the beat fills a second animation slot the power-on rule leaves open and `animation-name` in position 0 never changes. (c) A flight leaves the hero `position: fixed`, and **measuring an element in that state answers its STATIC position** — React's StrictMode double-invoke made the second measurement 422 instead of 273.5 and flew there, while a standalone harness of the same components without StrictMode measured perfectly; the entrance effect now restores what it borrows on cleanup. (d) The landing beat has to be played in an **effect after the reveal render**, not in the close handler: React owns `className` on that node, so a class added imperatively before its next reconcile is wiped by it. **The e2e the plan asked for exists** (`frontend/e2e/hero-lift.spec.ts`, 8 tests) and two traps in writing it are recorded: compare the last **in-flight** frame, not the last sampled one (once the flight releases its `position` the hero snaps to the CSS box whatever the animation aimed at — the first version passed with the landing box hardcoded wrong, verified by hardcoding it), and do **not** read the aim from `effect.getKeyframes()` the way the handoff spec does (measured: it reads back the element's resolved `auto` offsets, not the values passed). **Still unbuilt: `in-transit` liftability** — §10 asks that variant for new CONTENT (the seat, the landing zone shift, what is first on the ground) plus the transit progress as the hero's foot, which is why it is its own small phase rather than folded into the motion PR. **PLAN MODE ANSWERED, AND THE REBUFF CAME BACK (2026-08-03; [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §H).** Asked _"should we handle the plan mode hero?"_ — **no lift**, on structure rather than taste: the lift exists to close a distance to depth that lives elsewhere, and Plan's `.prep` hero has none, because what it summarises (the readiness percent) is the **checklist rendered immediately beneath it on the same screen**. A lifted Plan hero would show the checklist to someone looking at the checklist. Two smaller costs recorded so they are not rediscovered as blockers: `.prep` is the one always-violet surface while the lift card is the `--on-dark` ramp (so a violet card on a scrim is either two loud things or a hero that stops being violet mid-flight), and `.prep` has no press affordance today, which is what keeps the grammar consistent rather than breaking it. **But a tap is now answered** (owner: _"For plan mode at least give it the nudge animation for now"_) — a press that produces nothing reads as a dead surface, so §9's **rebuff returns** for the surface that actually has §9's condition: 7px up and back, `linear`, no colour, no text, and `BEAT.REBUFF` is back in `lib/one-shot.ts`, which amendment §A said should happen only once something claimed it. **Deliberately not `BEAT.NUDGE`** (a lateral shake meaning something is WRONG — a tap on the prep hero is not an error) and deliberately still a `<div>` (announcing a control that does nothing on activation is ADR-0150 §8 from the other side). **Revisit when** Plan's hero summarises something it does not show inline — a per-traveller readiness breakdown, or a "what is blocking departure" that is not the checklist re-sorted. One more browser-only finding: the beat's keyframes had to be verified **running**, not merely applied, and the spec that checks it first clicked the wrong element — `PlanHome` is lazy-loaded and `HomeSkeleton` renders its own placeholder `.prep` with no handler, so a spec waiting for `.prep` rather than the real hero reports a working beat as broken. **IN-TRANSIT LIFTS, AND THE EPIC IS BUILT (2026-08-03; [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §I).** §10's content list read as four new things and **three already existed**: the booking is the `Reach` part any point with one gets, **what is first on the ground** is simply `הבא בתור` (during transit the flight is `now`, so `deriveNow`'s next IS the first thing after landing — no new derivation), and the landing zone shift rides in with the transit progress, where ADR-0107 already put it. The fourth, **the seat, is a datum this app does not store** — `Booking.details` carries `room`/`wifi`/`notes`, no form offers a seat, and what the app actually does is visible in its own fixtures, where `מושב ליד החלון בשתי הטיסות` is a NOTE on the flight booking that the lifted hero already reads. Deliberately not invented: a seat field added to fill a hero slot is the fixture-for-an-unbuilt-feature ADR-0045 forbids, so it stays a schema-plus-form question to decide separately. What shipped: the gate opens, the **settle verbs drop per POINT** rather than per variant (derived by asking whether the point is the flight you are sitting inside — §10's reason is about the flight, so a soft event running alongside it keeps its own verbs, which a variant check would have taken away), and the foot swaps to the flight's own progress (ADR-0059 §2's rule reaching the lifted state). That last one also removed a duplication phase 3 shipped: `Home` hand-wrote a copy of `.wp-board-progress` beside a `rail` prop whose own comment claimed it was "the same node the collapsed board renders, passed in rather than rebuilt so the two cannot drift". It was rebuilt, and nothing failed while the copy existed — **a prop comment claiming two surfaces share a node is not enforcement**. `DayRail` and `TransitProgress` are exported from `Board.tsx`, both hosts render the same components, and the prop is now `foot` because in transit it carries neither a rail nor a copy of one.
- **Hero 2.0 · the in-transit hero** — **BUILT (2026-08-05, session 215; [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md)'s session-215 amendment §K-§O + [ADR-0059](decisions/0059-booking-presentation-on-home-and-index.md)'s, mockup [`hero-in-transit-v1`](../mockups/hero-in-transit-v1.html), session note [`planning/2026-08-05-session-215-the-lifted-hero-in-transit-design-session.md`](planning/2026-08-05-session-215-the-lifted-hero-in-transit-design-session.md)).** Four reports off a real device mid-flight, one structural cause: `HeroLift` had no `in-transit` variant, so a flight arrived as an ordinary hard now-event while its own rail was pinned as the card's foot, 258px below the route it describes. All seven changes shipped: the rail's middle slot says `נותרו X` instead of a second copy of the arrival time; the travelling mark is the event's glyph and the live word comes off `CategoryTimeProfile.midSpan`, so a train stops reading `בטיסה` with a plane; `איפה` is a name line plus ONE action row (the wrap was `flex-wrap` breaking lines on hypothetical sizes, 70-100px short at every phone width); the lifted hero wears the collapsed board's own mid-span grammar with the rail inside the point and nothing in the foot; `איפה` mid-journey resolves to the DESTINATION rather than the airport you left; the zone crossing is a sentence (`מזיזים את השעון שעה קדימה`) plus the destination's clock, in the lift only; and a same-day car hire leaves the journey grammar for a held resource with a return deadline. Gate/seat/belt/delay/weather stayed **out** — not in `bookingSchema` or they need a live feed (rule 5).
- **An empty press on the Trip board is answered** — **BUILT (2026-08-05, session 215; [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §Q).** Owner, from the shipped surface: with nothing to lift, a tap did nothing at all. §9 designed the rebuff for this board and §A withdrew it (an empty tap is the rare end-of-day case, so silence); rare turned out not to mean unremarkable, and §H had already made the same argument for Plan's prep hero. The board now plays that beat and stays a `<div>` — no role, no tab stop, no large press step, because a surface that opens nothing must not announce a control. **One beat, one rule:** Plan's private `prep-rebuff` copy is retired and the motion lives once in `styles/beats.css`, following `.is-nudging`'s precedent that a beat is a surface-agnostic class; the only per-surface line is the board's `--board-beat` plug-in, which it needs because the Plan→Trip power-on already owns `animation` on that selector. Guarded in `e2e/hero-lift.spec.ts` over both surfaces, beside the Plan test that was already there — a class assertion in jsdom passes whether the keyframes exist or are outranked.
- **A journey that crosses the day boundary is still happening** — **BUILT (2026-08-05, session 215; [ADR-0054](decisions/0054-ambient-span-events-off-the-day-schedule.md)'s session-215 amendment + [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §R).** Owner: a flight that crossed midnight was not recognized as happening, and the hero offered the landing as the next event. `ambientWhenMultiDay` was answering two questions: the booking form sets `endDate` for any end on a later day, so a red-eye is `isAmbient` — which made `Home` drop it from `deriveNow` once airborne and sent `classify` down the ambient branch, whose only windows are check-in/check-out. **Ambient is how a span RENDERS across days; `midSpan.kind` is what its middle IS**, so `isJourney` now exempts a journey at three call sites (classify, the `deriveNow` filter, and the mid-stay strip — which would otherwise have claimed the flight in parallel with the board). A multi-day hire or stay is untouched, and so is the day view's backdrop-plus-transition-markers treatment. **And the line it unblocked is built too** (same day, owner's word): §M's arrival day. `מחר` joins the **arrival time** on both elevations, only when the landing is not today — the countdown answers "how long", the day answers "which morning is that `06:00`", and mid-journey the live zone is already the destination's so the comparison needs no new zone work. Measured at 360/390px with the fullest row before shipping (§O's flex rule).
- **The board's countdown while you are in transit** (open question, 2026-08-05, session 215) — `הבא בתור · איוש 07:00 · 10:24 שעות` counts from the clock, so mid-flight it counts **through the landing** when the traveller's question is "how long after we land". Recorded rather than designed: the honest answer is a decision about what the board's countdown MEANS (and whether it differs per variant), not a layout change to the in-transit hero. See `hero-in-transit-v1` §6.
- **The hero says `זמן חופשי` whether you are on a bus, asleep, or on a day nobody planned** — **BUILT (2026-08-29, designed and shipped the same day; [ADR-0211](decisions/0211-a-gap-has-a-character.md), [`mockups/the-gap-is-not-free-time-v1.html`](../mockups/the-gap-is-not-free-time-v1.html), [session note](planning/2026-08-29-the-gap-is-not-free-time.md)).** The title slot was `Board`'s final `else`, so it printed a positive claim resting on an absence — which [ADR-0208](decisions/0208-a-claim-needs-something-to-stand-on.md) forbids everywhere else on this surface. Five derived characters, first match wins; night and early morning are one member split by the ADR's only new number (`NIGHT_ENDS_HOUR`); one answer rendered at both elevations. Zero new controls, one CSS declaration.
- **The day rail pins its knob to a lie for eight hours a night** — **BUILT (2026-08-29 with [ADR-0211](decisions/0211-a-gap-has-a-character.md) §4).** `dayProgress` clamps, so at ⁦02:40⁩ the board drew a knob at ⁦0%⁩ under a label reading `עכשיו`. The rail now comes off wherever the day is not the frame you are in — [ADR-0059](decisions/0059-booking-presentation-on-home-and-index.md) §2's `in-transit` gate generalised into one `showRail` boolean rather than a second condition beside it.
- **The evening lookahead exists and is silent about which day it points at** — **BUILT (2026-08-29, [ADR-0211](decisions/0211-a-gap-has-a-character.md) §6/§7).** `deriveNow` carries no date filter, so `הבא בתור` always crossed midnight and never said so. `BoardNext.day` now carries `relativeDayLabel` beside the time it qualifies — [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §M's `endDay` extended to the sibling meta row — and `אחר כך` stops stopping at midnight (`thenAfter` was filtering `dayEvents`, and it is that field's only consumer). **What is left, deliberately:** on the trip's LAST day both slots say `סוף היום` — two ways of saying one nothing, and now the only place that phrase appears. Small, and wants a decision about what the second slot should say when there is genuinely nothing after it. `סוף הטיול` is drawn for that slot in [`mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v1.html`](../mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v1.html) §3, awaiting the owner's pick with the rest of that file. **And a THIRD slot of the same shape turned up while drawing it:** `heroTravel` hangs off the same midnight-crossing `horizon.next`, so the lifted hero prints `t.travel.leaveAt` → `צאו ב־06:40`, a bare clock ⁦40px⁩ under a row that says `07:12 · מחר`. Same fix, same words, one more caller — and independent of which lookahead option is chosen.

- **What Home says about tomorrow once today is done** — **DESIGNED, nothing built (2026-08-31; [`mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v2.html`](../mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v2.html) is the live answer, [`v1`](../mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v1.html) the five-option survey it chose between, [session note](planning/2026-08-31-tomorrow-lookahead-design.md)).** **Round two rewrote the recommendation on the owner's correction** — _"packed with too much info and everything is represented on the same level of importance"_ — and the answer is a **subtraction**: at night the board has ONE subject and it is tomorrow. The hierarchy needs no new token, because `board.css` already separates its two slots on three channels at once (`--amber` vs `--on-dark-faint` label, ⁦21px⁩ vs ⁦17px⁩ title, `--on-dark-strong` vs `--on-dark` ink) and at night rank 1 is spent on `סוף היום`. Three moves: `[data-rank]` swaps the two existing treatments (rank 1 is whichever slot holds tomorrow — an empty tomorrow needs no swap, no tomorrow leaves the board as it ships); four runs come off (the code, a duplication measured ⁦240px⁩ from the `הכרטיס הבא` tile; the `קשיח` lock; v1's `מוקדם` tag, **withdrawn**; and the meta row's day token, since the rank-1 label now says `מחר` — legal only because of move 1); and `סוף היום` plus the ribbon's header go, taking the ribbon ⁦73px⁩ → ⁦45px⁩. Measured at ⁦360px⁩: the board goes ⁦273px⁩ shipped → **⁦195px⁩**, information runs ⁦16⁩ → **⁦9⁩**, the biggest type bucket ⁦2⁩ → **⁦1⁩**, head-font titles ⁦2⁩ → **⁦1⁩**, mono runs ⁦5⁩ → **⁦2⁩**. "Lift only" is drawn and rejected on the ask's own words (a tap is not a glance), and every detail move 2 removed now lives in the lift and nowhere else. Still open for the owner: the recommendation as a whole, the ribbon's marks (emoji vs plain), `סוף הטיול` for the trip's last night, and the travel line's missing day token. The rest of this line — the derivations, the emptiness test, what needs no backend — is unchanged: Owner: _"after the day is over … if they opened the app and instantly got the idea at a glance of where they're headed, what's planned, it would be perfect"_, and it must read **friendly and inviting**. The lookahead itself is not missing — `הבא בתור` has crossed midnight since the first build and says `מחר` since ADR-0211 §6 — so the ask is a **shape**, not a point. Recommended, and awaiting the owner: **א׳** the day rail's own tree (track + three-slot `ends`) re-spent on tomorrow's `buildDayGlance` window in the slot `day-done` leaves idle (`gapDrawsDayRail('day-done')` is `true`, so today's rail is still measuring a day that is over — the one ADR-0211 §4 never asked about), the events' own emoji over the track as the friendly channel, and a hollow ring pointing at the block `הבא בתור` already names; **ב׳** the title slot saying where you are tonight instead of `סוף היום`, which is `at-the-stay` without its `DAY_WINDOW` bound and costs no CSS; **ו׳** the same ribbon in the lift's foot plus the one control the collapsed `<button>` board cannot carry (ADR-0160 §4), a `ליום של מחר` hand-off at the ⁦44px⁩ floor. Rejected with reasons in the file: **ג׳** the shipped `GlanceCard` re-derived for tomorrow (cheapest reuse, lands ⁦212px⁩ below the fold at ⁦360×640⁩, and its `נותרו היום` lead is date-bound copy), **ד׳** a board that flips (the tap belongs to the lift; a rotated box breaks the FLIP's measured origin; no reduced-motion form), **ה׳** a warm one-line band (ADR-0064 removed that band from that position). Costs measured: ribbon ⁦53px⁩ plain / ⁦73px⁩ with emoji against the rail's ⁦23px⁩; the recommended lift is ⁦617px⁩ against a ⁦622px⁩ cap at ⁦360×640⁩. Open forks: the ribbon's character, the `מוקדם` tag (needs `EARLY_START_HOUR`, the drawing's only new fact), `עצירות` as the count's noun, and whether to build ב׳ at all. Nothing here needs the backend or a new entity field — `buildDayGlance` and `dayBookendStays` already answer for a future date, and `glance.empty` is **not** the emptiness test (an unplanned tomorrow with a hotel booked reports `empty: false`, ⁦2⁩ anchors; use `segs.length === 0`).
- **Phase 6 — authoring a place on a map** (two ADRs, the only phase that spends) — **(a) search with a map**, so you can see where a result is before adding it: blocked on a fact, not a design — an Autocomplete **prediction carries no coordinates** (why ADR-0115 dropped distance from a result card), so mapping one needs a Details call **before** the pick, against a mask ADR-0111 pinned at Pro tier precisely to control that. Cost the three options (vet-on-tap / no map, keep the free `place_id` deep-link / Details-per-preview under the session token) against ADR-0108 §3's envelope **before** designing. **(b) drop a pin manually** — a `Place` with coords, no `googlePlaceId`, a user-typed name. Settle: the `@@unique([tripId, googlePlaceId])` assumption (nullable, so several should coexist — verify); `geo-tz` is unaffected (lat/lng based); **ADR-0112 means a pin with no reference is cache-only and wouldn't list at all**, so dropping must also create one (most plausibly the uncategorised `MaybeItem` that ADR-0115's `＋ אולי` creates); and whether to reverse-geocode an address (paid — likely skip). **(c) tap one of Google's own sight icons — new, and it sidesteps (a)'s blocker** (noted 2026-07-28, session 147): [ADR-0125](decisions/0125-map-canvas-terrain-vocabulary.md) §6 re-enabled Google's `landmark`/`entertainment`/`zoo`/`peak` labels, so the canvas now renders POI icons it did not have when this phase was triaged. A tap on one arrives as a map click carrying a **`place_id`** — already positioned on screen — so "see where it is before adding it" is answered with **no Details call to browse and no Autocomplete session at all**, which is exactly the fact that blocks (a). `resolvePlaceSchema` already takes `{ googlePlaceId }` with an optional `sessionToken`, so the pipe exists end to end. It still spends **on the add**, which is why it stays in this phase and not Phase 10 — but the spend lands on an explicit confirm, the "armed by intent, not by opening" rule ADR-0115 §1 already set. Cost it alongside (a)'s three options rather than after them. Note the collision to design around: a POI tap currently clears the selection so Google's place card and ours never stack (ADR-0125 §6), so an add-from-canvas flow has to decide which card owns that tap. Sequence (a) before (b): whatever surface (a) builds is what (b) drops a pin on; (c) may land before either, since it needs no new browse surface. **Scope note from session 145:** (a) is the **picker's** search only. The Map tab's own search is over places already in the trip, which already carry coordinates, so putting _those_ on the canvas is free and is Phase 10's — not this phase's, and not cost-gated. **Narrowed 2026-07-28 by [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md):** (a) keeps **only** the preview-before-pick cost call. Phase 10 put Google's half in the Map tab's sheet in both modes behind ADR-0115's existing arm, and a **picked** result is a pin immediately (the pick already pays for the coordinates), so "see where it is" is answered for free _after_ the commit. **(a) is CLOSED (2026-07-29), by a session that was not looking at it** — the surviving question was whether a **prediction** can be shown on the map _before_ you commit, which needed a Details call against ADR-0111's Pro-tier mask. [ADR-0132](decisions/0132-search-reclaims-the-chrome-and-a-google-result-is-a-ring.md) §7 answered it by **not using a prediction**: the Map tab's paid half switched SKU to **Text Search**, which returns each result **with its location**, so N results cost one call and a result is a **ring on the canvas before anything is picked**. The cost gate that held this phase for three weeks was then settled by the owner rather than by an envelope (_"we'll live with the expenses"_, [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md)): past `PLACE_SEARCH_MIN_CHARS` every search spends, and what stands between a keystroke and a billed call is the floor, the pause-gated debounce, the free half answering first, and ADR-0108 §6's cap. The in-form `PlacePickerSheet` still runs Autocomplete with no map beside it — that is **Phase 13 §9's to retire**, not a surviving piece of 6a. **So this phase is now (b) and (c) only, and (b) is the one with something waiting on it** (the long press, below). And (b) now has its surface: §9's long press is where a pin is dropped, so 6b keeps its data-model questions (the nullable `@@unique`, the write path) and none of the design. **DONE (2026-07-30, sessions 196 design + 197 build; [ADR-0147](decisions/0147-a-place-is-made-on-the-canvas.md) + [`mockups/map-make-a-place-v1.html`](../mockups/map-make-a-place-v1.html)).** The scope grew mid-design and got better for it: the owner added **naming and renaming any place**, and it turned out to be the same act — a place's NAME is the user's — so **four sources** (long press · Google's own sight · a Google **search result** · rename) share **one form** (name · the app's own `IconPicker` · nine categories incl. `other`). Not a new policy: `enrichExisting` already omitted `name`, so a user-authored name has always survived Google enriching the row; `icon` now sits on the same protected side of it. Shipped: a real **`Place.icon`** column at the BOTTOM of the resolution chain (**only a PICK is stored** — a derived glyph would freeze the icon and shadow the category), which **disqualifies a cross-trip global place cache** since a chosen icon is trip-scoped; the POI name stays unprefilled so an exploratory tap is free (prefilling is costed, not rejected); the rename affordance **revealed by selection**, because every in-row slot is measured-spent; `event.stop()` on a POI tap so ADR-0125 §6's "never two cards" holds by construction; and the timezone resolved in `create`/`update` for any caller with coordinates, closing a gap that would have let a pin dropped across a border inherit the trip's zone. Both rule-8 debts paid: `addResult`'s three-way destination branch extracted to one `landPlace`, and `Field`/`.map-gbtn` reused. **One shipped behaviour changed** (ADR-0131 §11's "picked → shelf"): outside an errand a search result's `＋ אולי` now opens the form first. **(c) was then REMOVED, the same day, on a report from the phone** ([ADR-0148](decisions/0148-the-place-form-has-the-room-it-needs.md) §6): _"opening a create place form for every hit on a Google suggestion is very annoying, I prefer if we removed this one."_ ADR-0147 §4 designed the act and not the **frequency** — Google's sight icons are scattered across the whole canvas, so a form on every one of them is noise rather than an offer, and that is what a device pass finds and a mockup structurally cannot. So ADR-0125 §6 stands **unamended** (Google's own card answers the tap, and its Maps link is most of the point of making it), the `event.stop()` is removed rather than commented out, and the source, the ring marker, `mapsPlaceIdUrl`, `nameOptional` and the free owned-sight rename path went with it. **The form is three sources, and this phase now spends nothing at all** — 6c was its only paid gesture, so the search half's Text Search (ADR-0132 §7) is the tab's only Google spend. Renaming is the pencil and only the pencil. **The Maps & Places epic's feature work is complete** — what remains on it is ADR-0146's device-pass sitting (including ADR-0131 §9's unanswered question of whether a long press survives Google's own tiles at all) and the paid-Routes enhancement.
- **The place form under a keyboard** — **DONE (2026-07-30, session 198; [ADR-0148](decisions/0148-the-place-form-has-the-room-it-needs.md) + [`mockups/map-form-visibility-v1.html`](../mockups/map-form-visibility-v1.html)),** from three reports off a real phone against the form shipped hours earlier. The first looked like one bug and **measuring it made it three**: the card is bottom-anchored and grows upward, so a split shrinking for a keyboard clips it at the **top** — the actions survive and the title and the field die, which means you can commit a form you cannot read. The obvious fix is half a fix: the chrome reclaim buys 122px and the card wanted 243, leaving it **103px short at `half`** with the missing part being the field. Two findings neither report contained, both measured before anything was built: at the `full` stop the room is **−38px, constant, keyboard or not** (so ADR-0147's pencil opened a form that could not be drawn, in a state that ADR argued could not arise), and **on iOS every layout number is healthy and the card is invisible** — the viewport does not shrink and the keyboard is drawn over it, the same shape as ADR-0132 §4's iOS attribution failure. Shipped: the card **bounded** by arithmetic over the numbers its own `bottom` already uses, three rows with a pinned head and foot; the form **implying the `map` stop from every origin** with the sheet not shown at all (`display: none`, +52px), returning to the stop it came from — which reverses ADR-0147's rejection on a fact it was argued without; **one** quiet line instead of a hint and a coordinates row (−20px), so the card is **223px for every source**; `lib/useKeyboardInset.ts`, one hook for both platform models because **the difference between them IS that number**, feeding a `max(sheet + attribution, keyboard)` bottom; the pin **framed from every source** through a **measured** `cardReserve` (`MapPane`'s `cardOpen: boolean` became `cardReserve: number`) rather than the row-sized constant that had been landing the pin behind the form — **the fourth time this repo wrote a landing position as a constant**; the chrome down for the **form's** life rather than the keyboard's, since a pill tap blurs the field and a keyboard-literal trigger flickers the shell, through one `chromeReclaimed` with **one writer**. The owner **refused the scroll** the first pass offered as acceptable degradation (_"some of the form is cut off, this shouldn't happen and I don't want to allow this"_), so the pixels were found instead and the bound survives as a floor for a device nobody measured. Verified across twelve states (360×640 / 390×844 / 411×914 / 430×932 × no keyboard / Android / iOS, with safe insets where such a device exists): 223px, head and actions on screen, no scrolling, in all twelve. **Two things it leaves open, both deliberately.** A **discard guard** — closing a dirty form loses what you typed, and the recommendation on the table is a toast with an undo (the grammar every write here already uses) rather than a confirm dialog, which on a one-field card is the nag ADR-0109 §6 refuses; not built, because the report was about closing the form, not about protecting it. And **two device-pass questions** for ADR-0146's sitting: the real keyboard heights (approximated per width here) and whether iOS's `100dvh` in an installed PWA behaves as the overlay model predicts — the one assumption the whole iOS column rests on.
- **Phase 8 — the canvas's own chrome, restated** (#19, #23) — **done (2026-07-28, sessions 149 design + 150 build):** [ADR-0126](decisions/0126-map-canvas-chrome-two-camera-controls-and-an-area-sort.md) + `mockups/map-chrome-v1.html`, amending ADR-0121 §12 and ADR-0122 §1/§2. **#19** split `recentre`: locate is locate-only and routes to the existing reason-first pre-prompt (the card stays the only thing allowed to ask), and "frame the filtered set" is its own control with a new `frame` icon, absent when the day has no pins to frame — which closes ADR-0122 §2's gap, since at the `map` stop nothing on the canvas could ask for the permission. **#23** made the area a **sort**: `X באזור` is a `<button>` inside the live region that still wraps it (`role="status"` where it always was), the order is in-view-first with two `.map-grouphead` headers, bounds are snapshotted at the tap, and it is exclusive with `קרוב עכשיו`. **ADR-0106 §4 is unamended** — the viewport is still the only area _filter_. **The ghost prerequisite** is answered by keeping the count spatial and having the list state the shortfall in session 144's grammar, with `כל הימים` beside it. One horizontal furniture band at 44×44 (which also fixed `.map-recenter` at 38×38 and `.map-areacount` at 64×26, both already under ADR-0017's floor); a vertical stack was rejected on measurement (−3px of clear canvas at 360×640 `half`, overlapping Google's attribution). `MAP_CONTROLS_H` and the camera's inset are untouched. **Three things still owe a real phone** (ADR-0126's device pass, and they join Phase 3's tuning cluster): whether the crosshair and the corner-bracket frame read as distinct over real tiles, whether 44px is heavy at `half` on a 360×640 phone (49px of clear canvas), and whether the pill reads as tappable at all.

- **A maybe is not a past place** — **done (2026-07-28, session 156; [ADR-0130](decisions/0130-a-maybe-is-not-a-past-place.md)):** two reports off the shipped map. **Past places are no longer faded in Plan mode** — the behind-you tier joins `nowStop`/`nextStop` as Trip-only, because a day you are arranging has no past (ADR-0121 §10's connector argument, run backwards); the clock is still passed, since `placeDay` reads it to resolve which day a place is _live_ on, so it is an explicit `planning` flag rather than a silent absence. (The same message asked whether Plan mode always shows full pins in day scope — **it already did**, session 155's rule being mode-independent.) **And a maybe stops reading like a past place:** the code drew both distinctions on the SAME axis (a maybe was the hue at 55%, a passed stop is the hue desaturated), so `design-language.md`'s soft grammar — "dashed border, **diagonal-hatch background**" — comes back onto the pin and the tint goes: a maybe keeps its colour and loses its solidity, a past stop keeps its solidity and loses its colour. Five per-category tint overrides deleted, not rewritten. **A defect fell out of designing it:** a dayless shelf maybe was drawn as a **ghost**, i.e. as another day's business, when a place no day has claimed is exactly the one available today — so the ladder gains a `shelf` rung wearing the maybe's paint at the subordinate ratio, prioritised below today's maybes by size, z-order and the dot tier (the owner's constraint: **tens** of general maybes against a **handful** earmarked for today). `isAsidePin` names the five call sites that meant "the day scope did not choose this" and only _looked_ like "is a ghost" — the area readout would otherwise have under-counted and a dayless idea would have started pulling the camera. `MAP_PIN.GHOST_SCALE` → `ASIDE_SCALE`. **The pins were rendered and looked at**, which moved three numbers: the first hatch read as a barber pole (a maybe louder than a committed stop, inverting the ladder), and at dot size the sub-pixel dash reads as a ragged rim and now goes solid.

- **The router's `Platform, design & device targets` row now carries a whole epic** (noticed 2026-07-29, session 180, while repairing it) — that row is the map's home by accretion: it runs 0007 → 0134 and holds twenty-nine ADRs, most of them the Maps & Places epic, next to PWA/zoom/token entries that share nothing with them. A reader told to "read only the ADR(s) for your domain" cannot, because the domain is one cell. A **Maps & places** row would fix that, and splitting it is a judgement call over ~25 entries (0105–0134 plus 0108/0111/0121 currently under `Infra & security`), so it is the owner's, not a side effect of the next map task. Cheap side effect if taken: the table is padded to its widest cell, so the longest row costs the file ten times its own length.

- **A dot at `DOT_SCALE` is under the touch floor** (found by scenario audit, session 155) — a pin at 0.4 of `MAP_PIN` is roughly **14–22px**, and pins are tappable, so at all-days dot zoom the canvas's targets are well under ADR-0017's **44×44**. Two candidate answers and they conflict with each other's precedent: give the dot a transparent hit area (which ADR-0126 §3 explicitly refused for `באזור` — "the floor is met by geometry, never by a hit area painted smaller than it is"), or raise `DOT_SCALE` until the ink itself clears the floor (which weakens the density mitigation the tier exists for). A map pin is arguably the case where an oversized invisible target IS the convention, unlike a pill whose box reads as its affordance — but that distinction should be drawn deliberately, with the thing on screen. **Goes with the device pass**, since "is a dot still a place" is the same look question.

- **Phase 9 — the canvas takes a one-finger zoom** (#17) — **done (2026-07-30, session 193; [ADR-0145](decisions/0145-the-canvas-takes-a-one-finger-zoom.md) + `mockups/map-onefinger-zoom-v1.html`).** **Reconfirmed three ways first, and the phase brief was right to insist:** the typings (the whole 45-field `MapOptions` surface has no such flag), the live reference read on a phone by the owner (four `gestureHandling` values, byte-identical, no gesture named beyond pinch and double-tap), **a device** (nothing happens today), and **an independent web-access pass briefed to falsify it** (CONFIRMED against v3.65 weekly; it also found that Google exposes **no gesture-handler extension point**, which retires one of the brief's three arbitration options, and that Google's Android docs document the owner's chosen direction — slide down zooms in). Note why the first three were needed — _no flag_ is not _no gesture_, since a gesture Google already shipped would need no option, so the typings alone could never have closed it. **The brief's expected hard part dissolved on measurement:** `.map-pane` is `bottom: var(--sheet-h)`, so the canvas and the sheet's drag region **never share a pixel** and were never going to fight; they contend only for the finger after it leaves its origin, which pointer capture already settles on both sides. **The real competitor was unnamed — Google's double-tap zoom is enabled**, and the first two taps of this gesture _are_ a double-tap. So: a **three-phase recogniser** (a tap passed straight through, a _provisional_ second press, a commit past the slop) arbitrated by a **capture-phase guard**, and ADR-0122 §4's "both listen and the loser bails" deliberately **not** reused — that is safe only because the sheet's loser renders nothing, where **Google's pan writes the camera on the first move** and ADR-0129 §4 would then read the stray pan as the user's own. Blocking Google's double-tap means owing it, so the step-zoom is ours now — which finishes ADR-0129 §3, since Google's was the one camera move left that could not be asked to ease. **Centre-anchored** (the tapped point sits under the thumb; holding a pixel needs Google's projection per frame; ADR-0129 §1 already refused a lateral move bundled into a zoom) — point-anchoring stays **additive**. The dot tier crossing live costs nothing: ADR-0128 §1's `zoom_changed` data attribute already had this shape. **The mockup reversed two derivations in one session**, which is the argument for drawing a gesture first: the **direction** (down zooms in; "up means more" from the sheet was a good argument and wrong) and the **sensitivity** (0.18 → 0.5 over two rounds). **Corrected on a phone the same day (session 194, ADR-0145 §2/§4 amended in place):** the device pass returned two defects the suite had agreed were fine, plus a tuning report. **The sensitivity model was backwards** — it was a _share_ of the pane's height, derived by false analogy from ADR-0123, so a taller canvas demanded MORE finger travel (250px at the map extreme against `half`'s 122px): a pin's _size_ is a share of the canvas because a pin competes for canvas _area_, but a drag's _sensitivity_ belongs to the **finger**, which does not scale. Now a calibrated absolute `PX_PER_LEVEL` (120, anchored on `half`) with the share surviving only as a cap below ~240px; tested as a **property** (no canvas may cost more travel than the calibrated stop) rather than as a value. **The double-tap was a regression:** paying Google's suppressed step-zoom with _locate's_ `zoomStepIn` inherited its floor, so a double-tap from a globe view jumped to city zoom instead of stepping — and the test **asserted the buggy ceiling**. Rule 8's trap, named: "step the zoom in" and "take me somewhere readable" are different intents that both move the zoom. **And recognition sometimes failed** (Google panned instead): `TAP_GAP_MS` 300→500, `TAP_SLOP_PX` 24→44 (ADR-0017's touch floor). **This is the epic's fourth device correction of something derived and tested** — after ADR-0129, session 139's re-fit guard and ADR-0121's session-134 camera — and in all four the tests asserted what the code did while the _model_ was wrong. **And the anchoring question was taken in the same session** (§3 amended): it **splits by gesture** rather than going one way — the **drag stays centred** (the finger rests on the anchor for the whole gesture, so a point anchor converges on the one spot you cannot see) while the **double-tap anchors at the tapped point**, because by then you have lifted _and_ because Google's own double-click zoom anchored there, making the centred version an unnoticed downgrade rather than a choice. It goes through **Google's own projection**, which is the distinction ADR-0129 §3's warning turns on: `zoomAboutPoint` works in world coordinates where the world-to-screen relation is a pure power of two, so **there is no Mercator in our code** — and nothing constructs a `google.maps.Point`, since `fromLatLngToPoint` hands one back. Tested as the behaviour (the geography under the finger must not move) against Google's documented Mercator in the fake map, because a linear stand-in would pass while the code was wrong about latitude. **And the drag's centre anchor was then affirmed** with both behaviours in hand (owner: _"drag zoom should be anchored to the center as it was"_), so §3 is settled in **both** directions and nothing about anchoring is open. It needed no code — the drag was already centred, and `zoomTo` writing the zoom and nothing else is the guard that keeps it so.
- **Phase 10 — search overlays the map instead of replacing it** (#18; **DESIGNED and mostly BUILT 2026-07-28 (sessions 157 + 158).** §1–§8b are shipped; **§9 (a long press drops a pin) is all that remains — §10 was superseded by Phase 13**, whose own §9 is the last piece of it. See the lines under this one. See [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) + [`mockups/map-search-v1.html`](../mockups/map-search-v1.html); the brief below is kept because the design's own Context reads against it, and the **outcome note at the end of this line** says what it settled and what it grew into) — **the Map tab's search is `SearchOverlay`**: ADR-0101's full-screen `Modal` `'full'` variant, shared with the Index, which **covers the canvas completely**. So on the one tab where the question is _"where is this?"_, the answer renders as a list with the map hidden. That is the report — search should overlay the map, not replace it.
