  **One more thing lands on this phase (owner, 2026-07-28, session 148): `＋ הוספת מקום` should open THIS overlay and return to where it was invoked from.** Adding a place is a spatial act and deserves the map, not a list sheet — so the affordance a placeless booking now carries ([ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s 2026-07-28 amendment §5) opens the shared `PlacePickerSheet` only as an interim, because this overlay does not exist yet. When it does, the picker's trigger becomes a way **into** it with a return path, which means this phase has to decide what "return to where you came from" is: the invoking surface is often an overlay itself (`BookingDetail` is a `Modal`), so the trip back is a back-stack question (ADR-0090), not just a navigation.

**Session 145 traced this wrong and the correction matters, because the wrong trace makes it look cheap.** It reported the defect as "`query` reaches `searchRows` only; `pinsNow` never sees it and `cameraSignal` omits it, so typing changes the list and the canvas does not move." Every clause is true and it describes a symptom **nobody can observe** — the canvas is not on screen while you are searching. Pin-syncing is a consequence of the real job, not the job. The real job is the **surface**, which is a layout change on a canvas whose control budget ADR-0122 just spent a design session setting, so it needs a mockup and the "no new shape" reading was wrong.
**What the design has to settle.** (1) **Reuse or fork** (root `CLAUDE.md` rule 8): `SearchOverlay` is shared with the Index, so either the Map stops using it — a divergence that needs justifying — or the primitive gains a variant. (2) **The candidate that removes a mechanism rather than adding one:** the sheet is _already_ a list over a map, so "search overlays the map" may mean **retiring the overlay on this tab** and making the query just another list-changing control at the `half` stop, which is the shape ADR-0120's shared reveal already gives every other control here. Design toward that before inventing a floating panel. (3) **Plan mode's research half needs a home:** the same overlay also hosts `PlaceResearch` (ADR-0115's paid Google half), which is genuinely list-shaped with its own results and cannot simply move onto the canvas. (4) **The pin cue**, if results mark the canvas: prefer giving matches a cue over dimming non-matches — the prominence ladder (five tiers plus the amber now/next cues plus selection) is already full — and whatever it is, it must **compose** with the tier the way selection does, not replace it. (5) **The camera**: fitting the matches is a control, so ADR-0121 §7 permits it, but refitting per keystroke is exactly the churn §9 keeps out of the `באזור` readout — debounce or gate on submit, and add `query` to `cameraSignal` deliberately rather than by accident.
**On the cost gate, stated more carefully than session 145 did.** The trip-places half is genuinely **free and un-gated** — those places already carry `lat`/`lng`, which is what pinned them, so nothing is called and nothing is spent. But both halves share **one overlay**, so reshaping the surface is exactly where Phase 6a's "search needs a map" (#7) lives. The design must therefore either cover both — inheriting 6a's cost gate for the **research** half only — or explicitly scope itself to Trip mode and leave Plan mode's overlay alone. Session 145's flat "not cost-gated" papered over that scoping call.
**Read first:** ADR-0101 (the overlay being reshaped), ADR-0122 §1/§2/§9 (the canvas's control budget and the no-prop-on-a-tap rule), ADR-0120 (the shared reveal), ADR-0121 §7/§8/§9, ADR-0115 §1/§6 (the research half). The day-scoped-grammar defect above is **not** part of this — it is a wrong context object with no design content, and it should ship without waiting for any of this.
**DESIGNED — what [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) settled, and how the owner doubled it mid-session.** All five questions above are answered: **(1) reuse or fork** → neither, the Map tab **withdraws** from `SearchOverlay` (which is unchanged and keeps the Index), because what replaces it is not an overlay — the query becomes the **second occupant of the disclosure slot `סינון` already covers the row with**. **(2) removing a mechanism rather than adding one** → yes, and the "just another control in the row" candidate was **killed by measurement**: the row at rest leaves 163.5px in Trip mode at 390 but **12.8px in Plan mode + day scope, and −17.2px at 360**, where it already overflows today, against ~150 a usable field needs. So the field **takes** the row (44px inside 46, the split pays nothing). **(4) the match cue** → there is none, because a query is a filter and a match is **what is left**; the ghost trap is answered by ADR-0130 §3's own paint/size split (the paint stays, the `aside` **ratio** comes off), reaching three of `isAsidePin`'s five readers and deliberately **not** the amber cues or the day connector. **(5) the camera** → `query` stays **out** of `cameraSignal`, and not for ADR-0126's reason: a chip is one discrete act, a query is a **stream**, and since ADR-0129 the ease is ours. Typing never moves the camera; **committing to a place always does**.
**(3) the Plan/Trip scoping call was made and then overturned by the owner, which is the important part.** The design's first draft kept the overlay for Plan mode and scoped the paid half away with it. The owner's correction — _"there are actually two modes of searching: places on map and places from google maps"_, _"how does adding places from events/bookings work with this?"_, _"adding a place by long clicking on the map"_ — is that all three are about **adding** a place, so the question was never which mode gets which surface. The split between the two searches is **whether the thing has coordinates yet**: a trip place already carries `lat`/`lng`, a Google prediction carries none until the pick (ADR-0115 §2). **So the free half goes on the canvas and the paid half goes into the SHEET** (`PlaceResearch` re-parented, not rewritten), in **both** modes — **ADR-0115 §6's "Plan mode only" is withdrawn**, answered by the arm rather than by a mode gate. The cost surface widens by one screen and by nothing else (same SKU, same per-member·trip bounds), and the one-boolean lever back is named.
**Two things the phase grew.** **A long press on the canvas drops a nameless pin** — the only add-a-place route that spends nothing (no session, no Details, and deliberately no reverse geocode), named inside the place card ADR-0122 §7 already built; Phase 6b keeps its data-model questions. And **`＋ הוספת מקום` answers IN PLACE**: after a fourth owner correction (_"not refer you to the map if you want a place that already exists"_) the picker gains the half it was missing — `בטיול` above `מגוגל` — so picking a place the trip already has assigns it with **no navigation and no spend**, which is a cost _reduction_, since the picker can only ask Google today. Only "see where it is" or "the place isn't in Google" starts an **errand** to the tab, and on that the design's own draft **overstated ADR-0090** (it forbids _reading_ history; its Alternatives names this exact extension point, and `MapScopeProvider`/`useShowPlaceOnMap` already run the hand-over one field narrower). The map comes to the tab rather than a canvas into the picker, because a second live map is a **billed** load.
**A second owner round overturned two more of the design's own answers, and both are recorded in the ADR.** **The arm goes** (_"we'll live with the expenses"_) — ADR-0115 §1 withdrawn on this surface, on the better argument that the arm separated two intents on one field where the user has one, and the in-form picker has never had one; the min-chars floor, the pause-gated debounce, the session token, dedup-before-spend, the per-member·trip guard and the daily cap all remain, and the ADR says plainly that **past the floor every search now spends** in both modes, and **the exposure is taken back through the floor rather than a control**: `PLACE_SEARCH_MIN_CHARS` **2 → 3** (owner's number). Gating on the trip's match count was proposed and **rejected** — the count is not evidence about relevance, so the near-miss case (the trip has a similar-but-wrong place saved) reads as "there is nothing else"; the demand-gated deferral is designed, drawn and verified but **kept as a lever**, with ADR-0108 §6's budget alert as its trigger. And **at the maximized map, search moves nothing** — the draft normalised from both extremes, which shrank 516px of canvas to 250 for a query the canvas can often answer outright; only `full` drops to `half`, and the field's **count becomes the button** into the list.
**The build has three tiers, and the corrections are what made them separable:** §1–§7 (a day on one screen), then §8/§8a plus **the picker's trip-places half, the cheap win** (free, offline, no nav change, and it answers the common add), then the **errand**, whose return has to re-open a `Modal` the URL does not address and which is now a narrow exception path rather than the route.
**Still open to the device pass, one of which could block rather than tune:** whether a long press works over Google's own tiles at all (a platform context menu is neither detectable nor preventable everywhere); the real keyboard height and what iOS does with focus inside a `100dvh` PWA; whether a 44px field reads as a field over real tiles; whether a promoted ghost reads as "what you searched for, from another day" or as a contradiction; and whether a camera that does not move reads as quiet or as broken.

- **Phase 13 — the map is the route for a form's place, and a row tap commits** (owner, 2026-07-28 session 163; **DESIGNED session 163; §5-§8 + the errand mechanism BUILT sessions 163-164; the FORMS built session 165; **§9 BUILT session 179 — the phase is CLOSED**) — [ADR-0134](decisions/0134-the-map-is-where-a-forms-place-comes-from.md) + `mockups/map-errand-v1.html`, amending [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) §10, [ADR-0129](decisions/0129-map-camera-moves-like-a-camera.md) §1 and [ADR-0115](decisions/0115-plan-mode-place-research.md) §2. **This supersedes "Phase 10's remainder (a)", which is why that line is gone rather than pruned:** §10's first tier shipped in session 159 (`PlacePickerSheet`'s `בטיול` half — free, offline, no navigation, and the proof that "the trip answers first" is the right model), and the owner has now **reversed §10's conclusion**: the errand is the **route**, not the exception path. That is a change of mind, recorded as one. What reconciles it with the earlier correction (_"don't refer me to the map for a place that already exists"_) is a fact rather than a compromise — the map's own free half filters the trip's places **from the first character**, at no cost, offline, so nothing buys a search to find something the trip already holds. **Three things to build, in this order.\*\* **(1) The draft, first, because everything else is inert without it:** `{ target, returnTo }` becomes `{ target, returnTo, draft }` — a form is a `Modal` with local `useState` that no URL addresses, so leaving it for a tab loses a half-typed event, which is much worse than an extra tap. The forms gain a serialise/rehydrate pair and become a **third** consumer of the hand-over-and-consume-once channel `MapScopeProvider` already runs twice (`focusPlaceId`, ADR-0132's `queryOpen`) — generalise the pair into one named channel rather than writing a third copy (rule 8). `target.field` is **not optional**: a transport booking has two place fields, and without it a successful return can assign the right place to the wrong side. **(2) One pick, and the tap is not the pick:** in errand mode the verb _changes_ rather than being added — `בחירה` assigns and returns, no `＋ אולי` and no `MaybeItem` (the owner's "only choosing one place") — while the row tap still only **frames**, so you can look before committing, which is the whole reason to be on a map. `נווט` goes absent while an errand is live (the derived-affordance rule, and it keeps the trip row at 73px instead of growing). **(3) A row tap frames; a canvas tap pans**, which splits ADR-0129 §1 rather than reversing it — that section's reason ("being zoomed for a pin you can already see is a nuisance") is right for a pin and wrong for a row, since a row in a list is the one case where you cannot see the place. The span is **already derived** (`focusBoundsFor`, ADR-0129 §2), so this is two new callers plus one decision: a ring's neighbours are the trip's pins **plus the other rings**, and rings stay out of the camera's `points` because a query must not move the camera (ADR-0131 §5). From `full` the tap drops the sheet to `half` first, which is ADR-0121 §8's rule gaining a consumer. **What retires:** `PlacePickerSheet` (the sheet, not the field — `PlacePicker` stays as the form's display + launcher), and `AddLocationButton`'s two callers become errands too. **The result row's actions go side by side, measured:** `.map-right` is a column, so a second control costs **height** — stacked, a result row is 106px and the sheet shows 3 rows at 390×844 / 2 at 360×640, against 68px and 6 / 4 side by side. **And the device pass owns five things** (whether a 44×44 icon reads as "open in Google Maps" unlabelled and as subordinate to the verb; whether the sheet-drop-then-frame reads as one movement or two; whether `בחירה` reads right on a row that already says `כבר בטיול`; whether a derived span means the right thing for a result whose neighbours are other candidates; and whether the return lands where you left the form, since the draft carries values and not the view).
  **BUILT session 165 — the draft path.** `EventForm` and `BookingSheet` each own a draft type they write on launch and rehydrate from on return; `PlacePicker` gained one prop (`onFind`) so the FORM writes the draft, since the field cannot know what else is half-typed above it. **Five hosts, one hook:** `useReturnedPlaceErrand<D>(kind)` — `BookingSheet` is hosted by `DayView`, `PlanDay`, `PlanHome`, `IndexBookingsView` and the Map tab, and wiring only one of them would have returned an errand to a **closed** sheet with the rest of the edits gone, in four places. A host that renders a form owes it a way back. Each draft omits `error`/`saving`/`deleting` deliberately: they describe the last save attempt, not anything typed. **What is left is only §9:** the Map's own `＋ מיקום` enrich still opens `PlacePickerSheet`, so that sheet has exactly one caller — retiring it needs the errand to grow a fourth target (a coordless `Place` enriched in place, which is a row rather than an entity field), which is a small decision rather than mechanical work.
  **FIXED session 166, on four owner reports** (ADR-0134's second build-log addendum). **The one that was a real bug:** the re-open fired more than once — session 165's hook reported the payload as STATE, so it stayed readable while each host's effect depended on `events`/`bookings` (it must, to look the entity up), and saving the re-opened form re-fired it, re-opened the form on top of itself and wrote another copy of the event. A once-only channel does not give you a once-only effect, so the hook is a **callback** now (`usePlaceErrandReturn(kind, apply)`, `apply` read through a latest-ref). **Two prominence fixes:** under an errand every trip pin drops to the **dot tier** — same mechanism, different trigger, and `tier`/`aside` untouched so the camera still frames the trip — and a selected Google result now **fills** rather than outlines, on the row's badge and on the canvas ring alike, rising above the pins (`MAP_RESULT_SELECTED_Z`). The fourth is the map extreme, on the ADR-0132 line below.
  **§9 BUILT (2026-07-29, session 179), and it deleted more than it added** — ADR-0134's fourth build-log addendum. `PlacePickerSheet`'s last caller was the Map's own `＋ מיקום`, so the tab that **is** a search over a map was opening a second search surface over itself. The coordless row is now the **fourth errand target** (`{ kind: 'place', id }` — no `field`, because there is no form and nothing to assign: the pick writes the answer onto the row through `enrichPlaceId`), started locally because the tab is already the destination. `returnTo` became optional, and **that one field is what both exits branch on** rather than each asking what kind of errand it holds. **Only Google can answer a row errand** — our own list is where the coordless row already lives — which also removed a control that did nothing: the retired sheet offered the trip's own places for the enrich and the Map discarded the id it handed back. `PlacePicker`'s `onFind` is now required and the sheet fallback is gone; it existed for a `PlacePicker` rendered outside `MapScopeProvider`, and no such surface exists.

  **What the errand cost after it worked, recorded because the estimate was wrong by a lot:** sessions 166–177 are all follow-on, and only the first three are ADR-0134's own (its build-log addenda run to session 174). The rest became a **back-navigation** workstream under [ADR-0103](decisions/0103-back-navigation-typed-layer-model.md) — the errand is the app's first flow that navigates while its overlays unmount, and that is what exposed a global marker count (175), four hand-rolled panels that were never in the back stack at all (176), and markers left describing a URL the app had left (177). None of that is map work, and all of it was found by the map. The one thing 177 left open is the line below.

- **The errand leaks history, and unwinding it re-opens a rejected design** (found 2026-07-29, session 177, deliberately not fixed there) — each errand round trip permanently adds two history entries and strands a `?tab=map` behind the user; three trips leave you at index 7 of 9, against [ADR-0090](decisions/0090-back-is-computed-from-nav-state.md)'s flat in-trip history. **Nothing observable misbehaves today** — structural backs always `replace` and the stranded entries are never traversed into — so the cost is an unbounded forward stack, not a wrong back. It is on this list rather than fixed because ADR-0103's markers are **push-only by design**, on the stated grounds that programmatic `history.back()` reconciliation races Strict Mode and rapid re-renders; unwinding them is that decision being re-taken, which is an ADR amendment and not a patch. Take it when something observable depends on it, or when the forward stack is measured to matter.
- **Phase 10's remainder (b): a long press on the canvas drops a pin** — designed in [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) §9, and **blocked on Phase 6b rather than deferred by preference**: the only create path is `createPlace({ name })`, so a `Place` with coordinates and no `googlePlaceId` needs a backend + `@waypoint/shared` change, and 6b still owns verifying that the nullable `@@unique([tripId, googlePlaceId])` lets several coordinate-only places coexist. Everything else is decided: the gesture is a press-and-hold on the canvas **background** (a tap still clears the selection, and Google's own POI tap is a different act carrying a `place_id`), it spends **nothing** (no session, no Details, and deliberately no reverse geocode), the naming step is the shipped `.map-placecard` with a field where the name goes, and the write ends in the uncategorised `MaybeItem` `＋ אולי` already creates. Two things the build must not assume: whether the shelf's `useHoldToDrag` extracts cleanly for this (ADR-0121 §5's policy — **ask first** if it means a substantial refactor), and whether a long press survives Google's own tiles at all, which is the one device-pass item that could **block** rather than tune. **DONE (2026-07-30, sessions 196 design + 197 build; [ADR-0147](decisions/0147-a-place-is-made-on-the-canvas.md) §1–§3), with 6b unblocked in the same change.** The gesture is a **third phase of ADR-0145's existing recogniser**, not a second mechanism, so `useHoldToDrag` was never the question — the ask-first policy did not have to be spent. The point comes back **through Google's own projection** (ADR-0129 §3), never re-derived. The naming step is the `.map-placecard`, as designed, and the write creates the uncategorised `MaybeItem` ADR-0112 requires. **The one question the build could not answer is still open and still the same one:** whether a long press survives Google's own tiles on a real phone — simulated on a `div` in the mockup, and ADR-0146's device-pass sitting is where it gets answered.

- **Search must reclaim the app chrome, and unsaved Google results must be pins** (#device pass, 2026-07-28 session 159; **DESIGNED session 160, BUILT session 161; the decision it was waiting on was TAKEN by the owner and built in session 166, so what is left is one named gap — the coordless match at the map extreme, which has neither ring nor row** — two reports that share one surface, so they are one line). **(1) The chrome.** With the keyboard up the owner's Android screenshot shows a canvas squeezed to roughly **170px** between a full-height header and the tab bar: Android **resizes** the layout viewport, the ~490px header and ~130px nav keep their sizes because they are fixed content, and the split — the only flexible region — absorbs the whole loss. [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) §2 reasoned from an iOS model where the layout viewport does not shrink, and was therefore right about the wrong platform. The fix is what [ADR-0101](decisions/0101-index-search-mode-and-header-titles.md)'s Alternatives explicitly **rejected** — a search-mode flag through `AppShell`/`Shell` — and the trade is now fully visible: ADR-0101 chose a `Modal` variant to get chrome-hiding **for free** via z-index, and ADR-0131 threw that virtue away along with the defect it came with. **Reclaim the chrome without covering the canvas.** This is a concrete instance of the "app chrome's height on a full-bleed surface" line below, so read that one first and decide whether this is its own answer or the general one. **(2) The pins, and this one spends.** Unsaved Google results on our canvas contradicts ADR-0131 §8's central fact (a prediction carries no coordinates), so it cannot be built on the Autocomplete relay. Costed with the owner; **owner's call: switch this half to Text Search**, which returns coordinates _with_ the results so every pin costs one call instead of N — a new SKU, a new relay endpoint, and its own cost line, which is exactly what [ADR-0115](decisions/0115-plan-mode-place-research.md) §6 said it would need (it touches [ADR-0108](decisions/0108-maps-and-places-backend-architecture-key-model-and-cost.md) §3's envelope and [ADR-0111](decisions/0111-places-field-mask-tier-and-rating-deferral.md)'s mask tier). The rejected alternatives, recorded so they are not re-derived: Details-per-tap (cheap, one pin at a time, and free in the case that ends in an add) and Details-per-result (N calls per typing pause — the "opposite of dedup-before-spend" ADR-0115 §2 named). **And the pin is to be designed FRESH**, not inherited from the dashed "listed, not yet ours" mark: the owner's call is that a seventh population on this ladder deserves more than a reused one. **Sequence (2) with (1) rather than after it**, because results that are pins are visible at the map extreme — which may reopen the stop session 159 just closed while a query is live.
  **DESIGNED — what [ADR-0132](decisions/0132-search-reclaims-the-chrome-and-a-google-result-is-a-ring.md) settled (2026-07-28 session 160, `planning/2026-07-28-session-160-the-chrome-and-the-ring.md`, mockup `mockups/map-google-pins-v1.html`).** Both reports are answered and the mockup found three things the prose did not have. **(1) The trigger is the query disclosure being OPEN, not a live query** — the keyboard opens on **focus**, so ADR-0131's `searching` (non-blank) is right for the list and wrong for the chrome; the open-and-empty state is what the file draws. **(2) One modifier in the layout layer**, `.app[data-chrome='reclaimed']` hiding `.header` + `.nav` with `display: none` (not `visibility` — the space _is_ the point; nodes stay mounted per ADR-0078's one-frame invariant, so the day strip keeps its scroll and its all-days state; no transition, since animating it relayouts the split mid-animation). It is the **second consumer** of the surface-driven-modifier pattern ADR-0121 §5 created for this tab (`BODY_FULLBLEED` is the first), which **reframes** ADR-0101's July rejection rather than contradicting it. **(3) The safe-area insets live on the two nodes being hidden** — `.header` pays `--safe-top`, `.nav` pays `--safe-bottom` (App.css) — so the modifier pays them on the shell's body and **the reclaim is worth `276 − (top + bottom)`**, not 276. That one would have shipped as a notch bug invisible to every screen without insets. **(4) `back` gains one additive `resolveBack` rule**: a surface that hid the app chrome changed "where am I", so back closes the disclosure before leaving the tab — still not an overlay. **(5) The measurement is what makes this a condition rather than a polish pass.** Canvas at `half`, chrome kept → reclaimed: **390×844 43→165px**, **411×914 (the owner's) 61→183px**, **360×640 0→91px** — and at 360×640 the shipped surface **cannot legally render**, with a failure mode per platform: on Android the 28px pane cannot lay out Google's attribution at all, on iOS the pane does not shrink (so every number looks fine) and the keyboard **covers** it. Both are ADR-0106 §B, not density. **(6) The result mark is a ring, not a pin** — the prominence ladder is full, so the question was which **axis** is free, and shape is the only one: "not ours yet" is a difference of **kind**, not degree. No tip, no hue, a `＋`, sitting **on** the coordinate, off the ladder so nothing composes. The dashed teardrop (collides with the ghost rung, reads as "loading") and the dark inversion (out-ranks the trip's own places) are drawn and rejected in the mockup. **(7) Text Search's cost is stated plainly:** one call returns N results with coordinates, but against **what ships today** (Autocomplete + one Details on the pick) it may be **more expensive**, since the session token folds a session's requests into one billing when it ends in a pick and Text Search has no session. Per-1000 numbers deliberately unfixed (ADR-0108 §3's verify-at-implementation rule); `locationBias` to the canvas bounds is free relevance; the min-chars floor of 3 and the free-half-answers-first order are now the load-bearing controls.
  **BUILT session 161** (`planning/2026-07-28-session-161-the-chrome-and-the-ring-built.md`), in two tiers: **tier 1** the layout modifier + the safe-area rule + the disclosure-open predicate + the back layer (frontend only, spends nothing, and it is the half that clears the ToS failure); **tier 2** the SKU switch — `placeResultSchema`/`searchPlacesTextSchema` in `@waypoint/shared`, `GooglePlacesClient.textSearch` with its own field mask and `locationBias`, a `search-text` route under the same per-member·trip throttle, `usePlaceSearch` parameterised by **corpus** rather than forked into a second hook, and rings on the canvas as a population deliberately **not** on `PIN_TIER`. **Five things the build changed from the design, all in the ADR's build log:** the back rule already existed (`useBackLayer`, which `resolveBack` consults first — so `resolveBack` is untouched and nav-state learns nothing about this screen; it gained one `active` flag, for a layer whose owner outlives it); the place card's third occupant was **not** built, because the map extreme is still closed while a query is live so that branch cannot be reached — a ring tap selects its ROW instead, which is ADR-0122 §7's own rule; the **add got cheaper** and the design had missed it (`resolvePlace` takes optional client-supplied `details`, so a Text Search add spends **no** Place Details call — one call for the search, none for the add); `PlaceResearch` became presentational, forced by the SKU rather than by taste, since its results are now also rings and a component inside the sheet cannot hand anything to the canvas; and the search **bias is a ref, not a value**, because as a dependency a camera idle would re-bill the query.
  **The map extreme is BACK (owner session 166, ADR-0132 §8 amended in place).** Session 159 closed it while a query is live because the sheet shows no rows there — a coordless match had no pin **and** no row, and every Google result was a row with no pin. The first half still holds; **the second died when results became rings**, and the owner asked for the stop back (_"from the map search view you can't maximize the map"_). Shipped with §8's own condition built: a ring tap at that stop raises `ResultRow` inside `.map-placecard` (the card's third occupant), only `full` normalises on opening search, and each selection clears the other so two cards can never stack. The coordless-match gap is unchanged and still real. **And one more fix on top (session 167, owner: _"you can't see results that are already on the trip on the map"_):** §6 withholds the ring from a result the trip already owns "because it already has a pin" — true only while both halves match the same way, and they never did. Ours is a normalised substring (`matchesAnyTerm`); Google's handles transliteration and aliases, so `מון` finds `Moon Sushi Bar Pinsker` in Google's half and not in ours, and the place we OWN was filtered off the canvas while its row said `כבר בטיול` and pointed at nothing. A result the trip owns now counts as a match and is drawn as **our pin, not a ring**; its row and its pin select together, and at the map extreme the place card wins. **And the same report came back (session 168) because only the CANVAS half had been fixed:** the list's predicate gains the same clause, and Google's half now **drops** a result the trip owns rather than rendering it — so `כבר בטיול`/`על המדף` retires (copy and CSS deleted), the trip's own row saying all of it better, and a shipped duplicate goes with it (a place we own whose name matched our text was listed twice). **Three more from the same pass, all in ADR-0134's third build-log addendum:** `ביטול` now hands the draft back (§2 only ever spelled the draft out for the success path, so cancelling lost the half-typed event through the other exit) and the field assignment moved into the hook; arriving on an errand **opens the query field** so the keyboard is up; and the session-166 context demotion **exempts a search result, per pin** (`MapPin.match`, `:not(.match)`) rather than by switching off while a query is live — the owner's correction, and the shape ADR-0131 §4 already established. **Session 169 adds the second exemption and one camera fix:** `.selected` joins `.match` (the tapped place drew as a dot with the selection ring around it, i.e. a ring around nothing), and **[ADR-0129](decisions/0129-map-camera-moves-like-a-camera.md) §2's focus reach gains a cluster guard** — it took the furthest of the nearest three unconditionally, so one distant pin set the span even when another was right next door and the close neighbour sat in a frame sized for the far one. `CLUSTER_FACTOR` bounds a neighbour to a multiple of the nearest one's distance. **The far case is deliberately untouched** (owner: _"if there aren't any close pins, do the previous logic so that it doesn't zoom too much"_): when every neighbour is far they are all one far cluster, so the reach and the ceiling behave exactly as before and an isolated place keeps its wider frame. Nothing close is not the same as nothing. **Session 170:** a **double tap** on any row is the `בחירה` verb while an errand is live (errand-scoped on purpose; outside one the verb shelves a `MaybeItem`), and the **return owed to a booking** is fixed — `finishErrand`'s saved-booking path patched and navigated handing nothing over, so `＋ מיקום` from a `BookingDetail` landed on the bare screen behind it (`returnTo` is a URL; a `Modal` inside `IndexBookingsView`'s view state has none). Both exits now hand back, and a result carrying only a saved `target.id` re-opens that detail. **Session 171 corrected both of those:** the gesture belongs on the CANVAS and is not a double tap but **tapping what is already selected** (first tap selects, second commits — trip pin and Google ring alike, errand-scoped), and the booking return only worked for the three hosts that stay mounted. The Index's bookings screen is view state, not a route (ADR-0098), so returning to `?tab=index` rendered the landing — fixed with ADR-0050's existing `?booking=<id>` deep link via `withBookingDetail`, applied only when the destination is the Index tab. **Session 172 fixed the return properly, on the third attempt:** 170 and 171 both keyed on `target.id`, and neither addressed the real failure — the Index's bookings screen is view state (ADR-0098) and is **not mounted** when the return lands, so `usePlaceErrandReturn` never runs and nothing re-opens. True for saved and unsaved alike, and the unsaved case has no id to key on, which is why the id-keyed fix appeared to work for one and did nothing for the other. The return now asks the Index to MOUNT the screen via ADR-0050's `focus` param (`?focus=bookings`, no id — the pending result says which booking and what was typed), and **both paths land on the FORM** rather than the detail. Recorded in ADR-0134's fourth addendum with the general lesson: _"the host re-opens itself"_ is a rule with a precondition, and this app has two shapes that break it (a `Modal`, and a screen that is view state rather than a route). **A back audit came with it, and session 175 CLOSED it — reproduced in a browser, fixed, and written up in `planning/2026-07-29-session-175-back-parity-scan.md` (ADR-0103 amended).** The cause of the owner's reports was on none of the audit's five guesses: ADR-0103 counted history markers **globally**, so markers pushed at one URL made the next screen's layers look already-markered and a back rode an entry belonging to the screen you left — _"it sometimes exits to the main screen"_. Depth is per-URL now. Three surfaces whose visible back control had never been registered as a layer went with it (the Map's filter panel, Plan mode's two-step resolve sheet, the all-trips back arrow), and the app-wide rule is in `frontend/CLAUDE.md`. **Session 176 widened that rule and is the second half** (`planning/2026-07-29-session-176-implicit-dismissals.md`): what obliges back is that a surface can be LEFT, not that leaving it has a label — so a backdrop tap, an outside tap and Escape count too. `Modal` already tied those to its own `onClose`; the gaps were the hand-rolled panels that bypass it (`IconPicker`, `TimeField`/`TimePicker`) and a selected place on the Map, where back left the tab although a canvas tap only clears the selection. Same `useBackLayer`, gated on open state — which is also what orders the stack without reasoning about component trees. **One item still needs the owner:** a non-Home tab backs to trip **Home** even when you arrived from elsewhere (ADR-0090 §2 as designed; changing it is that ADR's amendment). Idle-resume draining overlays (audit #5) stays unverified — it needs a 30-minute hidden window and its own fixture. **Two more from session 177.** _Fixed:_ markers reconciled only on register/unregister, so a `replace` of a layer's own URL (every errand return does one) left the layer un-ridable — invisible on a cancelable press, and on the device's UNCANCELABLE consecutive press the traversal committed and closed the app; markers now reconcile on every location change (ADR-0103 amended, regression in `nav-state.system-back.test.tsx` because no browser harness can fire an uncancelable traverse). _Open, and a design call:_ **the place errand leaks two history entries per round trip** and strands a `?tab=map` behind the user (three trips → index 7 of 9), against ADR-0090's flat in-trip history. Nothing observable misbehaves — structural backs always `replace`, so the stranded entries are never traversed into — but fixing it means UNWINDING markers, which is the programmatic-`history.back()` reconciliation ADR-0103 rejected for racing Strict Mode. Re-opening that is the decision. **Guarded in the meantime (session 178):** the entries are harmless only because no back ever traverses, so that invariant is now enforced instead of remembered — a lint rule bans `navigate(-1)`/`history.back()`/`history.length` in app code (tests exempt: two harnesses simulate the platform), and `runStructural` is exhaustive over `BackAction` so a new kind cannot become the silent no-op that invites a `history.back()` "fix". **And the device pass owns five look questions** (the real keyboard heights per platform/screen — 336/365/300px are approximations; whether a ring reads as "not yours yet" over real tiles and whether N of them read as a group or as noise; whether `＋` is legible inside a 28px ring; whether a chrome-less map tab reads as "still in the app"; the owner's real safe-area insets), which join the existing tuning cluster rather than being tuned separately.

- **Phase 11 — a booking's phase reads on the canvas** (#22) — **done (2026-07-30, session 191; [ADR-0141](decisions/0141-the-pin-says-which-transition-is-next.md), mockup [`map-pin-phase-v1.html`](../mockups/map-pin-phase-v1.html)):** the `.pin-tag` slot now holds the transition word the row's own meta line already led with — `Map.tsx`'s `dayMeta` was already calling `eventEdgeTransition`, so the list read `צ׳ק-אאוט` while the pin above it read `היעד הבא`, the same split ADR-0137 closed for outcomes one object over. **Which end it is carries pre-vs-during**, so no prefix word was minted and no new vocabulary exists: `pinTransition` reads ADR-0063's profile through `placeMetaDay`, the row's own wording day. The three open questions all landed: the slot takes **one** line and the word wins it (`עכשיו`/`היעד הבא` stay the fallback for a place with no bracketed end, which is what a restaurant reservation has); the **budget does not move** because amber stays the two live cues and every other tag is `plain`, on ADR-0119's own neutral-tag precedent; and the **neutral tag is day-scoped**, which the mockup's §B2 forced by refuting the vocabulary-only ration — a trip's worth of bracketed edges collides on a phone, and all-days has nothing on a pin saying which day the word belongs to (ADR-0121 §6's argument for dropping the number there, transferred). §B1 measured the clearance: five tags fit across 360, day-scoped days measure zero collisions. **One carrier was owed and paid:** replacing the word left reduced motion with no way to tell `nowstop` from `nextstop` (4.4% of pin height apart, pulse killed globally), so the tag gains `.map-tag.now`'s own leading dot — Home's board blip, completing the "one idiom, three surfaces" `map.css` already claimed. It also corrected `constants.ts`'s 1.10× ink-reach note, measured on copy the app stopped showing. **Left to the device pass:** `--muted` ink at ~10px over arbitrary tiles, and a neutral tag occluding a neighbouring pin (3 on a deliberately heavy day, 0 on ordinary ones; the ladder's z-order orders it). The owner's "next phase maybe small cute specific animations" stays deferred and is now session 189's motion brief.
- **The app chrome's height on a full-bleed surface** (design, named by [ADR-0122](decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md) §9 and deliberately left there) — measured on the Map tab: `.header.mode-chrome` is **207px** and `.nav` **69px**, i.e. **276px of a 390×844 phone (33%) before the tab's own controls**, and 43% of a 360×640 one. Every other tab scrolls, so it pays that once; the split pays it twice, and it is why `half` cannot make both halves large on a small phone. Not the Map tab's to fix — the header day strip is _why_ the Map is a day-scoped surface (ADR-0109 §1), and the mode bar is mode identity — so a condensing pass for full-bleed surfaces is **its own design session, agreed with the owner on 2026-07-27** rather than smuggled into a Map-tab ADR. What it has to decide: which parts of the chrome a full-bleed surface may drop or condense (the mode bar is mode identity, ADR-0028; the trip row carries the switcher, the members and the settings way-in; the day strip is a shared component the Map, the Day view and Plan all read) and whether the answer is per-surface or global. What it must not break: the Map is a **day-scoped surface** because it uses that same strip (ADR-0109 §1), the strip's own `allScope` behaviour (session 81), and `AppShell`'s one-frame invariant (ADR-0078 — header and nav never unmount). Measure it the way ADR-0122 did, against the real tree at 390×844 / 390×734 / 360×640, and read `mockups/map-split-v2.html`'s budget panel first: it already prints these numbers. **Partially answered 2026-07-28 by [ADR-0132](decisions/0132-search-reclaims-the-chrome-and-a-google-result-is-a-ring.md)** — but only for **one state** of one surface: while the Map tab's query field is open, the header and the nav are hidden by a layout-layer modifier and the shell's body pays the safe-area insets they were paying. That is a precedent (the second consumer of ADR-0121 §5's pattern) and explicitly **not** the general answer: it is triggered by a surface entering a state, not by the surface being full-bleed. The general question — what the 276px is worth on a split that pays it twice, at rest — is still open and still not the Map tab's to fix.

- **The place viewer's way-in block: too many lines, and it overflowed the card** — **done (2026-08-05; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §8's 2026-08-05 amendment),** both from one owner screenshot of Ben Gurion at all-days. **Lines:** a booking-linked reference was drawing **two** rows carrying the identical label, told apart only by the leading word, so three facts read as six rows sitting between the notes and the row's primary action. Now **one entry per reference** — §8's own wording — going to whatever holds the detail, with the event's clock on the entry's meta line and its settle pair beside it; and the block folds at `PLACE_REFS_CAP` behind `עוד N`, keeping an unanswered passed day visible whatever its rank. **Overflow:** `.map-refs` had no `min-width: 0`, so flexbox's intrinsic sizing (§9.9.1) floored the block at the widest `nowrap` label's width — measured **491px inside a 340px card at 390**, which put `היינו`/`דילגנו` off the screen edge on rows whose own label was short. One declaration. The settle slot is also reserved at its **widest** state (`דילגנו` + undo, 84px) so settling a row no longer reflows the label.
- **The canvas place card sat on its own pin** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0122](decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md) §7's 2026-08-06 amendment).** §7's _"a tapped pin's place surfaces as a card over the canvas, and **nothing moves**"_ was written when the card was a 73px row; with a summary, a note section and a way-in block in it, the literal reading produces the failure the rule exists to prevent. [ADR-0128](decisions/0128-map-dot-tier-and-the-cards-camera-reserve.md) §2's own amendment fixed the half that has a camera move to correct (a **pan**, on a new selection); this is the half it cannot reach — the card appearing or growing with **no selection change**, which is what a sheet-stop switch, an arriving enrichment and a loading note list all do. `nudgeToClear` moves the minimum or **nothing**, so a place already in the band still moves the camera not at all.
- **One reference drawn twice on a place that is both endpoints of one booking** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §8).** `edgeOnDate` resolves the edge from the date and the caller handed it the **event's** date rather than the **scope's**, so its `date == null` branch was dead code: a car hire collected and returned at the airport resolved both endpoints to `start`, at the same moment, and drew two identical rows while the collection was nowhere on screen. Un-scoped the endpoints keep their own edges; day-scoped they collapse to one entry keyed on the **resolved** edge. A same-day round trip through one station stays two, and is tested.
- **The selected place drifted off-centre when the band changed the other way** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0122](decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md) §7's amendment, corrections (a) and (b)),** both reported from the device on the shipped nudge. (a) The nudge lifted a covered place to the card's **edge** and could only ever push **up**, so when the card left and the pane shrank the place sat low in an empty canvas; the target is the band's **centre** now — `panShiftForReserve`'s own arithmetic, so the pan and the correction agree by construction — and the trigger is the pane's observed height as well as the reserve. (b) "Stand down while a move is in flight" blocked the correction's own re-run: the pane resizes and the card renders in **separate commits**, so the first run aims at the bare canvas centre and the guard sent the second one home. It measures against the move's **destination** now, which cannot fight a correct ease (delta 0, no write) and can re-target a stale one.
- **An arrival from an event / booking / idea did not bring its row** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §8's 2026-08-06 amendment).** `מפה` framed the camera and set the selection while leaving the list where it was, so the selected row could sit below the fold. The arrival path had grown its own half-copy of `select`; it goes through the real one now and inherits the scroll a row tap has had since ADR-0135 §8. **It opens at `half`, measured rather than preferred:** clear map is `0.44S − 54` there against `S − 136 − card` at the map extreme, so the extreme only wins while the card is under ~265px and the reported card was 336 — the card costs more canvas than the sheet does, and costs most on the places worth arriving at. Trim the card under that and the pixel case flips, which is the one condition that reopens it. Also split `select`'s `fromRow` into **provenance** and **treatment** (`land`), because an arrival wants the sheet/frame/scroll but must not have the next-tap-closes-it rule.
- **A route's two ends were being treated as one place** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §8's amendment).** An overnight FRA → TLV flight put Frankfurt on the **arrival** day, at the arrival's clock, under the arrival's word (`נחיתה 02:00` at the airport you took off from), and Tel Aviv on the departure day as a departure. `spanDays` took the endpoint's edge and discarded it for a multi-day event; **`placeRefs` had the identical bug on its day-scoped path**, found by sweeping for the shape rather than reported. The rule is `routeEndpointDay` now and both read it — two derivations of one fact had drifted into the same bug independently, which is what naming it prevents. A span whose ends are ONE place (a hotel; a hire collected and returned at the same counter) is untouched and tested.
- **A settled reference kept a place "ahead of us"** — **done (2026-08-06, same amendment).** `isDayUsagePast` honours ADR-0117 §2's "a human outranks the clock" only when EVERY reference on a day is settled, and `until` counted them all — so a day holding a passed landing plus an 18:00 car return already marked `היינו` read as still ahead, on the strength of the tick. A settled reference now contributes no `until`.
- **A place named the wrong one of its references** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §8's amendment item 3, on the owner's call).** The day merge took `at`/`edge` from the **earliest** reference and `until` from the **latest** — each right for its own question, misreading together — so a place genuinely still ahead sat under `מה שלפנינו` naming something already behind you, unable to say why it was ahead. The day carries its **moments** now and the read side (which has the clock) picks: the next thing that has not happened, else the last one that did; a settled reference is never "next". Ordering and block placement are untouched — only what the row says. Deliberately **not** a list of every timing on the meta line: that is one tag on a dense row, and selecting the row already enumerates every reference with its own day and time.
- **`connectionStops` dated an overnight layover by the leg's DEPARTURE day** — **done (2026-08-06, same amendment).** `dateOf` read the event's start, so an inbound leg landing at 02:00 filed its layover under the evening you took off, when you were still at the origin airport. Its own doc already named the right two dates ("arrives on one and leaves on the next"); the code implemented neither for an overnight leg. Third consumer of `routeEndpointDay`.
- **The pin number is the index of a STOP, not of a place** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §6's amendment).** A caused defect rather than a discovered one: numbering read a place's **earliest** moment, which agreed with the row only while the row named the earliest too — and the fix that made the row name the **relevant** reference desynchronised them, so an airport read `1 · 18:00` above a place reading `2 · 09:00`. A day is a sequence of stops and a place visited twice is two of them, so the stops are numbered and a place shows the one its row names (the airport becomes `3`; the gap at `1` is the landing it already made). **§6's "a tick can never renumber a pin" is preserved by construction:** the stop list is clock-free, and the clock only picks which of its OWN stops a twice-visited place displays — so no once-visited place can ever move.
- **A transition row named the whole route, and its `מפה` went to the departure** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §8's session-148 amendment, amended again).** Two reports, one root: the row knows which end of the route it is about and neither its title nor its map affordance said so. The title showed `origin ← dest` where the row's own label already says `נחיתה`, and it truncated the **wrong way round** — the airport you left survived in full, the one you landed at was cut to a letter. `eventMapPlace` resolved through `eventPlaceId`, which answers with a transport booking's origin. Both take the edge now. **Stated rather than oversold:** `shortPlaceLabel` strips category noise and not a parenthesised official name, so a departure row still reads `פרנקפורט (Frankfurter Flughafen – FRA)` — this buys one half of the row, and a test asserts the shortening it does _not_ do. Stripping a trailing parenthetical is a change to a helper every route surface shares, and is not made here.
- **The arrival scroll lost a race when it had to widen the list first** — **done (2026-08-06).** `showRowInList` waited exactly one frame, enough when the row is already rendered and not when the same gesture called `setAllDays` to find it — reported as _"I can see that it's expanded, but it just doesn't land there"_. Bounded retry (`ROW_SCROLL_WAIT_FRAMES`), so a row that never appears costs a handful of no-op frames and stops. **jsdom cannot reproduce the race**, so this one is reasoned rather than test-proven; the tests hold the already-rendered path only.
- **The canvas place card is simply large** (noticed 2026-08-06 alongside the two above, not fixed) — measured off the owner's screenshot it takes well over half the canvas at the `map` stop, which is why the nudge above has to move the camera at all on a fresh selection. Cheapest spend to reclaim: an **empty** note section costs a header plus an `אין פתקים על זה` line to say nothing, and the canvas card is the one host where that trade is clearly bad. Worth measuring the card's blocks before choosing, rather than trimming the first thing that looks droppable.
- **`.map-tag.mbadge`'s plan violet on a Trip-mode row** (noticed session 158, pre-existing) — the `על המדף` tag takes `--plan-deep`, and shelf rows render in **both** modes, so the tab has been using plan-mode identity outside Plan mode since long before ADR-0131. Not that build's to fix (it re-coloured only the controls its own change put in Trip mode) and not obviously wrong either: this is a **statement**, not a control, and the shelf's colour is violet everywhere else it appears. Worth one deliberate call rather than a silent drift — the design-language question is whether `--plan` names "the shelf" or "Plan mode", and rule 4 currently says the latter.
- **The pan clears the card** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0128](decisions/0128-map-dot-tier-and-the-cards-camera-reserve.md) §3, amended in place).** Owner: _"selecting a place on the map (or search) opens the place card with the info (or the various add forms) and sometimes the card covers the place and the pin."_ §2 gave the card's band to the FIT's padding and left the PAN — which is what a pin tap, a row tap, an arrival and a locate all do — centring the place in the whole canvas as though nothing were over it. **Measured, the form fails outright:** ADR-0148 put it at 243px of a 372px canvas at the `map` stop, so a centred point sits 57px inside the form the long press just opened. The pan now centres the place in the part of the canvas you can SEE — `reserve / 2` south of it, through Google's own projection, **at the target zoom** (a pixel is a different distance at every zoom, and `locate` moves both). **The first build bounded the band only at the bottom and that was wrong in exactly the reported case:** the form's 129px band centres at y≈64, inside the ~88px the controls row and a pin's upward reach take, so it would have moved the pin out from under the form and under the chips. Both insets now, with `mapFitPadding`'s own `top` passed in so the pan and the fit cannot disagree — and clamped at 0, so a small card on a tall canvas moves nothing rather than pushing the place toward it. **One ordering fact had to change or the offset would have been silently dead on the very gesture it exists for:** the reserve is measured in the screen and the pan runs in the pane, and passive effects run child-first — so the pan saw the card that was up _before_ the tap, i.e. none. The measurement is a `useLayoutEffect` now. §2's read-it-through-a-ref rule is unchanged and now load-bearing twice: a pin tap still moves nothing it was not asked to.
- **The map's sheet and pins, sixth device pass** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0167](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md) §18 and [ADR-0122](decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md)'s 2026-08-06 amendment, both amended in place).** Two reports. **(1) A pin draws its thumbnail wherever it draws its glyph.** §16's `@container (min-height: 436px)` gate is retired — and what is reversed is the **conclusion, not the measurement**: 48px of pin really is where a head clears ~30px of picture, and at `half` a 34px pin really does carry ~21px. What that weighing missed is only visible in use, which is where the owner found it — a pin that shows a photograph at one sheet stop and a glyph at the next is the same object changing what kind of thing it is, on a drag, and that inconsistency costs more than a small picture gains. So the one surviving axis is the pin's TIER, which is the threshold this canvas already had: the photo drops in the very rules the glyph drops in. `--pin-base`'s floor becomes the legibility lever in its place. **(2) The sheet's body drags while it cannot scroll.** §4 widened the target from a 76×16 handle to a 390×51 region and stopped, so a sheet whose list fits offers a thin stripe over a large useless area. **Shipped twice the same day:** first as a `flex: 1 0 0` spacer after the content, which is the same idea reaching a subset of the cases and under-delivered on the one named first — an empty state is a tall glyph-and-text block, so it leaves no gap below itself while scrolling nothing (owner: _"when the list doesn't scroll … we should be able to use the same gesture"_). The rule is now one fact on the body itself, and that fact is what makes it easy where dragging from a scroller is hard: `touch-action: none` is what lets a drag be seen and is exactly what makes a list unscrollable, and a native pan cannot be taken over once started — **none of which arises when the content fits**, because then no pan can start. The spacer went, and with it the flex column and the `flex-shrink` guard a scrolling flex column needs. Two extractions came out of it rather than two new copies (rule 8): `lib/scrollable.ts` (the ancestor walk `useCenterSelected` kept private) and `lib/observe-resize.ts` (the `ResizeObserver` boilerplate that existed **three** times, one copy documenting itself as "the same trade `CreateTrip` makes"). **What it hands on:** whether a photograph reads at all at the 34px floor on a real screen is now the only thing standing between "consistent" and "noise", and it is a device question — if the answer is no, the lever is ADR-0123's floor, not a new gate. And a **full** list that fills the sheet still has no drag: that needs overscroll chaining — the list running out of scroll and handing the gesture on — which is the hard half of report (2) and the one place a drag and a scroll genuinely compete. The handle row and the view toggle both still work there, so it is a convenience gap on dense lists rather than a dead end.
- **The map's search and card, fifth device pass** — **done (2026-08-06; [ADR-0168](decisions/0168-the-search-answers-on-the-canvas.md)).** Five owner reports on one surface. **(1)** At the map extreme a result outside the view gave no sign anything was found, so a **settled** result set now moves the camera — [ADR-0131](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) §5's "a query is a stream" described the keystroke, not the response, which is one discrete fact already gated by the min-chars floor and the debounce. Four rules: nothing when all results are on canvas (the anti-jitter one), **pan** at the zoom you are on when the extent fits it, **fit** when it does not, and past a spread cap the **top-ranked result framed among its own cluster** — the answer to the owner's own open question, and a no to zooming out, since `דואומו` across four Italian cities fitted whole is four specks. Deliberately not `boundsFillView`, whose dwarfed re-fit is right for a filter and is the unasked-for zoom [ADR-0129](decisions/0129-map-camera-moves-like-a-camera.md) §1 removed. **(2)** The `＋`-in-a-circle ring was, unnoticed, wearing the **day style's own POI palette** (`#c9ccd4`/`#4b5568`/`#ffffff` in `design/map-styles/waypoint-map-day.json`), so it read as basemap furniture — and that is why it "looked better in dark": at night the POI is `#414b61` on `#191e2c`, so its competition got quieter, not the mark better. Redrawn in the canvas's own marker grammar in **teal**, which ADR-0028 reserves for location and the category palette may never take; the `＋` goes for naming the wrong gesture (a ring tap SELECTS). Five candidates measured beside a synthetic Google POI in `mockups/map-result-ring-v2.html`. **(3)** The reveal's scroll animates, and the ring tap's second `center`-aligned copy of it retires into `showRowInList`. **(4)** A row's tap reads three ways innermost-first (expanded → back, selected → close, neither → select), so the whole expanded card is the way back and the canvas card inherits it — but a row closes only once its OWN tap opened it, because a pin or ring tap only pans and the row's tap is [ADR-0134](decisions/0134-the-map-is-where-a-forms-place-comes-from.md) §6's framing. **(5)** A second tap on a **ring** shelves the result outside an errand: session 171 gated that gesture on "nothing to commit to", and there is — the shelf. **Two things it hands on**, both named in the ADR rather than fixed: `MAP_SEARCH_CAMERA`'s two numbers join the device-pass tuning cluster (how much unasked-for movement reads as helpful is exactly what a phone answers), and **the `אין מקומות באזור` readout is now visibly odd during a search** — it counts OUR places in view, which [ADR-0126](decisions/0126-map-canvas-chrome-two-camera-controls-and-an-area-sort.md) §5's area sort depends on, so it can say "no places in the area" over three teal rings. Changing what it counts is a decision, not a fix.

## User profile & members (scope + phasing in `planning/2026-07-28-session-159-user-settings-and-member-info-scope-and-phasing.md`)

Three requests triaged together: a user settings page reachable from inside a trip and outside it, a choosable/uploadable profile picture, and members reachable from the top bar with more than a name. The session note carries the per-claim code trace, the four scope calls and the rejected candidates with their reasons; the phases below are one branch / one PR each. **Two findings shape the whole epic:** `avatarColor`'s default is `#E9A63C` — **the amber accent** — so today's avatars spend ADR-0028's semantic palette on identity (and `app-shell.md` says shell chrome is never amber or teal), meaning identity needs its own non-semantic ramp and design-language.md gets amended; and the avatar circle is copy-pasted at ~8 call sites, so `ui/primitives/Avatar` is extracted **before** either surface, not alongside them (root rule 8 — ADRs 0078/0079/0094/0095 exist only to undo this shape of pile-up).

**DESIGNED — [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-the-user-is-a-surface-identity-ramp-and-a-reachable-roster.md) + `mockups/user-settings-v1.html` (2026-07-28, session 162). Phase 0 is closed; every question it was given is answered there.** The four that mattered: **`/settings`** is user-scoped and not trip-nested, and it is the first shell route with **more than one legitimate parent** — the entry writes `?from=` as a **closed enum** (never a path — an open-redirect shape) and `parentRoute` reads it, so back stays a pure function of nav state (ADR-0090) and survives reload. **The picture's three sources are a stored `avatarChoice`, not a fallback chain**, because someone with a Google photo who prefers a letter must be able to say so; a missing source falls back to initials and never to a broken image, which is also the designed **offline** state (the photo is a hotlink — the roster's names, roles and colours still come from the cached snapshot, so only the decoration degrades). **The tap is the choice** (no separate source radio), and upload is **absent, not disabled** until Phase 4 — the near-me rule. **The roster names who is here and a tap opens who they are** — the row is avatar · name · role, the joined date moved to the member surface, and that surface is the existing `MemberSheet` **generalized** rather than a second sheet beside it (one component, admin verbs gated by role; the gate is server-side and singular per ADR-0039, so two entry points are not two gates — correcting the design's first draft). Once one tap lists everyone the `+2` dead end is deleted rather than redesigned. **The ramp is five hues defined by two constraints** — outside the semantic trio, and **lower chroma** than the `--cat-*` pin hues, since hue angle alone cannot carry it (avatars in the chrome co-occur with pins on the Map canvas) — with the default **derived from `user.id`**, because one column default is what made every real user identical. Now named in `design-language.md` beside the pin hues.

- **Two shipped defects the mockup's render exposed** (ADR-0133 §10; **Phase 3 fixes them rather than propagating them**, since it reuses both elements) — `+{overflowMembers.length}` (`App.tsx:266`) renders as `n+` in the RTL chrome, the ADR-0118 isolate bug the frontend `CLAUDE.md` already documents for `−3`; and `.role.owner` (`screens.css:2218`) paints the admin badge `--amber`/`--amber-deep` for a state that is neither time nor commitment (ADR-0028 / rule 4), wrong since trip settings shipped. Found only because the mockup inlines the app's **real** css via the `APP-CSS` manifest — the same binding caught the first pass drawing a `⚙` emoji where the app renders `Icon`'s cog SVG.
- **Phase 1 — identity foundation** — **done (2026-07-28, session 164):** `ui/primitives/Avatar` is the one renderer, replacing all 8 hand-drawn circles (`App.tsx` ×3, `TripSettings` ×3, `AllTrips`, `ZeroState`) — it owns source resolution, the initials fallback, the ink-on-hue pairing and the ring, and `size="inherit"` lets the in-trip chrome keep owning its own 31px/border/overlap geometry while taking only the resolution. `AVATAR_INITIAL_LENGTH` now has exactly one reader, and `.acct-av` is deleted. **The hue is a KEY, not a hex** (`avatarHue`, `plum|rose|moss|denim|cocoa`) so the five new `--id-*` tokens in **both** `tokens.css` theme blocks reach a stored pick — the refinement ADR-0133 §5's build note records, along with the column being **nullable** (null = never chosen) and the **DTO** being what is always resolved, via `resolveAvatarHue` in `toUserDto`. `deriveAvatarHue` (djb2 over the id, `packages/shared/src/identity.ts`) is the default, so the amber column default that made every real user identical is **gone**. `GoogleUserinfo` declares `picture` and sign-in stores it (refreshed every time; `avatarChoice` defaulted only at create, so a returning user who chose initials is not flipped back). `PATCH /me` added — the write path that did not exist — with `getMe` routed through `toUserDto` rather than its own spread, so there is one user-shaping path. Migration `20260728190000_user_identity_adr0133` drops `avatarColor` outright (every row held the meaningless default) and was applied against a real Postgres, not just generated. Tests: `identity.test.ts` (stability, ramp-membership, spread, and that a legacy hex derives rather than passing through) + `Avatar.test.tsx` (11 cases incl. the no-referrer photo and the `inherit` geometry rule).
- **Phase 2 — the settings page** — **done (2026-07-28, session 165):** `/settings` + `/settings/picture` as real shell routes (`screens/UserSettings.tsx`, `screens/UserPicture.tsx`), and **`AccountSheet` is deleted** along with the two wrapper components that existed only to hold its open-state — the avatar now navigates from all three shells. **Back is entry-dependent:** `parentRoute` gained the `?from=` rule (a closed enum, `SETTINGS_FROM`; anything unrecognised falls to `/trips`), the picture page hands the target back up so two backs land where you came from, and `NavSnapshot` gained `search` because the parent is a function of the whole location now. Name editing saves on blur (LWW; an emptied field reverts rather than writing a nameless user) through `patchMe` on the auth context, which replaces `me` **and** the cached copy so the offline cold-load renders the new name. The picture page is the **two-state** design: a photo in use → the photo + remove, no ramp; no photo → initials + `צבע הרקע` + the ramp + the way back, with `google`-but-no-URL treated as no photo so a revoked photo can't strand the page. Upload/camera **absent, not greyed** (Phase 4). `app-shell.md` §6 rewritten — it had said "Profile editing is **deferred**" since the shell was specified. **Verified in the running app, not only in tests:** rename and hue-pick round-trip against a real backend + Postgres, and the `picture → /settings?from=home → /` back path lands in the trip rather than `/trips`. 20 new tests (nav rule, both screens); frontend 1524 green.
- **Phase 3 — members from the top bar** — **done (2026-07-28, session 166):** the header cluster is a real `<button>` (74×44, so it meets the touch floor the old `<div title=…>` never did) opening a `RosterSheet` that lists **every** member — which is what turns `MEMBER_AVATAR_CAP` back into a rendering detail instead of a truncation. Row = avatar · name · role · `אתה`; the joined date moved to the member surface. **One member component, two entry points:** `MemberSheet` moved out of `TripSettings` into `ui/domain/` and gained the detail rows, and `MemberRow` is now the one row both lists render (`.set-member`), so they cannot drift. Email is deliberately off it. **Both ADR-0133 §10 defects fixed and verified in a browser, not just asserted:** the overflow chip renders `⁦+2⁩` (an `ltrIsolate`, so the sign stays in front — it read `2+`), and `.role.owner` is now `--ink` on neutral with an inset hairline, measured as `rgb(22, 35, 61)` where it had been `--amber-deep` on amber-16%. Plus one the roster exposed: `.set-member .av` kept `.av`'s **chrome** border, a 2px indigo ring meant for avatars overlapping on the dark header — invisible as a one-off in settings, obvious as five in a column, now dropped on paper rows. **The admin verbs are host-provided:** `MemberSheet` takes optional `onPromote`/`onRemove` and only trip settings passes them, because it owns the confirm prompt and the "Removed" list a kick must refresh — extracting that flow to give the roster the same verbs is a real refactor and rule 8 says ask first, so it is the line below rather than silent scope. The joined date renders through a newly-exported `formatDayMonth` (`lib/time.ts`) rather than a hand-rolled `14.3` — the app already had one numeric date shape (`he-IL` `2-digit`, as in `20.07–29.07`) but its formatter was module-private, so reuse meant exporting it. 16 new tests; frontend 1559 green.
- **The member cluster is absent on a solo trip** — **done (2026-07-28, session 167):** reported straight after Phase 3 shipped — "when the only member on a trip, I can still click on 'nothing' and get to the החבורה". The cluster renders `others`, so with no co-members it drew nothing while its **button still rendered**: an invisible ~8×44 tap target (the `.avatars-btn` padding + the touch floor) opening a roster that listed only you, which the ringed account avatar beside it already shows. Now gated on there being someone else — **absent, not empty** — which is the rule every other derived control here already follows (ADR-0045's quick tiles, ADR-0109 §6's near-me chip): no source, no control. The three inline expressions behind it (`filter` me out, `slice` the cap, `slice` the rest) became one pure `memberCluster()` in `lib/member-cluster.ts` with the `show` rule named beside them and 6 tests, because that is where the bug came from and the header itself has no test harness. Verified both ways in the running app: solo → 0 cluster buttons and 0 empty `.avatars` spans with the account avatar untouched; two members → the cluster back and a 2-row roster.
- **Admin verbs from the roster** (open, deliberately not taken in session 166) — the roster's member surface is read-only because promote/remove are entangled with trip settings' own confirm dialog and its `reloadRemoved()` refresh of the ADR-0067 "Removed" list. `MemberSheet` already accepts the two callbacks, so the UI side is done; what is missing is extracting that confirm-plus-reload flow into something both hosts can call. Worth doing only if an admin actually wants to act from the chrome — the gate has always been server-side (ADR-0039), so this is ergonomics, not permissions.
- **Phase 4 — avatar upload** — **done (2026-07-28, session 169):** the phase Phase 2 shipped **absent**, asked for twice by the owner. `POST`/`DELETE /me/avatar` plus a `@Public` `GET /users/:userId/avatar/:key`, and the trust-class call it owed is now [ADR-0133 §12](decisions/0133-the-user-is-a-surface-identity-ramp-and-a-reachable-roster.md): **an avatar is not a document, and the divergence is the design** — not encrypted (encrypting a face we publish to the group is theatre, and it would cost the hard caching an `<img>` wants), unauthenticated (an `<img>` cannot send a bearer token; the opaque key is the same unguessable-capability class as the `googleAvatarUrl` we already hotlink, which Google itself serves to anyone holding it), and **inline** — which is safe only because the type is **sniffed from the bytes** and pinned with `nosniff`, never trusted from the upload, with SVG rejected for free since it has no binary signature. The key rides in the PATH, so the URL is immutable (`immutable` for a year is honest), a replace mints a new URL, and a retired key 404s — which is what makes "remove the photo" actually stop serving it. The client centre-crops and re-encodes to a ≤512px JPEG **before** upload, so the 512 KB server cap bounds a hostile client rather than real users, and the square is honest rather than CSS hoping a face is centred. `usePickFile` replaced §6's proposed `FilePicker` variant — the plumbing is shared, the two presentations share nothing, and camera **facing** became a parameter (a document wants the rear camera, a self-portrait the front). `storage.ts` + `blob-cache.ts` moved to `common/`, since a second caller made "in the documents module" a lie. **Four defects found by rendering and by the repo's own guard**, all in the session note: two visible `<input type=file>` controls (a hook emitting DOM styled by a stylesheet only `FilePicker` imported), a hint claiming the initials were shown under a visible photo, a silent + mis-labelled rejection from sniffing an error string, and `users` missing from `SERVER_ROUTE_PREFIXES` — which the **service worker** reads too, so the PWA would have served the app shell for every avatar URL in production.
- **Copy the Google photo into our own storage at sign-in** (an ADR-0133 alternative, refused in Phase 1 and **now cheap** — Phase 4 brought the infrastructure in) — today `googleAvatarUrl` is a hotlink, so a Google-photo avatar is the one thing on the roster that does **not** work offline (it degrades to initials, ADR-0133 §4) and it costs a third-party request per render. With `common/storage.ts`, the sniffer and the content route already built, copying the bytes at each sign-in is a small addition rather than the "drag `storage.ts` in to buy a decoration" it was refused as. What it still needs decided: a refresh policy (the URL changes when the user changes their Google photo, so "re-fetch every sign-in" is the obvious answer and worth stating), and whether `avatarChoice: 'google'` then becomes indistinguishable from `'upload'` on the wire — it probably should not, since the picture page's "use my Google photo" way back depends on telling them apart.
- **Presence is broadcast and the frontend drops it** (considered, deferred as scope in session 159 and again by ADR-0133 — **not** as a bad idea) — `sync.gateway.ts:165-173` sends `{ userId, connected }[]` on every connect/disconnect; `frontend/src/lib/ws.ts:38` declares `PRESENCE` **with no payload** and no handler reads it. So "who's online" is the cheapest live signal in the codebase, needing FE plumbing only. Deliberately off the member card for now (a roster that states identity + role + joined answers the reported need), but the FE/BE type mismatch is a latent trap for whoever touches the stream next: either consume it or say in the type why it's ignored.
- ~~**The identity ramp owes a device + dark-mode pass**~~ — **answered 2026-08-02 (ADR-0158 §12), and the answer is "no change".** Rendered in both themes at every size it ships at (26px chrome stack, 31px account, 44px roster, 92px profile) beside the category pins it must never be mistaken for. `plum`/`rose`, the pair §5 flagged as closest, is ΔE00 13.4 light / 12.1 dark and separable at 26px. The measured worry was elsewhere and did not survive the render: §5 keeps identity clear of category by **chroma**, and in dark that margin collapses on paper (`--id-moss` 25.5 vs `--cat-leisure` 26.2, ΔE00 6.5) because the two sets were lifted for the dark board independently — but a saturated teardrop on a canvas and a muted disc with an initial in it stay obviously different things. What the pass did settle for real: §5's non-negotiable that **one dark ink serves every hue in both themes** is now measured, not assumed — `#12203A` clears **5.42 minimum in light, 6.61 in dark**.
- **Account deletion** (from session 159) — the settings page ships without it by decision. The schema's cascades would carry most of it, but it needs its own consequence design: what happens to a trip whose **last admin** deletes themselves (`app-shell.md` §7 auto-promotes on leave; deletion is not leaving), and what happens to their authored events/bookings/documents. Write the ADR then.

## Tasks (משימות)

- **The tasks feature** (owner ask, 2026-08-15; [`planning/2026-08-15-tasks-design-brief.md`](planning/2026-08-15-tasks-design-brief.md) Part 1 · [ADR-0188](decisions/0188-a-tasks-tick-is-a-sibling-and-the-leading-element-says-who-owns-the-outcome.md) · [`mockups/tasks-row-and-refusals-v1.html`](../mockups/tasks-row-and-refusals-v1.html)). **Six phases, one PR each — the plan and the handoff are [`planning/2026-08-15-tasks-build-plan.md`](planning/2026-08-15-tasks-build-plan.md)**, which carries the scope, the status and every decision taken during the build; read that rather than re-deriving from here. ~~**Phase 1 — the spine**~~ **built 2026-08-15** ([session note](planning/2026-08-15-tasks-phase-1-built.md)): the entity across all three layers, the sync plumbing (one entry in each of the three registries, no new mechanism), the Index's **fourth** tile, the flat urgency-ordered screen with its one facet axis, create/edit/due/assignee/`important`, and done/dismiss/delete. `ListRow` gained **one** slot (`lead`) and no other consumer changed. Five build-time calls are in the note, two worth knowing here: **the task PATCH is SPARSE where the note PATCH is whole-content** (a task has two edit surfaces — the editor and the tick — so absent-means-cleared would let one tick erase the task's words; a spec caught it), and **a date-only deadline resolves to the END of its day** (`DAY_DEADLINE_HHMM`), because 00:00 would make a task due today overdue one minute past midnight. **Remaining first: the phase-1 FOLLOW-UP**, designed 2026-08-15 and not built — [`mockups/task-editor-and-read-v1.html`](../mockups/task-editor-and-read-v1.html) + [session note](planning/2026-08-15-tasks-editor-design-session.md), from four owner reports on the shipped build. ADR-0188 designed the row and the bands and **never designed the editor**, so phase 1 built one from `NoteSheet`'s shape unrendered; the reports are that gap. Measured: `חשובה` is **29px against ADR-0017's 44px floor** (it is missing `EventForm`'s shipped `יש הזמנה` idiom, ADR-0136 §1), the assignee row is the app's **filter** grammar spent on an assignment and comes with a default nobody chose, **`של כולנו` is the word phase 6's _everyone_ state needs** and phase 1 spent it on _unassigned_, and **`body` is write-only** — the editor writes it and nothing renders it.
