The proposals reuse rather than add: `ChoiceGrid layout="pills"` with an `AvatarPerson` where the glyph goes (**46px** against the shipped pill's 28px, and a new host is explicitly not bound by the 44px debt `choice-grid.css` records in place), and ADR-0153 §4's open-in-place for the read (**+46.3px** against **216.5px** for the rejected detail sheet). **The owner answered all three forks on 2026-08-15 and they are folded into the mockup, which re-rendered clean against each** ([session note](planning/2026-08-15-tasks-editor-design-session.md)). **`חשוב`, not `חשובה`** — a label does not inflect for `משימה`. **`לא משויך`** for the unassigned state, over `מישהו מאיתנו` (accurate, but it _promises_ someone will take it) and `של הקבוצה` (repeats the phase-6 collision in other words) — **and its default comes back**, reversing the session's own first recommendation, because the word is what reverses it: a presumed `של כולנו` is a **claim** that can be false, a presumed `לא משויך` **describes the form's state** and cannot be, and `assigneeUserId: undefined` is saved either way. **The Index order is `הזמנות · משימות · מסמכים · פתקים`** — the three below the spine ordered by whether a tile can be LATE, with bookings keeping the lead it has held since ADR-0047/0049; the earlier `משימות`-first recommendation was dropped against `Index.tsx`'s own comment that a task's prominence comes from the **Home bands** rather than from chrome. ~~**Phase 1b — the editor and the read**~~ **built 2026-08-15** ([ADR-0189](decisions/0189-the-editor-uses-the-idiom-the-app-already-had-and-a-task-is-read-where-it-sits.md) · [session note](planning/2026-08-15-tasks-editor-built.md)), which also promotes the mockup from Proposed. `חשוב` as `EventForm`'s `יש הזמנה` row (**44px** measured in the running app, against 29), the assignee row as `ChoiceGrid layout="pills"` with a person where the glyph goes (**45.7px** against 28, the density scoped at the new host so the deferred pill debt stays where `choice-grid.css` records it), `לא משויך` selected by default, the row **opening in place** so `body` finally has a reader, and the tile reorder pinned by a spec. **Four build-time calls in the note, two worth knowing here:** `Choice.lead` is a **`ReactNode`, not the `AvatarPerson` the design specified** — the unassigned option is a person-shaped absence with no person to pass, and a typed field would have forced the default answer into a different shape; and the open-foot was **generalised, not copied** (`RowOpenFoot` + `.row-open-*` in `ui/domain/`, two consumers on day one, four test files and one e2e spec renamed with it). **And a bidi defect that only the running app could catch:** `.tsk-open-body` renders stored content and needs `dir="auto"` (ADR-0118) — without it the first glyph of `2-14-5 Kabukicho, Shinjuku` painted at **x=404** in a box spanning 53–449, and at **x=67** with it, while the unit suite stayed 3818 green because jsdom reports every rect as zero. ~~**Phase 2 — automatic tasks**~~ **built 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0190](decisions/0190-a-readiness-check-is-a-task-row-and-the-checks-sit-inside-the-urgency-ladder.md) · [the six questions](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-2-open-questions.md) · [the build](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-2-built.md) · [mockup](../mockups/tasks-open-questions-before-phase-2-v1.html)). It needed **six decisions first**, and the session stopped to draw them rather than settle them in a commit. **The order is the one worth knowing:** the checks sit **inside** the urgency ladder — urgent (`important` or overdue) → checks → the rest — in **one list**, which is the owner's revision of _both_ orders the mockup drew and what finally makes brief §2's "one noun, one list" true on screen. The checks show under `הכל` alone (the other chips read a `Task` row an untouched check does not have) and never reach the Index tile, which would otherwise have read **7 against 3**. A check's verb goes **where the thing lives** — only flights and lodging need Home, via ADR-0050's `FOCUS_PARAM` pointed at a second tab. `.chk-row` and its parts are **deleted**, `.chk-ok` survives. **And ADR-0188 §3 is amended:** the row shows who owes it as a **person**, because that ADR's "a second identity system per row" premise expired when ADR-0189 put `Avatar` in the editor for the same field — measured at **no cost** to the title column (201px) or the row (61px), and it also gives an unassigned task something to say where it said nothing. Two build calls: `createTaskSchema` gained `status` (dismissing a rowless check is a create, not a create-then-patch), and **opening the `⋯` writes nothing** — the first draft minted the row on menu-open, making a read a write, which a spec now pins. **Then phases 3–6**, scoped in the plan — **2** automatic tasks (`computeReadiness` renders as task rows, `derivedKey` wired, Plan Home converges, `.chk-*` retires; watch 360px), ~~**3** the Trip Home band~~ **built 2026-08-16** ([note](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-3-built.md)) — manual only, because an automatic task's deadline is departure and mid-trip every unmet check would sit there permanently overdue; due today and overdue straight off `taskBand` rather than re-derived; capped at 3 with the overflow row **inside** the card; **absent entirely** when nothing is due. Measured live at **226px** for three rows plus the overflow. Two judgement calls in the note, neither of them ADR-0188's to make: the band sits **above `גישה מהירה`** under the board, and a row's tap reaches the tasks **screen** via ADR-0050's `focus` deep-link (`INDEX_FOCUS.TASKS`) rather than the Index landing, **ship after 3**; then the expensive half — ~~**4** hosts~~ **built 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0191](decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) · [note](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-4-built.md), which also carries **phase 3r** — both Homes now order through the same `orderTaskRows` the Index runs, and Plan Home's completed collapse counts manual tasks as well as checks): a new **checkbox** glyph (✓ is the tick's mark now that the automatic row has no badge), counting **open only** — the one place a task's mark parts company with a note's, since a note has no lifecycle — on all **five** hosts, each getting a section in the surface it already had, ordered documents → tasks → notes. Two things the running app added to the drawing: the three-mark line costs **0.6px** of baseline spread on a 60px booking row, and sharing `.note-sec` as a root class made `querySelector('.note-sec')` start finding the **tasks** section (four shipped specs caught it; the root is `.note-sec.tsk-sec`). **The host-cascade generalisation was taken and it covers TWO of the four appliers, not three** — `dropAttachmentsForHostChange` reads a different table and carries an extra case, `clearPlaceRefsForChange` clears a field rather than dropping a row, and unifying either needs a flag argument whose job is "behave differently"; ADR-0191 §5 says so in place, because the next reader of "generalise the appliers" would otherwise finish the job wrongly. ~~**5** the hero slot~~ **built 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0160 §U](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) · [mockup](../mockups/a-task-in-the-hero-horizon-v1.html) · [note](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-5-built.md)) — brief **§E designed and signed off first**, then the **amendment in place** to §3/§13 that brief §11 required by name. **Two of that ADR`s own premises were stale and §U says so:** §3 has admitted **four** things since §T added an attached document without renumbering it, so a task is the *second* fourth-thing; and §13`s "no note on the **next** event" was **false in the shipped app** since commit `9b284e57`. §U0 replaces the list with a rule — **content is a block, a way OUT of the point is a chip** — which is why a task is not §T`s chip. Owner answered three forks, all as drawn: the **checkbox** mark (reads-as-pressable risk scheduled onto the device pass, fallback drawn), the assignee **face** (0px), **one** task plus `ועוד N`. The measured half: **0px** of line-start delta against the note beside it (ADR-0191 §5 shipped that number at **40px** and was reversed on a device — here it was measured before the build and again in the app), **87.1px** of card, **0** interactive descendants, and the paper deadline inks measure **2.93:1 / 2.44:1** on the board in **light mode only**, which is why the dark screenshot looked healthy. One build call: `tasksForContext`— a task about a flight is written on the **booking**, so the hero reads tasks through the same host context its notes and documents already go through, and the running app is what proved it. **6**`everyone`tasks. **Still owed as design before its phase: §C** (an`everyone`task partially complete, phase 6). **§E is closed** by ADR-0160 §U and **§F** by ADR-0191. Three feel questions stay on the device pass — whether a 26px ring reads as pressable, whether control-versus-badge reads as "who owns this" unexplained, and the final Trip Home band cap (drawn at 3, 242px); the owner's call was **ship as drawn, restyle later**. **A fourth joined them 2026-08-16:** the Trip Home band's cap of **3**, which is two characters in`constants.ts` (`TRIP_HOME_TASK_BAND_CAP`) if a real phone says otherwise.

- **An automatic task's tick is the SAME control, and it works** (owner, 2026-08-16, built — [ADR-0188](decisions/0188-a-tasks-tick-is-a-sibling-and-the-leading-element-says-who-owns-the-outcome.md) §4 **reversed in place**). _"Makes no sense that the indication for a complete task is different for automatic and manual tasks. Both should look (and behave!) the same."_ §4's corner was real — a derived row's done-ness could not be pressed, so a tick there would be inert — and there were always two ways out of it: a different leading element (§4's answer) or **make the circle work** (the owner's). The second is better on brief §2's own terms, since §2 wanted one noun with one row shape and §4 had to carve an exception out of it. The rule is symmetrical with no special cases: **a human answer wins in both directions; until there is one, the derivation answers.** `.chk-ok` and its trailing `הושלם` are **deleted** — the last of the `.chk-*` family, and two indications for one state was the defect. **And the badge goes too** (same day: _"we don't want both an icon and a done tick circle"_), for a reason stronger than crowding — it **restated the title**, `טיסות` beside a plane, the same sentence twice six pixels apart; which also settles §1's own walked-back finding that _"a task has no icon slot to fill"_. The two kinds now open identically; the only remainder is the reserved sync column a manual row keeps and a check does not (§7), measured at 286px of title column against 259px. **The cost, taken knowingly:** a stored `done` can go stale (tick "book a hotel" without booking one). Bounded by un-ticking handing the question back to the data, and by `resolvedReadinessPct` reading the same resolution so the prep hero and the list can never disagree on screen — `computeReadiness` stays pure, so brief §3 is untouched.

- **Four owner corrections to the tasks surfaces** (2026-08-16, all built — [ADR-0190](decisions/0190-a-readiness-check-is-a-task-row-and-the-checks-sit-inside-the-urgency-ladder.md) §1 amended in place, [phase-3 note](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-3-built.md) corrected in place). **The readiness checks now COUNT** toward the Index tile and **appear under `הושלמו`** once satisfied or dismissed — reversing ADR-0190 §1's exclusion, whose measured objection (a new trip reading "5 משימות פתוחות") turns out to be the right answer rather than the wrong one: such a trip does have five things to do. `שלי` is unchanged and stays a construction rather than a rule — a check with no row has no assignee, and delegating one puts it there. **The Home bands look a WEEK ahead** (`TASK_BAND_LOOKAHEAD_DAYS`), not just today-and-overdue: a task due Friday is actionable now, not on Friday. The section reads `משימות קרובות`, since `להיום` is false at a week. **And Plan Home is genuinely converged at last** — ADR-0188 §6 always said "automatic first and manual after", phase 2 built the automatic half and phase 3 put the manual half on Trip Home only. Both halves now order through the same `orderTaskRows` as the screen, over a shared `TaskBandRow`. One consequence named because it is not a wrinkle: `done` is **derived**, so a satisfied check can _leave_ the settled facet on its own when a booking is deleted, where a manual task never does.

- ~~**Plan Home says "הכול מוכן" over open tasks, and the prep hero lifts**~~ — **BUILT (2026-08-16; [ADR-0193](decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md), mockup [`the-plan-hero-lifts-and-the-checklist-counts-everything-v1`](../mockups/the-plan-hero-lifts-and-the-checklist-counts-everything-v1.html), [session note](planning/2026-08-16-plan-hero-lift-design-session.md)).** Owner: _"the home readiness should also show non automatic tasks … right now it says all done where it isn't true - that's misleading"_ and _"we should create a lifted plan hero for upcoming tasks"_. **The screen already renders manual tasks and the WINDOW is the bug:** its list is `tasksDueSoon`, which needs a `dueAt` and admits only overdue-or-within-7-days, so an **undated** task and anything a week out are invisible on the one screen whose countdown reads `בעוד 47 ימים` — Trip Home's rule, inherited without ever being argued here. **And the two halves have never asked the same question:** `completedManual` has no date window at all, so an undated task is invisible while open and appears under `הושלמו` the moment it is ticked, which is what makes widening the only consistent repair rather than one option. `הכול מוכן 🎉` survives, re-gated on nothing being open at all (the mockup keeps a genuinely-empty state so deleting it would be visible as a loss). The readiness bar keeps its five checks and gains a **second readout with its own noun** — one mixed denominator makes writing a task read as losing ground, and a second bar needs a denominator an open list has not got. Urgent + checks inline, the far and undated behind one **44px** row (`CollapseToggle` at a second density). **[ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §H is superseded by its own "Revisit when" clause**, not against it: once the remainder collapses the hero summarises what the screen no longer shows, so it lifts — five bands cut against the **departure** (`דחוף · מה חסר · לפני היציאה · בזמן הטיול · ללא תאריך`), which is the one thing this hero can say that no other surface can, since the countdown 40px above is the line they are cut on. Every row is `.hero-task` as §U ships it: zero new row rules, and a read. **The skin took three owner rounds and the third reversed the second's technical answer:** violet throughout, on one card-wide ramp with **no seam** — a flat deep ground would have reused `--on-dark-*` untouched, but a _gradual_ ramp's top is bright violet, where that ramp is 1.45–2.02:1 in light and **passes** in dark (4.04/5.63), so the dark screenshot looked healthy for the third time in this area. Cost: one surface token `--plan-surface-3` plus `--on-plan-*`, which is **a name for the six white-alpha literals `.prep` has always carried** rather than a new family (ADR-0158 §3's rule reaching the one surface that never got it) — **convert those six literals in the same change**, it is a no-op refactor and leaving them beside the tokens is how a seventh gets written. Also owed at build time: **the prep hero becomes a `<button>` and inherits ADR-0160 §4's no-interactive-descendant constraint permanently** (it has none today; a detached-tree guard is owed, since no snapshot can see that failure), and `BEAT.REBUFF` loses its `.prep` consumer — check `lib/one-shot.ts` for others before deleting the beat. **A shipped defect found on the way and fixed with it:** `.tsk-more` — Trip Home's `עוד N משימות` overflow row — has **no CSS rule in any stylesheet** and `App.css` has no global `button` reset, so it renders as a **bare 19px UA button** inside a `.checklist` card, under the 44px floor; §3's new `.chk-more` is the geometry it should point at. **What the running app corrected, and neither the drawing nor the unit suite could:** §5's gradient stops were PERCENTAGES, which makes legibility a function of list length — the mockup's 6-row card measured the top band label at 4.8:1, the real 828px card measured the same label at **4.28:1** and the overdue deadline at **2.93:1**. Now in px, and the worst case inverts to the **shortest** card. The overdue ink needed lightening again (`#f0a09b` is the board's and this ramp is brighter everywhere → `#ffc4be`). And a readiness check rendered a **clock** beside its meta, because the build mapped `meta` onto `HeroLiftTask.due` — a check has no `dueAt` and never can, so the glyph asserted what ADR-0190 §2 forbids. Verified in the real DOM: `.prep` is a `<button>` with **0** interactive descendants, the collapse row is **44px**, the card is **828px** capped by `--lift-max-h` with its body scrolling. **The e2e caught two more:** `hero-lift.spec.ts` asserted the retired behaviour verbatim and is replaced by a lift test that guards ADR-0160 §4 in a real parser (jsdom cannot reproduce the reparenting, and the pieces still EXIST after it — so the test asserts the hero still _owns_ them); and its locator had to become `.prep.is-tappable`, because a lifted card is also a `.prep` with `.prep-dates` in it and the design succeeding is what broke the selector. **Plus a latent flake it surfaced and fixed:** `hero-in-transit.spec.ts` waited for `.wp-board` and read `.wp-board.transit`, and `HomeSkeleton` renders its own placeholder `.wp-board` — the identical trap that file already documents for `.prep`, never applied to the board. Intermittent under 12 workers, invisible in isolation. **AMENDED THE SAME DAY on four owner reports against the built screen** ([ADR-0193](decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md) §3/§4 amended in place): the lift drops its five date-keyed bands for **one list in `orderTaskRows`' order, capped at 5 with `ועוד N`** (`planRunUp` DELETED, not left unused) — the bands put `ללא תאריך` last regardless of `important`, so a flagged undated task fell below every check, bending the one ladder ADR-0190 §2 set; and the far-group collapse moves from a full-width `.chk-more` row into the section head as a `.chk-toggle`, in `הכול מוכן`'s own slot (they can never collide). **The row was ugly for a reason that traps every caller of `CollapseToggle`, and the first diagnosis was wrong:** **`.wp-collapse-toggle` sets the `font` SHORTHAND to `inherit`**, and `collapsible.css` loads AFTER `screens.css`/`tasks.css` — so at equal specificity it wipes any `font-size`/`font-weight` a caller declares. `.chk-toggle` has declared `font-size: 11px; font-weight: 700` since it was written and **never once applied them**: it inherited its parent's font and merely looked right in `.sec-title` (12px/700), then rendered at **16px/400** the moment it moved to a section foot. The trap hid because the only other caller, `.index .past-toggle`, is scoped at (0,2,0) and out-specifies the primitive — one of two callers was broken, invisibly, from the start. Fixed at the primitive: `font-family: inherit` instead of the shorthand, which is all that rule ever wanted (stop a `<button>` wearing the UA's Arial). Size and weight belong to the caller. Hebrew reworked on both toggles (`הצג`/`הסתר` as a real pair, count inline rather than bracketed, `רחוקות` retired). **One more ink leak found by sweeping rather than reading:** `.hero-task-more` kept `--on-dark-faint` at **3.89:1** on the violet ramp, surfaced only when the amendment made that line load-bearing — the e2e now sweeps every text node in the lifted card for AA, verified to fail by putting the leak back. **THIRD ROUND, same day** ([ADR-0193](decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md) §2/§3 amended again): the hero's `משימות פתוחות` **counts the readiness checks**, via `taskPreview` reused rather than `+ liveChecks.length` written beside it — ADR-0190 §1 made a check an open task and the Index tile has counted them since, so the hero was answering one question with a second number; `overdue` stays manual **by construction**, a check having no `dueAt` to have passed. Both toggles move from the section head to a `.chk-foot` row under the card (the head had held a title, a status and two controls). And **`.chk-done-sum` is DELETED** rather than extended — its own comment said it existed to give the collapsed toggle "something legible to point at", which expired when the toggle became `הצג 3 שהושלמו`, and it listed automatic checks only so a completed manual task was invisible in it. The deletion cascaded through `CHECK_ICON`, `summaryLabels`, `completedSummary`, three CSS rules and two imports — none had another consumer. **FOURTH ROUND, same day** ([ADR-0193](decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md) §3 amended again + new §6, mockup [`what-is-missing-when-there-is-nothing-missing-v1`](../mockups/what-is-missing-when-there-is-nothing-missing-v1.html)). **A reported STRIPE, and it was a card with nothing in it** — `.checklist` is 1px-bordered and held only a collapsed drawer, measuring **2px** with **0 of 2** open tasks visible, because both were due in 16 days and the near/far split called both "far". Replaced by a **cap** (`PLAN_TASK_CAP`, 5, now shared with the lift): the first N of a list is never zero where a predicate can empty it at any length. A deletion — `soonIds`/`isNear`/`nearTasks`/`farTasks` and `tasksDueSoon` leave `PlanHome`. **A real empty state** via `EmptyState` (ADR-0078, which this section had never used), rendered INSIDE the card so the card can no longer be drawn with nothing in it, `--ok` mark because it is a status; copy `אפשר לנשום` / `אין מה להשלים לפני היציאה`. **And the two controls stop being peers:** `עוד N` is the card's last ROW (`.tsk-more`, 44px), `N שהושלמו` a quiet link below — the matched pills measured **22px painted and tappable** against ADR-0017's 44px floor and had since they were written. **And the assignee was half-migrated** ([ADR-0190](decisions/0190-a-readiness-check-is-a-task-row-and-the-checks-sit-inside-the-urgency-ladder.md) §6 completed): that amendment moved the assignee to the title row as the face alone and reached `IndexTasksView` only — `TaskBandRow`, the row BOTH Home surfaces draw, kept the avatar-plus-name meta line, which wrapped and put the assignee on a **third line**, so one task rendered taller on Home than on the screen it links to. No new CSS: `.tsk-who-row` is scoped to `.wp-listrow-title` rather than to that screen. Measured after: face on the title line, **0px** from the line end, row **+1px** instead of +1 line. **The shape of the mistake, twice in one session** (here and `.chk-toggle`'s font): an amendment applied to ONE call site of a shared component is not applied, and nothing fails. **FIFTH ROUND (2026-08-17).** (a) **The lifted card's ramp follows the THEME** ([ADR-0193](decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md) §5 amended): `.prep` already lightens downward in light and darkens in dark, and the lifted card was the one surface darkening in both, which is why it read as a dark-mode artefact in a light theme. `--plan-surface-3` is now `#5f4dbf` light / `#1a1442` dark. **The light value is a ceiling, not a taste** — white-alpha at 0.80 clears 4.5:1 only below L 0.112, and `--plan-surface` itself is L 0.153, so the card cannot ramp to the collapsed hero's own brightest violet. Measured worst ink 4.65 light / 9.07 dark. The middle stop and the px positions are DELETED: they existed for a ramp crossing bright→near-black, and both ends now sit inside the band the ink survives — **percentages are safe only while both ends are**. Overdue ink retuned `#ffc4be` → `#ffd2cd` (4.21 → 4.89). (b) **A task deadline can pin its zone** ([ADR-0194](decisions/0194-a-task-deadline-can-pin-its-zone.md)): `Task.displayTimezone` added, nullable, absent = derived, additive migration that cannot change how any stored task reads. The rejected cheap option — authoring-only, store nothing — is the exact bug `Event.displayTimezone` prevents. `dueZone` prefers the pin, and its **two** call sites were counted before the change, which made "store it" and "make every surface honour it" one job. Still owed: a real phone.

- ~~**Owner reports on a task's HOST surface**~~ **built 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0191](decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) §5 reversed and §6–§8 added · [session note](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-host-surface-corrections.md) · [mockup](../mockups/a-task-row-that-matches-its-neighbour-v1.html)). Seven reports from a device after phase 4 merged. **The one worth knowing: §5 was wrong on paper.** It wrote down that the two sections on a host surface would not share a row shape, called that "the decision rather than an accident", and shipped — and the number that would have settled it was available the whole time: the task's text started **40px** past the note's on the same 31→330 box. Now `0px`, with the task row falling 61px → 35px onto the note row's own height and the notes paying nothing. **Naming a cost is not measuring it.** **Two defects phase 4 had shipped:** the Map place card's sections **painted over each other**, because every rule on that card is keyed by `grid-row` and `> .note-sec` matched both — four unit specs caught the identical collision inside components and none could see this one, since a positional stylesheet is not a DOM query; and pinning tasks instead put the card at **411px against its own 420px cap** with one task and one note on it (ADR-0182 §9's cut-off, nine pixels of slack), so the flexible track is now one scroller holding both sections at the stated cost that the notes header stops pinning. **A latent defect the host chip triggered:** `.tsk-who-mini` overhung its line by 7px so an 18px circle would not stretch an 11.5px meta — sound only while that line is one line, and it painted through the deadline the moment it wrapped. 15px and fitting now. ~~**Still open:** `שיבוץ ליום` hidden on an already-linked place~~ **built 2026-08-16, narrowed to DAY SCOPE** on the owner's call ([ADR-0191](decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) §7a). Unscoped it failed ten specs encoding [ADR-0135](decisions/0135-a-place-becomes-an-event-or-a-booking.md) §1, because the Map's list is **built from** places events and bookings already use — day-scoped it says something narrow and true instead. **And narrowing it exposed a coverage hole:** `Map.test.tsx`'s `allDaysOn()` is a READ, and three call sites used it where the action was meant, so blocks documented as covering "both day scopes" were running one scope twice. Same round: the **assignee moved to the title row as a face** (ADR-0190 §6 amended, on the owner's comparison with Microsoft To Do — with the name in a visually-hidden span, since `Avatar`'s non-interactive form is `aria-hidden`), tasks became addable on **create** forms via staging, and the Map card's sections got the `flex-basis: 100%` the wrapper had been missing.

- ~~**Two more owner reports on the tasks screen**~~ **built 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0189](decisions/0189-the-editor-uses-the-idiom-the-app-already-had-and-a-task-is-read-where-it-sits.md) §4 amended and one Consequence added · [session note](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-host-surface-corrections.md), third round). **The open row's foot stops naming the assignee** — the tail of the previous round, since a face on the title row is on screen the whole time the row is open, so the foot said the same fact twice three lines apart; `RowOpenFoot.lead` is optional now and a note keeps its own. **And an edit that emptied the description saved nothing at all**, along with a cleared deadline and a cleared assignee. Worth knowing beyond the fix: `updateTaskSchema`'s sparse patch (absent = untouched, `null` = cleared) was **built, documented and tested on the reader's side only** — schema, service, `taskPatch`, `coerceClearedFields` and a backend spec all handle the `null`, and the editor, the one caller that submits the whole payload and therefore the only one that can mean "cleared", sent `undefined`. `TaskDraft` now types the three clearable fields as `string | null` so a call site cannot quietly go back, and `createTaskInput` drops them for a create. **A third defect fell out of the same type change:** `HostTasks`' staged-draft row never carried `body`, so a task typed on a **create** form lost its words the moment it was re-opened.

- **`createTaskSchema` rejects the seeded dev user as an assignee** (found 2026-08-16 while measuring the hero slot in the running app — [phase-5 note](planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-5-built.md)). `assigneeUserId` is `idSchema`'s strict create-side form, `^[a-z0-9-]{8,64}$`, and `prisma/seed.mjs`'s ME user is **`u-assaf`** — seven characters. So `POST /trips/:id/tasks` with an assignee returns a 400 under `DEV_AUTH=1` that a real cuid-keyed user never sees, which makes every headless session's assignee path untestable through the API. **Not a product defect and not fixed where it was found**, because the repair is a decision about which side is authoritative — widen the regex (and lose whatever it was guarding) or lengthen the seed's ids (and touch every fixture that spells `u-assaf`). The second is probably right and is the cheaper one to verify; either way it wants one pass over the seed rather than a patch at the schema. Worked around for the measurement by writing the column directly.

- **The Index tasks tile can read a count beside "no open tasks"** (noticed 2026-08-16 while verifying phase 2 in the running app; pre-existing since phase 1, unrelated to that change). `taskPreview.next` only considers **dated** tasks, so a trip whose only open task is **undated** gets `tile.empty` ("אין משימות פתוחות") as its subtitle beside a count of 1 — the two halves of one tile contradicting each other. Not fixed where it was found, because the fix is a **new string** for "open, none dated" and that is a copy decision rather than a defect repair. Cheap once the word exists; the alternative (counting only dated tasks) is worse, since it would hide undated work from the landing entirely.

- **Every `Sheet` but two is unbounded, and a short viewport clips its top** (found 2026-08-16 while fixing the task editor, which was the reported one). `Modal`'s `sheet` variant bottom-anchors its card and sets **no `max-height` and no `overflow`**, so a body taller than the viewport grows past the top edge with nothing to scroll back with — and a phone keyboard is exactly what makes the viewport short. Measured on the task editor at 401px of viewport before the fix: card **545px tall starting at −144**, `max-height: none`, nothing scrollable, first input at **−62**. **`EventForm` was the only consumer that had a scroll container** (`.modal-form`, `max-height: 75dvh; overflow-y: auto`, shipped for the sticky action bar since U-01); `TaskSheet` is the second now. `NoteSheet`, `DocumentUploadSheet`, `MemberSheet`, `DocumentManageSheet` and the rest still have none — they are short enough today, which is why nobody has seen it. **The real fix is probably in the primitive** so a consumer cannot forget, and the reason it was not taken here is `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s own rule that _a bounded card that clips cannot host an anchored panel_: the task editor's `TimePicker` is `position: fixed` and verified unclipped, but capping every sheet's CARD needs each anchored panel re-checked. Wants one pass over the sheet family, not a blanket `overflow` on `.modal-card`.

- ~~**The lifted hero's task mark read as "done"**~~ **fixed 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §U3 amended in place). Owner: _"the lifted hero shows a tick icon for tasks, this reads as 'task complete' and is misleading"_. §U3's argument was that the `checkbox` is _the same role_ the section header and the host mark already carry non-interactively — and it is not: those name a **noun**, this sits beside one task's **title**, where the ✓ is read as that task's state, on a block that only ever shows an OPEN task. `checkbox-empty` is `checkbox`'s path minus its ✓ segment. **What this makes worse, and is on the device pass:** §U3's open question is whether a checkbox reads as pressable on a read-only surface, and an EMPTY box reads _more_ pressable, not less — and the bare `✓` fallback §U3 drew is no longer available, since it is the mark that caused this. If the device pass calls it, the answer is a glyph that is neither, or the label alone.

- ~~**A host section's tick rendered as a white square**~~ **fixed 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0191](decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) §4 amended in place). Owner, from a device: _"what's this tick?? Why is it white? Why not like in the mockups?"_ — and the answer is that **`.tsk-tick-sec` had no CSS rule anywhere**. ADR-0191 §5's row reversal renamed the section's tick off `.tsk-tick` and left the paint behind, so a `<button>` with no rule drew platform chrome on all five host surfaces in both themes. The two densities share every declaration now and differ in `--tick-ink` / `--tick-inset`. **What stays open is the class of bug, not this instance:** a `className` is a claim that a rule exists and the unit suite cannot check it, because jsdom loads no stylesheet — `tasks-section-paint.contract.test.ts` parses that claim for `TaskSection` only, and the same sweep would be worth having for `NoteSection`, `DocumentAttachField` and anything else whose paint lives in a sheet its own tests never read.

- ~~**Five more owner reports on the tasks surfaces, from a device**~~ **fixed 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0191](decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) §4 and [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §U3/§U5 amended in place). Reproduced in the **running app** (`DEV_AUTH=1`, system Postgres, clock pinned) rather than reasoned about, which is what produced every number here. **(1) The section tick sat 3px from its title** — a 20px circle centred in `--sec-lead`'s 26px column. Fixed by moving the tick to the cell's start rather than widening the column, because a note's rule is a quote bar that belongs against its text and a tick is a control that must not; 6px now, and §5's both-texts-start-at-the-same-x invariant is untouched. **(2) The lifted hero showed ONE task and said `משימה`** — now `HERO_TASK_CAP` = 3 and the label is the plural `משימות`, since it names a list. §U5 had borrowed `פתק`'s rule and the amendment says why that was the wrong neighbour: a note is prose you read, a task is an obligation you owe. Measured live: the block is **127.5px** for three rows plus the overflow line, the card **622px at 360×640** with 66px inside the scroller and **688.5px at 390×844** with none. **(3) `ועוד 1 משימות`** — Hebrew inflects at one. Both this and `moreNotes` (whose remainder-of-one was the _common_ case and had been wrong the whole time) now read `ועוד … אחד/אחת`. **(4) A focused field's border was clipped on its vertical edges** in every sheet-form, not just the task editor: `.modal-form`'s `overflow-y: auto` makes `overflow-x` compute to `auto` too, and it clips at the padding box where a full-width field sits flush. Inline padding + a matching negative margin, so nothing moves. **Worth knowing before reaching for the obvious alternative:** `overflow-clip-margin` cannot help here — CSS Overflow 3 computes `overflow-x: clip` to `hidden` beside a scrolling axis, measured and confirmed inert. **Still possibly open:** the owner's last reading is that the vertical strokes stay lighter than the horizontal ones, which past the clip is sub-pixel antialiasing of a 1px border on fractional device pixels — wants a device screenshot taken after this change before anything else is done.

- **Two tick reports that did NOT reproduce** (owner, 2026-08-16): _"when you click on the circle the first time it does nothing, second time it ticks"_ and _"when clicking again it still stays marked (not ticked, just an outline)"_. Driven in the running app at 390×844 with touch emulation: **every tap toggled**, `aria-pressed` and the `--ok` fill flipped on each one, and the PATCHes went out `done → open → done`. The hit box was probed too — the 44px target answers everywhere except ~16px on the title side, where `.note-item-main` legitimately paints over it, so the effective target is ~40×44 and above ADR-0017's floor. Three candidates ruled out: it is not the click-swallow guard (`armClickSwallow` has exactly two call sites, the media viewer and the map canvas, neither on a day surface), not a stale optimistic update, and not `tickedStatus`. **What was fixed the same round and plausibly explains it:** the tick was 3px from the title, so a thumb aiming at a 20px circle very easily landed on the title button instead — which opens the editor rather than doing nothing, unless it was dismissed before it registered. Re-check on a device after the 6px gap ships; if it survives, the next step is a real-device trace, not more reading. **SOLVED 2026-08-17 while designing the tick's beat** ([mockup](../mockups/a-tick-that-is-seen-v1.html) §6, [session note](planning/2026-08-17-task-tick-beat-design-session.md)) — the second report is a **stuck `:hover`**, and the reason this session could not see it is that it measured the two things that were never wrong. `:hover` survives a tap until you tap something else (probed: true after the first tap, still true after the second, cleared by tapping elsewhere), and `.tsk-tick:hover .icon { opacity: 0.4 }` + `:hover::before { border-color: var(--ok) }` then paint **a ghost ✓ inside a green ring on an OPEN control** — "still marked, just an outline", verbatim. Every tap really did toggle; `aria-pressed` and the fill really were correct. **`:focus-visible` is innocent** and was the other candidate: the same probe shows it matched by neither a mouse click nor a tap, so the focus ring stays untouched. Repair is in the mockup and unbuilt: the hover stops borrowing the status colour and the state's glyph, gated on `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`. **The general lesson, since it will recur:** a report about a control's _appearance_ after an interaction is not answered by asserting its _state_ — the state was right and the paint was lying.

- ~~**The form's notes box is revealed by `＋ פתק`, and its shell moved inside the component**~~ **fixed 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0192](decisions/0192-a-form-has-bands-and-its-content-sections-have-one-look.md) §2 and [ADR-0152](decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) §6b, both amended in place). Owner, on the form as built: _"notes looks really bad, they should look and behave the same as tasks (+ פתק)"_ and _"clicking the + פתק should open a new inline task, not the entire form"_. **Two defects in one report.** The visible one was mine: the build dropped the composer's `Field` **shell** at the call site — the exact mistake the mockup had already caught and the ADR had already written down — so the textarea rendered with no border, no padding and no `min-height`. The shell lives inside `NoteComposer` now, because a shell a host must remember is a shell a host forgets. The design one was that the box was **permanently open** where the tasks section beside it is a header, a `＋ משימה` and an empty line; it is revealed by `＋ פתק` now, which opens the inline box and never `NoteSheet`, so ADR-0152 §6b survives whole. The box lost its own `＋` on a host form (one control, not two six pixels apart) and the section reads `אין פתקים על זה` again when closed. `useNoteComposer`'s **`standalone`** names the two surfaces with no section header — `DocumentUploadSheet` and `MapPlaceForm` — which keep the always-open box and its button. **One trap worth knowing:** the first pass used `autoFocus`, which cannot tell "revealed" from "present" and made the Map place card raise the keyboard on arrival — a shipped spec caught it; the focus is bound to the false→true transition instead.

- ~~**The event/booking form has bands, and its content sections have one look**~~ **BUILT 2026-08-16** ([ADR-0192](decisions/0192-a-form-has-bands-and-its-content-sections-have-one-look.md) · [mockup](../mockups/a-form-has-bands-and-one-content-section-v1.html) · [session note](planning/2026-08-16-form-bands-and-one-content-section.md)). Owner's two reports on the event and booking forms. One `.sec-h` in a new `ui/section-head.css` that all three content sections import — and the two copies it replaced had already drifted, sizing the header glyph differently on the same form. The notes are ONE section whose last row is the composer, on create and edit alike, which retires `t.notes.composer.labelMore` and the second `פתקים` heading. Five bands (`מה · איפה · מתי · הזמנה · מצורף`) in `EventForm` and matching `BookingSheet`'s existing step order, with **where before when** because the place derives the zone the times are read in; `סוג` joins the time band. The rule is in `frontend/CLAUDE.md` and **asserted by a spec**, which is the part that stops the drift recurring. Measured **−0.7px on a create**, **−48.7px on an edit**, reorder itself **0**. **Two follow-ups it deliberately did not take:** `DocumentUploadSheet` and `MapPlaceForm` still wrap their composer in a captioned `Field` and are now the odd ones out — `MapPlaceForm` sits on the Map place card whose height is arithmetic (ADR-0148 §1), so it wants its own pass; and `tsk-sec-quiet` stays unbuilt for the reason ADR-0192's alternatives list gives.

- **`tsk-sec-quiet` is a specified variant that was never written** (found 2026-08-16 by the sweep above; pre-existing since phase 4). [ADR-0191](decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) §7 says a host FORM's task section reads quietly — the header at the form's own field-label weight, the `＋` without its `--cta` ink — and `TaskSection` emits the class for it, but **no rule exists**, so the `quiet` prop does nothing at either call site (`EventForm`, `BookingSheet`). **Deliberately left open rather than built**, because the owner's same-day report asks for the notes section to match the tasks section's _full-strength_ look, which makes "does a form want a quieter density at all" a live design question — answer it with the form-ordering work rather than by writing the CSS the ADR describes. Listed in the contract test's `KNOWN_UNPAINTED`; delete that entry either way.

- ~~**Sub-tasks — a task holding a checklist of its own**~~ **BUILT 2026-08-18** ([ADR-0196](decisions/0196-a-task-can-hold-a-checklist-and-the-exclusion-is-paid-once.md) · [mockup](../mockups/a-task-that-holds-a-checklist-v1.html) · [design session](planning/2026-08-18-sub-tasks-design-session.md) · [build note](planning/2026-08-18-sub-tasks-built.md)). One table, a derived parent, a per-step assignee, and the exclusion paid **once** at the state boundary — nineteen of twenty-three derivations untouched. The ADR's **build log** carries what building it changed, and **two owner reports against the built screen are amended in place** (2026-08-19): **§3 reversed** — a parent's lead is a control again and its press settles every open step (or reopens them once all are settled), behind one toast and one undo, in the single verb `taskVerbs.tickTask`; and **§5 corrected** — the checklist's indent is derived from where the parent's title starts (`calc(var(--kid-title-x) - var(--sec-lead))`) rather than the 58px that landed 7px short of anything. A third report the same day (_"task editing doesn't have the option to add or remove sub tasks"_) is a second addendum: **§12's field was documented to hold the composer open once a task has steps and passed only the reveal flag**, whose control lives in the empty branch, so a task with a checklist got a read-only list; the fix also splits what `open` means at mount, since a composer that is merely present must not take the caret. **What is left, and it is deliberate rather than forgotten:** **(a) `assignedToAll`, and with it the brief's §C** — the owner's call to ship it after (_"I think that assign to all could be shipped after"_); the arc and the count are built and carry §C unchanged when it arrives, so nothing there is re-decided, but `assignedToAll` is not in the model yet and phase 6 of the tasks build plan still owns it. **(b) No reordering of steps** — creation order is the order; a drag handle on a 26.8px row is a target problem. **(c) The cap of 20** is a constant from 26.8px per row against a 640px viewport, and a real phone can move it. **(d) Two feel questions for a device pass:** whether a 2.5px arc at 26px reads as a fraction under a thumb, and whether the count alone carries a parent at `0/n`, where the ring is the same 1.21:1 / 1.33:1 hairline the line below already records. A **fourth** addendum (2026-08-19) fixes the composer committing into its own controls: a tap on `✕` or on the assignee chip blurred the box first, so the pending words were written before the press landed — a new step from the chip, and on `✕` a `reset()` that unmounted the control mid-gesture. Three guards, one per way focus leaves the box, and only the `pointerdown` one works on iOS. A **fifth** addendum fixes two more from the same surface: the assignee chip's 44px target was **displaced onto the ✕** (measured: `elementFromPoint` at the chip's own centre returned `.tsk-kid-x`), because the overlay was pushed 22px sideways rather than centred and was given a width the recipe it copied never had; and the editor's checklist wrote through immediately, so `ביטול` did not undo it — both modes now stage, and the save is a diff. A **sixth** adds the discard guard to `TaskSheet` — the shipped `useUnsavedGuard` on both close paths — with the false-positive rule ADR-0136's session-188 follow-up paid for: values diffed against a baseline captured once, never a `touched` flag, and `composing` deliberately excluded. Capturing that baseline also fixed a real bug in the staging: the save was diffing against LIVE trip state, so a step a peer added mid-edit would have been deleted by someone else's save.

- ~~**Neither CI job had a `timeout-minutes`, and the browser install could stall forever**~~ **fixed 2026-08-19, in two passes.** `e2e (dev)` hung inside `playwright install --with-deps chromium` — before a single test ran — and with no timeout the PR's check sat `in_progress` for 90 minutes on its way to Actions' 360-minute default. A stuck leg is indistinguishable from a slow one while it is running, so nothing tells a reader to re-run it. Both jobs are now capped at **20 minutes**, roughly triple their real ceiling (ci ~3.5, e2e preview ~5.5). **It then hung a second time the same day**, in the same step and on both e2e legs at once, which made the cap the thing reporting the failure rather than a backstop nobody reaches. **And the naive retry made it worse, which is the part worth keeping:** `timeout` signals only its DIRECT child, so the orphaned `apt-get` kept the lock and attempt two died instantly on `Could not get lock /var/lib/apt/lists/lock` — a step that used to hang now failed in five seconds. The log named the real culprit: `apt-get update` stalls on the runner's `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` mirror, minutes of `Ign:` retries before falling back to `archive.ubuntu.com`. So the retry **drops `--with-deps`** rather than repeating it (the libraries either landed in the first attempt or ship with the image; fetching the browser itself has never failed), kills the leftover `apt-get` first, and the e2e job's cap moved to 30 minutes to leave room for both attempts.

- **Six services still broadcast a create's `Change.after` as the caller's INPUT rather than the created row** (found 2026-08-19 by the one that bit: [ADR-0196](decisions/0196-a-task-can-hold-a-checklist-and-the-exclusion-is-paid-once.md)'s third addendum). A client merges `after` over what it already holds and on a create it holds nothing, so every field the server defaults is missing until the next resync. `applyControlChangeToList`'s comment blesses this — _"the Index reads only type/title/code/place, so it renders fine"_ — which was true of the surfaces it was written for and false the moment a defaulted field carried meaning: a task's `status` did, and a peer's fresh sub-task rendered ticked. `tasks.service` now sends `toTaskDto(entity)`; `events`, `bookings`, `places`, `notes`, `maybe-items` and `document-attachments` do not. `ChangeService.mutate` already accepts a function of the applied entity, so each is one line — the work is checking what each client's applier and cache do with a suddenly-complete payload, which is why it is a sweep and not a patch.

- **Twenty-five test files hand-roll the `taskVerbs` mock** (noticed 2026-08-19 while adding a fourth verb, [ADR-0196](decisions/0196-a-task-can-hold-a-checklist-and-the-exclusion-is-paid-once.md)'s build-log addendum). Every spec that renders anything inside `useTrip()` writes its own `{ createTask, updateTask, deleteTask }` literal, so adding `tickTask` meant touching all of them — and a spec that forgets one gets a `not a function` at press time rather than a compile error, which is how this surfaced. The fix is one exported test factory the specs spread and override; it is a mechanical sweep across 25 files and did not belong inside the change that found it.

- **Three of six `scrollIntoView` call sites skip the reduced-motion guard** (noticed 2026-08-18 while adding the composer's scroll, [ADR-0196](decisions/0196-a-task-can-hold-a-checklist-and-the-exclusion-is-paid-once.md) §13). `Map.tsx`, `DayView.tsx` and `useFormErrors` pass `behavior: prefersReducedMotion() ? 'auto' : 'smooth'`; `EventForm`, `BookingSheet` and `DocumentUploadSheet` pass a bare `'smooth'`. One sweep, three lines, and `lib/motion.ts` already holds the helper. Not fixed where it was found because it is three files this change does not otherwise touch.
  - **And all six share a second hazard, found 2026-08-20** ([ADR-0168](decisions/0168-the-search-answers-on-the-canvas.md) §3's amendment): `scrollIntoView` clamps its destination to the scroll extent that exists **at the call**, so any of them aiming into a surface that is still arriving lands short and never re-aims. The three landings that matter now go through `lib/land-at-top.ts`, which watches instead (the Map's row, and an event's card on both day surfaces); the other call sites are **centring** scrolls inside forms, where the same trap is likelier to read as "the field is half under the keyboard" than as a wrong list. Unaudited, and the sweep above is where to answer it, since it is the same six lines.

- **`App.css` and `field.css` both define `.field`, and only one of them is reset** (noticed 2026-08-18, same session). `App.css:1037` carries `margin-top: 18px` — a form's gap from the birth form — and `field.css` sets `display`/`gap` and never touches it, so every `.field` in the app inherits a margin from a sheet that is not the primitive. Inside a grid row that is a shove rather than a gap; it cost 9px of misalignment on the sub-task composer, fixed there by scoping. Harmless where a `.field` really is a form row, which is why it has survived — but the next new host will hit it too. The fix is to decide which sheet owns `.field` and delete the other's copy.

- **`ListRow`'s title span carries no `dir`, and it holds stored content at every consumer** (noticed 2026-08-15 while fixing the same class of bug one element over — [ADR-0189](decisions/0189-the-editor-uses-the-idiom-the-app-already-had-and-a-task-is-read-where-it-sits.md)'s Consequences). `.wp-listrow-title` renders a booking's name, a document's name, a note's words and a task's title — none of them written by the app — and inherits the page's RTL, which is the failure `frontend/CLAUDE.md` names as _"the absence is the bug"_: a value opening with a Latin or numeric run comes apart. **Not fixed there**, and deliberately: the span holds **mixed** content at several hosts (a task's title sits beside a star, a note's beside a link mark), and `dir="auto"` on a box that also holds Hebrew chrome lays that chrome out LTR too — which is the wrong repair the same rule warns about. So this is per-consumer, at the element that holds the value and nothing else, and it wants one pass over all four hosts rather than a fix at whichever one is being touched. Cheap per host; the work is the audit, not the attribute. Unmeasured so far — the probe that found it is in the ADR and is two lines.

- ~~**A task's tick gets a beat, and the row waits for it**~~ — **BUILT (2026-08-17; [ADR-0195](decisions/0195-a-tick-is-answered-once-and-the-row-waits-for-it.md), mockup [`a-tick-that-is-seen-v1`](../mockups/a-tick-that-is-seen-v1.html), [session note](planning/2026-08-17-task-tick-beat-design-session.md)).** `BEAT.TICK` as the family's fourth member, `TaskTick` replacing four hand-copied buttons, the row's departure sequenced on `playBeat`'s return value with a flush-on-unmount so a held tick can never be lost, un-ticking on a `transition` the OPEN state declares for itself, and the hover repaired. What is left of it is the two lines below — one narrowed by this, one untouched by it.

- **~40 `:hover` rules and not one `@media (hover: hover)`** (found 2026-08-17 by probing the tick's stuck hover, above). On a touch device `:hover` latches after a tap until you tap elsewhere, so every hover rule in the app is also a **stuck state** — and the app is phone-primary (ADR-0017, "no hover-only affordances"), which is the rule this quietly breaks 40 times. Most are harmless by luck: they lift a background a few percent, so a latched one reads as a highlight. The tick's was not, because it borrowed `--ok` and the state's own glyph, and it is **repaired at the tick and shipped** (ADR-0195 §4) — by **deletion**: that control now has no hover state at all, since a hint on a stateful control could not be measured as cheaply as it could be dropped and was mouse-only on a phone-primary app either way. **So the guard is still unwritten anywhere in the app** — the first rule that genuinely needs a hint is also the one that has to introduce `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`, and for a control that carries a STATE the cheaper answer may again be to delete rather than to gate. The sweep is one mechanical pass (wrap each hover block; nothing else changes) and it wants doing at the layer, not per-report: the same latch is waiting on `.wp-listrow`, `ListRow`'s kebab, the chips and every `.li` row. Check each one for whether it borrows a **status** colour or a state's mark before assuming it is cosmetic — that is the whole difference between "a highlight that lingers" and "a control asserting the wrong state".

- **The OPEN tick's ring measures 1.21:1 light / 1.33:1 dark** (found 2026-08-17 by rendering [`a-tick-that-is-seen-v1`](../mockups/a-tick-that-is-seen-v1.html); pre-existing since ADR-0188 shipped the control). `1.5px solid var(--line)` on `var(--card)` is a 10%-alpha hairline on the surface it sits on, against the **3:1** floor a graphic control owes — and it is the **measured answer to ADR-0188's own open device-pass question** _"whether a 26px ring reads as pressable under a thumb"_: it barely reads as anything. Also the standing candidate cause of the two tick reports above that did not reproduce — a control you cannot see is one you aim at badly, and that session fixed a 3px gap without ever measuring the ring. **Not fixed where it was found, deliberately:** the repair is a token decision (the open control wants a stronger ink than the app's hairline), `--line` has ~200 consumers that are genuinely hairlines, and the same question applies to `SettleControl` and every other outlined control before a one-off is added here. A beat cannot rescue it — motion is seen once, the resting state is seen on every row of every list.

- **`.checklist` and `.index .listcard` are the same card under two names** (noticed 2026-08-15, session 271, while drawing [ADR-0188](decisions/0188-a-tasks-tick-is-a-sibling-and-the-leading-element-says-who-owns-the-outcome.md) §6). Both are `background: var(--card)` + `1px var(--line)` + a radius + `overflow: hidden`, and the only real difference is that one is scoped to `.index` and the other is not — which is why the Home bands could take `.checklist` and the tasks screen has to take the scoped twin for exactly the same drawing. Folding them is a rename across four screens and a scoping change, so it belongs to whoever next touches the Index rather than to a feature passing through; ADR-0188 deliberately used both rather than silently taking on the refactor (rule 8's "ask before the larger change"). Cheap and low-risk when taken, and it removes one of the two names a future reader has to know are synonyms.

## Screens not built

- **Archive presentation** — ADR-0044 settled the behavior of a finished trip and explicitly left how the archive _looks_ as a follow-up (the Index's read-only archive state is designed in ADR-0049; other tabs' archive presentation is still open).

## Documents: performance & caching

**ADR-0055 (blob read caching) and ADR-0056 (outbox uploads) both shipped** — this section had carried them as "Both **Proposed**" long after the fact, which also made the CLAUDE.md rule-5 offline-documents gap look open when ADR-0055 closed it (`backend/src/common/blob-cache.ts` with its spec, `fetchDocumentContent`'s version-keyed read-through plus `evictDocumentBlob`, and `OUTBOX_VERB.UPLOAD_DOCUMENT` are all in the tree). The decomposition is in `planning/2026-07-17-session-29-document-caching-and-fast-uploads.md`; the why is in the two ADRs. What remains are the two follow-ups each deferred at the time.

- **Streaming server ingest** (deferred follow-up) — stream multipart → encrypt → S3 multipart upload, dropping the full in-memory buffer + base64 inflation. The "sequence it after the read-cache task to avoid a merge fight" note is discharged: that task has shipped, so this is free to take whenever the buffer actually hurts.
- **Redis for documents** (deferred) — a shared cross-instance read cache and/or upload write-buffer only matter past one backend instance (ADR-0031); Redis stays reserved for its earmarked BullMQ role until then. **Still reserved, and the notifications epic declined it** (2026-08-20, [ADR-0197](decisions/0197-a-notification-is-a-derived-obligation-and-the-sweep-is-its-clock.md) §3 — a delayed-job queue is a second copy of a schedule Postgres already holds), so the role's remaining candidate is Gmail import, which is what `docker-compose.yml`'s comment has always said and is the workload a queue is actually for: fan-out with retries.

## Integrations

- **Enrichment SOURCE coverage — Wikidata does not have the small places** (owner report, 2026-08-12; measured in [session 255](planning/2026-08-12-session-255-a-backfill-that-finishes.md) §4). Three places on one Iceland day render empty and **no matcher change reaches any of them**: `Sigöldugljúfur` and `Stútur crater` have **no Wikidata item at all**, and `Bláhylur` has none under that name (the lake is `Ljótipollur`, `Q653324`). The two that did enrich are exactly the two Wikidata holds — so this is coverage, not recall, and it is the hole [ADR-0166 §Context 2](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) always named. **Measured: OSM has two of the three** (waterfall @ 64.1601,-19.1166; volcano @ 64.0131,-19.0407), which turns Phase 2 from a plan into a costed one — and the stronger argument is not coverage but the **`wikidata=Q…` tag** (Ljótipollur's OSM node carries `Q653324`), since `MATCH_METHOD.WIKIDATA_TAG` is already reserved and unused and is an EXACT join, so it would reach places Wikidata has but no name of ours can find. **Blocked on the same thing §5.4 always said**: public Overpass is not for production volume, and the hosting call is unmade. **Rejected with a measured counter-example: Commons-by-name** — already allowlisted and it has categories for two of the three, but `Category:Stútur`'s files are photographs of **Frostastaðavatn, a different lake**, with no QID and no coordinate to check against; Commons stays a sink reached through a QID, never a source of identity. **`Bláhylur` is unreachable by every free source** and must not be answered with a one-off alias.
- **Place enrichment — the multi-source pipe** ([ADR-0166](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md), designed 2026-08-04, session 212; **Phase 1 built 2026-08-05**). The frame: **Google Places content may not be cached** (`place_id` exempt/indefinite, coordinates ~30 days, everything else request-live-and-attribute, a photo name not at all), so the storable fields come from **open-licensed** sources into a **global** `PlaceEnrichment` row (no `tripId`) and **no Google-sourced value is ever written there** — `Place` stays trip-scoped and unmigrated, because `icon`/`category` are the trip's opinion. Three phases: **(1) summary + image/thumbnail** — Wikidata (CC0) + Wikipedia (CC BY-SA) + Commons (per-file license), the provider registry with **per-field** precedence, the store, negative caching, and the avatar pipeline as a second consumer (`storage.ts` + `image-sniff.ts` + a `@Public` immutable route + `blob-cache.ts`); **(2) opening hours** — OSM/Overpass, short TTL + a visible "as of", and only then the arrive-after-closing warning; **(3) ETA** — Routes API per ADR-0108 §4, a short-TTL keyed derivation, **not** this store, and the only phase that spends.
  - ~~**Phase 1 is gated on a design session with a mockup**~~ — **done (2026-08-05, session 214): [ADR-0167](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md) + [`mockups/place-enrichment-v1.html`](../mockups/place-enrichment-v1.html)**, rendered and measured in both themes. All four accumulated questions answered: **the badge is the thumbnail's frame** (no new slot — a photo fills the existing 40×40 `r12` square and the category hue moves from fill to ring, so the majority of rows with no image look exactly as today), **a picked icon beats a fetched photo** (ADR-0166 §1's boundary on a 40px square), **attribution renders on the card under the image** (never overlaid, never on the badge — and at 27 of 32 files it is the default line), the language marker is `באנגלית` in the existing tag grammar, and `עוד בגוגל` sits in the selection reveal — becoming its entire content when we know nothing, which is the common case. Numbers the build inherits: rows stay **69–71px**, an enriched card is **392px** (46% of 390×844), the empty one **134px**, hero **132px**, summary clamped to three lines.
    - **Two defects the mockup found by rendering, to pre-empt in the build:** an `inset` box-shadow paints **below** the element's children, so a photo covers the hue ring entirely and the badge silently loses its category (needs an overlay `::after` plus a hairline for bright skies); and a Latin credit line under `dir="auto"` makes the **whole element** LTR and aligns it to the opposite edge from every other line in the RTL card — the **mirror** of ADR-0118's bug, invisible to its lint guard, and it will recur on every enriched Latin string (credit, English summary, place name).
    - **Phased for build: [`2026-08-05-place-enrichment-build-plan.md`](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-build-plan.md)** — six phases, each reviewable alone and none depending on a later one. ~~**1** store + registry + Wikidata/Wikipedia providers~~ **done (2026-08-05, [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-phase-1-built.md)):** `packages/shared/enrichment.ts` (sources + per-source `SourcePolicy`, `ENRICHMENT_FIELD`, `FIELD_SOURCE_PRECEDENCE`, TTLs, match methods/refusals, the payload schema with `lang`-required localized variants), the global `PlaceEnrichment` table (`Place` unmigrated), and `backend/src/enrichment/` — the allowlisted/timeboxed/size-capped fetcher (host re-checked on **every redirect hop**), the refusable match with both refusal scopes, per-field negative caching, the `storable` guard that makes §2's invariant testable, the registry, the orchestrator, and the Wikidata + Wikipedia providers against recorded spike fixtures. **Invisible and uncalled:** `resolvePlace` is untouched and the trigger arrives with Phase 3. Three build decisions worth knowing before Phase 2, all in the note: the granularity check **cannot key on the name** (Meguro River's saved name is the river's label), a contradicting distance **vetoes** a perfect name match while a missing one only discounts it, and `hours` inverts §6.4's "shorter" miss TTL on purpose. **Wikidata provides no field value** — it is the identity spine, and `FIELD_SOURCE_PRECEDENCE.image` names Commons because §11.1 puts the per-file license there; ~~**2** the image pipeline~~ **done (2026-08-05, [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-phase-2-built.md)):** the Commons provider (`imageinfo` with `iiprop=url|size|mime|extmetadata` + `iiurlwidth`, per-file license string and author, HTML stripped out of `extmetadata`), a **subject-agnostic** fetch → sniff → store pipeline, and a `@Public` immutable `GET /enrichment/images/:key`. Avatar infra as second consumer throughout (`storage.ts`, `image-sniff.ts`, and `blob-cache.ts` for free via `getObject`) — **no resize dependency and no new infrastructure of any kind**. GFDL-only is refused in `enrichment.policy.ts` rather than in the provider, which is what makes it fall through to the next candidate. Four build calls in the note: a provider still never stores (Commons returns a pointer, the orchestrator materializes it, which is what keeps the pipeline reusable for link previews); **attribution is required per FILE not per source** (5 of 32 files owe no credit, and `extmetadata` says so); we store the **bucket's** dimensions rather than §11.4's "original" ones, since §12.1 removed the resize that phrasing assumed; and the `@Public` route's access check is the `enr_` key prefix, because `storage.ts` is one flat keyspace shared with document ciphertext. **It also closed a Phase 1 gap:** confidence was being laundered — a sitelink is an exact hop off an item that may itself have been matched fuzzily, so an identity provider now settles `identityConfidence` and downstream providers cap by it. `api-contract.md` updated. Left open: `ENRICHMENT_IMAGE_NOMINAL_WIDTH_PX` is **800 and unmeasured** (the spike only weighed the 500 bucket), and no real image byte has been fetched yet — egress to Wikimedia is blocked in the build environment; ~~**3** delivery~~ **done (2026-08-05, [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-phase-3-built.md)):** `TripSnapshot.enrichments` keyed by `placeId` (the join from a global store with no `tripId`), `WS_MESSAGE_TYPE.ENRICHMENT`, and the frontend mirrors — Dexie via `snapshotMeta.enrichments` + `cacheEnrichment`, memory via one reactive map on trip-state. Two shape decisions: the delivered read model **drops the `absent` state** (the negative cache is fetch-scheduling, and a surface renders "we know nothing" identically either way — ADR-0167 §6), and an image arrives as a **URL not a `blobKey`** (the `documentSummarySchema`/`uploadedAvatarUrl` precedent). **The plan's "one `CACHE_CHANNELS` entry" was not possible and was not forced:** both client registries are keyed by `EntityType` and driven by a `Change`, and enrichment is deliberately neither, so joining them would have meant inventing a fake `Change` — the exact fiction ADR-0166 §6 refuses. It gets one declared home per mirror instead, with no per-type branching, and the code says so. **`enrichment` is the first WS message with no `seq`**, so a client must neither advance its cursor nor gap-check on it (tested). `api-contract.md` and `sync-and-offline.md` updated. ~~**4** the badge becomes the frame~~ **done (2026-08-05, [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-phase-4-built.md)):** the first visible change — a fetched photo fills the Map row's 40×40 badge and the category hue moves from fill to a 2px ring, aligned to **`mockups/place-enrichment-v2.html`** (v1 is stale). `lib/place-photo.ts` owns §2's fill order as a pure function (a picked icon beats a photo; `undefined` covers all three "no photo" situations, which render identically); `PlaceBadge` gains the clip and the ring, and degrades a 404 to the glyph — a refreshed enrichment mints a new immutable URL, so a failure belongs to one URL only. Three things measuring found that reading would not ([ADR-0167 §12](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md)): the ADR's **69–71px is the mockup's box** (a real `.place` is 64px, pitch 73px — the number §Context already cited), the ring must be a **real element** because both of this badge's pseudo-elements are already the order counter and the hit-area expander, and clearing the category fill needs a rule in `map.css` too (it ties with `.map-badge.cat-food` on specificity and loses on import order). Geometry is a Playwright spec against real Chromium with **real PNG bytes** (`e2e/place-photo-frame.spec.ts`) — jsdom reports every rect as zero, so nothing here is assertable in jsdom. `apiAssetUrl` generalizes the avatar's one-off API-origin prefix (rule 8). **The device pass is still owed** and the note lists what it must answer, including the soft-line stacking, which I chose without being able to see it. ~~**5** the collapsed card~~ **done (2026-08-05, [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-phase-5-built.md)):** the pinned **two-line summary block** under the identity (its own grid row on the bounded card, `auto` so it collapses to 0 on the majority of places that know nothing), `באנגלית` in the row's existing tag grammar, and **`עוד בגוגל`** in the reveal's footer on `mapsSearchUrl`'s third wrapper (`mapsKnowledgeUrl`) — never the retired `mapsPlaceUrl`, which meant "view the location". `placeSummary` is thin because Phase 1 already shipped the resolution (`resolveTextVariant` + `SUMMARY_LANG_PREFERENCE`); the marker is a **lookup**, so a language with no Hebrew word for it goes unmarked rather than mislabelled. Two build calls: the marker is a **sibling** of the prose (`dir="auto"` would sniff the Hebrew chip and lay an English extract out RTL, and `-webkit-box` would give the chip one of the two lines), and the clamp lives on the prose element so §9's specificity trap cannot recur. **Measured, and the mockup was smaller than the app again ([ADR-0167 §13](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md)): the third footer control leaves the footer full** — 349px against 332px at 390px and 302px at 360px — because v2 drew a bare `🗑` where ADR-0157 §2 ships a labelled delete. It wraps at 360 and is marginal at 390 (**it wrapped locally and fit in CI**, which failed the first version of the e2e and was right to: a layout one label's width from wrapping cannot be asserted as wrapping when Hebrew metrics differ per machine). Shipped as-is, since the control that drops is the destructive one; **the owner picks** between accepting it, unlabelling the delete, or moving the way through out of the footer. Two bullets did NOT ship: **hours** (blocked upstream — no OSM provider, and the raw `opening_hours` expression is Phase 2's to render) and **`עוד ›` + the credit line** (§9.3's expansion is what §11.1 made a mode change, so it cannot ship before the mode). **That second one is a licensing gap and the reason Phase 6 is next:** §4 lets the 40px badge carry no credit because the card one tap away has it, and until Phase 6 that card does not exist — so the app renders CC BY-SA photographs and prose with no attribution anywhere, against 84% of measured files requiring visible credit. ~~**6** the expanded card~~ **done (2026-08-05, [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-phase-6-built.md)):** expanding is a **mode change** — the hero (130px), the credit and the whole summary come on while the notes, the references and the schedule footer come **off**, which is what dissolved §10.2's measured 31px notes scroller rather than working around it. The full picture is the app's own zoomable preview, so **`DocumentViewer` was generalized** (owner's call; four of its 381 lines were document-shaped) into `MediaViewer` with a discriminated source, `DocumentViewer` kept as a thin adapter so the document path is byte-identical. **The attribution gap is closed:** photographer `·` license under the picture, license string verbatim, absent attribution treated as normal, and the Latin run isolated inside an RTL element — §8.2's prescription, the half of ADR-0118 its lint guard cannot see. Rule 8 twice: `useFailableImage` generalizes `PlaceBadge`'s one-off so the hero shares it, and the viewer is extended rather than duplicated. Three things the phase found ([ADR-0167 §14](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md)): **§10.3's `⤢ תמונה מלאה` must not be built** (§11.1 retired it, and the mockup's `.creditrow`/`.full` are dead CSS proving it), the expanded card needs **its own grid** because `:has(.note-sec)` stops matching, and the expansion **owes the back stack a layer** that no section of the ADR mentions (it peels expansion → selection → tab). Also fixed from the deploy: **the order stamp needed `z-index`** — a pseudo-element paints before its originator's children, so the frame's ring was drawn over the disc and cut an arc through it; the mockup's `.ord` carries that line and it had not survived the port. **The build plan is now complete except hours**, which stay blocked on the OSM provider.
  - ~~**⚠ NOTHING TRIGGERS A PASS YET**~~ — **decided and built (2026-08-05, [ADR-0166 §14](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) + [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-trigger-built.md)):** **two triggers riding existing requests** — a pick (`resolvePlace`) for immediacy, and a **snapshot read** for backfill, TTL refresh and recovery-after-a-redeploy, which is free because Phase 3's join already reads the rows that say what is stale. **No scheduler** (ADR-0157 §6 refused one for the orphan sweep in this same service; this is the weaker case), surplus work **dropped rather than queued** (the read trigger is idempotent and `attemptedAt` is written only on completion), at most 3 passes in flight and 3 per read, one pass per real-world place across trips. Ships **enabled behind `ENRICHMENT_DISABLED`**. It narrowly revises §6's "`resolvePlace` is untouched" while keeping the guarantee that clause protected — and a test caught that the bare call left the pick failable, so it is wrapped at the call site like `sweepAfterMint`. **The next deploy is the first time any of this code can make a live Wikimedia request** — watch the `800 → 840` bucket assumption, `wbsearchentities` ranking on Hebrew names, and whether 3 concurrent passes is polite during a cold-start backfill. **That last one is now ANSWERED (2026-08-12, session 255; [ADR-0166 §14](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) amended in place; [session note](planning/2026-08-12-session-255-a-backfill-that-finishes.md)) — and the answer was the opposite of the question.** 3 concurrent is fine; **3 per read and drop the rest** was the defect. "The read trigger re-fires on the next snapshot" assumed a next snapshot, and a snapshot read happens when somebody OPENS THE TRIP — no clock, no polling — so a cleared cache advanced three places per app-open and a 40-place trip needed fourteen of them. The owner reported it as slowness and it was: `פסל החירות` resolves at 0.900 on the name route and had simply never been asked about. **A pass's completion now takes the next stale place** (`pump`, called on queue and from every pass's `finally`), so a backlog drains from one open in ~80s. **The concurrency cap deliberately did NOT move**, and that is the measured half: §22's probe is the first real Wikimedia traffic this repo has generated — ~2,000 requests at ~1.3/s from one client with a proper UA, drawing occasional 429s a single retry cleared — which does not support a higher ceiling. Still not a scheduler (ADR-0157 §6 stands): nothing ticks, nothing persists, a restart loses the backlog and the next read rebuilds it free. Backlog bounded by memory, and what it drops is **logged**. _(The original gap, kept for the lesson: the plan described scheduling in the present tense — "enrichment is scheduled after the fact" — so no phase owned it and three PRs shipped a pipe that never ran.)_
  - ~~**Enrichment RECALL**~~ — **fixed and re-routed (2026-08-05, [ADR-0166 §15](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) + [session note](planning/2026-08-05-enrichment-first-live-run-fix.md)).** The first live run filled the store with `not_found`: `uselang=en` made every search hit come back labelled in English, so a Hebrew saved name scored ~0 against `Eiffel Tower` and was refused **before the entity was read** — the search had found the right item and the scoring threw it away (`Stokksnes` matched the same day because its saved name is Latin). Fixed by scoring against every name a hit offers (`match.text`, label, aliases) and every label the entity returns. **The same run produced the mirror-image defect and it is fixed with it: `Piccadilly Circus` matched a SONG** — an item named after a place has an exact label and no `P625`, so it took §5.5's "no coordinates to corroborate" discount (1.0 × 0.8) and won. That discount is right for a coordless place of OURS and backwards when the absence is the candidate's: on Wikidata a real place carries `P625` as a matter of course, so an item without one is evidence about its **kind** (a song, an album, a film). Refused outright — structural rather than a curated "not a place" type list — with the pre-filter keeping its own veto-free question (`nameOnlyConfidence`), because a search hit's missing coordinate is an artefact of the endpoint rather than a fact about the item. **And the recall hole behind it is answered by a coordinate-first route, which the owner chose over buying an English name from Google:** `generator=geosearch` on `en` then `he.wikipedia.org` returns what is within 500m of the pin with no name matching at all, `pageprops.wikibase_item` gives the QID, and the name becomes the CHECK rather than the FINDER — two requests, and only for a place the name search already failed. Two rules make it safe: **a name comparison across disjoint scripts is uninformative, not negative** (the same insight as the bug, generalized — `namesComparable`), and **a broader entity found only by proximity is skipped rather than accepted with per-field refusals**, because §11.2's asymmetry assumes the name established the identity and a district's photograph on a museum is the confidently-wrong failure.
