New `MATCH_METHOD.GEOSEARCH` at 0.8 against the name route's 0.9, so a coordinate-only identity is always outranked. **Left open on purpose: an English `displayName` from Google** — reliable, and it would help Wikipedia's own search too, but `displayName` returns one language per request so it is a second billed Details call on every pick (ADR-0108 §3's envelope); recorded here, to be built if the coordinate route's coverage turns out not to be enough. **Round two the same evening ([ADR-0166 §16](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md)): the coordinate route matched the Underground station UNDER Piccadilly Circus.** Three flaws — **proximity was carrying matches it cannot arbitrate** (distance is 35% of the blend and free for a facility at the place, so `Piccadilly Circus tube station` scored 0.707 on the name and 0.810 blended; now `MATCH_MIN_NAME_SIMILARITY = 0.8`, calibrated so `Meiji Jingū / Meiji Shrine` → `Meiji Shrine` at 0.816 survives, and whose real value is letting the **square win** rather than refusing both), **`ggslimit=5` dropped the subject** in a city with dozens of articles inside 500m (now 20, still one `wbgetentities` call), and **ambiguity now refuses** — an uncorroborated winner with another scoreable candidate inside the trust radius is a coin toss, not a match. All four defects across the two evenings are the same mistake in different places: **treating an absence of discriminating evidence as evidence** (a missing coordinate, an unreadable script, a shared point, a truncated list). **Re-testing anything here means clearing the negative cache first** (30-day miss TTL): `delete from "PlaceEnrichment"` is safe, it holds no trip data.
  - ~~**Enrich a place BEFORE it is saved — the deciding surface**~~ — **done (2026-08-05, [ADR-0166 §17](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) + [ADR-0167 §15](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md) + [session note](planning/2026-08-05-place-enrichment-before-you-save.md)):** the pipe now reaches a place nobody has added, and **§9.1's deciding card is the last piece of that design to ship**. A **third trigger** (`enrichNow`), and the first with a person waiting: the other two deliver by push — the snapshot join and the WS nudge, both keyed by `placeId` — and a candidate has none, so the answer travels back down `POST /trips/:tripId/enrichment/lookup`, behind `MembershipGuard` + `PlacesThrottlerGuard` (the class imported, not re-minted — rule 8). **The trigger is on tap only** (owner's call from three): a query returns several candidates and most are never kept, so one fetch per place a person actually looked at, with answers kept for the session — **including empty ones**, which are the majority and the ones that must not be re-asked. **It waits, bounded at 5s**, which does not contradict §6: that guarantee is about requests existing for something else (a pick, a snapshot), and this one exists _for_ the enrichment — and nothing is lost when the wait lapses, since the pass finishes into the store. A waiter may also take a slot above the background cap (6 vs 3): a tap is somebody looking at a blank card, a backfill is nobody. **Rule 8 on the surface too:** `ResultRow` and `PlaceRow` are different components, so the hero/credit/summary became `ui/domain/PlaceKnowledge` at three densities rather than two copies with two clamps to keep in step — it emits a **fragment**, because each block is a child of the row's own layout, and the dividend was that the deciding card needed no CSS of its own beyond one clamp. The mockup's own three-line clamp (`.clamp2` is the _committed_ card's variant), and it stays clamped rather than expandable because this card has nothing to swap off. **Deliberately not built:** the mockup's deciding column draws `ניווט` and moves `＋ אולי` into a foot, but ADR-0134 §5 settled this row's two controls _after_ that mockup, with stacking measured and rejected. **It also fixed a latent Phase 6 defect:** the expanded card's `:has(.map-hero)` grid rules were **inert** — `display: grid` and the height bound were only selected by the collapsed card's `:has(.note-sec)`, which the mode change stops matching — so the one state with an unclamped summary was also the one with no bound. **Left open:** the trust boundary is accepted rather than closed (a client-supplied identity lands in a **global** store, bounded by invite-only membership and a one-place blast radius — revisit first if the app opens up), and the device pass gains a question: whether a 130px hero inside a result row in a scrolling list reads as one card.
  - ~~**A photograph on the PIN, not only in the row**~~ — **done (2026-08-05, [ADR-0167 §16](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md) + [`mockups/place-pin-thumbnail-v1.html`](../mockups/place-pin-thumbnail-v1.html) + [session note](planning/2026-08-05-enrichment-live-build-fixes.md)):** the owner's fourth report on the live enrichment build (_"Map pins should also show the thumbnail (think how to do this aesthetically)"_), answered with a mockup against the app's real pin CSS and then built. Three treatments drawn and measured: **A** the photo fills the teardrop head with the hue moving to a ring (the badge's own move, §1) — **21px of photograph at a 34px pin, 35px at 56px**; **B** A gated on the size the canvas resolves; **C** a second silhouette — **31% more picture, and the canvas stops being one shape**, rejected by the owner and by the render (it reads as a sticker rather than a point). **The owner took B and it shipped**, with the gate as a **`@container (min-height: 436px)` query** on the pane rather than a prop: pin size is already `clamp(34px, 11cqh, 56px)` against a container, so a stop change draws or drops every photo with no re-render and no marker re-diff (ADR-0121 §4). Three build facts worth keeping: the photo comes from the same `badgePhoto` the row's badge uses (so §2's picked-icon-beats-photo cannot hold on one surface and not the other), the clip is on an **inner** element because `.pin-b` deliberately has no `overflow` (the order counter overhangs it — §11.2's trap), and the photo is dropped at the **dot** tier for the same reason the glyph and the numeral already are. ~~**What no test can see:** the gate is a container query and the canvas needs a Maps key.~~ **Both halves of that were wrong, and the fix is in ([ADR-0167 §17](decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md), owner report 2026-08-05: _"the thumbnails aren't rendering into the pins … even when zoomed out the pins are full size"_).** The gate has **two axes** — the canvas's height, and whether this pin is a dot, which is scoped (day scope degrades only `.aside`) — so hiding the photo off the pane's `data-pins` took it off full-size stops, and because `:has(.pin-photo)` tests presence rather than display the photographed paint stayed over the gap: an empty head. The photo now drops in the very rules the glyph drops in, and a pin that stops drawing it takes its hue back. **A second, unreported defect came out of the same read:** `:has()` carries its argument's specificity, so the category ring outranked `.nextstop` and a photographed next stop lost the canvas's one amber cue. The hue is now `--pin-hue` (the `--badge-ring` move, §1) instead of fifteen rules. And the canvas needs a Maps key but **the rules do not** — `e2e/map-pin-photo.spec.ts` measures them over the app's own stylesheets, five of nine failing against the shipped CSS. Only 35px legibility is still the device pass.
  - **Still not settled, and it is the last thing before a build:** whether a real photograph is legible at 40px. The mockup's images are synthetic (this environment cannot reach `upload.wikimedia.org`), so crop geometry is honest and content is not — a device pass with real Commons files. Also deferred: the app's **other surfaces** (shelf ideas, event rows, the Index, the place form ADR-0148, the hero ADR-0160) get a second pass once the badge's behaviour is real, and the empty card's 44px of chrome for one button is measured but untuned.
  - **Images and licenses are measured — the design session is unblocked for them** ([ADR-0166 §12](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md), re-run 2026-08-04): **32 real thumbnails** across **0.54–1.78** aspect (six portraits), all licenses resolved with **zero exclusions**, and **27 of 32 (84%) requiring visible credit** — so the attribution slot is the default case, not an edge one. The URLs are in the dataset and need fetching from a networked machine. Two rules fell out: **`iiurlwidth` ignores exact widths** (buckets: 400 → 500) but always returns a working 36–250 KB thumbnail, so we fetch the bucket and store it and the backend needs **no image-resizing dependency**; and a **GFDL-only `P18` is treated as no image** (one file in 32 — its attribution terms are far heavier than CC's).
  - **One measurement still worth taking, and it is not the one we thought** ([ADR-0166 §12.4](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md)). The re-run reports hours at 34.9%, but that counts 11 never-queried rows as failures; of rows actually queried it is 48.4%, and **8 of the 11 unqueried are stratum C**, so the restaurant fill rate rests on three famous places. The gap is an **artifact of the spike**: it had no coordinate column and fell back to the matched Wikidata `P625`, so unmatched places had nothing to query with — whereas **in production every place has coordinates** from the Google pick (ADR-0108 §3). So hours coverage was accidentally coupled to Wikidata matching when the two are independent, and the open question is a good one: **query Overpass by coordinate for ordinary businesses, no Wikidata step** — because the restaurant hole is a Wikipedia hole, and OSM is the source that covers businesses. Hours may be the enrichment that works best exactly where image and summary fail.
  - **Phase 2 is better costed but still not costed for the case that justifies it.** Landmarks are at 71% hours fill, restaurants effectively unmeasured (above). Settled regardless: the raw OSM `opening_hours` expression is **stored always** and any display derived from it (13 distinct syntax shapes across 15 values — seasonal overrides, single-date closures, `24/7`, past-midnight ranges — so it is provably not a seven-row weekly table), and **public Overpass instances explicitly disclaim production volume**, so the phase chooses a hosted instance, a commercial mirror, or an accepted best-effort posture.
  - **`QID → OSM` is often an exact join, not a fuzzy one** — 10 of 31 objects matched via the OSM object's own `wikidata=Q…` tag. The matching pipeline orders itself accordingly: exact tag, then settled id, then proximity + name similarity, with the recorded confidence naming which fired (ADR-0166 §12.3). This materially de-risks the matching problem the ADR called its biggest.
- **A way through to the place on Google Maps** ([ADR-0166 §13](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md), owner ask 2026-08-04; narrowly revises [ADR-0121 §8](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)). **This is the answer to the enrichment coverage hole, and it costs nothing** — a free universal Maps URL hands over everything Google holds (hours, photos, reviews, phone, live busy-ness) for the 0-of-7 restaurants open sources cannot describe. **Place detail / selection surface only** — the Map-tab row keeps its single Google exit (`נווט`), which is what §8's density argument was actually protecting; what returns is a different question (_what does Google know_, not _where is it_), sitting beside the enrichment it supplements. `mapsSearchUrl` in `lib/places.ts` already builds this URL for research predictions, so this widens an existing builder's job rather than adding one. Wants the same design pass as the thumbnail and attribution slots, since it lands on the same surface.
  - **Rejected on the way, recorded so it is not re-litigated:** filling missing fields from the **Google Places API** when open sources miss. The arithmetic inverts — the open pipe is cheap because it queries **once, ever, for all trips**, and Google content may not be cached, so a fallback costs **per render** (~1,000 Details calls for one trip of 20 Google-only places × 5 members × 10 opens), fires on the most-viewed rows, cannot go offline, and replaces a testable invariant with a judgement call. Widening the pick-time mask does not rescue it: the blocker is the caching prohibition, not call volume. **One condition would reopen it** — needing hours as _data_ to compute the arrive-after-closing warning, which a link cannot serve; that would be a live, unstored, single-field fetch with its own cost decision.
  - **The `0/7` number that should govern the design** (ADR-0166 §11.3): Tokyo restaurants and cafés scored **no match, no image, no summary** against 14/14 for landmark sights. An image-led card is right for landmark discovery and **wrong for a mixed itinerary**, where the ordinary branch is the common row. Ignore the spike's 76.7% headline — it is selection-biased upward by unrequested famous non-Tokyo restaurants; Tokyo's honest rate is 70.4%.
  - **Recorded, unbuilt:** Tier-B fields (website, phone, price level, source-suggested types — the last would let ADR-0165's `category` be _suggested_ rather than only hand-set, which is that ADR's call); matching a coordless Place-lite by name + coords (the alias-column design permits it, nothing builds it); and an **LLM summary provider**, which would answer the coverage hole Phase 1 accepts but needs its own decision for cost, hallucination on facts a traveller acts on, and attribution.
- **`event-arrival-scroll.spec.ts:144` is flaky under `e2e (preview)` only, and it has now blocked three PRs** (2026-08-21, seen on #657, #659's predecessor and #660). Always the same case — `Plan day: the same arrival lands the builder row` — always the `preview` leg, never `dev`, and `ci` green alongside it. **Twice it failed on diffs containing zero `frontend/` files**, and the third time on a diff whose frontend changes are provably unreachable from it (two new exported constants, plus two paths gated on query params that spec never sets, on a screen it never visits). So it is the spec, not the changes.
  **Why preview and not dev is the lead.** `ci.yml` says it itself: "dev too means a logic bug, preview only means a bundling/asset issue". Under the production bundle the chunks load in a different order and `.builder-side` mounts later — the spec already waits for it with a **20 s** timeout, which is the tell that this surface is slow there. `expectLanded` wraps its measurement in `.toPass()`, so retrying is not the gap; the assertion that retries cannot rescue is `scrolled > 0`, which stays false forever if the arrival scroll is **undone** by a layout pass that lands after it. That is the hypothesis, and it is the frontend `CLAUDE.md`'s own "a landing position written as a constant instead of measured" family — the class it warns is invisible to the unit suite by construction.
  **FIXED (2026-08-21), and my first hypothesis above was wrong** — worth leaving on the record because the wrong guess was the plausible one. It was not the landing being undone by a later layout pass. The CI log says `expect(top).toBeLessThanOrEqual(141)` received **883** in an 844-high viewport, unmoved through thirty seconds of `.toPass()` retries: the scroll **never happened at all**. `landAtTop` already re-asks `find()` every frame for exactly this reason, so the mechanism was right and the **budget** was wrong. `LANDING_WATCH_MS` (2.5s) was measured against a surface that EXISTS and was also being spent waiting for one to ARRIVE — and `land-at-top.ts`'s own header records Plan's day view as a lazy chunk that mounts **~5s in under 6× CPU throttling**. On a loaded runner the whole budget went before the row existed, the watch closed, and nothing aimed. Split into two: `LANDING_WAIT_MS` (10s) to wait for the element, and the measured settle window starting on the frame it appears. A frame of waiting is one `querySelector`, and any touch still ends it instantly. **The reason no test caught it is worth more than the fix:** `land-at-top.test.ts` drives `requestAnimationFrame` by hand, so pumping frames advanced no WALL time and no budget could ever expire — its "waits for an element that is not there yet" test passed against the broken code. It now stubs `performance.now()` with a clock the test moves, and the three new cases fail against the single-budget version.

- **Push notifications — DESIGNED (2026-08-20; [ADR-0197](decisions/0197-a-notification-is-a-derived-obligation-and-the-sweep-is-its-clock.md) the pipe, [ADR-0198](decisions/0198-we-notify-what-you-can-still-miss.md) the catalogue, session note [`planning/2026-08-20-notifications-epic-design-session.md`](planning/2026-08-20-notifications-epic-design-session.md)). Nothing built.** Owner ask 2026-08-15 from the tasks PM session; scoped as its own epic, tasks its first consumer, exactly as this line said. **Three of the four costs it named survived; the scheduler one did not.** Web Push + VAPID + a per-device `PushSubscription` stands; iOS-installed-PWA-only stands and is now stated **in the settings row** rather than in a field report; the multi-zone question is answered by moving ADR-0107's `currentZone` and ADR-0194's `dueZone` into `packages/shared` so the send time and the printed time are one derivation. **But the scheduler is not BullMQ** — a queue is a second copy of a schedule Postgres already holds (`Task.dueAt`, `Event.startsAt`, the ADR-0184 window bounds) that every edit path must keep honest, including the cascades that write no `Change` rows (the ADR-0152 §2 / ADR-0157 §3 hole) and an outbox replaying hours late; so it is a **60-second sweep** plus a `NotificationSend` ledger keyed on the instant a send was **aimed at**, which makes a moved deadline re-arm and an edited title not. Insert-and-send in one transaction is the exactly-once mechanism across instances, so nothing assumes one process (ADR-0065). **Redis stays reserved** (see "Redis for documents" above) — this epic was its expected first consumer and does not use it; the switch is behind one sender interface if volume ever asks. **Two things the design found that the costing did not:** phase 0 is the **service worker**, because a `push` listener means `injectManifest` and hand-writing what ADR-0185's atomic swap currently gets from the generated template (a missing `SKIP_WAITING` listener is a silently degraded PWA, not a build error) — so it ships alone, with a build-failing contract test; and the **seed has no dated tasks at all** (`grep -c dueAt` → 0), so fixtures are a blocker for the first real kind rather than a chore after it. **The catalogue is closed by ADR-0011:** hard events, ambient span **edges** (ADR-0164's own set), task deadlines, plus one addressed social send (`task.assigned`). A soft event is never notified, at any preference. **"Notify before the flight to check in" is not a notification** — we do not store the airline's window, so it is an **automatic task** at departure − 24 h that rides `task.due` and shows up in Plan/Index/the booking section besides. **"Leave now" is refused** until Routes travel time **and** a position exist (ADR-0006/0106). **PHASE 0 BUILT (2026-08-20; [session note](planning/2026-08-20-notifications-phase-0-built.md)):** the service worker is ours — `injectManifest` + `frontend/src/sw.ts`, hand-carrying the six calls the template used to emit, with `globPatterns` left in the config because choosing which files are precached is manifest generation and not worker behaviour. Byte-verified equivalent (same message handler, same 68 precache entries / 3196.94 KiB) and proved in Chromium by `scripts/deploy-swap-check.mjs`: worker parked, an old-build chunk still served at 200, the new build taken with no user action. **Four findings:** the option list omitted `cleanupOutdatedCaches()` (emitted anyway, and without it every old precache lives forever), the plugin registers `type: 'classic'` in production while `rollupFormat` defaults to `es` (so the shipped default is only accidentally valid — now `iife`, and note `rollupFormat` is silently ignored at the top level), the worker needs a **second** TypeScript program because `WebWorker` collides with `DOM` (`tsconfig.sw.json`, wired into `pnpm typecheck` so it is not a file nothing checks), and the import must be the shared **file** rather than the barrel or `inlineDynamicImports` would inline zod into the worker. `src/sw.contract.test.ts` was made to fail eleven ways before being kept, including the three a review would not ask for: flipping back to `generateSW` (which deletes nothing and fails nothing), losing `rollupFormat`, and leaving a dead `workbox:` block. Also fixed on the way: `deploy-swap-check.mjs`'s documented invocation had never worked (bare ESM specifiers resolve from the module's URL, not the cwd), and its "make any source change" advice is insufficient because an unused export is tree-shaken. **PHASE 1 BUILT (2026-08-21; [session note](planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phase-1-built.md)):** the pipe end to end and no product UI — VAPID through the fail-fast config (checked as a SET, and size-checked so a swapped keypair fails at boot), one table (`PushSubscription`, control plane, keyed on the push service's own endpoint), `POST`/`DELETE /notifications/subscription`, a dev-only `POST /notifications/test`, `NotificationSender` + `WebPushSender`, the worker's `push`/`notificationclick`, `lib/push.ts`, sign-out revocation, and `push: { vapidPublicKey }` on `/me`. **Five decisions:** the subscribe upsert **re-owns** the endpoint (or a shared device keeps notifying the previous user); `DELETE` takes a **body**, because an endpoint is a URL and a bearer capability and has no business in an access log; the test send is a **route not a button**, because push exists only in a production build where `import.meta.env.DEV` is false; the one unavoidable gesture is an **instrument** (`PushDebugPanel`, behind `VITE_PUSH_DEBUG`, sitting where phase 1b's designed row lands); and the payload contract is **zod-free** because the worker bundles with `inlineDynamicImports`. Verified in Chromium by a new `scripts/push-handler-check.mjs` (CDP delivers a push straight to the worker, the page reads back what it drew — no push service needed): a well-formed payload draws itself, and not-JSON, a non-payload, an empty body and an **off-origin url** all draw the fallback rather than supplied text. **Three things were wrong before they were right, all found by mutation:** a `toContain('parsePushPayload')` assertion satisfied by the import line, a fake Prisma **stricter** than Prisma that let the "one user cannot unsubscribe another's device" spec pass against a query with the ownership scope removed, and a harness that accused the code because its CDP `disable`/`enable` cycle disturbed the worker. **Deliberately unbuilt:** `NotificationSend` and `NotificationDispatcher`, which land with the sweep that reads and feeds them. **The phase list is re-cut and has exactly one design phase:** 1b (the Notifications settings surface + the permission ramp — permission in four states × installed-or-not × server-keypair, plus three preferences and a managed device list), then 2 (zones into `packages/shared`), 3 (sweep, ledger, quiet hours, cap), 4 (seed deadlines + phase A's kinds, with a lock-screen **device pass** rather than a mockup), 5 (phase B + `notifyLeadMinutes`), 6 (phase C). Remaining work is phases 1b-6 in the session note, and four owner questions, of which only phase D (`group.imminent`, which the ADR leans against building) changes the shape. **Two owner corrections landed the same day:** the sweep spans **every trip that has not ended, pre-trip included** (the draft's "inside the access window" read as ADR-0040's _Trip-mode_ window and would have excluded the run-up, which is where task deadlines actually live), and the volume cap is **per source rather than per trip phase** — we ration what the app decided to say (1 nudge/day), not what a person asked to be reminded of (6 task sends/day, `timeCritical` uncapped); the draft's "1/day before the trip" would have dropped one of two real pre-trip deadlines. The gap underneath both: a dated-**no-time** task never fires `task.due` by design, so the **digest** is the mechanism, and it now names **today and tomorrow**. Redis/BullMQ gets named thresholds instead of adjectives (ADR-0197 §3.1: a second scheduled workload, a tick over 30s, non-410 failures over ~1%, or spreading delivery load) and the swap is `NotificationDispatcher`, with `NotificationSender` the transport beneath it — the queue would carry "deliver now", never "fire later". **PHASES 2 AND 3 BUILT (2026-08-21; [session note](planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phases-2-3-built.md)):** the zone derivations are one derivation (`packages/shared/src/zones.ts` + `task-time.ts`, with `todayInTz` and `TaskClock` **re-exported** from `lib/time`/`lib/tasks` rather than churning 31 call sites), and the clock exists — `NotificationSend`, `NotificationSweepService`, `send-policy.ts` (quiet hours 22:00-07:00 in the recipient's zone, the per-source caps, `fireKeyFor`, staleness), `NotificationDispatcher` + `DirectDispatcher`, and a 60-second ticker that **starts no timer while no kind is registered**. **No kind is registered, so nothing can notify anybody** — that is the deliverable. **The owner's scalability question changed the design:** the first version looped over live trips (`1 + 3T` sequential queries per tick, paid with zero sends — past §3.1's own 30-second threshold at ~1,000 trips, over the 60-second interval at ~5,000), so the loop is **inverted** — a kind is never handed a trip and runs one indexed range query across all of them, zone context is lazy and memoized per tick, the caps are one `groupBy` plus an in-memory decrement (a snapshot taken before the claims would let four nudges in one tick each read "0 spent"), and two indexes were added because `Task.dueAt`/`Event.startsAt` had none and every existing index on those tables leads with `tripId`, which a cross-trip query cannot use. **§3.1's threshold was itself wrong** — "a tick over 30 s ≈ 4,000 sends" measures sends when the expensive thing in a sweep is the per-trip work; amended in place. Also amended: `packages/shared/CLAUDE.md`'s `Intl` ban, which is really a ban on `Intl` reading the **environment** — an explicit `timeZone` argument is deterministic, and `schemas.ts` had been doing it all along. Remaining work is 1b (the one design phase), 4 (seed deadlines + phase A's kinds), 5, 6. **PHASE 1b DESIGNED (2026-08-21; [mockup](../mockups/notifications-in-settings-v1.html), [catalogue entry](design/mockups.md), [session note](planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phase-1b-designed.md)) — the epic's one design phase is done and nothing is built.** [ADR-0197](decisions/0197-a-notification-is-a-derived-obligation-and-the-sweep-is-its-clock.md) §7.1 and [ADR-0198](decisions/0198-we-notify-what-you-can-still-miss.md) §6 amended in place. **The section is two cards in one `התראות` section** between תצוגה and מפות אופליין (235px at 360px), because the device half and the preferences half have opposite persistence and ADR-0180 §2 already settled that this means one hint per card. **Where a blocker cannot be cleared there is no control, not a disabled one** — and with no server keypair the section is absent entirely. **The boolean is a new primitive and four call sites arrive at once:** `grep -r 'role="switch"'` returns zero, and against the same four booleans a two-option `ChoiceGrid` costs +80px while the `.set-edit` verb costs +48px once ADR-0017's floor is honoured — as shipped it is 25px, so it was only cheaper by being illegal. **The device list is the map-storage rows, not `ListRow`** (which needs an `onOpen` a device has none of), renders only when a device other than this one exists, and is **two lines** because one line ellipsised the device name (116px of label against 296px, for 9px of height). **The second door is `StatusBanner` inside `TaskSheet`** under the deadline field — zero new infrastructure, against a sheet-over-sheet at ×3 the height plus a back-stack layer. **Two defects the render found:** `.set-note` already exists in `screens.css` with one call site and IS the right component (the name collision made the shipped `display:flex` split the iOS instruction into two ragged columns), and the one-line device row above. **The build order is the fork:** the device card ships when built, the preferences card ships **with the kind it gates** (`notifyTasks` with phase 4, `notifyObligations` with phase 5), and `notifyGroup` is not drawn until phase D is decided — so **1b and phase 4 land together** and `PushDebugPanel` stays the instrument until they do. Remaining work: build 1b+4 (seed deadlines, phase A's kinds, `GET /notifications/subscriptions`, the `userAgent` label derivation, a lock-screen device pass), then 5, then 6. **PHASE A + 1b BUILT (2026-08-21; [session note](planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phase-a-and-the-settings-surface.md); [ADR-0197 §7.2](decisions/0197-a-notification-is-a-derived-obligation-and-the-sweep-is-its-clock.md), [ADR-0198 §7.1](decisions/0198-we-notify-what-you-can-still-miss.md) + its phase-A note).** The registry stops being empty — `task.due`, `task.digest`, `task.assigned` — and the settings surface ships beside them, because a switch and the thing it switches had to go together. **Phase 3's claim held:** the registry was the only line that had to change to turn the machinery on. **One column was added and it is not a retreat from ADR-0197 §3:** `Task.assignedAt`, because being assigned is a TRANSITION and a deadline is a state — so the fact is recorded where the actor is known (null on self-assign, which is how "the actor is not the assignee" gets enforced exactly instead of guessed from `updatedBy`) and the send stays derived. **Two per-kind policies joined the interface**, for the reason `timeCritical` is there — `pref` (the switch that declines a kind, enforced by the sweep in one batched query; a missing user row reads as opted OUT) and `dedup` (`BY_SUBJECT` for `task.assigned`, which is what makes "passing a task back and forth does not multiply" true). **The digest is the one kind whose trigger is a wall clock** and it stays inverted by asking which trips have an open dated task first, zones only for those, then which are at 08:00. **The copy had to move to the server**, contradicting ADR-0198 §7's `i18n/he.ts`: ADR-0197 §6 shipped first and made `PushPayload` carry finished strings, so whoever sends composes — and composing in the worker would inline 2,600 lines of i18n into every install. **And the copy dropped `דנה הטילה עליך משימה`**, which inflected a feminine verb from a name about a real person out of a field the app does not have; Hebrew has no neutral form, so the construction has no verb. Surface: `ui/primitives/Switch` (the app's first boolean), the device list with **no endpoint on the wire** (`POST` returns the row id, the client compares ids), the preferences card rendering only while this device is subscribed, and the second door as `StatusBanner` inside `TaskSheet` where a dismissal counts as an answer. `.set-edit`'s 25px floor defect fixed on the way, by the same argument that fixed `.set-tz-trigger`. Seed: seven tasks, one per catalogue case, at the app's own 23:59 day-deadline convention, plus memberships for all five demo users (the trip had one, so nothing group-shaped was testable). **Still owed: the lock-screen device pass** on both platforms, which ADR-0198 §7 requires before phase A is called done and which no sandbox can do. **PHASE B BUILT (2026-08-21; [session note](planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phase-b-built.md); [ADR-0197 §5](decisions/0197-a-notification-is-a-derived-obligation-and-the-sweep-is-its-clock.md), [ADR-0198](decisions/0198-we-notify-what-you-can-still-miss.md)'s phase-B note).** Six kinds now — `event.hard.soon`, `span.edge.soon`, `trip.tomorrow` — plus `notifyObligations` and the card's second switch; the sweep, ledger, quiet hours, caps and ticker were untouched again. **§5's measurement finally happened** (the seed now has a flight and a seven-night stay, which is what it was waiting for): ticking every minute of three days against the seeded trip gives **1 digest + 1 `task.due` + 1 `event.hard.soon`** on a trip day, **1 digest + 1 `span.edge.soon`** on the check-out day and **nothing at all** on a pre-trip day — §5's computed shape, and no cap binds. **And the measurement found a phase-A defect nothing else could see:** `task.digest`'s hour gate passes for all sixty minutes of 08:00 while it reported `aimedAtMs: nowMs`, so it minted a **new** `fireKey` every tick — 60 ledger claims per person per morning, 59 refused by the 1/day cap rather than by the ledger, with the comment beside it asserting the opposite and no test disagreeing. Nothing wrong ever reached a phone, which is exactly why it survived. Fixed by `hourStartInZone` (local minute, not a UTC floor — `Asia/Kathmandu` is `+05:45`); `trip.tomorrow` had the same shape and was fixed before it shipped; the rule is now ADR-0197 §5's — **a wall-clock kind keys on the hour it fires for, not on the tick that noticed**. Three more things worth carrying: the two event kinds split on `isAmbient`, which is ADR-0164's own line and transfers unchanged; §2's "`startWindowEnd` when there is a window" is only true on the **start** side, because at the end `endWindowStart` is the earliest you may leave and `endsAt` is the deadline, so the kind reads three of four bounds on purpose; and `task-audience.ts` became `trip-audience.ts` (every line was already trip-scoped) with `event-shape.ts` collecting the three questions all three kinds ask, rather than an `event-audience` that could disagree about what "live" means. Seed: a flight and a stay, one per kind — and the event map was silently overwriting per-event dates (`date: date(DAY)`, now `?? `). **PHASE C's FIRST HALF BUILT (2026-08-21; [session note](planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phase-c-readiness.md); [ADR-0198](decisions/0198-we-notify-what-you-can-still-miss.md)'s phase-C note).** `readiness.nudge` is registered — 10:00 local at T-14/T-7/T-2, only when a check is still open, naming just the gap (`חסרים: טיסות, לינה`). Seven kinds. **`computeReadiness` moved into `packages/shared`**, which is the third derivation this epic has moved for ADR-0197 §5's reason and the first that is a _fact_ rather than a clock: a nudge disagreeing with the tasks screen about an open check teaches the person the channel is unreliable. `addDays`/`tripDates`/`zonedIso` + the night-window constants went with it, re-exported from `lib/time.ts` so 22 call sites did not churn, and `offsetAt` collapsed into one `zoneOffsetAt` instead of a private copy per side. Shared 276→306 tests, frontend 4133→4103 — the same thirty, relocated, which is the proof the move was clean. **It rides `notifyTasks`, not a third switch** (ADR-0190 made a readiness check a task row), and its query starts from `Trip` without breaking the inverted loop because a trip has one start date. **Hebrew duals for the second time** (`שבועיים`/`יומיים`, after phase B's `בעוד שעתיים`), so milestone labels are a lookup a fourth milestone must extend rather than a template that would generate a wrong form. The "all five checks satisfied ⇒ no send" branch needed a fixture nobody had built; it passed first run, so the guard was mutated away to prove it wasn't vacuous. **STILL OPEN, and it is a schema fork §C states two ways in one paragraph:** the flight check-in is "minted" and "rides `task.due`" (a **stored** row, needing `dueAt` cascaded at the three sites that can move a departure) _and_ "ADR-0190's rule applied one row further" (a **derived** overlay, which cannot go stale but has no `dueAt` for `task.due` to find and would need the `flight.checkin` kind §C rejected — plus `TaskDerivedKey` is a closed five-value per-trip enum while a check-in is per-booking). Recommended: stored, with the cascade as one shared util. **THE EPIC IS CLOSED AT SEVEN KINDS (owner, 2026-08-21).** The **flight check-in is dropped**, which closes §C's stored-vs-derived fork by not needing an answer — phase C is complete as `readiness.nudge` alone, and if a check-in ever returns it returns as a task, for §C's own reason (we do not store the airline's window, so a send claiming check-in is open would be a guess printed as a fact). **Phase D is dropped**: `group.imminent` will not be built, `notifyGroup` is never added, and §6's two switches are the final set. ADR-0081's change feed stays the home for a peer's edits, unpushed. **`task.assigned` no longer names the assigner** — the body is `‹title› · עד ‹due›`, the title's `בשבילך` carries the addressing alone, and a `User` query per tick went with it (the name was never solid: `updatedBy` drifts if a third party edits inside the six-hour window, and fixing it properly meant an `assignedBy` column for a courtesy). **AND EVERY NOTIFICATION HAD BEEN LANDING ON THE APP HOME:** `/trips/<id>/index/tasks` and `/trips/<id>/day/<date>` match **no route** — the router has `login`, `trips`, `new`, `join/:token`, `trip/:id/settings`, `settings`, `*` — so both fell through to `*`. The app is **query-addressed** (ADR-0098), so the URLs are rebuilt from `?tab=`/`?day=` plus ADR-0153 §8's way-in ids, and two params were added: `?task=` (the fifth way-in id; a task had none because nothing outside the app had ever named one) and **`?trip=`**, which is the one that matters most — the active trip lives in `localStorage` alone, so a reminder about Japan tapped while Iceland was active opened _Iceland_. `?trip=` counts as an explicit pick so ADR-0033's landing rule cannot redirect a notification about a not-yet-started trip, and an empty `?trip=` is treated as absent (a test caught that blanking the active trip). A single-row kind opens its row; the digest and the nudge open the list, because picking one row out of a set-shaped send misrepresents it. The service worker needed no change — `notificationclick` already focuses and navigates. **Still owed: the lock-screen device pass** on both platforms, which no sandbox can do. **Still owed from A: the lock-screen device pass** on both platforms, which no sandbox can do. **A FIRST REAL SEND FAILED IN PRODUCTION AND THE LOG COULD NOT SAY WHY (2026-08-21; [ADR-0197 §10](decisions/0197-a-notification-is-a-derived-obligation-and-the-sweep-is-its-clock.md) amended).** Owner testing on prod: no notification, and `WARN [WebPushSender] push send failed (no status) to fcm.googleapis.com` — which proves the sweep derived a candidate and attempted a send (so everything upstream works) and says nothing about the transport. **A no-status rejection never reached a push service**: `web-push` refused before the request, or the socket did. So the reason is now appended for that case, with the endpoint **subtracted** from the text (`WebPushError` carries an `endpoint` property) and the text capped. **The same failure corrected a documented decision:** `MAX_PUSH_KEY_LENGTH`'s note claimed "the push service is the authority on whether the keys work, at send time" — true for a rejected key, false for a malformed one, because the library checks both lengths locally and throws before any request. The lengths were then **measured against the library instead of read off RFC 8291**, and they are not one rule: `p256dh` must be exactly 65 (any other length throws locally) but `auth` is only checked as **at least** 16 — a 32-byte one is sent and answered. The first attempt wrote both as equalities and would have refused a subscription the sender would have delivered; **a guard must never be stricter than the thing it guards**. Checked at the subscribe, which is `validateVapid`'s own argument applied to the device half it had been left out of. **Also measured, and it narrows the production failure sharply:** a well-formed request always comes back with a status (a bogus FCM token answers 410), so a status-LESS failure is a local refusal by `web-push` — which is a wrong-length `p256dh` or an `auth` under 16 bytes, checkable in one query (`p256dh` is 87 chars, `auth` 22). **A SECOND FIELD REPORT, AND IT IS ABOUT THE PUSH SERVICE RATHER THAN US (2026-08-21):** _"I got the notification as soon as I opened the app. But when it was closed I got nothing."_ The worker is fine (synchronous parse, `showNotification` inside `event.waitUntil`) — what happened is FCM **held** the message while the device was unreachable and released it on reconnect, which it was entitled to do because `web-push` defaults to a **four-week** TTL and `normal` urgency. So a send the sweep refuses to re-derive after an hour could arrive 672 hours later saying "your flight is in two hours". Both knobs are RFC 8030's and both were already per-kind policy nobody transmitted: `timeCritical` → `Urgency: high` (§5.3's "time-sensitive alert", the row delivered on low battery) and `staleAfterMs` → `TTL`, rounded up so it cannot expire inside the window the sweep still honours. Derived by id in `deliveryFor` rather than tabulated a second time, and an unnamed kind (the dev `test` send) gets 15 minutes instead of inheriting a month. **The honest limit: this does not wake a browser the OS has stopped** — Android battery optimisation and a force-quit browser both prevent delivery and no header changes that; what it fixes is that a late delivery no longer arrives as a lie. **AND THEN THE IMPROVED LOG ANSWERED IT IN ONE LINE, on the owner's next send: `webpush.sendNotification is not a function` — the transport had NEVER worked.** `web-push` is CommonJS, so `await import('web-push')` returns a namespace whose named exports are only what `cjs-module-lexer` detects statically (`WebPushError`, `supportedContentEncodings`), and `sendNotification` lives solely on `.default`. Every send the epic ever attempted threw, and since a `TypeError` carries no `statusCode` it logged as an ordinary transport failure throughout. **The mock is why it shipped:** the spec stubbed `{ sendNotification }` at the top level, a shape no build of the library has — looser than reality, so eight specs asserted the sender's contract against something impossible. The mock now mirrors the real namespace and those eight fail against the old code; a `shape` switch covers the ESM branch too. Third fake-vs-reality defect in this epic and the only one to reach production, so the rule is recorded in ADR-0197: **a fake's SHAPE is part of the contract under test** — when you stub a module boundary, copy its real export shape. **Also fixed, and the reason a retry would not have saved it:** the owner's `p256dh` and `auth` are both valid (87/22 chars, 65/16 bytes) and encrypt-and-send correctly against FCM, every VAPID fault returns a **403** rather than no status, and a well-formed request always gets a status at all — so the failure was the transport, and **the claim was never released**. `NotificationSend` is written before the send so a crash loses a delivery rather than double-sending it; but a claim outliving a _failed_ send makes the loss permanent, so one DNS hiccup consumed that notification for good, and a retest of the same task would log `already sent`. `SEND_OUTCOME.FAILED` had documented the opposite since phase 1 ("the sweep re-derives on the next tick"), which the ledger made impossible. Now the dispatcher reports which sends reached **no** device and the sweep deletes those rows; the claim still stands when _some_ device took it, and when there were **no** devices (an empty audience is not a failure), with `staleAfterMs` as the bound. Keyed by the send's **value**, not object identity — `DirectDispatcher` returns the objects it was handed, so a `Map<DueSend, …>` would work today and break silently under the `QueueDispatcher` the seam exists for. **A testing lesson worth more than either fix:** the cap assertion was green and **vacuous** — a `mockRestore()` written as a test's last line does not run when an assertion above it fails, and because the spy is on a shared PROTOTYPE the next `spyOn` reuses the leaked one with its predecessor's calls, so reading `calls[0]` read the previous test's line. Hook-scoped teardown plus a `lastWarning()` that reads `calls.at(-1)`; found only because the mutation pass asked why removing the cap did not fail anything.
- **A leave-by notification — `תתחילו להתכונן ליציאה · כדאי לצאת לפני X`** (owner, 2026-08-26). **[ADR-0198](decisions/0198-we-notify-what-you-can-still-miss.md) §4 excluded it and its own "reopens when" clause has now fired** — see the ⚠ REOPENED box there, which is the entry to read. Both premises changed: travel time exists ([ADR-0205](decisions/0205-routes-are-computed-not-bought-and-a-route-is-a-cache.md)/[0206](decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) built it, OSM-derived and cached rather than the Routes API §4 was waiting on), and the position premise was **wrong when written** — ADR-0006 defers only member-to-member sharing and `lib/useGeolocation.ts` has shipped since ADR-0109 §6. §4 says the shape is a **dynamic lead on `event.hard.soon`, not a new kind**, but three things it does not answer: one send or two (the owner asked for a _prepare_ beat as well as a _leave_ beat, and a dynamic lead fires earlier, not twice); volume (§5 exempts `event.hard.soon` from every cap for a handful of hard commitments, not two sends per stop across a day of stops); and whether soft events qualify at all, which reopens §1's ADR-0011 filter. **Blocked on the notifications epic, not on routes** — ADR-0197's sweep is designed and nothing is built, so there is no pipe. Take it with that epic; do not add a routes milestone for it.
- **Google Cloud project setup** (human) — OAuth consent, Maps/Places, Calendar. Gates the Maps & Places epic (ADR-0106, Phase 0) and calendar sync.
- **Calendar one-way sync** (trip → personal, ADR-0003) — the feature itself; nothing reads `Membership.calendarSyncEnabled` today. When built, a linked event's location must resolve via its booking/`Place` — there is no `Event.location` anymore (ADR-0051).
- **Lazy incremental OAuth consent** — before calendar sync first fires for a member, check `AuthIdentity.scopes` and run Google's incremental-consent redirect if the calendar scope is missing. Per `auth-and-google.md`, scopes are never front-loaded at sign-in. Needed by the item above, not before it.
- **`/login` promises two integrations that do not exist** (found 2026-08-18) — `t.shell.login.noteExtra` (`i18n/he.ts`, rendered at `screens/Login.tsx:126`) tells every user _before they sign in_ that המסלול מסתנכרן ליומן האישי and that הזמנות נכנסות מ-Gmail. Calendar sync is the item above (nothing reads `calendarSyncEnabled`); Gmail import is a deferred v1.1 row in the feature catalog. This is the same rule the push item states for itself — **no copy may imply a pipe that does not run** — so either the line comes off the login screen or it waits for ADR-0003. The owner picks which.
- **Link previews on a note — title, thumbnail, site** (owner ask, 2026-08-02: _"could we get previews for Instagram, YouTube, Facebook, TikTok, like WhatsApp does"_). **Wants its own design session** — this is a card, and a note surface that already spends its density budget twice over (ADR-0149/ADR-0116) has to be told where one goes and what it costs. Interim shipped instead: `prettyUrl` (`lib/external-url.ts`) makes the LABEL readable, which is most of the reported ugliness. What a real preview needs, so the session starts from the constraints rather than discovering them: **(1)** OG/oEmbed cannot be fetched from the browser — no CORS — so it is a **backend** fetcher plus a cache table, i.e. this is an ADR-0004 pipe (feeds the note, never its own screen); **(2)** the four platforms named are the four hardest — Instagram and Facebook oEmbed need a Meta app token, TikTok's is unauthenticated but rate-limited, and only YouTube is trivial (`noembed`/oEmbed, or just the `i.ytimg.com` thumbnail from the video id) — so a **graceful-absence** path is the norm, not the edge; **(3)** a thumbnail is a remote image on an offline-first surface, so it wants the same treatment documents got (fetch once, store, serve from cache) rather than a hotlink that is blank on a plane; **(4)** previews are group-visible content fetched from a url one member pasted, which is an SSRF seat on the backend and a tracking beacon on the client — the fetcher needs an allowlist and a timeout, and `externalHref`'s protocol allowlist is the existing precedent to extend. Cheapest useful slice if the full thing is too much: **site + favicon + a platform-derived title** for the handful of hosts we can parse from the url alone, with no fetch at all. **Half of (1), (3) and (4) now arrive from elsewhere ([ADR-0166](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md), 2026-08-04):** its store and image/thumbnail pipeline are built **subject-agnostic** precisely so a preview reuses them rather than growing a second fetch-metadata-and-cache-a-thumbnail machine beside them (the ADR-0096 mistake), and its host-allowlist + timeout + size-cap fetcher is the same seat as (4). What stays this item's own work is the **provider** half (per-platform oEmbed and its tokens) and the **design** half (where a card goes) — so build this one **after** ADR-0166 Phase 1, not before.

## Security & correctness

- **Admin role permission matrix** — ADR-0005 is admin/peer only; if roles grow, decide the matrix in an ADR first.

## Known shortcuts (each names its own ceiling in a `ponytail:` comment)

- `constants.ts:46` — a scheduled maybe-item lands on a fixed demo slot instead of a real prompt.
- `packages/shared/src/schemas.ts:17` — `entityIdSchema` is a loose charset+length regex, not exact cuid2/uuid grammar.
- `lib/active-trip.ts:57` — overlapping in-progress trips are an explicitly deferred case.
- `lib/time.ts:220` — a wall-clock input this can't resolve correctly.

## Frontend review follow-ups (open findings)

Full write-up + evidence in [reviews/frontend-architecture-review.md](reviews/frontend-architecture-review.md). F-01–F-04 shipped session 35; F-05–F-08 + F-10 shipped session 36; F-13 shipped session 239 (ADR-0181), then re-cut in session 263 when its premise turned out to be the freeze itself (ADR-0185); F-11 shipped session 240; F-12 + F-14 + F-15 shipped session 241 (the flush drains until the queue is empty rather than until its opening snapshot is; `lib/id.ts`'s `generateId` collects all 16 `crypto.randomUUID` call sites behind a `getRandomValues` fallback, held by a lint guard; the outbox's three in-memory mirrors are re-derived from the store after each flush, without touching the F-03 failure store); F-09 is a deliberate non-fix (ADR-0062). **Every Low/Informational finding is now closed.** One optional item is left, and it is a deliberate deferral rather than a follow-up:

- **(Optional) Route verb optimistic in-memory writes through the applier (ADR-0094 tail)** — the cache mirror + member keying are unified (`applyOutboxOpToCache` → `applyChangeToCache`); the one remaining parallel path is each verb's own optimistic `setState`/dispatch. Routing it through `applyEntityChange` too would need each verb's rollback rewritten as an inverse-change and entangles the event one-slot undo (ADR-0019) — real risk for little further dedup. Low priority.

## The change feed still narrates in the trip's primary zone (found 2026-08-21, session 258)

- **`change-feed.tsx`'s "moved X to 12:30" reads `trip.timezone`** — the last surface painting one zone after ADR-0107's session-258 amendment wired the two booking reads. Deliberately not folded into that fix: a change row carries the `after` payload, not the entity, so resolving its zone means looking the live event up by id and answering two questions the feed has never had to — what a moved event's zone is when the event has since been deleted, and whether an old entry should re-read in the zone the event has _now_ or the one it had when the move happened. One coherent change, not a one-liner.

## UI/UX review follow-ups (open findings)

Full write-up + evidence in [reviews/ui-ux-review.md](reviews/ui-ux-review.md) (advisory, 2026-07-19). No production code changed. Ordered roughly by the review's phased roadmap; the design-system consolidations are the point — fix the shared root, not each screen.

- ~~**U-01 `EventForm` outside the modal/focus system**~~ (High) — **shipped** (Wave 2 E): `EventForm` now renders inside `Modal variant="sheet"` (ADR-0079), so system-back/Escape/backdrop close it and focus moves in/restores; `.event-form-*` deleted.
- ~~**U-04 per-item save/sync state**~~ (High) — **shipped** (ADR-0080, Waves 2–3): the `SyncStatus` model (`synced/pending/failed(reason)`) derived frontend-only from the outbox (id-keyed) + the F-03 failed store, `useSyncStatus(entityId)` + a non-color-coded `SyncBadge`, and a persistent review/retry (dead-letter) sheet replacing the timed failed-badge; `SyncBadge` now on document rows (Wave 2) + booking rows (Wave 3 D-index) + event-timeline cards (session-35: `EventCard` gained a `sync` prop, DayView wires `useSyncStatus(event.id)`; the card shows ↑/! for a queued/failed edit and nothing when synced, so a settled day stays uncluttered).
- **U-08 tokenize spacing + type** (Med, foundation) — token layer **landed** (ADR-0082): `--space-*`/`--text-*`/`--leading-*`/`--radius-*`/`--elevation-*`/`--bp-*`/safe-area/sync tokens now in `tokens.css`, matching the documented ramps. _Remaining:_ opportunistic migration of `App.css`/`screens.css` raw px onto the tokens (rides the U-03 extractions + screen migrations); plus a CI lint budget on raw px in `ui/` CSS (review §15). The Phase-4 dark-mode **color** hex sweep is **done** for hexes — and [ADR-0158](decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) found what a hex-only sweep could not: `rgba()` literals it never looked at (`.nav` stays white in dark), ~24 on-fill and on-dark inks that needed tokens rather than markers, and the fact that the remap had never been **rendered**. _This line's "left for dark mode" clause is closed_ — the live contrast pass ran (and inverted the expected result), and the toggle + persistence are ADR-0158 §10 phase 4, tracked on the dark-mode lines above.
- **Dark mode — BUILT, all six phases (2026-08-02 → 2026-08-03); two follow-ups open** ([ADR-0158](decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md), 2026-08-02 session 209; mockup [`mockups/dark-mode-v1.html`](../mockups/dark-mode-v1.html), generated from the app's real CSS + captured DOM). The colour work really was done and inert — and had therefore **never been rendered**. Setting `data-theme` on the running app found that two of the remap's own recorded conventions are wrong, and that **the shipped LIGHT theme fails contrast 78 times of 238 text nodes against dark's 12** (all twelve one root cause). The ADR carries the measurements, the rejected alternatives and the phase table; the six phases were the lines below, each independently shippable and revertible, and every one of them shipped — `lib/theme.ts` owns the pick, `main.tsx` applies it and `UserSettings` exposes it. **What is still open is the last two sub-items only:** the contrast sweep's corpus and `--miss` as ink.
  - ~~**Phase 1 — on-fill ink**~~ — **shipped**: `--on-fill` (`#FFFFFF` light / `#12203A` dark) + `--on-amber` (`#3A2405` both), generalising the `--cta`/`--cta-text` one-off rather than adding a second (rule 8); `--cta-text` is now an alias of `--on-fill`. 22 rules converted, and the two `background: var(--ink); color: #fff` sites (`.choice-pill.on`, `.wp-event-act:hover`) moved onto the `--cta` pair that existed for exactly them. **Verified two ways rather than asserted:** light mode is **byte-identical** (same sha256 on a rendered screenshot before and after), and a probe instantiating each converted rule in both themes measures every dark pair clearing AA — `.choice-pill.on` **1.20 → 13.49**, `.wp-event-check` 2.31 → 7.03, `.wp-placebadge-mark` 2.56 → 6.34, `.hdr-sync-badge` 2.64 → 6.16, `.confirm[danger]` 2.93 → 5.55, the `--plan` group 3.50 → 4.64 — while every light value is unchanged to two decimals. Both stylesheet headers amended: the conflated "on-fill ink is theme-fixed" rule is split into the two rules it always was (always-dark chrome keeps light ink; a re-mapping fill flips its ink). `.prep`'s `#f2effc` is deliberately left — it is phase 3's, not a miss.
  - ~~**Phase 2 — the `--on-dark` ink ramp**~~ — **shipped**: four theme-fixed tokens (`--on-dark-strong` `#FFFFFF` · `--on-dark` `#EAF0FF` · `--on-dark-dim` `#93A2C4` · `--on-dark-faint` `#7688AC`) folding **14 distinct hex values across 42 declarations** on the always-dark surface family — the board, `/login`, `/join`, the ticket, the teaser, the feature strip, the trip hero and the dormant board. ADR-0082's argument in a different channel: the ramp lived nowhere, so it drifted. **Two deliberate exclusions, neither a miss:** the 8 hue-bearing amber/teal inks on the board stay literal (they are semantic accents at small sizes, and folding them into a blue-grey would spend the budget the wrong way), and `.prep`'s `#f2effc` waits for phase 3's surface tokens. **The chrome's own children were also excluded and moved to phase 3** where §5 already owns them — a first pass lumped them in and produced the worst shifts, which is what made the ADR's own §3/§5 boundary the right cut. Largest remaining shift is `.wp-board-clock` at ΔL\* 12 (brighter); rendered before/after at full resolution in both themes, the board's hierarchy still reads white title → light clock → dim label → faint rail ends. Five redundant `fixed:` markers deleted — a token named `--on-dark-dim` states what the comment could only assert — and both stylesheet headers rewritten to state the three ink rules separately instead of conflating the first two.
  - ~~**Phase 3 — surface + chrome tokens**~~ — **shipped**: `--plan-surface`/`-2` for the prep hero (light values byte-identical; dark goes **deep** `#2A2158`/`#332866` instead of the accent's `#A99AF2`, so plan stops rendering the brightest surface in the dark app at 2.16:1 ink and mode identity stops being inverted — rendered side by side, trip is near-black-plus-amber and plan is a lifted violet slab, unmistakable at a glance); `.nav` onto `color-mix(in srgb, var(--card) 92%, transparent)`, which is what turns the tab bar dark; and the **chrome contract**, 38 declarations rewired onto six names. _Two corrections the build made to the ADR, both amended in place:_ the contract needs **six** names not five (numerals — the day number, the anchor's value — are a real rung between labels and titles), and the **washes are not a token**: they run at eight different alphas, so each element mixes its own off `--chrome-ink` (`color-mix(… var(--chrome-ink) 9%, transparent)`), preserving every alpha exactly while still flipping with the chrome. _Verified per mode and per theme:_ **plan/light 0 pixels differ** (plan's own overrides win, so no `color-mix` is in play — the control); **trip/light 286 of 375,654 px** at ≤25 on one channel, all antialiasing on 1px border curves where `color-mix` rasterises as `color(srgb …)` rather than `rgba()` — same colour, not a visual change; trip/dark and plan/dark differ by exactly the two intended fixes.
  - ~~**Phase 4 — the theme itself**~~ — **shipped, and dark mode is now live.** `lib/theme.ts` owns the pick (`THEME_PICK` system|light|dark, `system` default), the resolve, the `matchMedia` follow and the apply — **generalising `map-config.ts`'s `MAP_THEME`/`documentMapTheme`**, which were the same concept at one call site because the map needed a theme signal before anything set one (rule 8). A blocking inline script in `index.html` applies it **before first paint**; verified by sampling frames after reload, `data-theme=dark` is present at t+0ms, before the stylesheet has even resolved `--screen` — which is the whole argument for storing the pick on the device rather than the user row. `meta[name=theme-color]` follows the resolved theme. The `/settings` control is the existing `ChoiceGrid` primitive with three rungs and no new component or CSS; **this amends [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-the-user-is-a-surface-identity-ramp-and-a-reachable-roster.md) §7's July rejection by its own stated condition** ("a switch that does nothing is worse than a thin page" — true while the remap was inert, false once it is not). ADR-0105's **dark boot** ships as designed: the night board full-bleed rather than a remapped paper, so boot→login is one continuous surface in dark; measured 16.52 / 7.37 / 10.06 for clock / label / dots. 17 new tests on the theme module (2364 total). **The night map needs no code** — `readMapsConfig(env, theme)` already switches and `Map.tsx` latches at mount — but see the 👤 Cloud-console line below: until that import happens the night Map ID renders Google-default.
  - ~~**Phase 5 — the light theme's four failures, plus what the phone found**~~ — **shipped.** `--muted` `#6C7488`→`#61687A`, `--amber-deep` `#C9822A`→`#915E1E` (scalar darkenings, hue and saturation mathematically untouched; **`--amber` itself is not touched** — it lives on the board at 8.51:1 and is the product's signature). `--faint` **narrowed to transient text** — a placeholder, a ghost value the user's input replaces, a disabled control, a bare glyph — with **21 persistent-hint sites moved to `--muted`**; and the ADR's own §7 is amended, because narrowing turns out to make deepening possible rather than redundant: at a 3.0 floor `--faint` can be `#808694` and still sit **11.9 L\* below `--muted`**, where the 0.6 L\* collapse §7 feared only happens at 4.5. Three steps survive AND every value clears its floor. **Then the owner ran it on a phone and found three things (ADR §11).** (a) the active nav tab was **dark-on-dark at 1.27:1** — `--nav-accent: var(--indigo)`, a chrome _surface_ token used as an accent, the **third instance** of this ADR's own bug shape; now `--ink`. (b) **the event cards, settle actions and maybe shelf never themed** — hardcoded light grounds (`#f7f7f9`, `#f6faf7`, `#fafbfd`, `#fff`, and a `#fff`/`#fbfaf7` hatch), now `color-mix` off `--card`/`--muted`/`--ok`/`--paper` at percentages that reproduce today's light value. (c) the map is still the day style — **predicted**, and the 👤 console import below is why. _The method lesson, recorded in ADR §11:_ the audit swept **ink** exhaustively and never swept **grounds**, and the runtime contrast sweep _structurally cannot_ see a light card with dark ink because it passes contrast. **A contrast audit answers "can you read it", never "does it belong here".** A ground sweep now exists and runs clean — written after the phone, not before. Final state: **runtime sweep 0 failing in dark, 1 in light** (a 15px placeholder at 3.65, the accepted transient-text exception). Also fixed on the way, and never a dark-mode bug: `AllTrips`'s `+ טיול חדש` had been permanently dim in **both themes** because it reuses `.create-btn` and never set `data-armed`, a rule written for CreateTrip's not-ready submit — `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s own inherited-defaults anti-pattern.
  - ~~**Phase 6 — the device pass**~~ — **shipped, and it was not a formality** ([ADR-0158](decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) §12). **The map was never only the missing style import:** a Map ID names a light/dark PAIR of cloud styles and `colorScheme` picks between them, defaulting to `LIGHT` — so the night Map ID was being asked for its light face. `readMapsConfig` now resolves the slot from the same theme it resolves the ID from, and the 👤 console import below is still owed on top of that. **Three contrast defects the route sweep structurally could not reach**, because it walked eight URLs as one seeded user with trips and therefore never opened an overlay or hit the zero state: `--indigo` as heading ink at **1.10:1 in dark on `.modal-title`** (one selector behind every sheet, dialog and confirm), plus the zero-state `h1`, both `.placeholder` heads and the document-upload head; `--miss` at **4.38:1 as text on white** — a fill token doing an ink job at 37 sites, now split into `--miss-deep` the way `--amber`/`--amber-deep` already is; and plan's nav accent left at bare `--plan`, putting the **active** tab's own label at 4.32:1 in dark. _The method lesson, beside §11's:_ **a sweep over routes is not a sweep over the app.** **The identity ramp needed no change** — the measured chroma margin against the category pins collapses in dark (`--id-moss` vs `--cat-leisure`, ΔE00 6.5) but does not reproduce rendered at 26/31/44/92px, and §5's single-ink rule is now verified rather than asserted (`#12203A` clears 5.42 min in light, 6.61 in dark across all five). **And §9's light trip chrome shipped with it, at the owner's ask:** `--chrome-bg` is the mode's accent at **12% over `--screen`** — trip indigo, plan violet — so identity rides hue where it used to ride luminance. That exposed the dark chrome having the same defect unnoticed (`--indigo` vs `--screen` = 4.3 L\*, 1.09:1, with the card floating _higher_ than the band carrying mode identity), which withdraws §5's "the chrome is theme-fixed"; and it collapsed plan's **nineteen hand-written chrome rules** into one `--chrome-bg` declaration, which is the duplication §5 was written to end. Captured-frame sweep against `origin/main`: light **10 → 0**, dark **6 → 0**.
  - ~~**The light trip chrome reads dull, and the roster's names are off**~~ — **fixed (ADR-0158 §13), both owner reports on shipped phase 6.** The chrome was `color-mix(--indigo 12%, --screen)` and `--indigo` is a near-neutral navy (chroma **22.4**, chosen to be a dark surface), so no proportion of it yields a light band with hue — 12% gives chroma 4.4 where `--plan`'s 12% gives 10.0, which is the whole asymmetry. Now a literal `#C8D2EC`, and **not a trade**: indigo and violet are ~20° apart, so chroma-matching trip to plan collapses the pair to ΔE00 1.2, while pushing trip's hue _away_ from violet gives **ΔE00 8.4** — bluer AND more distinct than the grey it replaced. Both bands moved into `tokens.css` under each theme block after the first attempt put a light literal in the **mode** selector and painted plan's dark chrome lavender (23 nodes at 1.2-1.8:1 — caught by the sweep in one run). The roster was two things: **28 hardcoded `rgba(22, 35, 61, α)` washes** converted to `color-mix(… var(--ink) N%, transparent)` so they flip (three left alone deliberately — a modal backdrop is a scrim, dark in both themes; alpha is the tell at ≤.34 vs .45/.72), and the real find, **`font: inherit` does not carry `color`** — a `<button>` takes the UA's `buttontext`, near-black and therefore indistinguishable from `--ink` while the app was light-only. **Fourteen rules** did this; one base rule on `button, input, select, textarea` fixes all of them at element specificity.
  - ~~**Two accents that assumed a neutral ground**~~ — **fixed (ADR-0158 §14).** The light chrome gave the app its first HUED surface and two things broke on it. The Map's list/map **toggle**: track `color-mix(--ink 6%)`, thumb `--card` — and `--ink` inverts, so the thumb sat **11.7 L\* above the track in light and 1.6 in dark**; both labels legible, no way to see which was selected. Track is now a `--board` wash (dark in both themes, so it recesses in both), unselected label `--ink` not `--muted` (4.09 was under AA), and the selection finally carries the WEIGHT the file claimed it did. **Amber on the chrome**: amber is hue +72°, the band −83° — near-complementary, so a 16% amber veil that read as a warm glow on near-black **cancels** on a cool light band, computing to chroma 2.8 (the khaki), with `--amber` on it at 1.31:1 and the 6px today dot at 1.39. The chip is now amber mixed into `--paper` (warm in both themes, so one expression serves both) and the ink and dot take `--amber-deep`. **Bounded on purpose:** 67 hardcoded amber alphas exist and **none of the rest are on the chrome** — every other sits on neutral `--card`/`--screen`/`--board`, where a warm wash just warms. Cancellation needs a hued ground; the chrome is the only one.
  - ~~**The selected filter chip was white-on-white in dark**~~ — **fixed (ADR-0158 §15).** `--idx-accent-text: #fff` on `.index`/`.map-screen`, over an `--ink` fill that inverts: **1.20:1**, which is literally §2's worst row. Phase 1 missed it because **the literal was behind a custom property** — the call site reads `color: var(--idx-accent-text)`, which a sweep over `color:` values sees as a token and passes. Now `var(--on-fill)`; light mode does not move, since `--on-fill` _is_ `#FFFFFF` there. Two **frozen ink rings inside `box-shadow`** went with it: §13's converter skipped `box-shadow: 0 …` as drop shadows, right for a shadow and wrong for a `0 0 0 Npx` **spread**, which is a ring and therefore a wash of the ink. _The lesson: an indirection is not a fix._
  - **Grow the contrast sweep's corpus from 6 frames to ~25** — the honest limit on everything above. The static sweep covers trip home, plan home, login, settings, plus a synthesized modal and zero state; the app has ~30 surfaces, and the **Map screen, Index, Day view, every form, `BookingSheet`, the builder and the search overlay have never been measured in either theme**. `mockups/extract-dark-mode-dom.mjs` already captures real DOM from the running app, so this is extending its walk to more routes **and to opened overlays** — which also closes §12's "routes, not states" hole permanently. Note what it still would not catch, because it measures text contrast only: a selection you cannot distinguish and a graphic mark that is invisible (§14 found both on a device, and both passed every sweep).
  - ~~**The plan-mode map connector is invisible in dark**~~ — **fixed ([ADR-0158](decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) §16), and it is a class no CSS sweep in this ADR could reach.** `MAP_CONNECTOR.COLOR` is a **TypeScript** constant (the Maps JS API takes a colour, not a CSS variable), so it sat out the entire dark remap while its own comment said it mirrored `--soft-line` and to "keep the two in step". On the night style's land `#191E2C` it measured **1.01:1**. Now per-theme — and the LIGHT value moved too, because a 2.5px dash is a **graphic** owing 3:1 and measured **1.77** on the day land, i.e. under the floor before dark mode existed; both are the lowest alpha that clears 3:1, keeping ADR-0106 §C's quiet base. It reads the **latched** `config.colorScheme` rather than `documentTheme()`, so after a theme flip the line cannot disagree with the canvas Google is still painting (§4's mount latch). A TS colour sweep now exists and flags one other, `JoinTrip`'s `AVATAR_COLORS` — harmless because `/join` is theme-fixed dark, but stale relative to the `--id-*` ramp.
  - **`--miss` as ink: 34 call sites left** — `--miss-deep` exists and three sites use it. A blanket rename would be wrong: a `--miss` label on an always-dark surface wants the bright value, not the deep one, so each site needs its ground checked. Cheap and mechanical, but not blind.
- **Light trip chrome — DONE, and the hero half is answered** (2026-08-02 → 2026-08-03). The chrome went light in [ADR-0158](decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) §12/§13 after the owner asked for it, which is what §9 had deferred; `--chrome-bg` is the mode's accent at 12% and identity now rides **hue**. The half that was pointed at Hero 2.0 — _should the hero itself lighten?_ — is answered by [ADR-0160](decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md): **no.** §9's four renders were right that the hero is load-bearing (in light mode darkness is its only prominence mechanism, amber-as-a-ground having been rejected in ADR-0105), `--board` is restated darker in the dark block, and lifted the hero **covers** the 72px identity band rather than competing with it. Nothing left to build; kept as the record of why the hero stays dark in both themes.
- **A mockup inlines the app's CSS but hand-writes its icons and copy** (found 2026-08-03, session 210 — three defects in one sitting). `inline-app-css.mjs` exists because a hand-copied token value drifts the day either side changes; the same argument applies to the two things it does **not** copy. In one session that produced a `flight` path pointing **right** where the app's points left (an RTL app, where `.tp-fill` grows from `inset-inline-start`, so the plane flew backwards), a hardcoded `←` where the app has direction-aware `NavArrow` (an SVG for optical centring, mirrored by `[dir='ltr'] .nav-arrow-forward`), filled icons (`caret`/`flight`/`star`) drawn as **outlines** because `Icon.tsx`'s FILLED set was not honoured, and paraphrased copy (`הבא` for the shipped `הבא בתור`, a sentence where the app says `פנוי` / `זמן חופשי`). Cheapest useful version: emit `ICONS`/`COPY` blocks from `ui/Icon.tsx` and `i18n/he.ts` under the same manifest-and-generated-block contract, so `git diff` shows drift the same way it does for CSS. Not urgent, and worth doing before the next motion mockup rather than after.
- **Eight mockups have no `<meta charset>`** (`dark-mode-v1` fixed 2026-08-02; `loading-states-v1`, `logo-v1`, `map-reach-v1`, `mode-switch-transition-v1`, `motion-join-v1`, `motion-primitives-v1`, `motion-trip-birth-v1` remain) — downloaded and opened from `file://`, Chrome guesses the encoding and the Hebrew renders as mojibake. One line each.
- **U-10 shared feedback family** (Med) — empty/loading/error/offline were ~6 bespoke shells; no skeletons; retry-less snapshot dead-end; full-screen trip-switch flash. _Landed:_ the feedback family (ADR-0078: `EmptyState`/`ErrorState`/`LoadingState`+`Skeleton`/`StatusBanner`) + the `AppShell`/layout primitives; **and** (Wave 3 D-route) the snapshot loading/error migrated onto a chrome-preserving `LoadingState` + `ErrorState` with a working retry (no more full-screen `<h1>` flash / dead-end). _Remaining (opportunistic / Phase-4):_ fold the ~6 bespoke empty shells (`board-off`, `glance-day.empty`, index/plan empties) onto `EmptyState` and the stacked `.offline-badge` onto `StatusBanner` as each screen is next touched.
- ~~**Loading states as one language, light boot + content-shaped skeleton (ADR-0105)**~~ — **shipped**: the bare `shell.booting` word replaced by `ui/feedback/BootScreen` (cool-paper `--screen` day ground, ink clock + a small amber halo with a warm-up glow ramp — not the reserved live pulse — and a sweeping departure-line) across every boot site (auth check, route-chunk `Suspense`, trips-list load); the snapshot `LoadingState` upgraded from anonymous shimmer to `HomeSkeleton` (content-shaped, per mode: Trip dark-board / Plan violet-prep + checklist) behind a mode-themed `ChromeSkeleton` header bar. _Remaining (gated on their own prerequisites):_ the **dark** boot — designed in the ADR, rides the `data-theme='dark'` toggle (U-08), inert until then; the pending **upload row**'s determinate `NN%` + mini-bar. **ADR-0056's outbox upload has since shipped, so the blocker this named is gone** — but the real one was never that: `uploadDocument` posts through `apiFetch`, and `fetch` exposes no upload-progress event, so a determinate bar needs the transport changed (XHR, or a streaming request body) and not just wiring. The row stays the indeterminate `מעלה…` spinner until someone decides that transport is worth it.
- ~~**U-02 / U-05 one editing grammar**~~ (Med) — **shipped** (Wave 2 E): one variant-driven `ConfirmDialog` (tone `neutral`/`danger`/`hard`, on `Modal variant="dialog"`) replaces all three confirm impls (hard-edit gate, booking `DeletePrompt`, TripSettings `Confirm`); `FormActions` (canonical order: primary then cancel, destructive on its own row) + `Field` + `DateTimeField` (no native `datetime-local`) + `useUnsavedGuard` wired into both forms; the superseded `.confirm-*`/`.event-form-*`/`.bs-modal-overlay` CSS deleted. _Follow-up (ADR-0083):_ `DateTimeField` was later superseded by the `WhenField` date/time standard (`day` + multi-day `span` variants) after its cramped `datetime` mode clipped the date on spans; `DateTimeField` removed.
- ~~**Trip date-range formatting drift**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-24):** trip settings rendered the raw `YYYY-MM-DD` start/end (a format used nowhere else), and four surfaces each formatted the same fields differently (`AllTrips` Intl numeric, `PlanHome` prose, `JoinTrip` raw string-slice, settings raw ISO). One `formatTripDates(start, end, {style, withYear})` in `lib/time.ts` (numeric `DD.MM–DD.MM` for meta rows/tickets/records, prose Hebrew day + long month for hero surfaces; dates read in UTC) now backs all four; settings reads `20.07–29.07 · 2026`. Separately, `DOT_SEPARATOR` was a bullet (`•`) against design-language.md's documented middle dot (`·`) — realigned to `·`, with the boot loader keeping its own decorative bullet. _Optional hygiene left:_ routing the remaining literal `·` sites (`BookingDetail`/`EventCard`/`ZonePicker`/`WhenField`/`BookingSheet`) through the constant — cosmetic, they already render `·`.
- **WhenField unified-duration span** (Low, refinement of ADR-0083) — the span variant's two endpoints could later collapse into a single control where the end is entered as a multi-day **duration** ("2 days 3h") with the end-date derived, matching the single-day TimePicker's start+duration feel. Deferred alternative from ADR-0083; layers onto the same `WhenField` span value contract without touching call sites.
- ~~**Elapsed-time duration ladder (ADR-0114)**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-24):** the duration read-out rounded on a calendar-date crossing, so a 23:00→00:00 booking read "יומיים". One `formatDuration(minutes, unit)` in `lib/duration.ts` now rounds by **elapsed time** through a shared ladder (minutes → hours → days → weeks → months → years, boundaries as named constants; `weekCount`/`yearCount` added to `hebrew.ts`); ADR-0084's per-kind **nights** (lodging) / **hours** (transport) overrides stand; a date-only multi-day span keeps an inclusive calendar-day count. Collapsed the three drifted hour/minute copies (`booking-timing`, `WhenField`, `TimePicker`) onto the one helper. The relative-day/countdown surfaces (ADR-0085) stay a separate concern. FE + docs only, no schema change.
- ~~**U-03 domain components**~~ (Med) — **shipped** (Wave 3): the `ui/domain/` layer (D0 — `ListRow`+`RowManageSheet`, `MaybeCard`, `Board`, `EventCard`, `DayStrip`, `GlanceCard`, `StatTile`, all presentational + unit-tested) **and** the screen migrations onto it: Home → `Board`/`GlanceCard`/`StatTile` (D-home), DayView/PlanDay → `EventCard`/`MaybeCard` (D-day), Index/DocumentsSection + both manage menus → `ListRow`/`RowManageSheet` (D-index), App.tsx header → `DayStrip` (D-route). The inline copies + superseded `screens.css` rules were deleted in each migration (screens shed ~2.6k lines net). The shared `.row-action`/`.kebab` rules stay (still used by TripSettings).
- **Captured-scan auto-crop / enhance** (Low, deferred from ADR-0086) — the upload `FilePicker` now has a first-class camera-capture path; a document-scanner pass (edge detection + perspective correction + contrast) on a captured passport photo is a larger surface left for later. The pick control ships without it.
