- ~~**U-09 group change-feed**~~ (Med) — **shipped** (Wave 4 C, ADR-0081): a bounded (last 20) WS-fed recent-changes buffer (`state/change-feed.tsx`) narrating attributed peer edits off the same `change` stream in `applyRemoteChange` (narrate, not re-apply), plus a quiet dismissable `ui/domain/ChangeFeed` strip on the Trip-mode Home below the board. Own edits filtered out; polite live region; auto-collapses when empty; attribution via F-05's `actorUserId` + roster.
- ~~**U-06 Map / location gap**~~ (Med, product) — **the list half shipped (2026-07-24, session 80):** the Map tab is no longer a dead placeholder — it renders the trip's places as a day-scoped, filterable list with per-place navigate/view deep-links (Phase 3). The live-location half (**navigate-to-next**, **near-me**) shipped as Phase 4, and the **rendered spatial map** as Phase 6 (session 133) — the gap is closed; only the epic's paid-Routes follow-up remains above.
- ~~**U-07 / U-11 / U-12 / U-13 quick wins**~~ (Low) — **all shipped**: U-11 (`settings` glyph in `Icon`, `⚙` control swapped) + U-12 (`Spinner` aria-label → `t.common.loading`) in Wave 1; U-07 (the `maybeMeta`/`fixtures` import removed from DayView/PlanDay, real derived `MaybeCard` meta) in Wave 3 D-day; U-13 (create-trip CTA always visible, disabled-with-reason via `t.shell.newTrip.ctaReason`) in Wave 3.

- ~~**The in-trip top bar is two rows**~~ — **DONE (2026-08-03, session 199; [ADR-0149](decisions/0149-the-top-bar-is-two-rows.md), session note [`planning/2026-08-03-session-199-the-top-bar-is-built.md`](planning/2026-08-03-session-199-the-top-bar-is-built.md)).** The header stacked five rows at 250px resting / 321px off-today and now answers two questions in two rows at **160px in every state, 108px condensed** — measured in a real browser at 390×844, matching the design's numbers on every axis (strip 184 against 182, mode 88, anchor 54, an 18-character name at 17px uncut). **Nothing in the chrome reflows:** going offline moves the header 116 → 116, because the ribbon became a fixed-width anchor slot with two cross-faded states and offline/pending became a badge positioned on the trip glyph, with the `aria-live` region and its strings untouched. Shipped with it: the `swap` mark, absent at one trip, on a chip that still navigates straight to `/trips` (a **deck cue** shipped beside it and was withdrawn days later on the device pass — it read as a box overlapping the glyph, ADR-0149 §2's amendment); the merged people stack (four circles, three at ≤370px) opening the roster with your own row leading to your account; `failed` as a `--miss` control in row 1; the 44×44 pill and the inline month divider; and `CHROME_CONDENSED`, the third `AppShell` modifier, which the rendered Map declares because an `is-fullbleed` body never scrolls. **Two things the build settled that the design left open:** the slack test's number is derived rather than picked (52px freed × 2 + 12 = 116px of slack before it may condense — first derived as 64 and doubled on the 2026-08-04 device pass, when the collapse became scroll-linked and a closed chrome needed a whole `FREES` of scroll left underneath it), and the failed-sync control does not survive the condense — amended into the ADR as a stated cost, with the badge following identity down so the state is never silent. **Still open, and it is the ADR's own question:** the resting day window is **3**, and whether that is enough in the hand is a device pass, not a measurement. If it is not, the refused alternative is drawn in the same mockup under ⟨הסרגל מתפרק⟩ (Home 44 · Day 102 · Index 44 · **Map 0**) — read it before redesigning from scratch.

- ~~**The maybe shelf when it crowds**~~ — **BUILT (2026-08-05, session 203; session note [`planning/2026-08-05-session-203-the-shelf-crowds-built.md`](planning/2026-08-05-session-203-the-shelf-crowds-built.md)).** All six steps of [ADR-0116](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md)'s session-202 amendment plus [ADR-0151](decisions/0151-a-suggestion-has-a-source-and-a-reason.md)'s contract: the `orderBy`, the compact tile (140x76 against 150x132, **0.88x** a collapsed event row where the card was 1.53x, verified against the mockup's own panel in a browser), the `Suggestion` shape + `SUGGESTION_STRATEGIES` registry with `near-the-day` as the one `LOCAL` strategy, both shelves ranked through `suggestFor`, the gap sheet ranked against its own slot and capped and searchable with `.gapfill-m` finally rendering, and the capped strip's tail handing off to the Map's `אולי` facet. `haversineMeters` moved to `@waypoint/shared` and the search field became a `SearchField` primitive rather than a second copy of either (rule 8). **What remains is a device pass, not code (ADR-0017):** whether §5's tab switch reads as "the rest are over there" or as being thrown off the surface — the owner's call was to build it as drawn and judge it on a phone — plus whether the two-line title truncates too much on real titles, and whether the capped strip reads as "the five best" or as "something is missing". **Still deliberately unbuilt:** ADR-0151 §4's endpoint, which is reserved and waits for the first `REMOTE` strategy.

- **Notes become one entity, and the Index gets its third tile** — **DESIGNED (2026-08-01, session 204; [ADR-0152](decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md), session note [`planning/2026-08-01-session-204-notes-become-one-entity.md`](planning/2026-08-01-session-204-notes-become-one-entity.md)); a MOCKUP comes before the build.** One `Note` entity where **what it is about is a field** — no host = a general note, a typed nullable host FK = that entity's note, and one row/editor/list/sync channel either way. Ships: the model + shared schema, the sync channels (snapshot, memory, `CACHE_CHANNELS`, outbox) **plus §2's applier rule — a DB cascade writes no `Change` rows, so a host's delete drops its notes through the ADR-0094 registry, not through Postgres**; authoring + reading on all five hosts as a **mark on the row** with the body in the detail surface / row menu (ADR-0138, ADR-0149's density budget); the third `IndexTile` on ADR-0098's landing with its chip row + search + `RevealList` from day one; and the one-time `Booking.details.notes` → rows migration (**`details.wifi` stays put** — one reader, ADR-0047 §6). Copy is `פתקים`/`פתק`, which renames the booking form's `הערות`; `מחקר` is not spent, discharging ADR-0098's naming debt. **Reserved, not built (§8/§9):** no strategy is registered, nothing calls out, nothing bills — an external tip is a **card that owns no row until a human keeps it**, and `NOTE_STRATEGIES` is a sibling of ADR-0151's registry (a `ref` points, a note carries content) sharing its placement split, structured reasons and rank-interleave. **Deferred with reasons recorded:** custom categories (the app's first open taxonomy — no icon, no exhaustiveness, no LWW merge rule, no i18n; `EventCategory` is the category and a link is a `url` field, which is what "social media" actually was), private notes (`Document.ownerUserId?` is the precedent, but the visibility filter reaches every read path and the offline cache), note-to-many hosting, rich text. **DESIGNED (2026-08-01, session 205; [ADR-0153](decisions/0153-the-notes-surface-the-mark-and-no-mode-gate.md), session note [`planning/2026-08-01-session-205-the-notes-design-session.md`](planning/2026-08-01-session-205-the-notes-design-session.md), mockups [`notes-screen-v1`](../mockups/notes-screen-v1.html) + [`notes-on-a-host-v1`](../mockups/notes-on-a-host-v1.html)) — §A, §B and §D of the brief are CLOSED; §C (Home + Hero 2.0) is a stated hand-off.** The screen is flat and ungrouped, the mark is one `clipboard` silhouette in the host's meta line, and notes are **mode-neutral with no Plan gate anywhere**. Notes are written **on the way** in the host's own form (ADR-0152 §6b): one input, committed notes as one-line chips, tap a chip to keep editing it, and whatever is still in the box at save becomes a note — so one note costs no extra press. Category and glyph are **inherited and resolved, never copied**. `EventCard` carries three changes of its own (ADR-0152 §6c), two of which touch rows with **no notes at all** — worth its own commit and tests. **The original scoping brief:** (2026-08-01, owner's request): [`planning/2026-08-01-notes-design-brief.md`](planning/2026-08-01-notes-design-brief.md) — it splits the work into **A** the new surface, **B** five hosts × (create, read) which is the part that gets underestimated, **C** Home + the **Hero 2.0 dependency** (a note on the now/next event is a candidate answer to that brief's sharpest open question, so notes puts **no mark on the Board** and hands its reach to that session), and **D** the mode boundary — proposed as **mode-neutral, no Plan gate anywhere**, per ADR-0025's blast-radius framework, with the paid-strategy exception refused on the precedent that ADR-0115 §6's "Plan mode only" was already **withdrawn** in favour of arming by cost. Two mockups (`notes-screen-v1`, `notes-on-a-host-v1`), then one ADR over A+B+D. **Read first:** ADR-0152, then ADR-0098 §2/§5 (the landing and its `useOverlay` sub-view shape) and ADR-0151 §5/§6/§7 (absent-offline, never-persist-until-picked, armed-by-intent — now keyed to the `cost` axis 0152 §8 amended into its §2). **PHASES 1-5 BUILT (2026-08-01, session 206). Phase 1: the entity and its sync channels — schema + migration, the shared zod schemas and `ENTITY_TYPE.NOTE`/`NOTE_SOURCE`/`NOTE_HOST_FIELD`, the Nest module through `ChangeService.mutate`, and the whole client plumbing (snapshot, `CACHE_CHANNELS`, memory channel, three outbox verbs, note verbs, the host-cascade applier rule, the change-feed subject). Phase 2: the flat notes screen, the third Index tile and the editor (general notes end-to-end). Phase 3: `EventCard`'s three changes — the `nowrap` meta line, the elementised text and the drop rule — which alter rows with no notes at all. Phase 4: the booking end-to-end — the mark on its row, the note section in `BookingDetail`, the composer replacing `BookingSheet`'s `הערות` field, and the one-time `details.notes` → rows migration (`details.wifi` stays). Phase 5: documents + ideas — the mark on the document row and on the idea tile's corner, the composer on the upload form, the section in `DocumentManageSheet`, and **the one shipped-gesture change in this build**: a tap on an idea opens a `RowManageSheet` with `שיבוץ ליום` first instead of going straight to the schedule form ([ADR-0116 §5a](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md), amended in place with ADR-0153 §8). Its section + editor pair was extracted to one connected `HostNotes` rather than copied a third time, so **phase 6's place is a one-liner**; an upload's notes are queued with `{ queue: true }` because ADR-0056 makes the upload itself outbox-first, and a note POSTed now would overtake it and be refused. Phase 5b: the EVENT, which no phase had owned — phase 3 shipped its mark and nothing shipped its body, so the app counted notes on an event it gave you no way to write or read. Its section first rode `EventCard`'s `⋯` sheet rather than the expanded card, because `.wp-event-actions` animated to a FIXED 220px and would have clipped it — **retracted the next day, see phase 5d**; the composer is in `EventForm`, and `verbs.create`/`schedule`/`book` now resolve to their host so the notes can be queued behind it — on the booked path they land on the BOOKING, because that event is materialized by the server from a seed (ADR-0093) and has no client id. Phase 5c: **a conversion carries its notes** — scheduling an idea, parking an event and booking an idea all lost them, and parking DESTROYED them (the event's delete cascades). `updateNoteSchema` gains the five host FKs, where absent means UNTOUCHED (the one non-whole-content part of that payload); the move is queued after the new host exists and before the old one is deleted, and both undo paths mirror it ([ADR-0152 §5](decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) amended: the host is immutable to a user, transferable by a conversion). Phase 5d (2026-08-02, three owner reports, all of them the same mistake — **a reader was being sent into an author's surface, and content was living inside menus**): an event's notes move from the `⋯` sheet **into the card the row expands**, and the cap that had kept them out is gone — `.wp-event-actions` animates `grid-template-rows: 0fr → 1fr`, so the strip's height is its content's and nothing can be clipped at any count ([ADR-0152 §6](decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md)'s 5b amendment RETRACTED in place; [ADR-0140 §8](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) added for the cost: every card's strip now travels its own height); `EventForm`'s **edit** half gains the existing-notes list above its one box, which is ADR-0152 §6b's own last paragraph and matters because **in Plan mode that form is the event's only detail surface**, so a whole mode had no access to the feature; and a note row's tap opens a **read** preview (`NoteDetail`, `BookingDetail`'s grammar, delete left on the `⋯`) instead of the editor, on the notes screen **and** in every host's section, since a section line clamps exactly as a row does ([ADR-0153 §4](decisions/0153-the-notes-surface-the-mark-and-no-mode-gate.md) amended; mockup [`note-read-surfaces-v1`](../mockups/note-read-surfaces-v1.html)). Phase 6 (2026-08-02, session 207): **places on the Map, and the notes feature is complete.** The mark is one more item in `.map-m` rendered LAST (so the thing that wraps first is the mark, never a semantic tag — which is why this row needed none of `EventCard`'s three changes: `.map-m` has been a wrapping line of discrete elements since it shipped, where `.wp-event-m` is a joined string); the section rides `renderRow` gated on `selected`, so it reaches the place card at the `map` stop and the list at `full` from one implementation; the composer is `.map-draft-scroll`'s second child, the only one with no hint (a place has no category to inherit). Two consequences: the selection card is now BOUNDED ([ADR-0148 §1](decisions/0148-the-place-form-has-the-room-it-needs.md) amended: `:has(> .map-draft)` → `, :has(.note-sec)`) with **only the note list scrolling** and the header, the way-in block and `שיבוץ ליום` all pinned — `.place` becomes a grid in that one variant, because a wrapping flex row can only distribute space between lines; and `.map-refs-foot` is now the ROW's child, which also fixes a shipped omission (a selected place with no references used to get no schedule action at all). The owner's one open question is answered by taking the mockup's recommendation: the mark costs one extra meta line at 360px (0 at 390) and that is accepted, since a drop rule would hide the mark exactly on the rows most likely to carry notes ([ADR-0153 §8](decisions/0153-the-notes-surface-the-mark-and-no-mode-gate.md) amended). Measured at 360×640 with three notes against the built CSS: the card lands on its cap exactly, the list is the only scroller, head and foot inside — **the place card cannot be reached in the hermetic e2e** (no Maps key → no pane → no card), so the plan's e2e line for it was answered by that harness instead, and is recorded here rather than quietly dropped. **The cascade safety pass (2026-08-02, session 209) — the plan's decision 7 and its G5, both halves in one branch** ([ADR-0152 §2](decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) amended in place): a host's delete confirm now NAMES the notes it destroys — one gender-free sentence on a new `consequence` slot on ADR-0079's one `ConfirmDialog`, which is also what finally folded the document's hand-rolled delete prompt, the last of that family — and an undone host delete RESTORES them, which it did not, because `verbs.ts` re-created the host with the same id and the cascaded rows were gone. The delete captures them into the undo descriptor (afterwards there is nowhere left to read them from) and the undo re-creates them under their own ids, after the host, through the ordinary note verb and so through the ADR-0094 registry. **It is three confirms and not five:** a soft event and an idea have no confirm and need none now that undo restores, and a place has no delete path in the app at all.** **PLANNED (2026-08-01, session 206): [`planning/2026-08-01-session-206-the-notes-build-plan.md`](planning/2026-08-01-session-206-the-notes-build-plan.md)** — six phases, one PR each, with the test list per phase and the gaps the ADRs do not answer. Three things it found that block or reshape the build: a **`Place` has no row menu and no detail surface**, so of §8's four entrances it appeared to have none — **owner's call (session 206): a place carries notes in v1**, resolved as three modest changes rather than a sub-project (the mark is a seventh `.map-tag` in a line already built to wrap them; the body rides the shared `renderRow` gated on `selected`, so it reaches the place card at the `map` stop and the list at `full` from one implementation; the composer is `.map-draft-scroll`'s second child, the region ADR-0148 §1 built for exactly that) — behind one small row-level mockup, `notes-on-a-place-v1.html`, which blocks only its own phase; the **change feed narrates every note create AND edit** the day the entity ships, which the brief's §C deferred but the code does not; the **resolved-not-copied category** means every note render needs its host resolved across five types, which is net-new derivation the reuse audit does not account for. Also: ADR-0153's `22 hosts / 1.4 rows per header` is **28 / 1.1** when the fixture is driven (the flat decision holds a fortiori), §7's `✕` collision note is wrong in both corner and tense (the remove variant ships today, at the opposite corner), and every webfont-dependent pixel number in both ADRs needs re-measuring on a device. A new syncable entity costs **eighteen** registration points, not three — two of them silent (the memory channel, the change-feed subject).

- **A task row's tick and `⋯` float on a tall row** (measured 2026-08-16 while rebasing the notes fix onto [ADR-0191 §8](decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md), not fixed) — giving a task on the tasks SCREEN a two-line meta (deadline, then host chip and assignee) makes its row **95px at 360**, and `ListRow`'s `align-items: center` then puts its tick and its `⋯` **26.5px below the title line they act on**. Identical to the defect `.note-row` fixes for notes (ADR-0153 §4's 2026-08-16 amendment), and the repair is the same three declarations — but it was left alone deliberately: that surface shipped with its own device pass, and whether a **tick** should top-align the way a **badge** does is a question about a control, not a mechanical repeat. If it is taken, the honest move is to lift the treatment out of `notes.css` into `list-row.css` behind one opt-in class rather than add `.tsk-row`'s copy beside `.note-row`'s (rule 8).

- **A long note's row, and its lost line breaks** (owner, 2026-08-16, two reports) — **BUILT** ([ADR-0153 §4](decisions/0153-the-notes-surface-the-mark-and-no-mode-gate.md) and [ADR-0152 §6](decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) amended in place, mockup [`notes-long-note-row-v1`](../mockups/notes-long-note-row-v1.html), session note [`planning/2026-08-16-the-long-note-row-built.md`](planning/2026-08-16-the-long-note-row-built.md)). "It looks really bad with long notes" and "the line breaks don't show" were **one** defect: a body renders in four places and only `.wp-listrow-meta` — where §4 sent a **titled** note's body — is a shared `ListRow` class with neither a clamp nor a `white-space`. Shipped: the body moves onto `.note-body-line`, the element that already clamps, already honours the newlines and already unclamps when the row opens (285→98.4px per row, 679→493px for the list, **0px** on a body-only note); `.note-row` tops-aligns the badge and the `⋯`; and `NoteSection` now shows a titled note's body under its title, which no read surface displayed at all. **Left for a device pass:** the preview clamp at 2 vs 3 lines — honouring the breaks means a wrapping first line can consume the whole preview. Shipped at 2 (what every body-only note on the screen uses); the mockup carries the control.

- **The note preview, round two** — **BUILT (2026-08-02, session 208; [ADR-0153 §4/§8](decisions/0153-the-notes-surface-the-mark-and-no-mode-gate.md) amended in place, mockup [`note-preview-v2`](../mockups/note-preview-v2.html)).** Three owner reports against the preview that shipped that morning, plus a fourth question that came after and turned out to precede them: _"I'm not 100% sure that notes should open from the bottom like other previews, wdyt?"_ — and the answer was no. **THE SHEET IS GONE.** `BookingDetail`'s grammar is an object with facts and verbs; a note is a sentence, so the ceremony WAS the "too compact for a short note" report. A note now opens where it is: on the notes screen the row's two-line clamp lifts and one foot line appears under it (measured at 390px: **+37px** short, **+89px** long, against the sheet's 151/199 which also hid the list); in a host's section a line never clamped, so opening adds only the foot — which corrects a claim the previous amendment had made about section lines clamping. The foot is one line, because a note has two things to say about itself and the author and time are already on the row: **the host's name as the way in**, and `עריכה`. Delete stays on the `⋯`. **The host way-in is the fifth entrance and points the other way** (§8 amended): five kinds, five destinations, and the owner's call was to **wire all five before the UI shipped** — place (the Map's focus channel, shipped), booking (`?booking=`, shipped), and three new copies of that same shape for document (`?doc=`), event and idea (`?day=` + `?event=`/`?idea=`), with the decision of where each kind lives in one pure table (`lib/note-host-target.ts`) and `canReach`/`goTo` returned together so a caret and a tap cannot disagree. Absent — never dead — for a general note, a someday idea (the pool has no tile) and a place with no Map scope. **(1) The link report is DONE** (`lib/external-url.ts`): a url stored without a scheme is a RELATIVE href, so the tap re-entered the app — the whole window in the installed PWA. Fixed at RENDER, which also fixes the rows already in the database; the displayed text stays what was typed, and only http/https/mailto/tel pass, so `javascript:` in a group-visible free-text field renders as text instead of as a link. **Two duplications the render caught and paper had not:** the expansion first printed the body under the row (the row's own words twice on a short note — it became the clamp lifting instead), and the foot repeated the row's host chip (the chip now hides while open). **And one report against the build itself:** the open row's 2.5% wash was refused on sight, and the seam beside it turned out to be `ListRow`'s shipped hover painting only the tappable half — **fixed app-wide** on the owner's instruction (`list-row.css` now paints the whole row via `:has(.wp-listrow-open:hover:not(:disabled))`), so bookings, documents and members stop reading as two tones too.

- **A note gets a full screen, Markdown, and detected links** (owner ask, 2026-08-22) — **BUILT (2026-08-22, designed and shipped the same day; [ADR-0202](decisions/0202-a-note-gets-a-full-screen-and-markdown-is-a-subset.md) with five in-place amendments the build forced, mockup [`note-full-screen-v1`](../mockups/note-full-screen-v1.html), session note [`planning/2026-08-22-a-note-gets-a-full-screen.md`](planning/2026-08-22-a-note-gets-a-full-screen.md)).** A **third container** above the in-place expansion, which is NOT taken back (ADR-0153 §4's measured +37/+89px against a sheet's 151/199px). Ships, when built: `Modal variant="full"` reachable from all five hosts and the notes screen (bar 56px, matching `.search-overlay-bar`); the way in as one more `.row-open-act` in the shared `RowOpenFoot` wearing `Icon name="frame"` at **0px**, plus `.row-open-lead`'s truncation, without which a third control wraps the line under a real host name (51→37px, and the wrap point is webfont-dependent — ADR-0153 §6c); `lib/note-markdown.ts` (~147 lines, 1.4KB gzipped) returning **nodes** rather than an HTML string, so the one group-visible free-text field has no injection surface; unconditional link detection through the shipped `externalHref`/`prettyUrl`; and `.note-prose` at two densities across three surfaces, split on two facts already in the CSS — _does it clamp_ and _is the body a `<button>`_. **Two shipped defects fixed along the way, both found by drawing and building rather than by a report:** `externalHref` allowed `mailto:` and never supplied it, so a typed email became `https://tokyo-stay@example.com/` — the wrong host, the address as HTTP userinfo, and `prettyUrl` labelling it `example.com`; and `NoteOpenFoot` printed `פתק כללי` under every hosted note on its own host's surface, because "no host" and "you are standing on its host" were one absence. **What the build changed about the design** (all amended in place): no `⋯` and no host chip on the screen, `#`/`##` sharing one size step, `תצוגה מלאה` instead of an invented `מסך מלא`, and the host section's newlines moving from `pre-wrap` to a `<br />` per line. **Four reports from the owner's first look at it on a phone, all fixed the same day (ADR-0202 §9):** `dir="auto"` on the prose laid a **Hebrew note out left to right** (it resolves from the first strong character, and the note opened `TL;DR` — 26 Hebrew letters against 14 Latin; omitting `dir` entirely would have been better than the attribute added to be careful, since the notes screen's row carries none and has always read correctly) → `lib/bidi.ts` gains `baseDirection`, which counts letters instead of reading the first one; the full screen read too small → the ramp gains `--text-reading: 16px`, spent there alone; and the way in was only reachable _through_ the thing it relieves (expand a long note, scroll past all of it, then the control) → **a hold opens the full screen** from either surface, collapsed or open, with the foot's control staying as the discoverable twin (ADR-0157 §2's condition). Two silent traps the gesture's tests caught: `NaN > slop` is `false`, so a pointer event without coordinates disables the scroll guard entirely; and jsdom implements no `PointerEvent`, so the guard can only be exercised with a real `MouseEvent`. **A second round on the phone found that TWO of those fixes had never applied** (ADR-0202 §10, same day): `.note-prose` declared `--np-base` on itself and **a property on an element shadows the inherited one**, so the reading size never arrived; and the block gaps live at specificity (0,1,0) while the per-element `margin: 0` resets are (0,1,1), so **every block gap in this file had been dead since it shipped** — which is what "clumped up" actually was, and re-tuning the numbers would have changed nothing again. Both are now guarded structurally in `notes.contract.test.ts` (the reset must sit on `> *`, no `.note-prose <tag>` rule may declare a margin, the default must live in the `var()` fallback), and the margin guard was verified by re-injecting the original offender and watching it fail by name. **The pattern is the thing to keep: three fixes in a row typechecked, passed 4,200+ tests and changed no pixel, because jsdom has no cascade, resolves no `var()` and computes no specificity — a green suite says nothing about CSS or bidi.** Also landed: a note past a threshold no longer expands at all, the tap opens the screen (`noteReadsFullScreen`, estimating rendered LINES rather than characters — twelve short lines is twelve lines tall and 150 characters, so a character threshold let exactly the wall through). **Still owed:** whether a host's section should now clamp a long note too (it renders one in full — the wall in a different room), a device pass on the two threshold numbers (they pick which of two containers a tap opens and the boundary is invisible), and the rest of it on a phone ( — whether three controls read as three under a thumb, whether `--text-body` is right for a long note, whether a truncated host name reads as truncated), and the owner's call on `העתקה כ-Markdown`, which was NOT built and has also lost the menu it was drawn in.

- **Two mockup-harness rules the notes session found, one of them affecting an existing file** (2026-08-22, written into [`.claude/skills/design-mockups/references/pitfalls.md`](../.claude/skills/design-mockups/references/pitfalls.md)) — `tokens.css` has declared `html, body { overflow: clip }` since 2026-08-21 (ADR-0200 §1, the app-shell scrolls inside `.body`), and **every mockup inlines that sheet**: a page of sections collapses to one viewport, `window.scrollTo` does nothing, and a full-page screenshot returns §1 plus blank rectangles — with no console error and the measurement table still filling in, so it reads as a page that renders. `note-full-screen-v1.html` carries the labelled two-line undo; **`a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v1.html` (2026-08-22) inlines the rule and does not**, so a re-read of that file today is truncated — left alone rather than retrofitted, since a mockup is a dated record and this is the owner's call, but worth knowing before anyone reads it. The second rule is that `document.fonts.ready` resolves before a lazily-fetched `@font-face` exists, so a measurement table built in that tick reports fallback metrics — it is what made this session's own table report a truncation fix as costing nothing (⁦38px⁩ vs ⁦38px⁩, against ⁦51px⁩ vs ⁦37px⁩ once Assistant is applied); the fix is `document.fonts.load()` per weight before measuring.

- **The now-line says where we actually are, including INSIDE an event** (owner, 2026-08-02) — today the marker can only land **between** rows: `nowLinePlacement` (`lib/now-line.ts`) answers an index, so an event you are currently in gets the line floating above it rather than through it. On the ground that is the common case — you are on the flight, at the dinner, in the layover — and the honest marker would say so. The groundwork is laid: the placement is **one derivation with two hosts** (Trip's live line and Plan's static reference, which used to compute it inline in two spellings) and it returns an **object**, so the day it grows `inside` (which entry the moment falls within, and how far through) every caller keeps working. The journey block ([ADR-0159](decisions/0159-the-day-says-what-is-between-two-events.md)) is the first place the line renders inside a container and is a hint of the shape, not the shape. Open when built: what it draws inside a row (a filled portion? a hairline across the card?), whether a multi-hour flight wants a progress read-out at all, and what Plan mode's static reference does with the same answer.

- ~~**`יש הזמנה` re-guesses itself on every edit, and `transport` should not open it**~~ (owner, 2026-08-03) — **BUILT (2026-08-03, session 187; [ADR-0136 §2](decisions/0136-an-event-can-also-be-booked.md) amended in place, twice over).** The row's initial value read the category and never the **event**, so an existing unlinked event had `bookingId == null` re-guessed on every open — re-arming the row, swapping its location field for a route field, and then taking the booked branch on the next save of any field, which is §3's **one-way** conversion performed by a default nobody touched and that `dirty` never counted. `kind` and `icon` had refused exactly that since §4; `booked` now carries the same `Boolean(event)` guard. And `CATEGORY_DEFAULT_BOOKED.transport` is `false`: §2 argued from _a flight_ and applied it to the category, which [ADR-0156](decisions/0156-a-bus-is-transport-the-third-mode-the-picker-always-meant.md) turned into a lie the moment `transit` made the bus, the ferry and the drive real members of it. **Deriving the default from the glyph was rejected by the owner** — _"glyphs should always be decorative and not dictate any behavior"_ — and so was hoisting the mode pills out of the collapsible, which would charge the quick journey +54px and a question to fix a default. Both recorded in the amendment. **One regression came back the next morning and is FIXED (2026-08-03, session 188; the amendment's own follow-up note):** the `Boolean(event)` guard landed while `dirty` was still reading `booked.touched` as "the human turned the row on", so every edit of every event opened dirty and leaving one without typing cost a discard confirm — the fix for symptom 3 inverted into a nag, worst in Plan mode where a tap on a row _is_ this form. The check diffs the value against its seed now, and `useDerivedField`'s own header says what `touched` is for, since it is the hook's contract that changed under its readers.

- **Day scheduling is hard to use, and a drag rewrites a length nobody touched** (owner, 2026-08-04, four reports read together) — **DESIGNED ([ADR-0161](decisions/0161-a-move-names-a-position-and-an-event-owns-its-length.md) + [`mockups/day-scheduling-grammar-v1.html`](../mockups/day-scheduling-grammar-v1.html)); phase 1 BUILT 2026-08-04 (§1/§2/§3/§8 — every drag-driven path) and phase 2a BUILT 2026-08-04 (§4/§5/§7 — the picker, the row's time as its way in, per-category lengths, `הזז` out of the `⋯` sheet). phase 2b BUILT 2026-08-04 (§4's remaining call sites and §5's readers — `שבץ` no longer offers "after everything" in either mode: Plan asks with the picker, Trip defaults to the first position with room, and a create's length comes from its category capped by that room). phase 3 BUILT 2026-08-04 (§6 — `החלף` opens the slot's own chooser instead of skipping the event and telling you to go looking: `GapFillSheet` generalised into `ui/domain/SlotFillSheet` with two headers, the displaced event parked on the shelf and the replacement inheriting its exact start and length, in one reducer action and one undo; the sheet's accent became mode-aware so no plan violet leaks into Trip mode). phase 4 BUILT 2026-08-04 (§9 — Trip mode's gap keeps its measurement and answers when tapped, amending [ADR-0159](decisions/0159-the-day-says-what-is-between-two-events.md) §1 in place; the join carries its whole `Gap` so the tap opens the slot Plan's chip offers, and the 44px target is an out-of-flow `::after` so the row costs the list none of its rhythm). **ADR-0161 is fully built.** One follow-up shipped the same day: `החלף` on an untimed row took the day view blank (no slot to take it on), fixed at both ends.** The root defect is one model: `lib/reorder.ts` permutes `{startsAt, endsAt, sortOrder}` **slots**, so dropping a 1-hour event on a 2-hour one trades their lengths — while a drop on a gap chip preserves duration by decision (`slotFor`), so the same gesture already means two things. §1 replaces the slot model with start-only writes, §2 adds a **seam** between every pair of rows (the gap chip generalised to zero minutes, drawn only mid-drag) so "put it here" stops requiring 60 free minutes, §4 makes the day its own time picker (one primitive replacing `ResolveSheet`'s before/after pair and `BuilderRow`'s `הזז` peer list) with per-category default lengths on ADR-0063's profile, §6 makes `החלף` the atomic decision it claims to be on the same component as the gap fill, §7 makes **the row's own time a button** (which is where `משך` and `הזז` go, so the `⋯` menu ends up **one row shorter** rather than growing to eight — the owner rejected that draft off the mockup, and it amends ADR-0138 §8's placement), §8 fixes the **drag clone**, which is opaque, 4% larger than the row it vacated and sitting under the finger, so it hides the target it is aiming at, and §9 amends [ADR-0159](decisions/0159-the-day-says-what-is-between-two-events.md) §1 so the Trip-mode gap keeps its measurement and gains one tap. **Left open by decision:** an untimed event still holds no position of its own; a **day-level "we're running late, push everything"** (real, but a day control rather than a row verb — the ripple already performs it, what is missing is asking for it without moving one event first); **`שכפל`**, dropped in review as inferred-not-reported and with no specified interaction — and if it returns, the cases behind it (breakfast on three mornings) are **recurrence**, a property stated once, not N copies to edit when the time changes; and a device pass on two feel calls the mockup carries as toggles rather than decisions — whether an 18px seam is findable under a thumb, and the drag clone's opacity and lift (ADR-0017).

- **Thirty researched places, and slotting them is the slow part** (owner, 2026-08-04: _"a common workflow will be adding lots and lots of places to the map, then people will want to slot these places … we want to make it super easy to choose from there"_). The premise checks out: every place added from the map outside an errand becomes a `MaybeItem` (`Map.tsx`'s `landPlace` → `verbs.addMaybe(title, { placeId })`), and `SHELF_POOL_CAP` is **5** — so at thirty places the shelf shows five and the rest live behind the Map's `אולי` facet. **Two of the five gaps are BUILT now**, as amendments to the ADRs that own the rules rather than as new decisions: an idea reaching its own pin (**BUILT 2026-08-04** — [ADR-0121 §8](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s badge was on every host except the one entity most likely to BE a place; `MaybeCard`'s glyph slot is a `PlaceBadge` now, a skipped event's shelf card got it too since that card is the only surface a parked event has, and the "costs the tile nothing" claim was **measured** at both widths in both modes rather than asserted — `e2e/idea-place-badge.spec.ts`) and a **`fits-a-day` suggestion** so a dateless idea names the day it belongs to (**BUILT 2026-08-04** — [ADR-0151](decisions/0151-a-suggestion-has-a-source-and-a-reason.md)'s second strategy, and the registry claim §2 made now tested: it is a registration plus `dedupeByRef`, since two strategies naming one idea would have rendered the pool twice. Registered FIRST so its sentence wins for the ideas it can speak about, which the owner chose from three options; accepting a proposal sets the day **and goes to it**, without which the feature punished agreement). **Three are left, and they are one design session, not three items:** (a) the Map has one posture and this workflow needs the other — a dateless shelf pin is deliberately `aside` ([ADR-0130 §3](decisions/0130-a-maybe-is-not-a-past-place.md) / [0121 §7](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md): no row in the sheet, no camera, quiet paint), which is right for reading a day and backwards for filling one, and the precedent for withdrawing the flag already exists in search ([ADR-0131 §4](decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md)); (b) everything is **one at a time** — `＋ שיבוץ ליום` slots one place, a drag slots one card, while the workflow is plural and spatial (_these four, they are near each other, Thursday afternoon_), so it needs the app's first multi-select; and (c) nothing helps with **order** — `haversineMeters` exists, and [ADR-0159 §13](decisions/0159-the-day-says-what-is-between-two-events.md)'s refusal was of a crow-flies **duration** claim, which is not the same claim as a sequence. The shape to design: pick a day, the unslotted ideas become the **subject** rather than the context, select several, drop them into the day's free time, the app proposes an order and times, you see it and confirm. **Not** auto-arranging — [ADR-0161 §10](decisions/0161-a-move-names-a-position-and-an-event-owns-its-length.md) refuses that and survives intact: you chose the things, you chose the hole, nothing already planned moves, and nothing commits before you look at it. Sequenced **after** ADR-0161's build (owner's call, 2026-08-04).

## Motion & the first run (mapped 2026-07-31, session 189)

**The pass is built.** Foundations in [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md), trip birth in [0142](decisions/0142-trip-birth-is-the-boards-first-departure.md), the invite pass in [0143](decisions/0143-the-invite-pass-arrives-and-gets-stamped.md), the anchored panels in [0144](decisions/0144-the-anchored-panels-arrive-and-leave.md), and Journey 4 as ADR-0140 §6. What remains:

- ~~**The birth sequence's e2e beat test**~~ — **unskipped (2026-07-31).** `bootIntoCreate` mocks the destination picker; both halves are our own relays (`/destinations/search`, `/destinations/resolve`), so the mock is exact and needs no key. Six specs, none skipped — and the spec caught a real stamp/name overlap on its first run.
- ~~**The removed-member refusal**~~ — **decided the OPPOSITE way and shipped (2026-07-31, [ADR-0143](decisions/0143-the-invite-pass-arrives-and-gets-stamped.md) §7).** The ADR had argued a block must not look like an expired link; the owner's correction is that declining to say which is the _point_ — naming it discloses a roster decision to someone who is no longer a member. A block now renders the same refused pass with the same words as an **invalid** code, which is both the commonest benign case and the one whose advice a blocked user can act on. `expired` stays distinct on purpose: it leaks nothing, and collapsing it would advise asking for a link that cannot exist.
- **Whether the invite countdown's count-up should run on a re-open** — charming once, possibly fussy every visit. Currently per mount.
- **The receding half of the route transition** — deferred with its cost stated in ADR-0140 §3: it needs the outgoing screen mounted, which for this app means two trip shells alive. Revisit if the shell stops being remounted by route changes.
- ~~**Every overlay exit in the app snaps**~~ — **fixed (2026-08-02, [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §8, which also amends [ADR-0144](decisions/0144-the-anchored-panels-arrive-and-leave.md)).** An exit written as the entrance's keyframes played `reverse` does not start a new animation — it retargets the running one, which keeps its current time, is already past the new `--t-quick` duration by the time anyone closes anything, and `fill: both` paints the reversed end state on frame one. All six rules now have their own `-out`/`panel-close` keyframes, verified in a real engine (a sheet after a 4.5s dwell travels y=0 → 200 over 140ms where it used to be at 200 on frame 0). Guarded by `frontend/src/styles/exit-animations.contract.test.ts` — a text test by necessity, since jsdom has no CSS engine and no rendering test in this repo can observe an exit at all. **What is NOT discharged:** the same two blind spots that hid it are still open — there is no test anywhere that watches an overlay actually animate, and the e2e suite would be the only place one could live.
- ~~**The document viewer's open and close**~~ — **built (2026-08-02).** The v2 sheet from [`document-viewer-motion-v2`](../mockups/document-viewer-motion-v2.html): four actors on four clocks (scrim leads, mount travels from the tapped row via `overlayOriginOffset`, chrome lands after the object it sits on, the document keeps its own clock), the frame reserved off the mime type before the bytes exist, and the mount opening around the page as a compositing opacity layer rather than an animated `background`. Measured in a real engine: scrim 0.26 against a card at 0.07 at 40ms, card opacity done by 90ms while the transform still travels, chrome from 150ms. **Two things it deliberately did NOT settle.** One is still open: whether the viewer folds onto `Modal` in ADR-0079's Wave 2 (it keeps its own portal for now — folding costs the `0.72`→`0.45` scrim, the dialog's 340px and padding, and `frontend/index.html`'s hardcoded `.doc-viewer` zoom-exception root). The other — **the letterbox colour**, where a warm `--paper` band beside a picture read as a stain — is **settled (2026-08-05) by deleting the band rather than by recolouring it** (owner: the viewer _"shouldn't hardcode the size of the box, it should fit the size of the image"_). The frame is still reserved before the bytes, but at the picture's own **ratio** instead of a `78vh` constant: a place photo is delivered with its `width`/`height` so the box is right on the first frame, a document settles on decode, and the placeholder until then is a portrait `3 / 4`. Measured in `e2e/media-viewer-fit.spec.ts` — the box is a rect, so jsdom can say nothing about it. **What would remove the document's settle** is dimensions on `DocumentSummary`, which is a schema change nobody has asked for; the transition covers it meanwhile.
- **`--ease-arrive` overshoots by 0.75% of progress, which is nothing.** Same mockup, §E. ADR-0140 added it as "the only overshooting easing — an object that must _settle_" and every entrance in the app now rides that promise; `cubic-bezier(.22, 1.16, .36, 1)` peaks at 1.0075, i.e. 0.04% over on a `.94 → 1` scale and 0.3px across a 40px translate (measured live: 1.0009, against 1.0116 for `cubic-bezier(.34, 1.56, .64, 1)`, ~13×). Decide whether the token should be re-pointed — it is shared by every overlay, the shell route transition and the trip-birth sequence, so it is one edit and an app-wide change.
- ~~**A device pass on the join sequence**~~ — **done (2026-08), and it found two things.** The anonymous return auto-joined, so the invitation flashed past unread ([ADR-0143 §8](decisions/0143-the-invite-pass-arrives-and-gets-stamped.md) — the return now waits for a tap, and the ADR's claim that the stamp sequence already made the detour "visible" is retracted there). And `/trips` with no trips was a grey void; it now renders the existing `ZeroState` rather than growing a second no-trips surface. **Every "the motion makes it visible" claim in this pass has needed a device to check** — three for three.

Brief with the full map, the constraints and the mockup plan: [`planning/2026-07-31-session-189-motion-and-first-run-brief.md`](planning/2026-07-31-session-189-motion-and-first-run-brief.md). Raised by the owner: the app should feel better, with the trip-creation journey and the invite join named as the two that should feel like a trip is imminent, and screen/overlay transitions (the Map's search) as the third. **The mapping pass found the reframe:** the app has a real motion ramp and good shipped motion — the mode switch, the board's power-on, the shared reveal (ADR-0120), the boot weights (ADR-0105), the Map camera (ADR-0129) — and **all of it lives inside a trip**. The whole first-run shell (`/login`, zero state, `/new`, the born screen, `/join/:token`) has one animation between the five surfaces. Ordered so the systemic fixes land before the set pieces, since the set pieces then assemble from parts that already move.

- ~~**G1 — overlays arrive and leave**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-31, session 190, [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §1).** Scrim on a pseudo-element so its opacity is independent of the card's transform, per-variant arrival (sheet rises / dialog summoned in place / `full` from the inline-end edge), one keyframe pair per channel played `reverse` to leave, exit briefer than entrance. The exit hangs off `onClose` — already the one owner of leaving per ADR-0103 §2 — rather than a second path, and `children` may take the wrapped close as a function because an in-card `✕`/`ביטול` is the most-used way out and would otherwise snap. `motionDurationMs` makes reduced motion correct by construction. **Still open:** the legacy families outside the primitive (`.sheet-overlay`, `.doc-viewer`, `.confirm-overlay`) keep their own `fade` until they fold onto `Modal` in ADR-0079's Wave 2 — they should ride G1 then rather than growing private motion.
- ~~**G2 — the shell gets route motion**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-31, session 190, [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §3).** Direction rides `location.state`, stamped by `runStructural` on a back that moves — never history — which finally gave `backSlides` a production consumer. Keyed on **pathname**, so an in-trip tab switch keeps `.body`'s fade and never gets two animations at once; RTL-correct off one `--dir` sign. **Deferred with its cost stated:** the _receding_ half. A full-width push needs the outgoing screen mounted, React has already unmounted it, and keeping it would mean two route subtrees — for this app two trip shells, two WS subscriptions, two clocks. Shipped as a 28px offset + fade instead. Revisit if a transition group becomes cheap, most likely if the trip shell stops being remounted by route changes.
- ~~**G3 — taps acknowledge**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-31, session 190, [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §2).** The build found the sharper problem: not 16 missing rules but 16 rules across **seven** different values, so press feedback was inconsistent where it existed. One element-level rule beside the line it makes true, two steps (`--press-scale` / `--press-scale-lg`, because the ratio is what stays constant, not the transform), scale not colour so it needs no per-surface table and spends nothing from the semantic budget. All seven converge; none is left. A new large surface owes one line: `--press-scale: var(--press-scale-lg)`.
- ~~**Journey 1 — trip birth**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-31, session 190, [ADR-0142](decisions/0142-trip-birth-is-the-boards-first-departure.md)).** Candidate A: the two screens become one `.app` root, the draft card travels as a **shared element** and goes dashed→solid, the chrome warms, and **the board powers on** with its flaps settling into the trip's own departure. Spends no second `--t-cinematic` moment — the same asset the mode switch plays, on a second trigger — so the budget rule stands untouched. Confetti drawn and rejected. Also shipped: the form assembling on `--stagger-step`, the CTA **arming** when the form completes, and the invite confirming in place. Skippable by a tap. **Open, and needing a device:** whether ~1450ms reads as generous or as a tax on your _third_ trip — a set piece is judged on the repeat. If it wears, the fix is rarity not speed (full sequence on a first trip, short version after: one condition, no new design). **And the sequence has not been watched** — no Docker/Postgres in the build environment, so the choreography is covered by nine component tests but nobody has seen it run.
- ~~**Journey 2 — the invite join**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-31, session 190, [ADR-0143](decisions/0143-the-invite-pass-arrives-and-gets-stamped.md)).** The pass arrives and settles, its notches punch, the avatars land staggered, the anticipation glow ramps with it; the stamp lands on the server's success and the pass tears along the perforation the shipped CSS already drew. Loading is the pass's own shape (sentence kept, visually hidden); an expired invite is a **refused** pass; offline keeps its sentence. Two mockup corrections built in: the stamp is `--ok`, not teal (ADR-0028's budget), and the refused pass shows no trip (the preview is what failed). `lib/useCountUp.ts` ships shared as the first of the Journey-4 value beats. **Still open:** the **removed-member** case must not reuse the expiry stamp — the same mark for "the link aged out" and "you were removed" is the app declining to say which — and it still renders as a `join-error` line; plus whether the count-up should run on a re-open (charming once, possibly fussy every visit).
- **Journey 3 — the rest, mostly free from G1/G2** — the Map search overlay, every sheet/dialog/confirm, the three hand-rolled panels that bypass `Modal` (`IconPicker`/`TimePicker`/`ZonePicker` — folding them on would close their known back-layer gap at the same time), and the doc viewer / `SyncReviewSheet` / legacy `.sheet-overlay` riding G1 as they fold on in ADR-0079's Wave 2 rather than growing private motion. The in-trip tab `.body` fade is deliberately **low priority** — it already works and is cheap to make worse. **`AllTrips`→trip shipped** (2026-07-31, session 192, [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §7): the trip's glyph travels onto the switcher pill, the arrival is a fade rather than a slide so the landing rect is measurable, and `lib/trip-handoff.ts` is the mechanism a second shared element would extend. It also exposed a live §3 defect — `data-nav` read live let the trip back guard's same-URL push restart the route animation — now latched at arrival.
- ~~**Journey 4 — micro-beats**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-31, session 191, [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §6, amended rather than a new ADR since these extend rules already decided).** `SyncBadge` resolves on `synced` only — not `pending` (a pulse there reads as strain, and ADR-0080 keeps sync non-colour-coded) and not `failed` (that needs the eye, not a flourish); `StatusBanner` settles in from the top edge instead of blinking into existence; a chip's/choice's **selection** settles, which is a different fact from the press — the press answers the tap, this answers the outcome, and a chip can be pressed without becoming selected. Values arrive via `lib/useCountUp.ts` from ADR-0143 §6. **The day/event/booking counts are claimed too** (`PlanHome`'s `StatTile`s) — "member counts" named alongside them turned out not to exist as a glanceable surface anywhere in the app, so there was nothing there to wire.
- ~~**Two token additions**~~ — **shipped (2026-07-31, session 190, [ADR-0140](decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §4):** `--ease-arrive` (the only overshooting easing — the other three are monotone, so an object that should _settle_ stopped dead) and `--stagger-step`. The one-`--t-cinematic`-moment budget is untouched. Three support values came with them and are documented in the design-language table: `--press-scale`/`-lg`, `--route-offset`, `--dir`.
- **Deliberately out of scope, already owned:** **Hero 2.0** (its own brief, and the biggest animation in the backlog — it gets cheaper once G1 ships, but it is not this pass) and the Map canvas's "small cute specific animations" (deferred on the Phase 11 line under the amber-budget and tag-overlap constraints).

## Backend review follow-ups (open findings)

Full write-up + evidence (incl. a reproduced concurrency probe) in [reviews/backend-architecture-review.md](reviews/backend-architecture-review.md).

B-01–B-06 and B-08–B-13 shipped (ADR-0068–0076); B-07 shipped (ADR-0067). Remaining are the pieces deliberately deferred out of B-12/B-13:

- **Orphan-blob reconciler** (deferred, from B-13/ADR-0076) — a periodic sweep listing storage keys not referenced by any `Document.fileRef`; the upload path still biases toward orphaning a blob over losing a document, with no reconciliation. Acceptable at current scale.
- **Standardize change `after` payloads** (deferred, from B-13/ADR-0076) — several services log the partial `input` as a change's `after` rather than the persisted DTO, so `after`'s shape is inconsistent across entity types (affects feed rendering / any future replay, not correctness today). **Field report #32 tested this and it held** (session 244): captured off a running server, an event create's `after` carries `date`/`title`/`kind`/`startsAt`/`endsAt` — everything a day or timeline surface needs to place the row — and a peer create/update/delete was confirmed to paint on every surface that renders events. What it omits is `createdAt`/`updatedAt`/`updatedBy`, which no render reads. #32's cause was the socket's gap test, not the payload, so the parenthetical above stands. **One asymmetry it did surface and nobody should rediscover:** the memory path (`applyRemoteEventChange`) spreads `after` raw, while the cache path runs it through `coerceClearedFields` — so a `displayTimezone: null` clear (ADR-0107, the one field whose schema is `nullish`) lands as a `null` in memory where the type says `string | undefined`. Nothing visibly broke on test, and both paths should coerce once this line is picked up.
- **Google email-change account-link policy** (from B-12/ADR-0076) — account-linking keys on `User.email`, so a changed Google primary email creates a new `User` the identity re-points to, orphaning the old one. Current policy: treat as a new account. Revisit if an identity-merge feature is ever wanted.

## Copy & voice

- **Hebrew voice/gender consistency** (from session 42, ADR-0085's item-4 pass) — most of the app speaks plural-neutral ("נסו", "בדקו", "הוסיפו"), but the zero-state and join screens switch to singular-masculine ("אתה זה שמארגן", "הצטרף", "פתח אותו", "מחובר"). Pick one voice (likely plural-neutral, matching the toast/settings copy) and sweep `i18n/he.ts`. A tone decision, not a bug — worth an ADR if the choice isn't obvious.
- **L — Explanatory copy goes unless it earns its place** (**#27**) — **done (2026-08-11; [session note](planning/2026-08-11-session-246-copy-that-does-not-earn-its-place.md); amends [ADR-0116](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md) §4/§5).** Eight strings deleted and four trimmed, against ~50 candidate sites; the protected categories (`ConfirmDialog` bodies, `EmptyState`/`ErrorState`, offline/join/permission notes, the disabled-primary `ctaReason`, and every constraint hint that states a scope or limit the control cannot) came through untouched, which is the result the rule asks for rather than a shortfall. **The triage's own two "lean remove" guesses both had to be re-decided on evidence, and split:** `nameHint` went as predicted, but `autoCaption`'s survival test — is its `cat-readout` the only place the booking's category shows? — came back **no**, `BookingTypeRow` states it on that very step, so the caption was a same-screen duplicate _and_ still claimed "נבחר לפי סוג ההזמנה" after a person had overridden the icon; it went, and its revert button stays as the whole control. **The one real reversal came from the owner mid-session, on a report from a real user:** the shelf's gesture hints, which the triage had filed as protected (a press-and-hold being "the gesture nobody guesses"), were the specific thing complained about — _the UI should be intuitive enough that you don't have to explain it, and the text makes the app look cluttered_ — so `shelfHint`/`tapToSchedule` are gone and the gesture clauses came off `skippedTag`/`inviteLabel`/the note composer's hint, leaving the state or the label alone. ADR-0116 is amended in place rather than reversed: capabilities are unchanged, the tap path still reaches everything the drag does, and the drag's discoverability is now recorded there as an **open question the ADR no longer answers** — if it goes unused, the fix owed is an affordance, not the sentence back. No ADR of its own and no mockup (no card changed height; the two removals that changed a row are the shelf headers and the booking caption, both verified rendering).

## Testing

- **Three e2e specs fail at `retries: 0`, and `main` fails them too.** Measured 2026-08-31 in a clean `origin/main` worktree beside the branch, while clearing ADR-0213's seventh pass — a full local run came back 286/4 and every one of the four turned out to predate the diff:
  - **`event-arrival-scroll.spec.ts` is reproducibly red on `main`**, both arrival tests, identically: `expectLanded`'s `toPass()` never sees the event's top reach the scroller's top. This is the one worth real attention — either the landing is genuinely late and `retries: 1` is hiding a scroll-timing bug, or the assertion's 24px window is too tight for a cold first paint. The spec's comment says that window is `scroll-margin-top` plus rounding, so it cannot be loosened without deciding which. Needs the measurement, not a nudge.
  - **`day-swipe.spec.ts:290` is flakier on `main` than on a branch**: 3/10 failures on `main` against 2/10 on the branch at `--workers=2`, 4/4 green at low load. A CDP-synthesised swipe that sometimes does not commit.
  - **`subtask-ring.spec.ts:407` is load-sensitive rather than flaky**: ~24s against the 30s default timeout, so 5/5 at `--workers=1` and failing at two different lines under parallel load, depending on where the clock runs out. It is the slowest test in the suite by a wide margin; what makes it slow is the thing to find, not its timeout.

  The shared cause of the blind spot is `retries: process.env.CI ? 1 : 0` — CI passes on the retry, so these are visible only to a contributor, which is backwards. Worth deciding whether CI should run one pass at `retries: 0` to surface them.

- **Real-device pass on the builder's drags** (ADR-0116 sessions 116-125) — the gesture is pinned by Chromium e2e, which is not the engine the bugs were reported from, and feel (does the 500 ms hold read as responsive?) is not testable at all. Hold it on a phone before calling the drag done; session 119 adds a specific thing to feel (whether `DRAG_DAY_DWELL_MS`, now 700 ms, is the right spring-load delay), session 125 adds another (a drag lifted in an edge band waits for the finger to leave the band or to push `DRAG_EDGE_SCROLL_RELEASE_PX`, 16 px, toward that edge — is 16 px the right ask on a thumb, or does the near edge still feel sticky?), session 123 adds two more (whether a thumb can hit an edge chip at the very top/bottom of the list, and whether three chips on a two-event day read as help or as clutter), and session 120 turns on iOS's touch-retargeting behaviour directly — the guard that keeps a drag alive after its source unmounts is pinned in Chromium only. Note the previously-stated fallback — "an explicit visible drag handle" — is no longer available: the row's grip was retired in 119 precisely because hold-to-drag made it redundant, so a regression there would mean reversing that decision, not reaching for a spare.

## Open question

- **General back-navigation inconsistency** (from session 63) — diagnosed session 65: the reported "sometimes not as expected" back traces to a **layer-lifecycle** defect, not the ADR-0090 resolver. `runBack('close-overlay')` destructively `pop()`s the overlay stack, but `IndexBookingsView`'s callback resets the category filter and stays mounted — so it's popped-yet-visible and the next system-back leaks past it (system-back then diverges from the visible arrow). Written up in **[ADR-0103](decisions/0103-back-navigation-typed-layer-model.md)** (Accepted, core shipped) + `planning/2026-07-21-session-65-back-navigation-overhaul-plan.md`. **Shipped + device-validated:** the typed non-destructive `BackResult { remainsActive }` registry (the filter fix); the **fresh-load guard** (fuel on any fresh document load, fixing the cross-document reload/camera exit); **history-backed overlays** (each overlay owns a same-URL history marker, system-back _rides_ the traversal to close it — the multi-back force-exit fix, since the platform only lets a page cancel ~one back per user activation); the **structural two-tap exit ride-and-correct** (an uncancelable armed 2nd back that the OS traverses onto the Home-duplicate guard is corrected to `/trips` in a microtask, `correctionForUncancelableBack`, instead of looping back to Home — no history reseed, so no shell remount); and an env-gated (`VITE_NAV_DEBUG`) nav-debug HUD. **Still open:** trigger-aware `resolveBack` + Escape unification (Escape is still a separate close path), URL-param durability for Index subviews.

## Reusable UI follow-ups

- **Emoji used as UI controls, swept out** (agreed 2026-07-28) — **done (2026-07-29, [ADR-0138](decisions/0138-the-row-menu-is-one-surface-and-icons-are-ui.md)):** swept in full, and the split came first as this entry insisted. `ICONS` is now `GLYPH` (content, strings) + `CONTROL_ICON` (`IconName`s), ~24 shapes joined `ui/Icon.tsx`, and ~130 call sites plus every inline literal moved over. The two things it said to decide rather than assume **both crossed on the owner's call** — the bottom nav's 🏠 🗺️ 📇 📅 (amending `design-language.md`'s Bottom nav entry) and the verb-row/toast marks, so `ShowToast` takes an `IconName` and a toast wears the icon of the action it confirms. ✓ / ⋯ / ＋ were judged on the arrow guard's own font-fallback grounds and converted with the rest. The lint guard now covers control emoji in rendered JSX, non-test source only; **its first version inverted the rule** — an astral-emoji character class without the `u` flag is a class of surrogate halves sharing `\uD83D`, so it matched every emoji in the plane and flagged 📍 and 🗺️ as controls. It uses an alternation. **Amended again 2026-08-02** on a second owner report (_"you missed a few emoji removals"_): the guard was a DENYLIST built from the sweep it was meant to outlive, so it could only catch a regression of the twenty glyphs already replaced — 📕 🗺️ 🗓️ 📍 📖 📄 🎫 👥 were never on it and CI stayed green while the Index's empty documents card drew a pink block. That card was the worst case: it borrowed `DOCUMENT_TYPE_ICON.passport`, a CONTENT enum used as decoration. The first amendment's "empty-state illustrations stay emoji" carve-out is **withdrawn** — `Map.tsx` disproved it in the same commit, with four `EmptyState`s in one ternary drawing two ways. The JSXText half of the guard is **positional** now (any emoji typed into markup fails; content comes from data or a named constant), which found **four more** on its first run that nobody had spotted by reading: ★, ✨, 📶, and `⎣` — U+23A3, a bracket PIECE for stacking math delimiters, i.e. the arrow-glyph font-fallback bug verbatim.
- **`Modal`'s Escape bypasses the back stack** — **done (2026-08-01; [ADR-0103](decisions/0103-back-navigation-typed-layer-model.md)'s 2026-08-01 amendment, which closes §2's Escape-unification deferral and its "Escape scope" open question).** Escape runs the resolver now: `useEscapeAsBack` in `useOverlay`, `useDialogFocus` reduced to what its name says. **Worse than this entry recorded, in three ways.** The §176 amendment's claim that _"`Modal` already honours this"_ holds only for a LONE overlay — `onClose` is that dialog's own dismissal, not whatever back would do next. So Escape in a form with an `IconPicker`/`TimePicker` panel open closed the **form and discarded what you had typed**, where back and a tap outside both only closed the panel: the very defect §176 was written to fix, surviving on the trigger it did not check. Every open Modal also added its own `document` listener, and `stopPropagation` does not stop siblings on the same target, so a nested prompt took **both** sheets down on one press. And `IconPicker`'s own Escape handler was **dead code** — bubble-phase, behind `useDialogFocus`'s capture listener. **The scope question needed no layer typing:** `useOverlay` is already exactly "dismissible overlays + search" and `useBackLayer` already carries filters and subviews, so the ADR's chosen default fell out of the split that existed. Escape's blast radius is unchanged; only which layer it peels there. Covered by unit tests for the step and nested cases and by two real-Chromium specs for the panels; reproduced and re-verified in the running app.
- **A booking row can wear the DEFAULT pin instead of its own type glyph** — **done (2026-08-01):** it was reachable, by a path the entry guessed at but did not name exactly. `EventForm` only re-derives the glyph while the icon is **untouched**, and _editing an existing event counts as touched_ — so an event created with no category keeps `📌`, and giving it a category later never clears it. From then on the pin shadows whatever is more specific. **Five sites, not the one reported:** the Index row and `BookingDetail` (a booking's type glyph), `TransitionRow` and `lib/glance.ts` (the CATEGORY glyph — more reachable, since no booking need be involved), and `lib/booking-draft.ts`, which is the one that mattered most because it **persists**: it seeds the booking form's icon picker, so the pin was saved onto the booking on the next edit rather than only drawn. Fixed with one shared `chosenIcon(icon)` in `constants.ts` — returns `undefined` for `DEFAULT_EVENT_ICON`/`DEFAULT_MAYBE_ICON` so the `??` chain behind it keeps running, leaving every call site's fallback order untouched. A **value test, not a stored flag**: a flag would need every writer to maintain it and would be wrong for rows written before today. The accepted cost is that deliberately picking `📌`/`💡` now reads as picking nothing. Verified in the running app — the same fixture that exposed it draws ✈️ where it drew 📌.
- **The filter/search reveal is shared** — **done (2026-07-26, session 129; [ADR-0120](decisions/0120-filter-reveal-is-shared-infrastructure.md)):** ADR-0098 §4's motion pass had two halves and only the collapse was generalized at build time (`ui/primitives/Collapsible`); the **per-row filter reveal** stayed a one-off on the Index bookings screen — a `BookingRow`-typed `visibleRows`, a `.index .idx-row` rule capped at `max-height: 140px`, and the wrapper markup copy-pasted at each of its three lists. So the **Map**, built on the same filter grammar (ADR-0109 §2 — same chips, same `SearchOverlay`, same accent), `.filter()`ed rows out of the array and jumped. Now `lib/filter-reveal.ts` (`revealRows` + `countVisible`/`visibleItems`; the caller keeps the predicate, and `index-bookings.ts`'s `visibleRows` is just the bookings one) + `ui/primitives/RevealList`/`RevealRow` (unscoped `.wp-reveal`, motion-ramp durations, `renderBefore` for group headers outside the collapsing wrapper). Two things the one-off never had: a hidden row is **`inert`** (it stays mounted to animate out, so it was focusable and read aloud), and the collapse is a **`0fr`/`1fr` grid track** instead of a per-surface `max-height` cap — with a layout-neutral clip bleed so a row's shadow/focus ring/next-stop ring isn't sheared (verified in a browser: collapsed rows measure 0px, the amber ring renders whole). The Map's chips, `אולי` toggle, and both search halves inherit it with no new mechanism. **Amended the same day (session 130), on the owner's call — _every_ list change is animated:** `כל הימים` and the day strip's day became predicates over the whole trip's places (rows leave and arrive in place, the row nodes reused rather than the list rebuilt), and a pure **re-order** — `קרוב עכשיו`, and any ordering control after it — slides its rows via FLIP (`lib/useFlipRows.ts`, WAAPI so it never fights the reveal's own transform/`transition-delay`, measured only when the row order/visibility actually changed), wired inside `RevealList` so no call site opts in. Verifying it turned up a real bug in `lib/useGeolocation.ts` (the liveness ref was cleared on unmount and never re-armed, so a remount discarded every fix and `קרוב עכשיו` sat on `מאתר…` in dev) — one line + a regression test. A filtered-out row is now hidden-in-DOM rather than absent — count `countVisible`, not `rows.length`.
- **Five hand-built copies of one task clock** (noticed 2026-08-29 while adding the trip window to it, not fixed) — `useTaskClock` in `ui/HostTasks.tsx` is the assembler its own comment says it is ("the clock every task derivation takes, assembled once"), and it is **private to that file**, so `Home`, `PlanHome`, `Index` and `IndexTasksView` each build the same object inline. Adding one field to it — `trip`, so a printed deadline can name its day (ADR-0085's 2026-08-29 amendment) — had to be typed into all five. Exporting the hook and deleting the four copies is a small extraction and reaches four screens, which is why it was left rather than folded into an unrelated change; the fifth copy is the one that will not be updated next time.
- **`SearchOverlay` beyond bookings** (from session 63, ADR-0101) — the full-screen search-mode primitive (`ui/primitives/SearchOverlay.tsx`) is wired up for the Index bookings screen and the Map (ADR-0109/0115). A documents search (or any other "type to filter, on a full screen" need) should adopt it directly rather than growing a second one-off, whenever one is actually needed — and it gets the reveal motion for free by rendering its rows through `RevealList` (ADR-0120).
- **A form's refusal is invisible** — **done (2026-08-04, session 201; [ADR-0150](decisions/0150-a-form-refuses-at-the-field.md) + [`mockups/form-refusal-v1.html`](../mockups/form-refusal-v1.html)),** from an owner report against the event form: _"the error for missing fields is nearly noticeable"_. Reading the code turned one caption into **three** refusal shapes, none of which marked the field: a form-level `<p>` above `FormActions` (`EventForm`, `BookingSheet`) that sits at the far end of the form from the field it names, does not name it, and — since both `return` at the first problem — refuses twice when two things are missing; `Field`'s error slot, right place but caption only (`DocumentUploadSheet`, `MapPlaceForm`); and a hand-rolled `.invalid` class with its own copy of the outline in two stylesheets (`CreateTrip`, `TripSettings`). One `ui/primitives/useFormErrors` now owns what happens **after** a refusal — the form still decides what is wrong and in what words — with every problem reported at once, the control outlined in `--miss`, one 240ms nudge (imperative, because a mark already on screen says nothing new on a second press), and the first problem in **document order** scrolled to centre and focused. One attribute, `data-invalid`, carries all of it, which is what let both `.invalid` rules be **deleted** rather than migrated. Two findings the build produced: the invalid rule needs its own `:focus-visible` pair (a refusal focuses the field it names, and the teal focus border out-specified it — caught in the mockup, invisible to the unit tests), and `EventForm` is now `noValidate` (the browser's own bubble was reaching an out-of-trip date first, in English, and `submit` never ran). **Swept the whole app, not the reported form** — event, booking, place, new trip, settings and document upload all refuse the same way now — which turned up a **fourth** shape the first pass had not counted: a **disabled primary**, in four places, three of which said nothing whatsoever (`TripSettings`' save was dead on a four-field `canSave` with no note beside it; `MapPlaceForm`'s confirm was dead while its own field bound the Enter key, so `confirm()` ran into a silent `return`; `DocumentUploadSheet` had a `fileRequired` string no path could reach). Disabled now means only "a press cannot work" — offline, or a write in flight — and `CreateTrip`'s CTA keeps both its dim and ADR-0142 §1's arming beat, which never depended on `disabled`. **And it found a fifth stale landing position** (ADR-0148's Consequences counts four): a refusal makes a form TALLER, which pushed `CreateTrip`'s birth-card slot 57px down while the card — re-measured only from a `ResizeObserver` on a viewport-sized root that a growing form never resizes — stayed put, covering the name field, the refusal under it, and a hint that had been invisible since ADR-0142 shipped. It re-measures on every render now, with the resize path kept for rotation and the keyboard. **Left open:** the four zod `safeParse` backstops in `EventForm` are unreachable behind the explicit checks and still use `titleRequired` as their message, which is the wrong words for an unexpected shape — they want one generic "something went wrong" string in `t.common`.
- **Setting a place's category (and the icon it drove) was a no-op** — **done (2026-08-04; [ADR-0165](decisions/0165-a-place-says-what-it-is.md), amending [ADR-0147](decisions/0147-a-place-is-made-on-the-canvas.md) §5 in place),** from an owner report: _"setting a place category & icon isn't working (it's a no op), no network error or console error."_ Two defects, and the interesting one was already written down as a limitation. ADR-0147 §5 sent the form's category to the `MaybeItem` an add creates and dropped it on a **rename** (`Place` had no category), promising the icon carried the tap instead — and it did not: a pill tap `redrive`s the glyph, which leaves `iconTouched` **false**, so on a place with no stored glyph (every place from a search or a drop without a pick) the patch came out **empty**, `applyAuthored` returned early, and nothing was requested. `Map.embedded.test.tsx` pinned that as intended, asserting `updatePlace` was _not_ called. Fixed with a nullable `Place.category` on §5's own argument for `icon` — a human's word about a place has to survive deleting the idea it was written through — resolved above the referencing entities in `pin.category` (hue, badge glyph and facet from one value; `commitment` stays the references'; the facet is a **union**), and a `categoryTouched` flag through the existing `useDerivedField` so the pills' **seed** cannot write (an untouched default performing a conversion is [ADR-0136](decisions/0136-an-event-can-also-be-booked.md) §2's defect a third time). "Only a PICK is stored" survives unchanged and is now load-bearing rather than lossy.

- **`.quick`'s columns are not equal, and the row can leave the screen** (found 2026-08-09 by rendering [`mockups/currency-becomes-a-feature-v1.html`](../mockups/currency-becomes-a-feature-v1.html) §1, session 238) — `Home.tsx` sets `grid-template-columns: repeat(N, 1fr)` inline from the visible-tile count, and `1fr` is `minmax(auto, 1fr)`: a grid item floors at its **min-content** width unless it says `min-width: 0`, which `.qa` does not. Drawn at five tiles the columns come out `56 · 68 · 81 · 56 · 110` and the row measures **407px of content inside a 326px box** — 81px pushed off the inline-start edge. Today's four tiles happen to fit, so it is **latent, not live**; what makes it worth recording is that the comment above `.quick` states the opposite ("the column count is set inline from the visible-tile count, so a derived tile with no source just reflows the row"), so the assumption is written down and untrue. **This is the third instance of the same flexbox/grid intrinsic-sizing trap in this file** (`.map-refs`, 491px in a 340px card, above; and ADR-0121 §8's amendment) — a one-line `min-width: 0`, and worth a sweep for the fourth. **Fixed 2026-08-09** with ADR-0180's slice 1, ahead of the fifth tile that would have exposed it.
- **A spinning glyph must turn the way it points** (owner report on the moving version, 2026-08-09, session 238) — `screens.css`'s shared `@keyframes spin` is `+360deg`, which is correct for the thing that uses it (`.spinner` is a rotationally symmetric border-arc **ring** with no head) and wrong for any glyph with a barb. `Icon.tsx`'s `reset` arc measures 151° → 85° → 21° → −139° about its centre — counter-clockwise — so it must animate `-360deg`. **A ring may spin either way; an arrow may not.** Nothing shipped spins a glyph today; this is the rule the first one inherits, and the reason it is written down is that the shared keyframe's name invites reuse without checking the shape.

- **`align-items: baseline` on a flex row containing an icon** (owner report against the mockup on a real phone, 2026-08-09, session 238) — an inline SVG has **no baseline**, so it aligns by its bottom margin edge; and on a flex line **taller than its content** (anything meeting ADR-0017's 44px floor), `baseline` parks the whole group near the top instead of centring it. Measured 13.0px off-centre in the mockup's currency chip, 0.0px with `center`.
