Nothing shipped is currently wrong — `zone-picker.css:43`'s `.zp-row` is the closest and is all text — but it is the rule the chip copied, so **check it before adding an icon to any `baseline` row**. The wider lesson is about the instrument, not the rule: a measurement table of widths and heights **cannot see this**, because the box was the right size the whole time and only its contents were misplaced; a design file that draws a control now measures **each child's centre against its container's centre** too.

## Field reports — 2026-08-07 triage (session 216)

21 owner field reports, triaged (not fixed) into nine workstreams in [`planning/2026-08-07-session-216-field-reports-triage.md`](planning/2026-08-07-session-216-field-reports-triage.md) — full per-issue interpretation, code/ADR trace, owner-decision-vs-open-question split, and screenshot evidence live there. Each line below is one coherent workstream, not one line per report; issue IDs are the triage note's own `#1`–`#21`, unrelated to the Map panel epic's internal numbering above. **A 2026-08-08 addendum (`#22`–`#26`) reconciled five more field reports against this same task system, continuing the numbering** — full reconciliation trace in [`planning/2026-08-08-session-224-incremental-field-reports-addendum.md`](planning/2026-08-08-session-224-incremental-field-reports-addendum.md); its lines are folded into the relevant entries below rather than kept separate. **A second addendum (`#27`–`#34`) followed on 2026-08-10** — eight reports into seven workstreams `L`–`R`, trace in [`planning/2026-08-10-session-242-second-incremental-field-reports-addendum.md`](planning/2026-08-10-session-242-second-incremental-field-reports-addendum.md). Six of them are lines below; **`L` (copy density, #27) lives in Copy & voice**, which is its domain home. **A third addendum (`#35`–`#41`) followed on 2026-08-11** — seven reports, **five of them the owner reporting that shipped work still fails on his phone** — into six workstreams: `M`, `N`, `P` and `Q` **reopened** under their existing letters, plus new `S` and `T`. Trace in [`planning/2026-08-11-session-249-third-incremental-field-reports-addendum.md`](planning/2026-08-11-session-249-third-incremental-field-reports-addendum.md). **The rule that governs every reopening here, and it is the owner's:** a live "it still happens" outranks a done status, a passing test or a documented invariant — the earlier work is preserved and linked, and the reopened line describes the **residual**, not the original task again. One caveat that applies to all five and is written once: six of the seven reports pull against workstreams that shipped on that same day, and nobody has established which build the reporting device was running — so **pin the build (and, for `N`, the negative cache) before reading "still" as "the fix failed"**.

- **D — Booking authoring ergonomics** (#2, #4, #8, #9, #11, #12) — **done (2026-08-07; [session note](planning/2026-08-07-session-221-booking-authoring-decision-session.md)). No ADR: nothing here revises a decision an ADR owns.** Decided with the owner, then built in one pass, and taking them together is what stopped the middle one going wrong: **#12** made a hotel's `provider` the booking CHANNEL (`הוזמן דרך`, hotel only — the other types keep ADR-0163 §2's per-type labels), which is exactly why **#9**'s derived title reads the linked PLACE and not the provider, or every hotel would now be called `Booking.com`. #9 is `place name → type label`, generalising ADR-0163 §3's "the thing it is called, else what it is" past the car hire it was written for; the `titleRequired` refusal is **deleted** rather than relaxed, since a fallback makes it unreachable. **#8** opens the direction `undefined` — ADR-0154 §4's 492px measurement only ever justified not pre-EXPANDING the return, and `false` went further and pre-selected `כיוון אחד`; the cost argument survives untouched, so §4 needed no amendment. **#2** gives the type its own create-only first step and a collapsed row + `שינוי` on every step after it; an **edit gains no step** (the type was never editable, so the step is folded away rather than shown with nothing to do) — the owner's "combine when it makes sense", resolved as a judgment call and flagged as such. **#4** pre-fills the end DATE per type (same day; hotel +1, read off `durationUnit === 'nights'` rather than a new column) and hides same-day end times before the start — the floor is passed only while the end's own day equals the start's, which is what leaves overnight flights and multi-day stays offering the full 24 hours. **#11 was answered, built, then RESTATED by the owner and rebuilt**: the first answer prefilled only ADR-0171 §1's non-`exact` edges (hotel/car); the restatement is broader — conventional clocks where the world fixes them, a 07:00 day start otherwise, an end a typical length later, and **an end that follows the start until it is touched** (`useDerivedField`'s latch, which the same form already uses for the icon and the kind). The `exact` line survives as the reason **flights/trains/transit offer nothing** — a guessed departure is a false instant on a hard commitment — pinned as a property against `eventMidSpan` rather than a list of type names. `duration` offers reuse `CATEGORY_TIME_PROFILE.typicalMinutes` (ADR-0161 §5); note the trap avoided, that `transport`'s 60 explicitly means nothing. Two things the build found: the direction control had to move OUT of the route `Field` (a nested `Field` renders its error ahead of its wrapper's, so a missing route was reported at the direction's box — ADR-0150's one-box-one-name), and `changeType` must clear an untouched offered end. **Left open:** the render has been seen in unit tests only — no device pass (ADR-0017) on whether a four-step create reads as one step too long, and `restaurant`/`other` offering 07:00 is correct by the rule and worth re-tuning if it reads oddly. No mockup was drawn: the owner resolved all three shape-changing pieces in conversation.
- **E — Flight place data** (#6, #7, **#23**) — **done (2026-08-08; [ADR-0166 §18](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md), amended in place per its own convention; [session note](planning/2026-08-08-session-226-workstream-e-airport-labels.md), research in [session 225](planning/2026-08-08-session-225-flight-place-data-research.md)).** An airport now reads as **the city it serves** (`תל אביב ← פרנקפורט`) wherever a route is glanceable, from **real** data: Wikidata `P238` (code) and `P931` (city served) as two new fields in ADR-0166's existing pipe, matched by the name route and **gated by a `P31`-is-airport guard** — which is what refuses the one hazard the research found, London's city entity carrying `P238 = LON`. `P931` is multi-valued with no reliable winner (Ben Gurion serves Tel Aviv _and_ Jerusalem at equal rank), so the automatic rule is preferred-rank-else-first and **`Place.nickname` overrules it** — a user-authored field on `Place`, `icon`'s pattern exactly (ADR-0147 §5), set on the rename form and surviving the idea it was written through. Precedence: **nickname → the served city → today's name-stripping** in `place-label.ts`, which is untouched and still the floor. **The IATA code is not in the label** (owner's call on seeing the first build, same day): every reader of it is a row and a route carries two, so `תל אביב · TLV ← פרנקפורט · FRA` spent the whole width saying twice what the cities say once — and would have pushed most real pairs past `ROUTE_INLINE_MAX_CHARS` into the destination-primary fallback. The code is its own fact on the **booking detail**, beside the confirmation code, which has room and is where you check it against a ticket. **#6 stayed on Text Search** (`includedType: 'airport'` + `strictTypeFiltering`), against the research's Autocomplete recommendation and for a cost reason: flight-leg picking lands on the Map, whose results are rings needing coordinates, and an Autocomplete prediction has none — covering both airport types there is a Place Details call per rendered result, the shape ADR-0115 §2 and ADR-0166 §13 have each rejected once. The mask and therefore the SKU tier are unchanged. **Followed up the same day on the owner's own bookings ([ADR-0166 §19](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md), [session 227](planning/2026-08-08-session-227-airport-label-followups.md)):** Frankfurt never matched — Google's stored name carries an appended `(Frankfurter Flughafen – FRA)` whose extra tokens scored a correct candidate at **0.756** against the 0.8 floor, so the search found the right item and the scorer refused it (§15's bug, second cause), and the 500m coordinate route cannot reach an airport whose `P625` is 1.1–1.4km from the terminal pin; fixed by scoring the de-parenthesised form too. The city read as `תל אביב-יפו` because that is Wikidata's **label**, i.e. the official name — now the longest alias that is a proper prefix of it (`תל אביב`, `Frankfurt`). And the booking detail reads as cities as well, narrowly revising ADR-0059 §3's "the detail keeps full names". **Keflavík still reads as Keflavík rather than Reykjavík, deliberately:** `P931` is correct by its own definition and the gap is with what a passenger means, which is `Place.nickname`'s job — one of the owner's three airports needed the alias rule and one needs a nickname, which is the honest rate. **Then Bangkok exposed a category-wide hole ([ADR-0166 §20](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md), [session 228](planning/2026-08-08-session-228-the-fourth-match-route.md)):** `נמל התעופה בנגקוק סוונאפום` could not be found by ANY route — a Hebrew query cannot return a Latin-labelled item from a label search, a transliteration shares no tokens with its source, and the coordinate route's 500m radius could never reach an airport whose centroid sits kilometres from the terminal pin. Fixed with a **fourth match method** (`wiki_search`: Wikipedia's full-text search, which matches article text and redirects, capped below `geosearch` because a text hit is the weakest evidence) plus an **airport-sized distance ruler** earned by the candidate's own `P31` rather than by our name. Built rather than left to a nickname deliberately: **a nickname fixes the label and leaves `iata` missing**, so the booking detail's codes row stays empty — an unmatched airport is a missing fact, not a cosmetic problem. **Two things left open, both in the session note:** the `airport`/`international_airport` overlap is still unverified (a strict filter that hides the departure airport is silent, so an empty restricted answer is re-asked once unrestricted — **delete that retry once the overlap is measured with a real key**), and the label has been seen in unit tests only, never on a 360px row (ADR-0017).
- **F — Itinerary temporal + sequencing semantics** (#10, #16, #17, #18) — **done (2026-08-07; [ADR-0171](decisions/0171-a-time-can-be-a-floor-or-a-ceiling.md), with the reversal written into [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §6 in place; [session note](planning/2026-08-07-session-220-temporal-semantics-decision-session.md)). Mockup and build still owed.** A timestamp's meaning is a third axis beside hard/soft: `exact` / `not-before` / `not-after`, **derived** from the time profile's own `midSpan.kind` (a `journey`'s ends are instants, a `held` resource's are a floor and a deadline) — no field, no migration, and a car hire inherits floor/deadline ends through ADR-0162's per-glyph refinement. A flexible edge bounds no gap, which is #16's answer. A `not-before` edge stays in `נותרו היום` until settled or the day ends. One derived connection is one stop number; a genuine revisit still takes its own. **§3/§4 (where a flexible row GOES) were reopened the same day** and are the one thing outstanding: the owner's own day — `צ׳ק-אין מ-15:00 · נחיתה 21:00 · רכב 22:00`, checked into after the car — is not orderable from the data, since the same day with the same bookings can equally be a check-in as soon as possible. Five treatments are drawn against the same three days in [`a-time-without-a-position-v1.html`](../mockups/a-time-without-a-position-v1.html) (ADR-0171 §10 has the table): today's instant interleave, §4's slide, the `.day-ambient` strip, an unplaced tail under one hairline, and two numbers (a reservation floor + a human-set position, the only path to "when" and the only stored field in the ADR). **Then the owner generalized it and two more things were decided (ADR-0171 §10a/§10b):** "holds no position in the day" is **one class** covering untimed events, so the tail treatment is not an addition but the tail `DayView` already renders finally named — but **one class is not one category on screen** — the owner's correction, and it undid the session's own next step: within the class `hard` and `soft` stay as different as ADR-0011 says, so it is **one derivation, two placements**. A commitment with no position (a check-in from 15:00, and an untimed hard booking) reads at the **top**, in the `.day-ambient` strip that already exists, gaining a `SettleControl` density so ADR-0164's count can clear; an **idea** with no position stays in the tail. The strip and the tail were never rivals — each is half. A **deadline is not in the class**: a ceiling is closed on the side you act, so it intersects with the day's first departure and keeps a list position — but it earns **no map number**, on the owner's own case (numbering a Reykjavik check-out from its ceiling puts you back in Iceland after landing in Tel Aviv). Hence the map rule: **a stop number is only ever the index of a moment the app knows**, which also fixes a shipped defect nobody reported — an untimed event's place is numbered today (`prominence: 'edge'` with `at: undefined` passes `hasScheduleSlot`). **Built** across `icons.ts` (`edgeMeaning`/`isExactEdge`), `day-entries.ts` (`placeDayEntries`, and the deadline intersect), `day-joins.ts` (a flexible edge is transparent to the gap measurement), `glance.ts` (the count), `map-pins.ts` (known moments only, one connection one stop), plus `UnplacedCommitment` and the tail's line — **in Trip AND Plan** (§10e, from an owner report off the first build: Plan had kept interleaving by instant, so the two modes were saying different things about one booking, which ADR-0159 §1 does not allow. Plan makes the same split and offers no control on it, since the only offer it wants is the unbuilt two-numbers path). **Two things remain, and both are the owner's:** whether the two-numbers path is worth its column — after §10a it is the only stored field the ADR would need, since an untimed row reaches the same outcome through ADR-0161 §7's `＋ שעה` — and a device pass (ADR-0017) on whether `ללא מיקום ביום` reads as a boundary or as an accusation, since "sometime today" is a legitimate thing to say. The render has been seen in the mockup only.
- **Do two separately-authored bookings name one airport through two `Place` rows?** — unfiled, and named rather than assumed. `connectionMinutes` matches on `from.toPlaceId === to.fromPlaceId`, so if the 11 September flight pair carries two distinct `Place` rows for the same airport, no layover is derivable between them and the band after Workstream F lands is a thin `פנוי · 2:45 שע׳` instead. That is the **place-identity** axis, not the temporal one (ADR-0051's normalisation is where it would be answered). Check against the owner's real data before treating it as a defect; if it reproduces, it is its own report.
- **G1 — Pre-save document preview** (#19) — **done (2026-08-08; [ADR-0086](decisions/0086-document-upload-pick-control-redesign.md) amended in place; [session note](planning/2026-08-08-session-223-the-picked-file-opens-full-screen.md)).** The feasibility question answered yes with room to spare: a `File` **is** a `Blob`, so `MediaViewer` takes a third `ViewerSource` variant (`{ kind: 'file', file }`) that joins the document's path at `URL.createObjectURL` and shares every step after it — no fetch, no ADR-0055 cache key, no version, nothing to bound, and one branch in the read effect rather than a second viewer (rule 8). `FilePicker`'s preview card body becomes a pressable open-body beside the ✕ — `ListRow`'s grammar, the same gesture a saved document's row uses — so the pre-save look inherits the ONE close (ADR-0103 §2), the focus trap, the arrival, ADR-0062's pinch and ADR-0052 §1's decode hand-off by being the same component. The card's thumbnail URL and the viewer's stay independent, so neither frees the other's. **Known caveat, recorded not papered over:** a PDF (and an undecodable HEIC) gets `פתיחה`/`הורדה`, not an inline render — one tap further out, and exactly what a **saved** PDF already offers, so the two looks agree. An inline PDF surface is its own decision, on its own evidence, for both. **No mockup:** the grammar shown is already shipped; the only new pixels are the fit-to-content brackets marking the thumbnail as a way in. **Not seen on a device** (ADR-0017) — whether the brackets read as "tap me" on a real phone is a human pass nobody has made.
- **Should an inline PDF preview exist at all?** — surfaced by G1 and deliberately not answered there. Both the pre-save and post-save looks send a PDF to a browser tab (ADR-0052 §1: inline only what the browser decodes as an image), which is consistent but is still the moment a passport-adjacent PDF leaves the app's own surface. If it is worth an embedded viewer, that decision is one decision for both, with evidence about how often documents actually are PDFs — not a special case bolted onto the pick control.
- **G2 — Document read reliability** (#20) — **diagnosed and bounded (2026-08-07; [session note](planning/2026-08-07-session-222-document-read-reliability-built.md)). No ADR: nothing here revises a decision an ADR owns, and no architecture changed.** The diagnosis found no single stuck phase to name: **all eight awaits in the read path were unbounded**, a harness confirmed each one wedges the read on its own, and the viewer's only route out of its spinner is a rejection — so any phase going quiet was a spinner with no end. Three explain the "restart the app" part specifically: the **Cache API phases run ahead of the network**, so a jammed storage handle wedged the first open and the cached one alike and the handle is per-page; the **write-back was awaited**, so a full or jammed cache held up a read whose bytes were already in hand; and **`img.decode()` is the one phase where image and PDF differ**, a decode requested while the document is hidden (a phone locked mid-load) being dropped and never settled. Fixed with `lib/deadline.ts`'s `withDeadline` — one mechanism, abortable phases actually abort — bounds in `DOC_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`, sized as "this is dead" not "this is slow"; the cache answers a miss on silence, the network phases reject, the write-back is no longer awaited, a decode timeout falls through to the `<img>` rather than the hand-off, and the failure lands in `ui/feedback`'s `ErrorState` **with a retry** instead of the bare caption that was a dead end. **Not confirmed on the owner's device** — which phase actually fired in the field is still unknown, and the bounds are what make that not matter. Redis/streaming ingest untouched and still unrelated.
- **Every other API read had the same missing bound, and #22 was it happening** (field report #22 from the 2026-08-08 addendum) — **diagnosed and bounded (2026-08-08; [session note](planning/2026-08-08-session-229-the-boot-that-never-ends.md)). No ADR: nothing here revises a decision an ADR owns, and no architecture changed.** The lead in the addendum was right about the mechanism and wrong about the phase: `fetchSnapshot` is unbounded, but the boot never reaches it. **`AuthProvider`'s bootstrap runs first** — `await refreshAccessToken()` then `fetchMe()`, both unbounded — so a radio with no upstream wedges the app on `BootScreen`, one screen earlier than the snapshot skeleton the report describes. A harness reproduced all of it: with a `fetch` that never answers, the boot refresh, `/me`, the snapshot, and a response whose **body** never arrives each hang forever on their own, and `TripProvider` sits on `טוען את הטיול…` indefinitely because its cached-snapshot fallback only runs on a **rejection**. **The domain-migration hypothesis did not pan out and is not needed:** the reproduction happens on any origin with no domain change involved, and an orphaned pre-migration origin would produce a _different_ symptom (signed out / nothing cached — both of which resolve instantly), not an endless spinner. It is not ruled out as a separate annoyance; it is ruled out as this report's cause. **Fixed with G2's own mechanism** (`lib/deadline.ts`, rule 8): `apiFetch` now bounds every request's headers phase and every JSON body read (`API_TIMEOUT_MS`, sized "this is dead" not "this is slow"), so the snapshot, `/me`, `/trips`, `/changes`, the invite preview, both searches, the enrichment lookup and `throwApiError`'s error body are all bounded by one change rather than per-caller; a caller's own `AbortSignal` is composed in, not replaced, so a superseded keystroke still cancels. `withDeadline` gained that linked-signal parameter and a shared `bestEffort`, which `doc-cache.ts` and the **Dexie snapshot read** now share — the offline fallback is itself bounded, because a jammed IndexedDB handle is the boot's last chance to end. `isNetworkError` now counts a `PhaseTimeoutError` as a network failure, which is what keeps a timeout landing on cached data rather than on a **sign-out**. **Deliberately not bounded:** multipart uploads (their headers only arrive after the bytes go up, so "slow" and "dead" are the same picture and the cost of guessing wrong is a lost upload), and the refresh itself — see the next line, which this fix does not touch. **Not seen on a device** (ADR-0017): the reproduction is a harness with a never-answering `fetch`, not a capture from a phone with no reception, and the numbers (20s headers / 60s body / 10s snapshot cache) have never been checked against a real connection abroad.
- **The token refresh can wedge the whole session, and the fix is a real trade** — the sharpest "only a restart recovers" mechanism found during the G2 diagnosis, and deliberately left alone because it is not document-specific and not a free change. `doRefresh`'s `fetch` has no bound, `refreshInFlight` only clears in `.finally()`, and the `wp-refresh` Web Lock is only released when the callback settles — so one refresh that never answers poisons every later 401 in the tab **and in every other tab**, until the page is reloaded. Bounding it means a slow-but-alive refresh becomes a **sign-out**, which is a product call, not a cleanup. **Still open after #22's fix, which went around it rather than through it:** the app's _boot_ no longer waits on the refresh forever (it gives up on the wait and carries on to the cached identity), but `doRefresh`, `refreshInFlight` and the Web Lock are untouched, so a refresh that goes silent mid-session still poisons every later 401 in every tab until a reload.
- **I — Notes shared across a linked context** (**#24**) — **done (2026-08-08; [ADR-0172](decisions/0172-a-linked-pair-is-one-context-and-a-place-may-inherit-it.md); [session note](planning/2026-08-08-session-225-notes-and-documents-share-a-context.md); PR #532).** The answer was **resolution, not storage**: `Booking↔Event` is strict 1:1 (ADR-0047 §1), so this never needed a note with two hosts — it needed two rows that are already one thing to the user to be one list. ADR-0152's single-host row is **reaffirmed, not reversed**; a context is derived at read time, reads union it, writes land on its **anchor** (the Booking, forced by ADR-0093's server-materialized event having no client id at save time), and a Place with exactly one relevant Booking/Event context **inherits one-way**. The owner's non-leak rule cost nothing: the row never moves, so a reused place simply stops resolving. **Zero schema, zero migration, zero backend, zero API.** Generalized `hero-horizon.ts`'s `notesForEvent` one-off, which had flagged this exact divergence and declined to fix it unilaterally (rule 8).
- **K — Documents attach to bookings/events, shared contextually like Notes** (**#26**) — **done (2026-08-08; [ADR-0173](decisions/0173-a-document-attaches-and-detaches-never-dies.md); [session note](planning/2026-08-08-session-230-documents-attach-to-their-host.md)).** A `DocumentAttachment` **join table** (`documentId` + `eventId?`/`bookingId?`, many-to-many), reversing ADR-0047 §4 — because a note is born owned by its host and a document is not, so the link row makes **detach-never-delete** the default cascade rather than a `SetNull` special case, and one confirmation PDF covers a round trip's two `Booking`s. Reuses ADR-0172's context derivation **but not its storage**, which is session 224's open question answered. The attach slot is one 40px control until something is attached (the mockup's variant D, measured against 86px). Three ADR sections were amended on contact with the code — the uploader takes one added callback, the single empty control opens a picker that also carries the upload entrance, and the cascade derivation owes the deleted-DOCUMENT arm too, which §7 had not asked for.
- **J — a real-device pass on the Map's stop track** (what remains of J, **built 2026-08-11**; [ADR-0182](decisions/0182-a-day-is-a-sequence-you-can-step-through.md), [`mockups/map-stop-traversal-v2.html`](../mockups/map-stop-traversal-v2.html)) — swiping the selected place card now steps through the day's stops, with the neighbouring card's edge as the whole affordance: no rail, no arrows, no indicator, no wrap. Two numbers ship as recommendations for a phone to settle, not as decisions: the **peek width** (`--map-peek`, 20px) and the **settle delay** before the selection follows the scroll (`MAP_TRACK_SETTLE_MS`, 120ms). The question a desktop render cannot answer is whether a horizontal snap on a card sitting over a pannable canvas fights the map's own pan, and whether the selection landing on settle reads as responsive or late. **THE PASS HAPPENED (2026-08-13, session 261; [ADR-0182](decisions/0182-a-day-is-a-sequence-you-can-step-through.md)'s 2026-08-13 amendment; [session note](planning/2026-08-13-session-261-the-card-over-the-map.md)) and both of its own questions came back UNRAISED** — the snap did not fight the map's pan and the settle did not read as late. What it found instead was three defects, all shipped: the swipe was reachable only from the top of the card (two unqualified one-axis properties — `overscroll-behavior: contain` on the card's scrollers refusing to chain the HORIZONTAL gesture out, and `touch-action: pan-x` on the track taking the VERTICAL pan away from the note list, against the claim in its own comment); the card took **85% of the canvas** (~535pt of ~629pt, ~218pt of it empty), now bounded by a second `min()` cap, `--map-card-max: 420px`, which can only bind lower than the existing guarantee so a small phone is unchanged; and the card had **three dismissals and no visible one**, now `.map-cardclose` in the identity row's fourth column (0px of height, ~28px of width, running `clearSelection` itself per ADR-0103). **Still owed:** the two numbers above were not reported on either way and stay recommendations — and if 420px still reads tall, the next lever is `.map-refs-foot`'s 44 → 82px wrap (38px of pinned height), not the notes. The empty space is deliberately NOT closed: it is the equal-density decision the owner asked for, and short neighbours would bring back the sliver it replaced. Precedents for the pass: ADR-0126, ADR-0171.
- **M — The Map's base map goes blank while our pins stay** — **the production fix is BUILT (2026-08-11, session 247; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s 2026-08-11 amendment; [session note](planning/2026-08-11-session-247-the-maps-base-map-goes-blank.md)); root cause is NOT confirmed — this is not a device pass, and says so plainly.** Field report **#28**: on some devices the Map screen kept drawing the place list and the app's pins while the Google-rendered terrain/tiles stayed blank, and only restarting the app recovered it. `MapPane.tsx` now keeps its own `mapFailed` state and swaps the canvas's own slot for `ErrorState` (with a bounded, one-tap retry that remounts a fresh `<APIProvider>`/`<Map>` under a bumped `key`, never reusing the failed instance — ADR-0121 §4's one-instantiation invariant) the moment either of two signals fires: `APIProvider`'s own `onError` (a failed script load) or — since a script that loaded fine and never painted a tile has no Google-exposed event at all — `onTilesLoaded` never firing within `constants.ts`'s `MAP_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS.TILES` (10s, a `withDeadline` heuristic exactly like field reports #20/#22's, **unmeasured and owed a real device pass** to size correctly). This reads as a third reason to land in the SAME list-only outcome §11 already names for offline/no-config, not a fourth grammar. The device-pass instrument (ADR-0146) gained a matching capture for when that sitting happens: `DevMapProbe` now also publishes `webglcontextlost`/`webglcontextrestored` and online state, `DevMapTuner` gained a `diag` tab reading those plus production's own `onError`/`onTilesLoaded` signals (reused, not duplicated) and the Map ID/style. **What is still open:** nobody has confirmed WHICH failure mode #28 actually is on a real device — tile/network, WebGL context loss, SDK lifecycle, service-worker interaction, GPU-specific — the fix above does not depend on knowing, and the diagnostic capture only becomes useful once someone sits down with an affected phone. **Separate from `O` below** — this was an intermittent client-side canvas failure, that was a deterministic server-side request failure. **REOPENED the same day by field report #35** ([session 249](planning/2026-08-11-session-249-third-incremental-field-reports-addendum.md) §2): _"still cannot load the map: an error is shown and the Reload/Retry button does not help. Sometimes there is a map and sometimes there is not."_ On the strongest reading **that error and that button are the fix above** — before it the Map had no error surface at all — so detection fires and recovery does not, and the next question is which of two opposite causes it is. **(a) A genuinely persistent failure:** retry already constructs a fresh `google.maps.Map` under a new `key`, so anything cured by new per-instance state is cured — a cause that outlives the instance is script/auth/referrer, GPU/WebGL, a service-worker-cached asset, or tiles-specific network. **(b) A false positive on the heuristic:** the tiles bound is `MAP_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS.TILES` (10s), which `constants.ts:177-180` itself labels **unmeasured**, so a first paint that is merely slow reports as failed and retry re-runs the same slow load — and _"sometimes there is a map and sometimes there is not"_ is the shape of a threshold, not of a hard failure. (b) is also a **spend** defect: every retry tap is another billed instantiation. So: **read the exact Hebrew string on screen first** (it distinguishes `t.map.loadError` from the trip-level error, from offline list-only, and from a browser page error), then **measure how long a successful tiles phase actually takes on that device/network before defending the bound**, using the `diag` tab the fix already shipped. A test that mocks a successful load closes nothing. **MEASURED 2026-08-12 in real Chrome against a real Google canvas ([session 256](planning/2026-08-12-session-256-the-map-load-bound-measured-and-a-retry-nobody-can-reach.md)) — the two-readings fork is settled in favour of BOTH, and `#35` has two independent causes.** The instrument first gained the clock it lacked (`DevMapReading.tilesLoadedMs`, stamped from the instant `withDeadline` starts counting, shown in `diag` + `emit()` against the bound) and stopped misreporting the map (`mapId`/`colorScheme` now come from the config `screens/Map.tsx` **latched at mount**, not a live `mapsConfig()` re-read that after any theme flip described the canvas that would be built next — ADR-0146 §5 amended; it had invalidated every capture of those two fields). Then, on localhost — **an allowlisted referrer, so the key is accepted and TRAP 1 does not exist there** — every sample below is a **successful** paint: **647–681ms warm, 857–1472ms cold, 2.5s Fast 3G, 8158/8154/8169ms Slow 3G, 8609–8698ms Slow 3G + 4× CPU, against a 10 000ms bound.** So **(b) is real**: a correct load finishes at **82–87% of the budget** on a bad network, the margin is ~1.3s, and it is **bandwidth-bound, not CPU-bound** (4× CPU adds only ~500ms) — _"sometimes there is a map and sometimes there is not"_ is a threshold sitting just above the real distribution. **`M.1` is settled**: the string is `לא הצלחנו לטעון את המפה`, i.e. `t.map.loadError`, our own failure. **`M.5` holds**: the list stays fully usable beside the failed pane. **`M.4`: the retry machinery is sound** — and that is where the second cause turned up, below. Desktop milliseconds do not transfer to the owner's phone; the bandwidth-bound character and the ratio to the bound do. **All three fixes then BUILT 2026-08-12 ([session 257](planning/2026-08-12-session-257-the-bound-the-wait-and-the-band.md); [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) amended in place):** the bound is **20s** (double the worst measured success, and the cost is asymmetric — a script failure still surfaces instantly via `onError`); the wait is **stated** (`t.map.loading` over the canvas until `onTilesLoaded`, `pointer-events: none`, no timer and no second signal — because 20s of unexplained blank is a worse bug than the one being fixed); and **a failed pane owns its band**, the near-me card yielding through one `:has()` rule. On that last one the routed recommendation was **built and rejected on looking at it** — raising the error above the card resolved the tap correctly and read as two cards printed over each other, so it is a room question, not a z-order one. Verified in real Chrome at 360×640, the geometry that failed: `retryHittable` false→**true**, topmost `.map-gbtn`→`.fb-error-retry`. **What is still open:** whether the owner's phone crosses even 20s, and **WebGL, which is untested rather than excluded** — every session-256 sample read `webgl context lost: n/a`, meaning the probe never observed a canvas, so if #35 survives these three, making that probe work is the next step. Plus the `S`/`Q` items, which need a handset. **#35 SURVIVED them, and the third cause is now FOUND, REPRODUCED AND FIXED (2026-08-13, session 262; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s 2026-08-13 amendment; [session note](planning/2026-08-13-session-262-a-key-bump-is-not-a-retry.md)).** The owner reported it on his own phone with the lead the two previous rounds lacked — it starts **after a peer adds places to the shared trip** — and the shape is _"loading map… until the timeout, the retry gives the same result, only restarting the app fixes it."_ **`M.4` above ("the retry machinery is sound") was wrong, and wrong in a way no per-instance reasoning could catch:** `@vis.gl/react-google-maps` keeps the Maps-API loading status in **module state and writes it once** — the first attempt stamps `serializedApiParams` and only sets `LOADED` while that stamp is empty, so every later mount takes the "already loaded externally" branch, re-imports `core`/`maps` **successfully**, and never moves the status off `FAILED` (a status stuck at `LOADING` is worse: the loader returns early with no error at all, which is why the symptom **opens on the loading cue rather than on an error**). `useApiIsLoaded()` stays false, so `new google.maps.Map()` is never called: **a `key` bump builds a fresh component over a dead loader**, and one transient failure poisons the map for the life of the page. Reproduced in real Chrome by failing the first Maps-script fetch once — the retry then re-fetched it **successfully** (`google.maps.Map` present in the page) and still painted **zero canvases**, through the watchdog and three further tab visits, until a reload. `retryMap` now calls the library's `__resetModuleState()` before bumping the key; both entry paths recover on one tap (a script that **errors** → `FAILED`, and one that **hangs** on a black-holed connection → `LOADING`, cue held the full 20s first), and the happy path is unchanged at one canvas per visit. **Why the peer's edits are the trigger** — a window, not a cause: `offline = useIsOffline() || usingCachedSnapshot` makes the pane **absent** (§11) and flaps on a warm resume, so the pane remounts straight into `trip-state.tsx`'s resume burst (`flushOutbox` + paged `changes?sinceSeq=` + a hello-ahead snapshot refetch) — **which only has real work to do when a peer has written** — on a link that has just come back; and if the pane unmounts mid-load, `onError` lands on an unmounted component and the global is poisoned with **nothing on screen to report it**. **What is left:** the silently-poisoned case still costs one bound's wait before the now-working retry appears (nothing outside an `APIProvider` can read that status; auto-retry was rejected on §4's billing arithmetic), `__resetModuleState` is vis.gl's own test-only hook and should be dropped if upstream ever makes the status recoverable, and the **WebGL question is still untested rather than excluded**. **AND IT WAS NOT THE LAST CAUSE — a FOURTH is found and fixed the same day** (session 262b; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s second 2026-08-13 amendment). The owner, on the third fix shipping and the map still failing sometimes: _"first thing I would do is shorten the map load timeout by a lot … when the map has a problem loading, we're waiting for 20 seconds for nothing."_ Acting on that exposed why the bound could never be sized well: **`mapFailed` fed the ternary choosing between `ErrorState` and the `<APIProvider>` subtree, so the tiles watchdog firing UNMOUNTED the live, mid-load `google.maps.Map`** — with the `onTilesLoaded` listener that was about to resolve it. Every retry restarted from zero, so **a load genuinely needing longer than the bound could never complete**, and the observable is exactly _"reloading the map solves the problem sometimes"_: each attempt re-rolls a dice requiring the whole load to fit inside the bound. That teardown is also what forced 20s in the first place (sessions 256/257 sized it so a working Slow-3G load could not be killed by its own deadline — right under teardown semantics). **So §11's two detection signals now diverge instead of collapsing into one outcome:** `onError` (a failed _script_ load) keeps the hard `ErrorState` and `mapFailed` is that signal only; the **tiles deadline** sets a new `tilesLate`, keeps the canvas live, and changes the wait's own words to `t.map.loadingSlow` (`הטעינה איטית מהרגיל`) with a `נסו שוב` pill inside the same `.map-loading` element — one slot, so §11's one-floating-object rule holds and a wait and an error are never both on screen. A late `onTilesLoaded` retires it with nobody tapping. **It is the CHEAPER branch under §4**, which counts instantiations rather than seconds: teardown-then-retry is what buys a second billed load. With expiry no longer a verdict the asymmetry inverts, so **`MAP_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS.TILES` drops 20s → 4s** (above every session-256 success but the Slow-3G edge, which now resolves itself). Verified in real Chrome on Slow 3G: cue at 1.5s/3s, **slow notice + retry at 5s with the canvas alive and no hard error**, and **at 10s the tiles landed and the notice cleared itself** — the exact load that was a hard failure and a destroyed map before. Retry hit-tested (topmost at its centre is `.map-loading-retry`; topmost over the cue's text is the map div beneath), 44px via `ValueToken`'s `::after` idiom. **This is also the experiment:** if the map still fails, the merely-slow reading is ruled out and the next step is getting `DevMapTuner`'s `diag` reading off the owner's own phone — which needs a channel, since `dev-tuning.ts` is deliberately tree-shaken out of production and shipping the constant-override layer to reach it is the wrong trade. **IT STILL FAILED — and the REAL cause is now reproduced deterministically and fixed (2026-08-14, session 264; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s 2026-08-14 amendment; [session note](planning/2026-08-14-session-264-the-map-starts-and-then-dies.md)). `M` and field report `#28` are CLOSED, and they were one bug.** The map does not fail to _start_ — **it starts and then dies**: a phone reclaims a backgrounded page's **WebGL context**, and the canvas that comes back is blank **forever**. Reproduced with `WEBGL_lose_context.loseContext()`, measured at 26s+ with the terrain **gone**, Google's own logo and attribution still drawn, the list fine, and **no cue, no error and no recovery** — which is field report **#28** word for word, so the two reports were always the same failure. **Why nothing caught it:** `MAP_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS.TILES` guards the **first** paint, and `tilesPainted` is already true by then, so no timer is armed and no signal exists; every earlier fix addressed _the map never started_. **And session 247 named this exact event and declined to act on it**, reasoning a post-paint loss is _"recovered mid-session rather than never loaded"_ — measured, it is **not**, and that one sentence cost five rounds. The fix is `ContextLossRecovery`: listen for `webglcontextlost` (**capture phase on the pane** — the event does not bubble, and a canvas-bound listener would not survive Google replacing its canvas), **rebuild rather than restore** (`restoreContext()` redraws at the **default world camera** — the Atlantic instead of Tokyo), **deferred until the tab is visible** (a map built while hidden is the failure that started this), and **bounded at 3** because a rebuild is a billed instantiation (§4), degrading to `ErrorState` past it with a human retry resetting the budget. **Measured after, in a real browser:** a single loss recovers (cue at +500ms, map back by +2s), **exactly one loss event** so there is no rebuild loop, still recovered 10s later, four losses degrade as designed, retry recovers, and the offline-flap (2/2) and never-painted paths are unchanged. **The resume nudge is deleted** — right observation, wrong inference, and inert anyway (gated on `useMap()` returning an instance, which does not exist in the failure state). **Also corrects ADR-0186's framing:** the offline-map migration is NOT the cure for #35, since MapLibre is a WebGL canvas too and inherits context loss identically. **Still owed:** the owner's own phone — the mechanism is device-independent and the recovery automatic, but the confirming resume is theirs. **AND THAT FIX SHIPPED A REGRESSION, found and corrected the same day** (2026-08-14, session 265; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s second 2026-08-14 amendment; [session note](planning/2026-08-14-session-265-the-canvas-supervises-itself.md)). The owner: _"I feel like today we introduced some regression that has made it way worse than it was before."_ Correct, and measurable: the recovery was **bounded at three rebuilds per mount**, and a phone drops the GPU context on roughly **every** background — so an 8-cycle soak measured **3/8 healthy, dead from cycle 4**, needing a manual retry where before the map merely went blank with its chrome intact. The count was per MOUNT, so it measured _how many times this pane has ever recovered_ rather than _how badly it is failing now_ — and recovering three times is evidence the mechanism **works**. Replaced by `MAP_RECOVERY_BACKOFF_MS` (`[0, 2s, 8s, 30s, 60s]`) counting **consecutive** failures and **reset by any successful paint**, so a resume recovers immediately every time and forever, a genuinely broken GPU backs off to one attempt a minute, and **no state exists from which the map stops trying**. One pending attempt at a time, and rebuilds only while visible (an attempt due while hidden is re-asked on resume). **The never-painted route now heals too** — the tiles watchdog used to show the slow notice and wait for a human, and now schedules the same recovery. Measured: **3/8 → 8/8, and 20/20** on a longer soak; offline flap 2/2 unchanged. The regression is kept as a named unit test so a fixed budget cannot return by accident. **AND THAT PREMISE IS ALSO WRONG** (2026-08-14, session 266; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s third 2026-08-14 amendment; [session note](planning/2026-08-14-session-266-when-rebuilding-has-failed.md)). Owner: _"Reloading the map (with the button for example, or the backoff) doesn't recover the map. Once it's dead, it's dead until you switch to another app."_ A brand-new `google.maps.Map` with a brand-new canvas is still dead, so **whatever is broken outlives the map object** and no rebuild can reach it. **Not reproducible on this desktop and that is stated rather than papered over** � `WEBGL_lose_context` (20/20), a full `Browser.crashGpuProcess` (Chrome restarts the GPU process, map back in ~4s), connectivity flap (2/2) and context-budget exhaustion **all recover here**, so the real mechanism is still unidentified and the previous soak measured a kinder failure than the reported one. So the fix does the thing that IS known to work: once every backoff step has been spent, the pane escalates to a **document reload** � automatically at the next **hidden moment** (ADR-0185's own instant for the build swap: nobody looking, nothing mid-sentence, no overlay to lose), or immediately on a tap, since `ErrorState`'s action now reloads rather than rebuilding something known not to recover. Guarded to **once per 10 minutes** through `guarded-reload.ts` � `lazy-chunk.ts`'s "one reload, then stop" cooldown **extracted rather than copied** now there is a second caller (rule 8), with `isEditingField` moved there too. **A mitigation, not a root-cause fix**, and still owed: a reading from the device while it is broken. **AND THE QUOTA WAS THE FAULT ALL THE RECOVERIES WERE FEEDING** (2026-08-15, session 268; [ADR-0121](decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s 2026-08-15 amendment). The on-device diagnostic read `gl:ok canvas:ok pane:411x596 painted:n tiles:0/36 sw:activated fails:2 self:91ms goog:85ms err:none` beside Google's own overlay _"This page can't load Google Maps correctly"_, and the owner's console showed the **Maps JS load quota at 97%**. Since §4 bills **per instantiation**, six sessions of automatic rebuilds were spending the exact resource whose exhaustion caused the failure. **But quota is NOT the original cause, and the metrics prove it** — the owner: _"I only recently started approaching the quota limit. The condition existed even before."_ The 4xx series is **flat at zero across Aug 7–13** while the bug was being reported daily, and spikes (11–55%) only on Aug 13–14 — the days the rebuild loops shipped and soak probes ran against the production key. So there are **two failures**: the original (tiles never **requested**, mechanism still unidentified) and a recent self-inflicted one (loads **refused**). **All automatic map construction is removed**: `MAP_RECOVERY_BACKOFF_MS` and `MAP_REBUILDS_BEFORE_RELOAD` are deleted, and the tiles deadline, a lost context and a loader error all route through one `markFailure()`. What remains is the hidden-moment **document reload** (the only recovery ever measured to work) and a manual retry that now reloads **first**. **Two defects found while removing it:** `markFailure` must clear `tilesPainted`, because the cue, retry and diagnostic all render under `!tilesPainted` — so a **post-paint** death showed **nothing at all**, field report #28 verbatim, previously hidden by the rebuild; and `MapDiagnostic` sampled `facts` at **render**, so a second failure (changing no state → React bails out) reported `fails:1` for two dead contexts, meaning the numbers earlier amendments reasoned from **under-reported**. It now takes a getter. **What is excluded by measurement rather than inference:** WebGL, the map's own context, layout, the service worker, the network and the loader. What remains is Google's minified SDK state, which this repo cannot instrument or reset — which is the case for [ADR-0186](decisions/0186-the-map-is-ours-and-it-works-on-a-plane.md)'s renderer swap, since it moves the failing component into code we can read and ends per-instantiation billing outright.
- **N — Enrichment recall** (field reports **#29**, **#41**) — **done (2026-08-11, sessions 248 + 254; [ADR-0166 §21](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) and [§22](decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md); [session 248](planning/2026-08-11-session-248-a-letter-that-is-not-an-accent.md), [session 254](planning/2026-08-11-session-254-what-a-name-says-and-what-it-cannot-carry.md)).** **#29 (Kerið):** the `ð` fold shipped on its own evidence (Gießen: 0 → 1) but was **not** the cause; what refused Kerið was the name floor vetoing the one route that had found it, fixed by `nameCanRefuse` — a name of ours that says strictly more has not disagreed — and verified end to end on live payloads (`Q1435393`, `geosearch`, 0.8). **#41 (Brúarfoss, `מפלי גולפוס`):** reopened the same day and found to be **three further defects, none of them #29's**, each measured against the live APIs and fixed: (1) `matchByName` verified only the single best-named hit, so Wikidata's **two** `Brúarfoss` waterfalls 130km apart meant the namesake won the tie and the right entity was never read — all hits are now read in the one `wbgetentities` call the winner already cost; (2) the coordinate route is Wikipedia-shaped and `Q2557346` has **no `en`/`he` article at all**, so its pin returned nothing — **Commons category geosearch** added as a language-neutral last step; (3) a same-script label in a **different transliteration** (`גולפוס`/`גאלפוס`/`גוטלפוס`) scored 0 and vetoed the coordinate match, so tokens now match within a bounded edit distance — at **0.75 credit**, because at full credit `Kensington`/`Kennington` (4.9km apart, inside the distance veto) matched each other; (4) **the descriptor-suffix gap session 248 §2 deferred is closed**, in the form §2 itself said was the only honest one: the candidate's own `P31` class labels are read, and a word of ours that names what the candidate IS is not a disagreement — so `Brúarfoss Waterfall`/`Brúarfoss` is 1.0 while **`Tsukiji Outer Market`/`Tsukiji` still scores 0.577**; (5) aliases and article titles now reach the scorer, which the labels-only pass had never shown it. **Measured, not argued: a checked-in opt-in live probe** (`ENRICHMENT_LIVE_PROBE=1`) runs the production pipeline against the real APIs over a **170-name corpus, mostly Hebrew** (the app saves what Google returns for `languageCode=he`) with each place repeated in Latin, plus refusal controls — **114/170 → 142/170** (Hebrew 54 → 67, Latin 60 → 75), **28 names newly matched and not one newly wrong**, refusal controls re-run and holding. Permanent offline fixtures for Kerið, Brúarfoss (namesake, no-article and type-noun arms) and Hebrew Gullfoss. Every route now records its candidates and the guard that refused each, logged on a miss, so the next one is classified from a log line rather than a session. _Left open, deliberately and named in §22:_ **every remaining Hebrew miss is §15's recall hole unchanged** — the name search returns zero hits because the item has no Hebrew label, leaving distance to decide alone, which then refuses on ambiguity, on the ~238m reach of distance-only credit (`Skógafoss`'s `P625` is 294m from the pin — widening it is what §21 measured as unsafe), or on a nearer broader entity (`BROADER_INSTANCE_OF_QIDS` is still country-shaped, and a Brazilian _bairro_ is not in it). Plus two Latin footnotes: a colloquial descriptor (`Glacier Lagoon`) is not a Wikidata class label, and `wbsearchentities` matches label prefixes so `Sun Voyager` never reaches `The Sun Voyager`. One candidate fix — scoring on the nearer of the article's coordinate and the item's `P625` — is identified and **not taken**, because it relaxes the one number §21 measured as load-bearing and that wants its own measurement. **Still owed:** nobody has seen the owner's actual stored `Place` rows, and the **negative cache must be cleared on the deployment before re-testing** (~30-day miss TTL; `delete from "PlaceEnrichment"` is safe, it holds no trip data).
- **P — A derived title follows its Place while the explicit title is blank** (field reports **#30** + **#37**) — **done (2026-08-11; #30 shipped as `#565`, #37 as session 252; [session note](planning/2026-08-11-session-252-a-blank-title-is-the-places-answer.md)). No ADR: this refines behaviour no ADR owns, and ADR-0163 §3's per-type fallback is untouched.** #30 built the latch (a derived value follows its source until a person overrides it) and the owner immediately refined the semantics into a **precedence**: (1) a nonblank explicit title, (2) the selected Place name, (3) otherwise the existing refusal. `EventForm` now holds the **explicit half only** — a blank box means the Place answers, so the derived name is the input's **placeholder**, changing the Place changes the effective name, and **deleting hands control back** instead of dead-ending the save (`useDerivedField.set` latched `touched` on the keystroke that emptied the box, and `titleRequired` then refused a save nothing in the open form could cure). The precedence is one shared `effectiveTitle` read by the box's placeholder, the errand label, the save and — through the save, which persists the RESOLVED name as `BookingSheet` always has — every consumer of `Event.title`. Provenance stays a **value test, not a stored flag**: a stored title equal to what its Place derives reopens as the derivation's answer. `titleTouched` left the event draft with the flag it served; `titleAfterErrand` survives for `BookingSheet`, whose box still carries the derived name as its value.

- **Q — The Maybe shelf's remote arm and the two-device validation** (field reports **#32** + **#40**) — **#40 is DONE (2026-08-11, session 253; [session note](planning/2026-08-11-session-253-a-map-add-lands-on-the-shelf.md); [ADR-0094](decisions/0094-one-pluggable-change-applier-registry.md) and [ADR-0116](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md) amended in place).** It had **two independent causes** and neither was a reactivity failure: (1) `memoryChannels` had **no `maybeItem` entry**, so every remote idea change was mirrored into Dexie and dropped from memory — a peer's Map add reached none of the four surfaces that read `maybeItems` until a route remount, and the `Partial<Record<…>>` type is what let the hole compile; the registry is now total, as `CACHE_CHANNELS` has always been, and deleting the entry fails `tsc`. (2) With sync healthy, `SHELF_POOL_CAP = 5` ranked a just-added undated idea out of view — reproduced on a real stack at 14 ideas, where the only thing that moved was `עוד 8` → `עוד 9` — so the idea this device added last is now **pinned to the head of the strip** (owner's call; the cap was deliberately not raised, and the twelve duplicated rank/cap/tail lines in `DayView`/`PlanDay` collapsed into `lib/shelf.ts`'s `poolStrip`). **Still open, and closed by none of that:** session 244 §6's `SyncGateway` **per-process socket map** as a second cause of the same symptom on a multi-instance deployment (production is Railway; this defect reproduces on a **single** instance, so it neither confirms nor excludes it), and the **two-device validation owed to the owner** for #32 — two browser contexts on one machine is not two phones (ADR-0017). _Attempted and not run 2026-08-12 ([session 256](planning/2026-08-12-session-256-the-map-load-bound-measured-and-a-retry-nobody-can-reach.md) §4): the ask is two physical handsets and there were none. The replica side-glance was made as far as the repo can answer it — `railway.json` sets no replica count — leaving only a hand-set count on the Railway service unverified._

- **Should a pinned shelf tile say it is pinned?** (from [ADR-0116](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md)'s 2026-08-11 amendment) — the just-added idea now leads the pool strip, rendering as an ordinary tile with its own ranking reason, and `recently-added` takes no line — so nothing marks it as being there because you made it rather than because it ranked. A drawing question, not a code one; worth a mockup only if the unmarked version reads as the ranking having gone wrong.

- **S — The one date control, under a real device** (field reports **#36** + **#38**) — **both fixes BUILT (2026-08-11, session 250; [ADR-0176 §2 amended in place](decisions/0176-a-date-reads-day-first-wherever-you-open-it.md), [session note](planning/2026-08-11-session-250-the-one-date-control-under-a-real-device.md)). What is left is the DEVICE PASS, and only that.** **#38** reproduced exactly as traced — including in a real browser, where the form leaves the DOM — and is fixed at the boundary: an empty date never leaves `DateField`, a Clear restores what was showing when the picker opened (upward too, when a tentative pick had already committed one), and an incomplete keyboard entry is held on the control rather than read as a clear. `isCalendarDay` + `authoringZone` answering the primary zone for an unreadable day keep the shared helpers total whatever a boundary forwards. **#36 was CONFIRMED in one look and then fixed**: the box reads the native input's own format, clipped, whenever the field merely has focus — which after a phone's picker closes is always — so the face now steps aside for a **keystroke** rather than for `:focus-within`. Session 249's second hypothesis is **falsified and needs no work**: `min-inline-size: 0` never squeezes the token, because `.wf-line` wraps from base sizes before it flexes anything (measured 107/133/160/195px in a 356px line at 1×–2× text); what clipped was the native line inside a box the Hebrew face had sized, which is the same single cause as the numeric read. **Owed on a physical Android device or installed PWA:** that the picker's Clear and a cancel/back do not double-fire; Samsung's own font at its own scales against the wrapped Hebrew face; and a look at whether the closed field should read the full `בספטמבר` rather than `formatDayDate`'s abbreviated `בספט׳`, which the owner's acceptance text writes long and ADR-0177 §4 has always drawn short (so changing it is an amendment, and the long form must be measured at the largest text scale first). _Attempted and not run 2026-08-12 ([session 256](planning/2026-08-12-session-256-the-map-load-bound-measured-and-a-retry-nobody-can-reach.md) §4): all three checks need Samsung's own font at its own scales on a handset, and the third is an owner call to be put while the phone is out._ **Still linked to `H` below** — #36 remains a witness that a control must survive the user's text scale, and is not a reason to keep text small.

- **H — Global typography** (#3, with field report **#36** as a concrete witness — see `S` above; design-system + real-device session, then a build sweep) — "text is too small" is confirmed app-wide, not one screen, against the documented type scale in `design-language.md` (body 14.5px / secondary 12–13px / caption 11px / micro 10.5px). No approved replacement scale exists; audit representative high-density surfaces on a real phone and update `design-language.md` (plus the decision that owns the scale) once the owner approves new numbers. Representative type-scale proof, not every screen mocked.

## The practical layer's two absent v1 rows (untracked until 2026-08-18)

Both are **Should / v1** in [`product/feature-catalog.md`](product/feature-catalog.md) and named together in [`product/prd-v1.md`](product/prd-v1.md)'s practical layer, and neither had a backlog line — which is how they stayed invisible beside the FX half that shipped in the same section.

- **Weather** — the slot is built and already reserved for it: ADR-0045 §4 promised “Weather / FX return as themselves … as their own glance cards”, the `מבט מהיר` section is back carrying `RateCard`, and both `rate-card.css` and `Home.tsx` name weather as that section's **second tenant** in comments. So this is a provider plus a card, not an open design question — and `FxService` is the shape to copy rather than invent (global cached row, serve-stale-never-block, a refresh riding the snapshot read, a `_DISABLED` kill switch, and the Dexie mirror that is what makes it work offline at all). Still to decide: the source, and whether a forecast is per-day (the day rail) or current-only (the glance card).
- **Emergency numbers by country** — **zero references anywhere in the tree**, and the only promised row nothing has ever tracked. `COUNTRY_CURRENCY` / `MULTI_ZONE_COUNTRIES` in `packages/shared/src/destinations.ts` are the precedent to follow exactly: a static table keyed off the `destinationCountryCode` ADR-0113 already stores, same “a miss degrades, never a wrong answer” contract. Static is the point — it is the one practical-layer row that has to work with no network and no trip data, which is exactly when it is wanted.

## Dates & locale

- **`button.bld-time` still draws its own chip** (follow-up from [ADR-0177](decisions/0177-a-when-reads-as-a-sentence.md), 2026-08-09) — the token primitive took its grammar and its `::after` target trick from the Plan builder's row time, and deliberately did not convert it: the two share the vocabulary and almost no values (12.5px vs body, plan violet vs amber, composite two-line content, an `empty` variant with an icon), so sharing a component would mean the primitive growing an option per difference or Plan mode overriding nearly all of it. Worth revisiting only if a third inline-editable value appears — at which point the shared shape earns the options. Both files name the lineage so this reads as one grammar, not two inventions.

- **A date reads day-first, wherever you open the app** — **done (2026-08-09; [ADR-0176](decisions/0176-a-date-reads-day-first-wherever-you-open-it.md); [session note](planning/2026-08-09-session-234-a-date-reads-day-first.md)).** Owner screenshot: the booking sheet's check-in read `08/09/2026`. A native `<input type="date">` is formatted by the platform, and session 14's `DEVICE_LOCALE` had made that official — so the fix is one `APP_LOCALE` and a `DateField` primitive drawing the value itself over the real input (six hand-rolled date inputs collapse into it). The "seek everything" sweep the report asked for took five more with it, including a weekday letter that named the day before for any trip west of UTC. _Left open by decision:_ nothing here needs a second locale yet — `APP_LOCALE` is the one place a second UI language would start, and no other date site is outstanding.

- **The booking row is crowded** — **BUILT (2026-08-09, sessions 237–238; [ADR-0179](decisions/0179-a-booking-row-says-what-then-when-and-the-code-is-a-read.md), mockup [`booking-row-crowding-v1`](../mockups/booking-row-crowding-v1.html), [session note](planning/2026-08-09-session-237-the-booking-row-is-crowded.md)).** ADR-0178's defect one screen over: `.wp-listrow-right` is `flex: 0 0 auto`, so the confirmation code — the one unbounded string any `ListRow` host puts in that slot — takes 133px and leaves the title **43px of 330** at 360px. **Built as drawn, with four divergences in the ADR's build log** — the biggest being rule 8: `.wp-event-timelock` and `.bld-timelock` were byte-identical, so the lock became one `HardLock` (`ui/when-line.css`) with three consumers rather than a third copy, and `.when-dur` collected the duration's vocabulary. Measured on the **real components**: title **182px at 360px / 212px at 390px** against the mockup's predicted 184/214. A render-only defect was caught and fixed on the way (`dir="auto"` on the clock made the box LTR, putting the `::before` separator on the wrong side of it — the isolate moved to an inner `<bdi>`). 17 tests added; the row's contract had no guard at all before, which is how three visible facts could come off it with 3183 tests green. **What shipped:** the code, the type chip and the 🔗 glyph came off the row (the detail already states all three, more precisely); the transition verb stays only on a span's **closing** edge, where it is the only thing that can say which end the time is; the when line becomes a sentence with the clock as its subject, which needs **`scheduleLabel` to return parts rather than a joined string** (ADR-0152 §6c's wall — flex cannot protect half a text node), with `.bk-day` fixed and `.bk-verb` shrinking so the fold never eats the day; the lock moves to the when line; the note/doc marks move to the title line; `typeChipAddsMeaning` gains a second term (the chip survives only where `chosenIcon` has overridden the badge glyph). `ListRow`'s contract does **not** change — the one shared edit is its meta line becoming a flex container. Nothing here is a new mechanism. **Still open, deliberately not folded in:** whether `עוד 33 ימים` is the right fact for an index surface at all, or whether a date (ADR-0176) reads better for "when is my flight" — a change to the fact rather than to the crowding, and the next thing worth measuring. **Device pass owed** (ADR-0017): whether the muted day beside a full-ink clock reads as hierarchy or as something switched off.

## Re-filing what is already saved (owner report, 2026-08-12)

- **You cannot change a category in an edit** (owner, 2026-08-12) — **BUILT (2026-08-12; [ADR-0183](decisions/0183-what-a-thing-is-is-editable-and-the-statement-is-the-control.md), designed and shipped the same day in [#579](https://github.com/assafmanor/waypoint/pull/579)), plus three follow-up reports off the built surface, all fixed** ([session note](planning/2026-08-12-session-256-a-category-you-can-change.md) §7): the chooser did not collapse after a pick; the update payload **never carried `type`**, so every other field saved and the category silently did not; and an edit now offers `שמירה` beside `הבא` through `FormActions`' new `alternate` slot. Design record: [`mockups/category-on-a-booking-and-a-note-v1.html`](../mockups/category-on-a-booking-and-a-note-v1.html) + [session note](planning/2026-08-12-session-256-a-category-you-can-change.md). No ADR yet** — one is owed on approval, since this revises a premise (a saved booking's type is immutable) that no ADR ever took. The report is one sentence over two mechanisms: a **note** has `Note.category` and a **booking** has none by decision (ADR-0038's 2026-07-19 amendment: the type owns the category, and the amendment exists because a picked glyph writing a category silently broke nights, check-in/out bracketing and the ambient backdrop). Shape settled with the owner on the mockup's first draft: **the statement already at the top of the editor becomes the control**, and the chooser reveals in place through the shared `Collapsible` — no step (a step is paid on every pass through the form and this is a rare edit: create stays at four steps, edit at three), no second overlay, 0px until touched. The `ChoiceGrid` sentinel pill (`IndexNotesView`'s `הכל` shape) is the way back to "no category" and to inheritance, which is the other half of the report. Build carries four pieces, each independently reviewable: the `ChoiceDisclosure` + `CategoryField` pair (the latter a straight extraction of three existing call sites), `BookingSheet`'s `isCreate` gate on the type control, `HostNotes` resolving its `NoteHostRef` from the one `buildNoteHosts` index, and §5's schema line.
  - **A lossy switch warns, at the moment it takes the fields off the form** (owner's third and fifth calls, 2026-08-12) — a `ConfirmDialog` on the **tap**, gated on a lossy switch so a switch that drops nothing is silent and instant. Reversed from save-time by the fifth call: the tap is what removes the route field, the span's end and the hotel block **from the form**, so it is the last moment the warning's subject is on screen — and save-time has a broken cancel, landing you back in a form whose boxes are already gone with nothing saying how to restore them. (A broken cancel path is the tell that a confirm sits at the wrong moment; it is also why the cancel label moved `ביטול` → `המשך עריכה` → `ביטול`, since the label could not be settled without settling the moment.) The copy is **three words** — `חלק מהפרטים יימחקו.` (the owner's wording; also the accurate one, since the fields are deleted on save rather than merely not carried, and it matches `manage.plainBody`'s one-future-sentence pattern), with `המשך עריכה` rather than `ביטול` as the cancel because this fires from **שמירה** in a form that already has a discard confirm. No itemised losses, no `consequence` row, and no in-grid banner beside it (owner's fourth call, same day: _"really short and no need to list everything that will be deleted"_ — and one warning in one place is also what keeps session 246's rule). The mechanism is therefore a **boolean**, `switchIsLossy(from, to, booking)` in `lib/booking-edit.ts` computed from `BOOKING_TYPE_PROFILE`'s axes, not a list anyone phrases; the itemisation lives in the mockup's §2c matrix as documentation, which is what makes "חלק מהפרטים" checkable. Explicitly **not** "everything resets": `hotel → restaurant` drops three things (the end clock and the day spread, the room+WiFi that `BookingSheet.tsx:633-635` already prunes on a non-hotel save, and conditionally the derived round-trip pairing) and carries eight across. Overstating sends the user back to delete-and-recreate, which is what this item exists to end; refusing the switch outright blocks the report itself.
  - **The wire cannot express a type change, and this blocks the feature** — `updateBookingSchema` is `createBookingSchema.partial()` with `placeId`/`fromPlaceId`/`toPlaceId` `.optional()` rather than `.nullish()`, so `null` never reaches the server; `bookings.service.ts:116` merges `before` under the NEW type and `assertPlaceShape` throws **400** on any switch across the route↔single axis. Fix is one word per field, in the wording the schema already uses beside `startDisplayTimezone` (**null clears**), mirrored to `schema.prisma` (rule 3).
  - **Clearing a booking's place is a silent no-op TODAY** (same root, independent of the above) — `pickPlace(undefined)` sends `{ placeId: undefined }`, `JSON.stringify` drops the key, the server keeps the old place. Fixed by the same schema line; worth its own test.
  - **`NoteSheet:132` omits `.category-pills`**, so the note editor's category pills render at the Index **filter** density — measured 28px against the 35px `EventForm`/`MapPlaceForm` get. Disappears by construction once the three call sites become `CategoryField`.
  - **`buildNoteHosts` drops `Place.category`** on the strength of an ADR-0147 comment that [ADR-0165](decisions/0165-a-place-says-what-it-is.md) superseded — so a note on a place cannot inherit what the place now says it is. One line.
  - **The reveal-into-view is hand-rolled three times and missing in the fourth editor** — `BookingSheet:931`, `EventForm:673`, `DocumentUploadSheet:116` each carry the same `onFocusCapture` → `scrollIntoView({ block: 'center' })`; `NoteSheet` has none. Found by rendering: at 360×640 the booking form with the grid revealed measures 686px against a 640px screen, and it only self-scrolls because the new statement is a focusable `<button>`. Lift the three onto one place rather than adding a fourth.
  - **Left unbuilt, deliberately, and each on its own evidence:** widening `inert` into `Collapsible` itself (four other call sites, none a radiogroup); and lifting the `onFocusCapture` → `scrollIntoView` reveal, hand-rolled in `BookingSheet`/`EventForm`/`DocumentUploadSheet` and absent in `NoteSheet`, onto one place.
  - **The mockup's one open fork is resolved, and it splits by MODE** (owner, 2026-08-13; **BUILT**, [ADR-0183](decisions/0183-what-a-thing-is-is-editable-and-the-statement-is-the-control.md) §4's amendment): whether the note's 44px statement row is worth it on a form whose whole point is to ask nothing ([ADR-0152](decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) §6b) has the answer **on an edit yes, on a create no** — the collapse earns its tap where there is a saved answer to state, and on a brand-new note it is a tap to open plus a tap to close paid for nothing. So `NoteSheet` passes `disclosure` only when it holds a `note`, and the category **leads the form in both modes**, as `EventForm`'s already does (ADR-0109 §11) and as `BookingSheet`'s type row already did. Nothing is asked either way: the leading pill arrives selected, carrying the value in force and its source. In a booking there was never a fork: the row exists today and the change cost 5px.

## A booking edge can be a window (owner brief, 2026-08-12)

- **Check-in is a range, not an instant** (owner, 2026-08-12) — **BUILT (2026-08-13, session 258; [ADR-0184](decisions/0184-an-edge-can-be-a-window.md), mockup [`an-edge-can-be-a-window-v1.html`](../mockups/an-edge-can-be-a-window-v1.html), [design session note](planning/2026-08-13-session-257-an-edge-can-be-a-window.md)).** Reading the code reframed the ask: `edgeMeaning` already derived `not-before` for a held span's start, so `15:00` on a check-in ALREADY meant `[15:00, end of day]` — the feature closes the open end of a window the app already drew, which scoped the control with no type check (any edge that is not `exact`: two hotel ends, two hire ends). `TimeMeaning` gains `window`, an authored one wins over the profile — the seam ADR-0171 §2 named, arriving as **data** rather than a field — and the model reaches an ordinary event even though the form deliberately does not (§8: a window and a duration draw the same pixels meaning the opposite). Two nullable `Event` instants + one migration, nothing to backfill. The affordance is a second `ValueToken` in the line that already exists, drawn dashed-when-empty and removable through `TimeField`'s own `onClear`, with the impossible bound **prevented** by a new `maxTime` rather than refused. A closed window regains a list position in **Trip and Plan from one `placeDayEntries`** and still earns no map number; the range reads **under** the title (45px of a long hotel name against 20px of row height) and goes through `ltrIsolate`. The hero drops `CHECKIN_GRACE_MIN` wherever a real ceiling exists, promotes a closing window above a departure, and can say a lodging edge was **missed** for the first time — one CSS rule in the existing `--miss`. **The gap needed nothing and the audit is why we know:** all six `edgeMeaning`/`isExactEdge` consumers were counted, five test `=== 'exact'` and are correct by construction, and `glance.ts` was the one that named a flexible value — untouched it would have counted a check-in whose window shut at 21:00 until midnight. _Left open by decision:_ whether a NARROW window should bound a gap (§5's "consumes no particular hour" is shaky at thirty minutes and any threshold is a guess), whether the control ever opens to ordinary events, and ADR-0017's device pass on the two new pieces of CSS.
  - **The device pass ran the same day and reversed §4 and §5** — **BUILT (2026-08-13, session 261; [ADR-0184 §9](decisions/0184-an-edge-can-be-a-window.md), amending [ADR-0171 §10a](decisions/0171-a-time-can-be-a-floor-or-a-ceiling.md) and [ADR-0064 §C](decisions/0064-day-transition-entries-and-home-band-trim.md)).** Three owner reports off the shipped build, and §4's split is what made the other two visible. **Every** edge holds a position now and the stay keeps its ambient line on its edge days (`staysOnDate`), because a floor in the strip and a window in the list was one hotel reading two ways depending on whether a second number had been typed. That needed somewhere defensible for a floor to sit, so §10b's `deadlineAt` generalises into `edgeAt`: an overlapping hard event bounds either end (excluding a `held` span — holding a car from 09:00 is not being occupied by it, which §10b's own test caught), and a journey bounds from outside its span (departing before a ceiling, landing after a floor). Overlap alone was drawn first and does **not** fix the report — the flight under that 15:00 check-in departed at 15:30. `edgeHoldsPosition` deleted. **The settle pair moved with the floors** and that is a count, not parity: `glance.ts` holds a `not-before` edge in `נותרו היום` until `DONE`, so without it the owner's 2026-08-04 number sticks all evening. §5 reversed too — the bare clock joins the range under the title, and the `dir="auto"` §5 relied on was resolving the **box** to ltr, so `text-align: start` meant left and the range hung off the wrong margin of an RTL card. Plus one authoring bug: the window token said `＋ עד` on both edges, but an end edge stores the _earliest_ check-out, so `06:00` + `עד 11:00` rolled a day back into a 19-hour window rendering `11:00–06:00`; the word is per edge now and `windowBoundIso` is untouched. _Left open by decision:_ a same-day **round trip** pushes a floor to its return landing, because only the place graph knows whether a leg relocated you and a flight's airport never matches a hotel's city; and ADR-0017's device pass on the row's new height.
    - **And the strip on those edge days still said `לילה 1 מתוך 1`** — **BUILT (2026-08-13, session 261 follow-up; [ADR-0184 §9f](decisions/0184-an-edge-can-be-a-window.md), amending [ADR-0064 §C](decisions/0064-day-transition-entries-and-home-band-trim.md) again).** §9a put the line on the edge days and left the day count on it, so two guesthouses on one day — one being left, one being arrived at — read identically. An edge day states the **edge** now (`צ׳ק-אאוט · עד 09:40`, `צ׳ק-אין · 17:00–20:00`, `החזרת הרכב · עד 18:00`; the car needs no branch because `transitionLabel` resolves per profile), a middle day keeps `N מתוך M`. `${label} · ${phrase}` is not a new shape — it is what `UnplacedCommitment` rendered in this same box until §9a emptied it, which is what settled it against a bare-label alternative. `edgeTimePhrase`/`edgeSentence` are shared with the row off `edgeEntryOf(placement.positioned, …)`, so one edge cannot print two clocks, and `.day-ambient .an` now ellipsises so the read-out is what survives a long guesthouse name. **Fixed a bug in §9b while doing it:** the range was built from the `edgeAt`-bounded `atMs`, so a 17:00–20:00 window pushed to a 22:00 landing rendered `20:00–22:00` — a window nobody typed, hiding that the real one had shut. A window reads its two **authored** numbers now; a single clock still reads the placed instant (§10b). _Left open:_ whether a windowed edge should show an INTERSECTED window (`06:00–09:40` against an 09:40 departure) — a real question, and inventing an answer is how the §9b bug happened.

## Eight document types: the picker, the badges, and a search (owner reports, 2026-08-13)

- **The type picker was three rows of cards, and two badges were the same white page** — **BUILT (2026-08-13, session 260; [ADR-0052](decisions/0052-document-lifecycle-view-manage-and-feedback.md) §6/§7, mockup [`document-types-in-one-row-v1.html`](../mockups/document-types-in-one-row-v1.html)).** `DocumentTypePills` is the third host of the pill row `CategoryField` has run at nine options since ADR-0109 §11 — zero new CSS, 202px to 38px — and `reservation` took 🛎️ off 🧾, which was 📄's silhouette twin at badge size.
