The render is what turned "ugly and hard to use" into a number: the grid put the upload sheet's primary action 140px below its own `80vh` fold.
  - **Still open, and the mockup closed the room half of it:** whether `.category-pills` rises to ADR-0017's 44px floor. The pill measures 36px today — so does the **shipped** event-category selector — and at 44px the documents row is 46px with still 0px of scroll, so cost is not the argument. The scope is: the wrapper is shared with `EventForm`, `NoteSheet` and `MapPlaceForm`, whose card height is arithmetic (ADR-0148 §1). Needs one owner call, then it is a one-line change plus a device pass.
- **Documents had no search** — **BUILT (2026-08-13, session 260; ADR-0052 §8).** Worded type chips + `SearchOverlay`, the bookings density the brief's parenthetical asked for, and the query matches the type label singular and plural plus a sparse synonym table (so `כרטיס` and `בורדינג` both find the boarding pass). **The type-grouped list stays** (owner's call, against a recommendation to let the chips replace it) — which is why the results reuse the screen's own grouped renderer and the whole search ships zero new CSS. Groups and rows both collapse through `RevealRow`/`RevealList`; nothing is filtered out of an array (ADR-0120).

## The app freezes on a blank screen after a deploy (owner report, 2026-08-13)

- **The build swapped underneath the open page, and there was no error boundary to catch what broke** — **BUILT (2026-08-13, session 263; [ADR-0185](decisions/0185-a-build-swaps-whole-or-not-at-all.md)).** `registerType: 'prompt'` + `skipWaiting: false` makes the swap atomic (a waiting worker leaves the old precache whole, so a lazy route can no longer 404 into a blank page), the build then takes itself when the tab hides / before the first tap / after 5 min idle and never over an open sheet or a focused field, and the reload hangs off `controllerchange` rather than the plugin's heuristic-gated `onNeedReload`. Plus the app's first `AppErrorBoundary` and `lazyRoute`'s one-shot self-heal. Verified against two real builds by `scripts/deploy-swap-check.mjs`.
  - **Still open, and deliberately so:** the harness is Chromium-only, so the hidden-edge apply on a **suspended iOS PWA** is unverified — the one platform where the OS may discard the page before the reload lands. Worth a real-device pass on the next field round rather than a build.
  - **Noticed while there, not fixed:** `componentDidCatch` writes to `console.error` and nothing else, because no crash reporting exists in this app. A blank screen with a silent console is what made the money.ts one hard to place; a real sink is its own decision, not this one's.

## Plan home asks for a hotel on nights nobody sleeps in one (owner report, 2026-08-14)

- **A night is only a night if there is a bed-shaped gap in it** — **BUILT (2026-08-14; [ADR-0061](decisions/0061-plan-home-readiness-rework.md)'s 2026-08-14 amendment).** The lodging check credited exactly one thing, a `hotel` booking's span, so every night spent in the air, on an overnight bus, or awake waiting for a 01:00 flight read as an uncovered night on a trip that was fully prepared. `nightNeedsABed` now measures the **sleepable stretch** inside a 22:00→08:00 trip-local window: it subtracts the time you were **in motion** and the time you were **somewhere else** (a leg out of the destination ends your presence, a leg into it starts one), and a night with less than five hours left in it is not a night the check asks about. The two subtractions are both load-bearing and the owner's own second report is why — an overlap test alone scores a 01:00 flight **out** and a 01:00 flight **in** identically, and they are opposite facts. `Booking.type` gained an `inMotion` axis (`spendsSpanInMotion`) rather than reusing `carriesRoute`, because a **car hire** carries a route and spans two instants while being parked through every night of it. Lodging-category events now cover their nights without a booking, so the friend's spare room stops reading as a gap. The check's denominator is the nights that need a bed, not all trip nights.
  - **Deliberately still open, and the reason the waiver may yet be worth building:** an **untimed** leg subtracts nothing, so a 01:00 flight entered with no clock on it still asks for a hotel. Every degradation here leaves the check open rather than passing it falsely (ADR-0061's own posture), which means the residual is always a false _nag_, never a false all-clear. **Phases 2-3 of the plan — a stored per-night waiver — are not built and may never be**; revisit only if the untimed-leg case shows up in a real trip.
  - **Not credited, on purpose:** an unbooked transport _event_. It carries no `BookingType`, so a taxi and a five-day car hire are the same shape to the derivation, and crediting the wrong one is the false pass the whole check is built to avoid.

## The Hebrew was two apps talking (owner ask, 2026-08-17)

- **One register, one word per thing** — **BUILT (2026-08-17, session 258; [ADR-0138](decisions/0138-the-row-menu-is-one-surface-and-icons-are-ui.md)'s 2026-08-17 amendment withdrawing §6, and a new **Voice and register** entry in [design-language.md](design/design-language.md)).** The ask was "make the Hebrew more natural, sometimes more casual", and the read found the problem was not word choice: the onboarding and hero copy is already the target voice (`עוד פעם ויוצאים מהטיול 👋`, `יאללה, יש טיול 🎉`, `היום עוד פתוח`, `אפשר לנשום`) and it never reached the utility surfaces. Three structural findings, ~120 strings: (a) `he.ts` held **~60 singular-masculine imperatives against ~46 plural ones**, colliding on the same screens (`הוסף מסמך` beside `העלו`; `index.sheet.save: 'שמור'` beside the canonical `common.save: 'שמירה'`; one clear action spelled three ways) — and the singular _genders_ an app whose subject is a mixed group of five, which is the half consistency alone does not fix; (b) **seven words for the same five people** (חבורה · חבר'ה · חברים · משתתפים · מטיילים · נוסעים · קבוצה), now `החבר'ה` for the group and `נוסעים` for the people in it; (c) **`הזמנה` meant two things** — a booking across the whole Index and an invitation in the join flow, so `טוען הזמנה…` read as "loading booking"; an invitation is a `לינק` now.
  - **Three carve-outs, named so a later pass does not "finish" them:** a disclosure toggle keeps `הצג`/`הסתר` (the owner's 2026-08-16 pair), a stepper keeps its imperative (`דחייה 15 דק׳` is not a thing anyone says), and an act on someone else's state takes the infinitive — `להפוך למנהל`, after `מינוי כמנהל` was rejected mid-session as _"very formal"_.
  - **One truth bug fixed on the way in.** `tasks.filter.noResults` had just become `הכל סגור`, which is a claim and not an empty state: under the `הושלמו` facet with nothing settled it said "everything is closed" about a list of open tasks, and under `שלי` it spoke for other people's rows. One string serves three facets, so it may not describe any — `אין כלום כאן`.
  - **The tests were the other half.** Eleven assertions across five files had the old wording frozen as literals; they read `t.*` now, so the next copy change cannot break them. Two of those sentences share a text node with a neighbour, which is why they assert on the container rather than on `getByText`.
  - **The sweep, and why it is not bigger than it is (CI, same session).** The reworded copy broke 11 unit assertions and 5 e2e ones, all frozen literals, so every test that looks for a string the component reads from `t` now reads **the same key** — 50 sites across 23 files, proven rather than guessed: a literal was only rewritten where the component under test (or its own imports, two levels) actually references that exact path. A value-match sweep would have rewritten 189, and 84 of those paths were ambiguous — it wanted to couple `BookingDetail` to a search-synonym array entry and `ChoiceDisclosure`'s own ad-hoc props to `t.eventForm.kindLabel`. **Left alone deliberately:** labels a spec supplies as PROPS (`ListRow`, `ConfirmDialog`, `SearchField`, `TimeField`, `MaybeCard`, `ChoiceDisclosure` all take their words that way), seeded fixture data (`ev('קשיח', …)` is an event title), and three value coincidences where the matching string is a fragment INSIDE a `he.ts` function (`zoneNoteTransport`'s `קדימה`, `day.join.free`'s `פנוי`) so no key equals it. The rule is in `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s Testing section.
  - **The sharp edge found on the way, and it is the quiet one:** an **absence** assertion against a stale literal goes vacuous and stays green forever. `queryByText('הפוך למנהל')` and `queryByText('הסר מהטיול')` both kept passing after those verbs were reworded, so `RosterSheet`'s "offers no admin verbs" was asserting nothing at all. Same shape as `new RegExp('הזז')` still matching `הזזה`. Loud failures announce themselves; these do not.
  - **A pre-existing e2e flake, recorded because it cost time to rule out:** `e2e/shelf-drag.spec.ts` ("the held card follows the finger", :460 as of 2026-08-22) fails roughly one run in two on the same tree with a drag-ghost offset of ~37px against a 3px tolerance, and passes in isolation. Not introduced here (it passed in the CI run for this branch) and not diagnosed. It is a measured-geometry assertion under a synthetic touch drag, so the suspect is the gesture's timing, not the tolerance.
  - **Deliberately still gendered, and the narration is why:** the change feed reports what a _named_ person did, so Hebrew demands a grammatical subject. A verbal noun drops the actor (`הזזה של האירוע` says nothing about who) and so does the passive. Masculine by convention, documented at `changeFeed` in `he.ts`. Worth revisiting only if `Member` ever carries a pronoun.
  - **Not swept, and it is a real remainder:** the ~40 `:hover` rules the frontend `CLAUDE.md` already backlogs are untouched, and so is every English-language string in the codebase. This pass was `he.ts` only.

## A hard event answers the hold (owner report, 2026-08-21)

[ADR-0199](decisions/0199-a-hard-event-answers-the-hold.md) is **built** (2026-08-21). What the render behind it found and left open:

- **`button.bld-time`'s touch target is 43px, one short of ADR-0017's 44px floor** (found 2026-08-21 by rendering [`mockups/a-hard-event-answers-the-hold-v1.html`](../mockups/a-hard-event-answers-the-hold-v1.html); not introduced by it). The chip renders 27px and its `::after` overlay is `inset: -8px 0`. ADR-0161 §7 chose the overlay deliberately — `min-height: 44px` on the chip itself took the row from 58px to 75px — but never measured where the overlay landed. `-8.5px` closes it at no cost to the row, since the overlay reaches into vertical padding no other control occupies.

## A day steps with a swipe (owner report, 2026-08-21)

[ADR-0200](decisions/0200-a-day-steps-with-a-swipe-and-the-shell-stops-scrolling.md) is **built** (2026-08-21) — both halves of the report. What it leaves open:

- **`gateEdgeStep` is asked at three moments and nothing states the rule once** (ADR-0116 §2d's fifth repair, 2026-08-22): at the press, at a row lifted inside an inline band, and now at a page arriving under a finger that wandered. All three are _the drag's geometry changed without the hand choosing it_, and each was discovered as a defect rather than derived. Worth one pass over the other gesture surfaces that read a live position — the Map's canvas drag, the sheet's snap — asking the same question, before a fourth one is found the same way.
- ~~**A full-page reverse on the edge-drag day step that a fast finger triggers "every time"**~~ **fixed 2026-08-22** ([ADR-0116](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md) §2d's seventh repair). Reproduced from the owner's screen recording, which is what the six previous rounds were missing: the day itself goes back — a step made and then unmade as the hand crosses to the other edge — where every sweep had been looking for a reverse **animation** on the same day. The far band is now latched the first time a drag reaches it while the step it just made is still on screen (`DRAG_DAY_REVERSE_MS`, the same window that prices the cheap undo), so going back has to be asked for the way every other band here is asked for. Measured both ways in the browser: without the gate, two reverse page turns in three dwells.
- **The edge day step's two feel numbers want the device pass** ([ADR-0116 §2d](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md), **built 2026-08-22**): `DRAG_DAY_EDGE_PX` (36, the band a drag has to reach) and `DRAG_DAY_LIFT_PX` (48, how far the strip lifts before it stops). **Now four numbers, not two** (and the lift is spent once per approach — the surface rests at LEVEL between days, so what wants judging on glass is whether one lift on the way in is affordance enough for a hold that turns several days) — the repair round added the band's exit hysteresis (`DRAG_EDGE_SCROLL_RELEASE_PX`, reused rather than a new constant) and the reversal pair (`DRAG_DAY_REVERSE_MS` 2s · `DRAG_DAY_REVERSE_DWELL_MS`, half the dwell), neither of which is drawn anywhere: whether 2s is the right window, and whether half is enough to feel like an undo, are device questions of exactly the same kind. Both ship at the drawing's recommendation and are wired as controls in [`mockups/a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v2.html`](../mockups/a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v2.html) (⁦40 · 48 · 64 · 80px⁩, plus the lift's easing) so the alternatives can be looked at rather than argued. 48 is a floor rather than a taste — below it the lift shows page background and none of tomorrow, since the 24px gutter is crossed first — so the device question is how much **above** it reads as "the page is cocked" at arm's length, and whether the band wants any mark of its own. §2c's `--edge-reveal` is gone with the pane-only lean.
- **Two feel levers named and measured but not pulled** ([ADR-0200 §9](decisions/0200-a-day-steps-with-a-swipe-and-the-shell-stops-scrolling.md), 2026-08-22, after the owner reported the turn as _"not smooth enough"_ and four other things were fixed for it). **The per-frame style cost:** `--swipe-dx` inherits, so every frame of a swipe invalidates style for the day plus both peek panes — ~1.1–1.6ms per move for a 241-node subtree on a desktop CPU, scaling with the day's node count (0.43ms at 29 nodes). `will-change` does not touch it (matched arms, 36.0 vs 32.4ms over 30 moves). The lever is to stop inheriting it: a registered `@property { inherits: false }` written to each moved box, or the panes' transforms computed in JS — the first costs the pager a way to find its movers, the second moves geometry the stylesheet owns into the recogniser. **The settle's duration:** always `--t-base`, whether 20px or 380px of travel remain, so a nearly-complete drag decelerates from a standstill instead of carrying the finger's momentum. A remaining-travel duration is the fix, published as one variable so the CSS and the JS timer cannot disagree — but it interacts with §8's wait for the arriving render, so pull it with a phone in hand.
- **`SWIPE_PAGER`'s numbers want a real-device pass**, in exactly the sense ADR-0182's peek width does. Five of the six ship as recommendations: `SLOP_PX` (24) and `COMMIT_SHARE` (0.22) are the feel — does a step ask too much or too little of a thumb — and `EDGE_RESIST` (0.28) / `EDGE_MAX_PX` (40) are whether the refusal at the trip's ends reads as a refusal or as a stutter. **`DECIDE_PX` (6) is the one that is not a preference:** it is pinned under Chrome's ~8px touch slop because the axis has to be answered before the browser claims the touch, and if iOS Safari's slop differs that is the number to move. The question a desktop render cannot answer is whether deciding the axis at 6px feels decisive or twitchy on glass.
- **A gesture that starts horizontal and turns vertical will not scroll for the rest of that touch** — inherent to claiming an axis at 6px (ADR-0200 §4), and worth a sentence of device feedback rather than a fix, since the alternative is the gesture not existing.
- **The page turn's own device pass** (§6/§7, added the same day from two owner reports: preview the day you are turning to, and open a day at its top). Three things a desktop render cannot answer. Whether **two extra day surfaces at the claim** are felt as a hitch on a loaded day — the cost is one render per gesture rather than per frame, and if it bites, the lever is what the pane renders and not how often. Whether the **turn's length** (`--t-base` for a commit, `--t-quick` for a refusal) reads right when the remaining travel is a nearly-full page versus a few px. And whether a **preview of _today_** showing the day's top while committing lands on the now-line is noticed at all — one day of the trip, and the alternative is a preview guessing where a watch loop will finish. **Two of these came back on 2026-08-22** and neither was the one expected: the turn read as **not smooth** (four causes, all measured and fixed in §9 — a 20px dead zone with a 24px jump, the refusal's overshoot spent on a commit, three unpromoted layers, and no velocity term) and **quick swipes did not register** (the flick, §9d). The two-extra-renders question is still unreported either way. **One more, added by §8 (2026-08-22):** the turn now ends by waiting for the arriving day's own render rather than resetting on a timer, which is what makes the swap unobservable — so on a slow phone with a loaded day the question is whether that wait is felt as a **pause** at the end of the turn. If it is, the lever is what a day surface costs to render, not the wait, and the number to watch is the two extra day renders above.
- **Two more `setTimeout`s that wait out a CSS motion, unaudited** (found while fixing ADR-0116 §2d's unwind jitter, 2026-08-22): the pager's three waits now anchor to a `requestAnimationFrame` because a timer started at the JS call fires inside the transition it is waiting for — measured at 30ms of drift behind a busy main thread. `grep` for `motionDurationMs` shows other callers doing the same thing (the overlay exit, the route direction, `one-shot.ts`'s beat removal), and they have the same exposure: the drift is invisible until the state being dropped is one a transition still needs. Not a reported defect anywhere else yet, and worth one sweep rather than six discoveries.
- **A third e2e flake in the same class, and the arms are recorded because ruling it out is the expensive part:** `e2e/shelf-drag.spec.ts`'s _"a builder row still auto-scrolls on its second drag"_ failed once in a full-suite run on 2026-08-22 and then passed **3/3** runs of its own file and a second full-suite run (259/260, the one skip aside) on the identical tree. Its failure text was not captured. The only time-bounded assertion in it is `expect.poll(scrollTop, { timeout: 3000 })` waiting for the vertical auto-scroll, so the suspect is the same one the two entries around it name: a budget that is generous alone and tight behind two Playwright workers and a dev server. Not diagnosed, and worth fixing as one sweep with them rather than three times.
- **A second pre-existing e2e flake, measured rather than suspected:** `e2e/event-arrival-scroll.spec.ts` fails roughly **1 run in 25** — **both** of its cases, seen on Plan-day and on Trip-day, so it is the file's shared arrival machinery rather than either surface. Established at **51/52** on a base with no change to it, while trying to pin it on ADR-0200's shell fix; that ADR's §1 writes up the wrong turn, which cost an hour and is the more useful half of it. Same class as the `shelf-drag.spec.ts` "held card follows the finger" entry above — a measured-geometry assertion, here behind a lazy chunk that mounts late under load, presenting as the row **unmoved** at `top: 883` in an 844-high viewport. `lib/land-at-top.ts`'s own docblock already names that signature, so the two budgets (`LANDING_WAIT_MS` / `LANDING_WATCH_MS`) are where to look: the question is whether the WAIT budget is still short of what two Playwright workers plus a Vite dev server do to a cold chunk. Not diagnosed.
  - **And a third of the same class**, measured the same evening: `e2e/hero-lift.spec.ts`'s _"the height genuinely interpolates rather than snapping"_ failed once in a full local run and once more in six, then went **12/12 on the branch and 12/12 on an untouched base** — like-for-like arms, no separation, and CI's `e2e (preview)` leg passed it independently. So **three specs now share one shape**: a value sampled mid-animation or mid-arrival, on a box laid out by a lazy chunk, under two workers and a dev server on the same core. That is the entry worth acting on rather than any one of them — the class is "a timing assertion competing with the machine that runs it", and the fix is a sampling strategy (poll for a monotone sequence, don't spot-read a frame), not three tolerance bumps. Until then: **match your arms before attributing one of these to a diff**, which ADR-0200 §1 records costing an hour the first time.
  - **And a FOURTH, now with the arms measured on both sides** (2026-08-22): `e2e/day-swipe.spec.ts`'s _"rightward is the next day and leftward is the previous one (Trip mode)"_ carried **two** independent failures, and only one of them was in this class. The one that reddened CI on `main` at `7d4e5ba` — `no day heading to swipe from` — was diagnosed to root cause and **fixed** (`e2e/measure.ts`'s `stableBox`, plus a peek-safe `> .day-page` selector; 0/30 with the fix against 5/28 without, at four workers). Do not re-attribute it. What is left is a `Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded` at roughly **1 run in 15**, present identically before the fix and on an untouched base at `2950db9`, and localised only this far: the marks show `boot`+first swipe+poll all inside 3.6s of a 5.1s test, and then the SECOND `swipeDay` never returns — so the hang is a box read or a CDP touch dispatch on a surface that has just swapped the day, not a slow budget anywhere. It does not reproduce running that test alone (0/8), only under the whole file's load. `stableBox` now bounds every box read it makes, so if the hang survives that, the remaining suspect is `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` blocking on a busy renderer — which would put it in a _different_ class from the three above, and is the reason it is worth localising rather than folding into their sweep.

## The transit form's two dates read as two flights (owner report, 2026-08-23)

[ADR-0203](decisions/0203-a-journey-has-one-date-and-its-arrival-is-a-clock.md) is **Accepted and built** (2026-08-23), design reference [`mockups/a-journey-has-one-date-v1.html`](../mockups/a-journey-has-one-date-v1.html). The rail is the schedule step for every journey: one absolute date, arrivals as clocks with derived relative days, one step per journey, and the nodes behind you summarising as you fill. §1–§5, §7 and §9 are in; the ADR's build log carries what the wiring changed. Two pieces are left, plus one decision that is not this ADR's:

- **§8's place suggestion in the form** needs the trip's existing transport legs derived and handed to `PLACE_SOURCES`; the sources themselves are done and specced.
- **`reachesDestination`'s name tier matches by substring in both directions**, so a one- or two-character Place-lite "reaches" almost any destination (`'a'` is inside `'Iceland'`). Shipped behaviour with the readiness count as its first consumer, pinned by a spec rather than narrowed inside this ADR — but it bounds §5's "can only remove a suggestion" to endpoints that are really placeable. Worth a decision of its own.

What the build already carried, kept for the reader who wonders whether it was considered:

- **The eight sections, and §1–§3 are the ones the report is about.** One absolute date per journey with every later moment a clock plus a derived relative day (`ValueToken`'s existing `vt-word`); the derivation on **instants** rather than wall clocks (a westward crossing is the case that separates them); the journey drawn as `day-join.css`'s own `.journey` with `ConnectionBand` unchanged. New CSS is a rail and two controls, in a new `ui/domain/journey-field.css` beside `route-field.css`.
- **Three shipped defects to fix on the way, each measured rather than inferred:** an empty stop cannot be removed (`pp-clear` renders `{current && …}` — the fix is a `removable` role on `PlacePicker`, **not** a second ✕, which ADR-0159 §5 already refused); that button's target is **32px** against ADR-0017's 44px floor; and `PLACE_SEARCH_KIND` has one member, so a train or bus endpoint searches the whole corpus (`findPlace`'s own comment names it). The third is one enum row plus one axis on `BOOKING_TYPE_PROFILE`, and it retires `findPlace`'s `type === FLIGHT` conditional.
- **`reachesDestination` gets exported from `@waypoint/shared`** — one line — so §5's date filter and §8's place suggestion read the same predicate the Plan readiness check does. `legCount` also stops being one number for both journeys (§6).
- **Three device questions, all wired as controls in the mockup with the recommendation as the default:** whether `באותו יום` shows always or only when it differs, whether the date suggestion is a pill or a pre-filled value with a latch, and whether `הלוך ושוב` is pre-offered from the trip's readiness. The last one is where the owner's approval was explicitly conditional, so it wants glass before it ships.
- **A cross-trip place memory ("the airport you usually fly from") is deferred with its reason**: `Place` is `@@unique([tripId, googlePlaceId])` and the schema comment states the deliberate reason. It is one more row in §8's source table when it comes, and the gap is only the very first endpoint of a first trip.

## §9 compacted a form that already fitted (owner, 2026-08-24)

Fixed the same day; [ADR-0203](decisions/0203-a-journey-has-one-date-and-its-arrival-is-a-clock.md) §9's threshold corrected in place and named (`SUMMARISE_FROM_NODES`). The finding worth keeping: **the ADR's own fold table already said 0–1 stops need no compaction**, and the code applied it from one stop up. A measurement that justifies a feature also bounds it, and nothing was checking the bound.

Still open, and deliberately not guessed at:

- **§10's time panel renders 96 rows to show five, and the test suite is where that first bit.** Each picker open builds the whole rotated list plus its day divider, so a spec driving several of them costs ~1.3-1.5s locally and roughly twice that on CI hardware — which is how one passed locally at ~2.4s and timed out at the 5s default in CI. **Four specs in `BookingSheet.test.tsx` now carry explicit budgets**, and that is the honest ceiling for this approach: a fifth means making the list cheaper (a window around the offered slot, or virtualising) rather than raising another number. Nothing is wrong on a phone, where one panel opens at a time — this is a suite cost that happens to be a fair proxy for a real one.

- **Whether the collapse should fire on FINISHING a node or on OPENING another.** Today it fires the moment a node's last time is picked, in the same frame the time panel closes — so a large layout change is already underway and the collapse rides with it. The owner's "and even then I'm not sure" is about that movement at two and three stops, where compaction does earn its height. The cheap alternative is to collapse when another node opens instead: same end state, movement attached to a tap already being made. Wants a device pass at two stops before either is chosen.

## Three more on the rail, and §6 lands (owner, 2026-08-24)

All three fixed the same day. [ADR-0203](decisions/0203-a-journey-has-one-date-and-its-arrival-is-a-clock.md) §6 is **built** and [ADR-0184](decisions/0184-an-edge-can-be-a-window.md) §2 amended; design reference [`mockups/the-way-back-is-its-own-route-v1.html`](../mockups/the-way-back-is-its-own-route-v1.html). Kept here for the findings that outlive the fixes:

- **§6 was never three layers, it was a NUMBER.** Both earlier deferrals named the draft, the errand field and `legBooking`. What actually made it hard is that `legCount` was one number for both journeys and nine call sites read it — the field's "a different number of stops" is the part no amount of `reversed` could express. `pointsFor(side)`/`legCountFor(side)` now carry it.
- **A hoisted `function` does not rescue a `const` it closes over.** `legCountFor` was reachable early; `backPoints` was not, so every round trip threw `cannot access before initialization` and eight specs caught it at once.
- **Two intents on one callback is a bug waiting.** "Give me my own route" and "I cleared the last stop" arrived as the same call, so emptying the return's list restored the stop just removed. And `[]` had to stop meaning "nothing remembered": it is a real answer — diverged, and the way home is direct.

Still open, both narrow:

- **The refusal's scroll target on a reordered window line.** An end edge's two tokens now render floor-then-deadline, so the marked `Field` is no longer the line's first token. The mark is on the box, which is correct, but the scroll-into-view aims at that box and wants a device pass.
- **An open-jaw round trip** — different endpoints, not just different stops (in to Tbilisi, out of Kutaisi). Declared out of scope for §6 rather than half-built; it needs the return's two ends to become their own fields.

## Six field reports on the shipped journey rail (owner, 2026-08-23)

All six fixed the same day, [ADR-0203](decisions/0203-a-journey-has-one-date-and-its-arrival-is-a-clock.md) amended with §10 and two §3 corrections, design reference [`mockups/a-clock-follows-the-one-before-it-v1.html`](../mockups/a-clock-follows-the-one-before-it-v1.html). Kept here for the two findings that outlive the fixes:

- **A refusal on a node the rail had summarised was undeliverable, and the form said nothing.** _"I'm unable to continue to the next step… Perhaps I had missing fields in the form, but I received no nudge so idk."_ A summarised node renders no `Field`, and `useFormErrors` needs that box to render the message, nudge it and scroll to it. Two bugs stacked: the node has to open when it is marked, **and** the delivery has to happen after the commit, because marking a field can be what puts it on screen. The second half is a fix to ADR-0150's mechanism and applies to **any** form with conditional rendering — pinned by a spec that fails without it. Only reachable from three nodes up, which is why every §9 spec missed it and a layover found it.
- **The sixth call site is the finding.** The return leg was offered the trip's _first_ day because `tripEdgeFor` got the outbound's endpoints on both sides — and five call sites already wrote `side === 'out' ? routePoints : reversed` inline before this one read past it. Named once now (`pointsFor`). Root rule 8's failure mode, caught in the shape the rule predicts.

Still open, and both narrow:

- **`scroll-snap` on a rotated time list, on a device.** `.tp-list` carries `scroll-snap-type: y proximity` and §10's day divider is the first row in it that is not a snap target. Nothing observed; it is simply past what a desktop render can settle.
- **An edit's type disclosure can open inside the pinned header.** A sticky element taller than its scrollport stops sticking, so the tall case degrades to scrolling rather than pinning most of the sheet, and it collapses on the next tap. Left as that deliberately — worth revisiting only if the grid grows.

## Three mockups could not be scrolled at all (owner report, 2026-08-23)

- **Fixed 2026-08-23, and the class is what matters.** ADR-0200 §1 put `html, body { overflow: clip }` in `styles/tokens.css` (the app's document never scrolls; an inner `.body` does), and **every mockup that inlines that sheet applies the rule to its own page** — which has no inner scroller and is several viewports tall. `clip` is not a scroll container, so the root's `scrollHeight` collapses to `clientHeight` and everything below the fold is **unreachable rather than hidden**; the obvious check (`scrollHeight > clientHeight`) then answers **no**, exactly like a page that fits, which is why four renders and a measurement pass all missed it and a human trying to scroll found it in a second. Measured at **9713px of content against 700px reachable** on `a-journey-has-one-date-v1.html`, 6650 and 5084 on `a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v1/v2.html` — **the latter two are the promoted build specs for [ADR-0116](decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md) §2d and [ADR-0200](decisions/0200-a-day-steps-with-a-swipe-and-the-shell-stops-scrolling.md), so that documentation was inert for a day.** All three now end with an `html, body { overflow: visible }` chrome block; the template carries it and `.claude/skills/design-mockups/references/pitfalls.md` carries the trap.
- **What is still open: nothing checks it.** `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` is the one place that knows a mockup inlines app CSS, and the renderer is the one place that opens a page — either could assert that the document scrolls (or that no app rule reaches `html`/`body`) and neither does. Worth doing when the next sheet-level rule lands rather than pre-emptively: the general form is "an inlined app rule that targets the document root is a mockup bug", and `overflow` is the only instance so far.

## Selecting a stop and seeing its path want the same canvas (owner report, 2026-08-27)

**All three reports are shipped** (branch `claude/map-polyline-rendering-ux-su4lqo`,
[session note](planning/2026-08-27-the-line-draws-and-the-card-folds.md)). Two were bugs —
the amber route sometimes not rendering at all on a stop tap, and the drawn line losing its last
turn or ending a block short of its pin as the camera pulls back —
[ADR-0206](decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) §AC7. The third was the
design question, settled with the owner in the thread rather than on a mockup, and built as two
halves: the camera **frames the leg** rather than the stop at the end of it (ADR-0206 §AC8), and
the place card **folds to its identity row**, sticky for as long as you are on the tab
([ADR-0122](decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md)'s 2026-08-27 amendment).

- **What is left is a device pass, and it is the honest kind.** Three feel calls this was decided
  without a phone for: whether the caret beside the `✕` is findable at 16px on a real thumb (the
  fallback named in the ADR is a swipe-down, deliberately not built — it competes with the track's
  horizontal snap and the sheet's drag region); whether folding the whole track reads as one choice
  or as three broken slides; and whether `DOT_BELOW` is the right floor for framing a leg, which is
  the one number here that wants a real day's walk under it rather than a unit test.
- **Not built, on purpose: a swipe-down to fold**, and persisting the fold across visits to the
  tab. Both are argued in ADR-0122's amendment §6/§4 — reopen them from there rather than from
  scratch.

## Three more off the shipped routes work (owner reports, 2026-08-27)

All three fixed on `claude/map-polyline-rendering-ux-su4lqo`; recorded because two of them name a
class rather than a case.

- **The camera re-centred on a leg it had refused to frame** — [ADR-0206](decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md)
  §AC8's correction. The selection effect and the band effect each derived the camera's subject for
  themselves, and `framePath` is allowed to say **no**; on a leg past the floor they disagreed and
  the map landed on empty ground. **The class:** two effects deriving one fact is one fact too
  many — the one that acts records what it did, and the other reads it.
- **A mode the gate refuses vanished the journey block** — §AM10. §D4's "absent is absent" is right
  for a mode the app picked and wrong for one a person picked, and the block is the only thing
  carrying the mode control, so the change could not be undone where it was made. **The class:**
  this is §AM6's failure (the declaration's) in the sibling case, which is worth remembering the
  next time something legitimately has no estimate.
- **The board marked you late for a departure the day view was printing as ⁦00:30⁩** — §AJ3.
  §AJ2's clamp lived inside `dayJourney` and the board reads `heroLeaveBy` directly.
  **The class:** `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s "changing a day-surface derivation in `DayView` only",
  reached from the third elevation nobody counts as a day surface.

**Still open, and named as such:** `TRAVEL_FIT_TOLERANCE_SECONDS`' flat bar (the backlog's
"proportional half" line, unchanged), and whether the ceilings themselves are right for a driving
trip whose legs are routinely near them — the Iceland day that produced two of these reports has
legs long enough that framing a leg is refused more often than it fires, which is a device-pass
question and not a code one.

## The transit row vanished for the third time, on its third cause (owner report, 2026-08-31)

- **Fixed, and the fix is the class rather than the case** — [ADR-0206](decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md)
  §AW. A ⁦50 m⁩ leg reading `הליכה · ~1 דק׳` was switched to `נסיעה`, which answers ⁦12⁩ seconds over
  the same distance; the 2026-08-26 sub-minute floor rounded that to nought minutes and deleted the
  journey, and the mode control lives on the journey. The owner named the class themselves
  (_"that happened before on different causes"_): this is the **third** disappearance of one row on
  its third cause, after §AM6's declaration and §AM10's ceiling. So the new `UNTIMED` arm is keyed
  on **somebody having picked the mode**, not on the sub-minute number — which also closes the
  member nobody had reached, a chosen mode the SERVER's gate refused (`sameClusterOnly` against a
  point missing from the cluster set) and that therefore had no estimate and no ceiling to blame.
  **The class:** a floor that suppresses noise must ask who made the noise; the app's own guess and
  a person's choice are not the same input, and the surface that took the choice owes the way back.
- **What is left is the distance on a leg that never routed** (§AW5). `distanceFor` gives the crow
  only to a declared or refused leg, so an `UNTIMED` leg reached by the no-estimate road prints its
  mode and its sentence with no kilometres. Widening that fallback puts crow numbers into the day's
  header for legs that never route, which is a decision about the total (§AP2's "the header counts
  what the list shows") and not about this row.

## Agent tooling

- **Nothing enforces the skill pins** ([ADR-0201](decisions/0201-vendored-skills-are-advice-and-they-are-pinned.md), 2026-08-21) — `node .claude/vendor/sync-skills.mjs --check` exists and exits 1 when `.claude/skills` has drifted from `skills.json`, and nothing runs it. The failure it guards is quiet: someone edits a vendored skill in place to fix one sentence, the next `--bump` reverts it, and the reason the edit existed is gone. `.claude/**` is outside `pnpm format`/`lint` (`.prettierignore`), so this needs its own CI step rather than a hook onto an existing one. Cheap, and worth waiting until the pins have actually been bumped once — the check is only as useful as the update habit it protects.

- **`impeccable`'s anti-pattern detector is the one thing worth reconsidering from the refused set** ([ADR-0201](decisions/0201-vendored-skills-are-advice-and-they-are-pinned.md)) — mechanical checks on a rendered page are exactly what [ADR-0175](decisions/0175-the-mockup-procedure-is-a-skill.md) exists for, and the skill was refused on its 3.5 MB of bundled browser JS and its competing design language, **not on merit**. If it comes back it comes back as the detector alone — `scripts/detector/` pointed at our own tokens, with the design-language half left upstream — which is a port rather than a vendoring, and wants measuring against what rendering a mockup already catches before anyone spends the day on it.

- **Nothing in the app ever offers to install it** — **BUILT (2026-08-24; [ADR-0204](decisions/0204-the-install-ask-is-three-doors-and-a-permanent-home.md), mockup [`install-is-three-doors-v1`](../mockups/install-is-three-doors-v1.html), [session note](planning/2026-08-24-the-install-ask-is-three-doors.md)).** [ADR-0007](decisions/0007-platform-pwa.md) made the app installable in 2026-07 and said nothing about asking; nothing had asked since, and the only sentence mentioning the home screen was `notifyNeedsInstall`, buried in the notification settings to explain a **push** blocker. Shipped: `lib/install.ts` (the `INSTALL_PATH` closed set, the `beforeinstallprompt` capture, the `waypoint:install:asked` budget, and `isInstalled()`/`isWebKit()` **extracted** out of `push.ts` rather than copied); `InstallAskBanner` for the two unprompted doors; `InstallSheet` with its three platform bodies; `InstallSettings` as the permanent home, which let `NotificationSettings` stop re-teaching the share gesture; and `PushAskBanner` **answering** `NEEDS_INSTALL` instead of returning `null` — the design finding, and the highest-intent install moment in the product, which was a wall. `ui/AppMark.tsx` came out of the same change: the mark was already inlined byte-identically in `Login` and `JoinTrip`, differing only in its gradient-id prefix, which is exactly the tell that SVG ids are document-global.
  - **Still open, and named as a guess:** ADR-0204 §5's budget of **two** unprompted asks. It ships as 2 and the mockup carries a 1-3 control, because a mockup cannot settle it — it wants a real-usage pass. Same for whether door C belongs outside the budget: if that reads as a stretch it is counted, and the change is one number.
  - **Unverified on a real device,** and both are field-round questions rather than build ones: the in-app-browser UA sniff (`IN_APP_MARKERS`) against the chat apps invites actually travel in, and whether iOS Safari's share sheet reads the way the sheet draws it.
- ~~**The day's rows all read as one shape**~~ **BUILT 2026-08-28** ([ADR-0210](decisions/0210-a-day-is-points-lines-and-envelopes.md) with its build log, mockup [`a-day-has-three-shapes-v1.html`](../mockups/a-day-has-three-shapes-v1.html), [session note](planning/2026-08-28-a-day-has-three-shapes.md)). Point / line / envelope, approved and shipped the same day. **What is still open, and both need an owner call rather than code:** (1) whether an edge with **no authored time** should print a clock at all — `מ-00:00` in the report screenshot is an unset pick-up, and §2's bound mark stops hiding it rather than causing it (ADR-0171's question); ~~(2) the hire is named twice one row apart with the same clock~~ **ANSWERED AND BUILT 2026-08-28** — owner: _"for consistency I'm voting no, same as hotel check in/check out days"_. Right about the hotel, and the strip's `!stayRowIds.has(e.id)` filter is the mechanism; but the hire does **not** take the hotel's treatment, because a stay's row carries the hotel NAME while a hire's row carries the PLACE and the strip carries the COMPANY — so the row stays and the **clock** gives way (the strip prints the span count). Named once, timed once, and it generalises to any ambient span whose edge holds its own row.
- **The mockup's inlined CSS is now deliberately stale, and the drift rule would tell you to fix it** — do NOT re-run `inline-app-css.mjs` on `a-day-has-three-shapes-v1.html`. Its block is the sheet as it stood before the build, which is what makes its before/after columns mean anything; refreshing it makes both columns draw the fix (`pitfalls.md`'s "grades its own homework"). Recorded here because the standing drift rule points the other way, and the next session to run the drift sweep will otherwise 'fix' it.
